## Legibility note

The main text of scans 6–10 is substantially more legible than the compressed contents on scans 3–4, but some cancellations cannot be separated confidently from overwritten letter strokes. The least reliable readings are the mathematical expressions in scan 4, the corrected definition at the top of scan 8, and the amendments to Proposition 1.7 in scan 9. These uncertainties are local; they do not justify treating the entire transcription as verified.

Only the clearly visible upper manuscript numerals 1, 2, and 3 are represented by `\mspage`. The lower-corner sequence and printed archive footers have not been treated as authorial text. Scan 5 is not called blank because three isolated ink numbers are visible. Mirrored show-through is not transcribed. The precise authorship of wrapper annotations and isolated numbers is not established by these images.

The diagram in scan 10 is described because the central arrow’s shaft is uncertain. The unusual sign in the last sentence is rendered by its visible arrangement of strokes, not expanded into a word or replaced by a conventional equivalence sign.

## Source

Grothendieck, A. (n.d.). *La “Longue Marche” à travers la théorie de Galois* [Handwritten manuscript, cote 140-2; supplied scans 1–10]. Université de Montpellier, Institut montpelliérain Alexander Grothendieck. The supplied archival title sheet is marked “s.d.”.

# Legibility and numbering

The main text is moderately legible; the darker additions are often clearer than the underlying pencil. The least reliable regions are the overwritten formulas and primes on scan 17, the revisions in condition b) on scan 19, and the ending of anabélique/anabélien and marginal f) on scan 20. Word counts inside illegible markers are estimates. Some insertion attachment points remain uncertain even where individual words can be read.

The top-centre handwritten numbers 4–8 are tagged with mspage. The separate lower-left sequence 10–19 is retained as margin entries; its hand is not attributed. Printed archive footers are outside the transcription.


# ===== LM_P03 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P03 (scans 21–30): research notes checked against the corrected transcription

Sources: `answers/LM_P03_apparatus.md` (first-pass notes R1–R17) and `second_pass/LM_P03_checked.tex`. The context
from §5 (scan 19 and the witnesses) is that K is a field of finite type over Q (so of characteristic 0),
Γ = Gal(K̄/K), and in 5.1 S is a finite reduced subscheme lying in the 1-dimensional components of X. Notes marked
"(second pass)" are mine; the ideas in them are checks or leads, not new results.

## First-pass notes

**R1. Scans 21–22, unproved claim. Keep (corrected).** "On trouve ainsi un foncteur … dans la «catégorie des groupoïdes
profinis sur Π₁(K)», définie comme au n°4". The first pass read "comme on sait", but the page refers to n°4 for the
target category. A check of functoriality must use the morphisms defined there, including how the commutation
isomorphisms α compose.

**R2. Scan 23, unproved claim. Keep (corrected, proof added).** The page reads "dont l'image *sera* d'ailleurs
nécessairement ouverte donc d'indice fini". (Second pass) Proof: a connected X, locally of finite type over K, has a
closed point x with k(x) finite over K, so the image of π₁(X) → Γ contains the open subgroup Gal(K̄/k(x)). That image
is compact, hence closed, and a closed subgroup containing an open subgroup is open, so it has finite index. The claim
therefore holds for every such X, not only for the X of 5.1.

**R3. Scan 23, Conjecture 5.5, conjecture. Keep.** "La restriction du foncteur précédent … aux schémas projectifs
lisses de dim. ≤ 1 et anabéliens … est pleinement fidèle." The margin beside it now reads with certainty "C'est un peu
faux cf §9". See S1 and S2 below for the convention on S and the status of the conjecture.

**R4. Scan 23, margin, qualification of 5.5. Correct.** The doubtful word is *faux* (confirmed), and the reference is
"cf §9" (the witnesses print "n°9"). §9 does exist: the author's table lists "9. Structure tangentielle en les s ∈ S
(sections d'extensions «de deuxième type»)", ms. p. 34. What exactly is "un peu faux" has to be read in §9; these
pages do not say.

**R5. Scans 23–24, unproved claim. Keep.** The equivalence "Il revient au même de dire ceci" (the *m̄* is now read)
between the groupoid form 5.5 and the pointed form, labelled on the page "Conjecture 5.5 bis" (all witnesses print
"Définition"). Pro is right that the comparison has to use morphisms up to a chosen path, "(ou classe de chemins)
f(ξ) ≃ ξ′". Strictly base-point-preserving maps are not enough.

**R6. Scan 24, unproved claim. Keep.** "Ces conjectures se réduisent à la théorie de Galois, pour des X de dim 0." For
connected X = Spec L, with L finite separable over K, full faithfulness is the Galois correspondence between finite
étale K-schemes and finite Γ-sets. Nothing in the corrected text changes this.

**R7. Scan 24, unproved claim. Keep (argument added).** (Second pass) For X projective, smooth and connected over K,
K′ = H⁰(X, O̲_X) is a finite field extension, because it is finite-dimensional, reduced and has no nontrivial
idempotents. X is geometrically connected over K′, and X_K̄ is the disjoint union of the [K′:K] conjugates of X ⊗_{K′} K̄,
which all have the same genus. So the two formulations in the parenthesis agree.

**R8. Scan 24, unproved claim. Keep (sharpened).** "On voit aisément (prenant X′ = Spec(K), X = P¹_K ou courbe
elliptique sur K) qu'elles deviennent fausses sinon". (Second pass, medium–high) For P¹_K, the map π₁(P¹_K) → Γ is an
isomorphism, so all K-points induce the same morphism over Γ: faithfulness fails. For an elliptic curve E, the
sections up to conjugacy are H¹(Γ, T(E)), and the K-points enter through the Kummer map E(K) → E(K)^∧ ↪ H¹(Γ, T(E)).
Over a field of finite type over Q, E(K) is finitely generated, so this map is injective; but E(K)^∧ is uncountable
when the rank is positive, so fullness fails.

**R9. Scan 25, conjecture (expectation). Keep (with ink detail).** "On trouvera des conjectures (p.ex) pour X courbe
géom. connexe sur K de genre 1 (resp. 0), pourvu que S soit de degré ≥ 1 (resp. ≥ 3)." The ink shows the genus digits
rewritten (1 over 0 and 0 over 1), and the degree condition replacing a struck "restriction de S :". These are exactly
the hyperbolic cases 2g − 2 + deg S > 0, where deg S counts geometric points.

**R10. Scans 25–26, (6.1), unproved claim. Keep (corrected).** The labels "surj. sur Hom" (i_s) and "fidèle" (q_s), and
Π₁(s) ≃ Π₁(D_s). The lower map is j_s, not f_s, and the composite on the curved arrow is p_s or ρ_s, which the ink
does not decide. (Second pass) In characteristic 0, a henselian discretely valued field and its completion have the
same absolute Galois group (Krasner's lemma), so "(ou le complété, si on y tient)" changes nothing. Surjectivity of
Gal(K̄_s/K_s) → Gal(k̄(s)/k(s)) and injectivity of Gal(k̄(s)/k(s)) → Γ (k(s) is finite over K) are standard.

**R11. Scan 26, unproved claim. Keep (corrected).** "dont le noyau, on le sait par Kummer, est canoniquement isom. à
T_?(k̄_s) ≃ T(K̄_s) [≃ T(K̄)]". (Second pass) Pro's worry about residue characteristic does not arise: since
char k(s) = 0, all inertia is tame and Kummer theory over K_s^{nr} identifies it with Ẑ(1). The comparisons of μ_n
between K̄, K̄_s and k̄_s come from the chosen K-embedding and from reduction (bijective because n is invertible), so
the isomorphism is canonical once ξ_s is chosen. The subscript of the first T remains unread.

**R12. Scan 27, bracketed lemma, unproved claim. Keep (reading corrected).** The defined notion is introduced as
"[qu'on] peut appeler le π₁ relatif"; the first pass had "qu'on appelle le". Pro's proof route is right. To identify
the kernels at ξ₁ and ξ₂ over the same η, lift an arrow ξ₁ → ξ₂ to one mapping to id_η: connectedness gives an arrow,
and surjectivity on Hom corrects it. Two lifts differ by a kernel element, which acts trivially because the kernel is
abelian.

**R13. Scan 28, own idea (consistency check). Keep (confirmed and sharpened).** The display reads
"π₁(Π₁(D_S)/Π₁(S)) ≃ j_S*(𝒯(K))" without a star (CSG agrees; IUT prints D_S*). (Second pass, high on the mathematics) As
written, the left side is trivial, because Π₁(D_S) → Π₁(S) is an equivalence (each D_s is henselian, cf. the ≃ in
(6.1)). The isomorphism with j_S*(𝒯(K)) needs D_S*, as on scan 27. This is a slip in the manuscript, and the
transcription keeps the ink.

**R14. Scans 29–30, own idea. Keep (confirmed).** The cancelled 4°) reads "où σ est fidèle, ρ surjectif sur les Hom
et à noyau abélien". The final version has a) σ an isomorphism on π₀ and epi on Hom with abelian kernel, b) ψ
faithful, and c) ρ faithful. (Second pass) In the geometric situation the final version is the correct one: σ_S is
surjective on Hom with kernel the inertia, and ρ_S is faithful under hyperbolicity (see R15). So the cancelled draft
had the roles of σ and ρ exchanged.

**R15. Scan 30, bracketed conditions c)–d), unproved claim (expectation). Keep (made concrete).** "[ c) ρ est fidèle
... d) φ est épi mod. gr. finis sur les Aut ... ]". (Second pass, medium) d) is the abstract form of scan 23's "image
… ouverte donc d'indice fini" for π₁(U) → Γ. c) amounts to injectivity of the decomposition groups
Gal(K̄_s/K_s) → π₁(X∖S). This holds when the cusp inertia Ẑ(1) injects into the geometric fundamental group, that is,
when no geometric component of X∖S meeting S is an affine line (π₁(A¹_K̄) = 1 in characteristic 0), which is where
the anabelian hypothesis enters.

**R16. Scan 30, unpursued implication. Keep (reading corrected).** The page reads "(qui est *même* l'identité … mais il
vaut mieux oublier qu'il en *soit* ainsi)". The inference stands: α is to be treated as structure, so morphisms of
such systems must carry compatibility with α. That is what the next pages begin to define (CSG: "un morphisme de Π′
dans Π est un système de trois foncteurs …").

**R17. Scans 23–24, own idea. Keep.** Testing full faithfulness on morphisms Spec(K) → X is exactly the section-
conjecture part of 5.5 (see S2). R8 gives the two failures outside the anabelian range.

## Second-pass notes

**S1. Scan 23, Conjecture 5.5, unpursued implication (a convention to fix).** The parenthesis takes "S est la réunion
des composantes de dim 0", which does not fit 5.1 b), where S lies in the 1-dimensional components. In 5.5, therefore,
"anabélien" can only mean that the curve components are geometrically of genus ≥ 2, with the isolated points kept as
0-dimensional objects. Scan 24 ("elles ne concernent que des X tels que les comp. connexes de X_K̄ soient de genre
≥ 2") confirms this. Confidence: medium.

**S2. Scan 23, Conjecture 5.5 and the margin "C'est un peu faux cf §9", own idea with literature.** For dominant
morphisms of hyperbolic curves over a sub-p-adic field, which includes every field of finite type over Q, the Hom form
of 5.5 is a theorem of S. Mochizuki ("The local pro-p anabelian geometry of curves", Invent. Math. 138 (1999)). The
part with 0-dimensional sources, that is L-points of X, is the section conjecture, which is still open. Whether §9
("Structure tangentielle en les s ∈ S") is about this part has to be read there; this is a lead, not a finding.
Confidence: high for the literature, low for the link to §9.

**S3. Scan 21, §5.4, unpursued implication (reading-dependent).** In "plus gén. à ∀ schéma X loc de t.f. sur S|ξ, on
associe … Π₁(X) … muni du [hom.] canonique Π₁(X) → Π₁(K)", if the glyph is S (the witnesses' reading, favoured by the
ink), the base of the generalisation does not match the displayed map to Π₁(K) ≃ [Tors(Γ)], since with base S the
natural target would be Π₁(S). Worth checking against how §5.4 is used later. Confidence: low.

**S4. Scan 26, "(i.e.|ou) sur Π₁(K)", own idea.** The two readings say different things. j_s: Π₁(s) → Π₁(K) is
faithful but not an equivalence unless k(s) = K. A structure over Π₁(s) gives one over Π₁(K) by composing with j_s,
but not conversely, so "ou" (a weaker alternative) fits the mathematics better than "i.e.". The ink does not decide
(confidence: low).

**S5. Scan 28, bottom margin, unproved claim.** "… des comp. conn. de X∖S, ou encore de X" is true under 5.1 b): S
is a finite set of closed points on the 1-dimensional components, and removing finitely many closed points from an
irreducible smooth curve leaves a nonempty irreducible open subset. So π₀(X∖S) = π₀(X) (confidence: high).

**S6. Scans 28–30, unpursued implication.** The cancelled draft built the system over Π₀, the groupoid of components
("Ce group. Π₀ … des comp. conn. …"), with ψ: Π_D → Π₀ faithful and φ: Π_U → Π₀. The final version uses
Π_e = Π₁(Spec K) instead. These pages do not say whether the final data (a)–d)) still determine Π₀; condition a)
only fixes π₀(Π_{D*}) ≅ π₀(Π_D), the points of S. Confidence: medium.

Source identification supplied with the task: Grothendieck, A. (1981). *La Longue Marche à travers la théorie de Galois* [Handwritten manuscript; Montpellier archive, cote 140-2, pages 1–292; supplied scans 41–50]. The transcription is based on the ten supplied JPEGs, `LM_P05_scan041.jpg` through `LM_P05_scan050.jpg`, not on a published transcription or an external mathematical source. No external literature was consulted for these notes.

The scan number is the primary locator. The printed archive footer lies outside the manuscript frame and is not transcribed. Two handwritten numbering sequences are visible; their different positions are not conflated:

## 3. Legibility and editorial limitations

The main prose and most large displayed formulas are substantially more legible than the marginal revisions. The least secure passages are the overwritten last equation on scan 41, both margins and several labels of the prism on scan 42, the short left and right notes on scan 44, and the long rotated/oblique notes on scan 45. The short annotation beside (a) on scan 48 is not recovered. Isolated primes, a Latin `p` versus a handwritten `\rho`, `k` versus `\kappa`, and the form of a superscript on a map are recurrent risks; doubtful instances have not been decided from the expected mathematics.

The transcription is a provisional diplomatic reading, not a critically established text. `\illegible{n}` counts are estimates of word-sized units; a crossed-out formula may occupy a single such unit without being literally one word. `\unsure{...}` does not certify that the first alternative is correct. A continuous unmarked phrase is the adopted visual reading, but its presence should not be treated as a guarantee against transcription error.

The crowded prism on scan 42 is described inside `\diagram` instead of being supplied with invented connections. For several small squares, the readable objects and arrows are drawn in `tikz-cd`, while the location and visible direction of an interior curved arrow are described in brackets; an unambiguous 2-cell with reconstructed endpoints is not substituted for it. Circles and separating strokes are recorded with `\figure`. Ordinary boxes are represented with standard LaTeX boxes; the multiple outlines around the homomorphism on scan 48 and the element on scan 49 are retained.

The delivered `.tex` is intentionally a fragment: no preamble, document class, or definitions of the requested editorial macros are included. A temporary wrapper with simple definitions for those macros, standard mathematical LaTeX, and `tikz-cd` successfully processed the fragment to DVI. This checks syntax only, not the handwriting readings; the wrapper and test output are not part of the ZIP. `ampersand replacement=\&` is used in diagrams so that their matrices remain valid inside the `\diagram{...}` macro argument.

## 4. Research notes — English; separate from the transcription

All items below are local to these ten scans. “Unproved claim” means that a proof is not supplied in the readable passage, not that the claim is false or unproved elsewhere. No idea below is represented as new or as an established result. The only fully readable explicit research question is identified below; the incomplete question in the margin of scan 44 is not silently reconstructed.

### R01 — Scan 41, approximately ll. 12–21
**Kind: unproved claim / explicitly qualified expectation.**

The phrase “sauf erreur” qualifies the reduction of the two compatibilities to the kernel-map equation and the displayed equation involving the conjugating elements. A verification needs the directions of the commutation isomorphisms and the convention for `int` fixed before cancelling factors. The distinction between `p` and `\rho` in the overwritten bottom line must first be settled from the image or further source evidence, not from the desired identity.

### R02 — Scan 42, left margin beside the prism
**Kind: unproved claim.**

“Comme les … sont injectifs” is used to argue that the local map is determined by the global map and a conjugating element. A proof should separate uniqueness, which uses injectivity, from existence, which requires a conjugated image to lie in the specified subgroup. If continuity is part of the intended category, it also belongs in the existence argument rather than following from the displayed abstract factorization alone.

### R03 — Scan 42, right margin beside the prism
**Kind: unproved claim; reading-dependent.**

The note appears to propose taking the two families of `\gamma`'s “égaux à 1,” but the choice-of-object explanation is only partly readable. The issue worth checking is whether the required choices can be made simultaneously with the kernel identifications and all the other comparison isomorphisms. This is a provisional research lead, not a secure reconstruction of the marginal assertion.

### R04 — Scans 42–43, final paragraph of 42 and first paragraph of 43
**Kind: unproved claim.**

The manuscript distinguishes commutativity on an image subgroup from commutativity on “\(\Gamma\) tout entier,” invoking the “relation plus précise \((*)\).” A proof should identify that referenced relation unambiguously and check which faces of the prism give each equality. An equality on the image of a structural homomorphism must not be promoted to an equality on all of the target merely by redrawing the diagram.

### R05 — Scan 43, lower half; scan 44, opening definition
**Kind: unpursued implication.**

The proposed object data combine a map to `\Gamma`, an indexed family of subgroups, and identifications involving `T(\bar K)`. It remains worth spelling out the exact dictionary between these data and the preceding groupoid presentation, including which identifications are part of the object rather than choices. The direction of the last displayed isomorphism on scan 43 is a transcription issue to resolve before using that dictionary.

### R06 — Scan 44, condition 2°, approximately ll. 8–11
**Kind: unproved claim.**

The target index is asserted to be “unique!” A proof needs a hypothesis on the indexed subgroup family that forces this uniqueness; no such hypothesis is stated in the displayed condition itself. Earlier manuscript context may supply it, so the claim should be checked against that context rather than rejected or repaired here.

### R07 — Scan 44, the same uniqueness condition
**Kind: own idea. Confidence: high for the conditional diagnostic, not for its applicability to the intended geometric objects.**

As a test of the displayed definition alone, allow two distinct indices to name the same subgroup with the same kernel identification. The inclusion condition would then be unable by itself to choose a unique index. The reason for this test is to locate the missing distinctness or geometric restriction, not to claim a counterexample to a category whose earlier hypotheses have not been supplied.

### R08 — Scan 44, left marginal note beside the ramification condition
**Kind: unproved claim.**

The note says that the integer “est aussi unique.” A proof must show that the scalar action of the allowed integers on the identified kernel distinguishes those integers, after the comparison maps and conjugations have been fixed. The readable page does not give that argument separately.

### R09 — Scan 44, upper left margin
**Kind: conjecture (explicit question; incomplete reading).**

A question beginning “Mais \(\alpha\) … ?!” is visible, but its intervening words are not recovered. It would be unsafe to turn this into a claim that `\alpha` is redundant, determined, or arbitrary. The research task is first to recover the question itself from better source evidence.

### R10 — Scan 44, paragraph below the horizontal separator
**Kind: unpursued implication.**

“Je m'aperçois qu'il vaut mieux remplacer” introduces the change from the conjugating elements to their inverses and a reversed orientation of the commutation isomorphism. The associated composition and change-of-choice formulas should be derived afresh under that stated convention. This is a reason to keep the earlier formulas diplomatic, not a licence to rewrite them retrospectively.

### R11 — Scan 44, bottom, continuing on scan 45, top
**Kind: unproved claim.**

The text proposes recovering the intermediate groupoid by “factorisation canonique” and describes a factor as “fidèle.” A proof should construct both factors, verify the stated effects on `\pi_0` and `\pi_1`, and specify the sense in which the construction is canonical. Its compatibility with the comparison data is also needed if it is to replace the four-cornered diagram at the level of morphisms.

### R12 — Scan 45, upper left rotated margin
**Kind: unproved claim; partly reading-dependent.**

The relative fundamental-group datum is said to form a “syst. local,” with a Kummer identification expressed using a pullback. The precise typeface and some symbols in the marginal formula remain uncertain. A proof would need to establish the descent/transport of the kernels and the naturality of the identification, not just identify each kernel separately.

### R13 — Scan 45, lower left oblique margin
**Kind: unpursued implication.**

The margin associates the ramification datum with an application from a component set to `\mathbb N^*`. This suggests checking how the componentwise integers transform under composition and under changes of representatives. The presence of the later character factor on scan 50 makes that compatibility a substantive check, rather than a matter of multiplying labels without regard to the comparison maps.

### R14 — Scans 45–46, paragraph on morphisms and its cancelled continuation
**Kind: unproved claim.**

The text describes comparison isomorphisms of the two functors and “deux conditions de compatibilité sur les deux carrés.” A complete formulation should write both conditions with their actual sources, targets, and order of whiskering. The cancelled reference to `k,k'` should be examined as evidence of a revision in the required data, not silently restored as an extra axiom.

### R15 — Scan 46, middle and lower paragraphs
**Kind: unproved claim.**

Changing the chosen isomorphisms or reference objects is said to change the resulting homomorphisms by “autom. int.” or to determine replacements “à aut. int. près.” A proof should give the comparison element explicitly and verify the effect of composing two changes of reference data. This is also the check needed before passing to an exterior description in the following pages.

### R16 — Scans 47–48, exterior-group discussion and explicit question
**Kind: conjecture (explicit question).**

The central question is: “mais comment en termes de groupes extérieurs exprimer les données de Kummer \(k_i\) ?” The challenge stated here is not merely to retain the homomorphism classes, but to express the additional Kummer data in that language. The subsequent return to systems of diagrams should be checked for an intrinsic answer to this question rather than presumed to supply one automatically.

### R17 — Scans 47–48, the same exterior-group question
**Kind: own idea. Confidence: medium; motivated by the explicit retention of the kernel identifications in the diagram presentation.**

A candidate formulation would retain an exterior homomorphism together with conjugacy classes of the kernel injections and explicitly transported `T(\bar K)`-identifications. The test is whether this extra marking makes the notion independent of reference-object choices while still recovering the displayed comparison data. Neither existence of such a satisfactory category nor equivalence with the manuscript's presentation is claimed here.

### R18 — Scans 48–49, items (a)–(d)
**Kind: unproved claim.**

The phrase “s'explicite par” introduces the global homomorphism, index map, local homomorphisms and two families of commutation data. The converse construction from those data to a groupoid-level morphism is worth writing out, together with the change-of-choice identifications in (a) and (b). The numerical grading of morphisms should be retained as written until the surrounding convention is established.

### R19 — Scan 49, last paragraph
**Kind: unproved claim.**

“\(c,d\) ensemble définissent” leads to the product `\beta_{i'}=p(\alpha_{i'})\alpha`. Verification requires pushing the inner-conjugation datum through `p` and keeping the order of the two factors. A calculation in a noncommutative test case would be more diagnostic of an orientation error than an abelian example.

### R20 — Scan 50, middle, boxed formula
**Kind: unproved claim.**

“on trouve” precedes `f^0_{i'}=d_{i'}\chi(\beta_{i'})\operatorname{id}_{T(\bar K)}`. A derivation should compare the Kummer identifications with the base conjugation in the exact-sequence diagram, checking whether the chosen convention gives the displayed character factor or an inverse. The page calls `\chi` the “caractère canonique”; no additional identification of that character is imposed in the transcription.

### R21 — Scans 44 and 50, ramification-index notation
**Kind: own idea. Confidence: low as a mathematical issue; the author's convention for `\mathbb N` is not established by this extract.**

The visible shift from `\mathbb N^*` to `\mathbb N` suggests a notation check before allowing or excluding a zero scalar in the kernel formula. If the latter set includes zero in the author's convention, testing the zero-kernel-map case would clarify whether some earlier condition rules it out. This is not evidence that the manuscript contains a contradiction.

### R22 — Scans 44, 49–50, conventions and composition
**Kind: own idea. Confidence: medium; the explicit inversion revision and the ordered product of conjugators make this a useful diagnostic.**

One could test the formulas in finite quotients for which the relevant action on a finite quotient of `T(\bar K)` is defined, checking identity morphisms, successive conjugations, and composition of ramification scalars. Such tests could expose a missed inverse, an order reversal, or a misplaced prime. They would not establish a general profinite statement or justify changing a doubtful handwritten symbol.

### R23 — Scan 50, final paragraph and final line
**Kind: unpursued implication / announced next step.**

The page ends by proposing to determine “les 0-morphismes de l'un dans l'autre.” Their actual formula is not present in the supplied ending. The continuation should be checked for the comparison of two index maps and for compatibility with the two commutation data before any composition law or equivalence claim is extracted from this announcement.


# ===== LM_P06 (first-pass notes) =====

# LM_P06 — Notes accompanying the diplomatic transcription

## Source, scope, and locators

The transcription is based only on the ten supplied JPEGs, `LM_P06_scan051.jpg`–`LM_P06_scan060.jpg`. The title, author, date, and archival identification below follow the supplied task brief; they were not independently verified. In the doubt table, confidence concerns the preferred visual reading or the stated placement, not the mathematical plausibility of an emendation.

### Readings deliberately not regularized

On scan 53, the words `du premier dans le second` are followed by `f:\mathcal E'→\mathcal E`; their direction has not been reconciled editorially. On scan 59, both the opening Hom-expression and the displayed section formula visibly place `X` before `X'`; the transcription retains that order. On scan 60, the opening sentence says `sur X`, whereas the nearby displayed groupoid has subscript `X'`. These observations concern the visible text, not a judgment about the intended mathematics.

The mathematical operator labels `int`, `Ker`, `Centr`, `Hom`, `Aut`, `Gal`, `Spec`, `Transp`, `Fix`, and `id` are also preserved. Their likely meanings here are, respectively, inner conjugation, kernel, centralizer, morphisms/homomorphisms, automorphisms, Galois group, spectrum, transporter, pointwise fixer, and identity. The pages do not restate all of their conventions, particularly the direction used for the field transporter. The handwritten `=` in `le = hom` and `il suffirait =` may be a shorthand for “même”; it remains an equality-shaped mark in the transcript.

## 4. Legibility and reliability

This is a provisional manual diplomatic reading, not an authoritative edition. The broad prose and most unaltered displayed equations are substantially more legible than the corrections. The weakest areas are the first scan's opening and membership annotations; the crowded margins and uniqueness clause of scan 54; the first paragraph and interlinear chain of scan 55; the oval addition and cancelled square on scan 56; and the first transporter quotient on scan 58. The last scan's proposed weakening and the exact scope of its final insertion also remain insecure.

The images were read visually, with enlarged crops and rotated views of the densest marginalia. Enlargement does not recover strokes that have been overwritten or lost in the scan. `\illegible{n}` therefore estimates unreadable visual word-units; it does not claim that a crossed mathematical symbol was literally a word. A proposed reading inside `\unsure` should not be cited without its uncertainty marker.

Paragraphs were not completed across missing context. Scan 51 begins in continuation; scan 60 ends in continuation. Large cancellations, including the affine-envelope draft on scan 59, remain in the fragment rather than being silently suppressed. The possible extent of that cancellation is documented above. No added mathematical argument in the research notes below changes a transcription reading.

## 5. Research notes — separate from the transcription

These notes are in English and are based on this extract alone. “Conjecture” below includes an explicitly qualified expectation, not necessarily a formally stated conjecture. A claim described as unproved here may have been proved earlier or later in the manuscript. Proposed proof tasks and examples are research suggestions, not claims of novelty or established results.

### R1 — Scan 51, approximately lines 5–19
**Kind: unpursued implication.** The equations `f'_{\mathcal E}=int(\mu)f_{\mathcal E}` and `\alpha'=p(\mu)\alpha`, together with their indexed analogues, suggest organizing the choices as objects and arrows of a groupoid rather than immediately taking a quotient set. The point worth checking is which simultaneous transformations preserve all local conditions, since the page displays both global and indexed conjugating elements. The doubtful ambient-group annotations must be settled before specifying that groupoid precisely.

### R2 — Scan 52, approximately lines 1–5
**Kind: unproved claim.** Grothendieck writes that injectivity of `\rho_i` determines the `\mu_{i'}` “de façon unique,” and gives the image-membership criterion `\alpha'_{i'}\alpha_{i'}^{-1}\in\rho_i(\mathcal E_i)`. A complete verification should distinguish uniqueness of a possible lift from compatibility of that lift with the local conjugation equation: the latter may use definitions or hypotheses outside these ten scans. The elementary uniqueness observation alone does not recover those omitted hypotheses.

### R3 — Scan 52, approximately lines 6–16
**Kind: unpursued implication.** The page identifies the residual choice of `\mu` through a centralizer condition and calls it “l'indétermination exacte.” It is worth separating existence of a compatible `\mu` from the stabilizer of an existing choice, and then checking the side on which the residual centralizer acts. This would make the subsequent passage from 0-morphisms to uniqueness transparent without assuming that the centralizer vanishes.

### R4 — Scan 53, approximately lines 1–4; continuation of scan 52
**Kind: conjecture.** The explicit qualification is “sauf erreur cela aussi implique `\mu_0=1`,” after the preceding page has required centralization of an open subgroup of `\pi`. The research question is the precise centralizer-triviality statement and the exact hypotheses on `\pi` under which it holds. The extract does not give a proof or a complete list of excluded cases, so it should not be quoted as an unconditional statement about profinite groups.

### R5 — Scan 53, approximately lines 2–7
**Kind: conjecture.** Grothendieck continues “il semble bien” and concludes that the relevant Hom-category is “discrète,” in the cases associated with dominant homomorphisms of algebraic curves. One should distinguish uniqueness of a 0-morphism when it exists from literal discreteness of a chosen presentation, as opposed to equivalence to a discrete category. The manuscript's preceding conventions on objects, 0-morphisms, and identification of conjugate representatives are needed to settle that distinction.

### R6 — Scan 54, approximately lines 1–2
**Kind: unproved claim.** “Le `i\in I` correspondant à `i'\in I'` est unique” produces the function `\tau`. A proof would need the relevant uniqueness property for the target peripheral subgroups and sufficient nontriviality of the source image. Those properties are not proved on this page and should not be inferred merely from the displayed containment in a conjugate.

### R7 — Scan 54, approximately lines 7–13, including the upper-left margin
**Kind: conjecture.** The main text contains “sans doute déterminé,” while the marginal note states that the centralizer in `\Gamma` of an open subgroup is “réduit à l'unité.” These concern different groups from the earlier geometric-kernel centralizer and should be checked separately. The exact uniqueness claim for `\alpha`, the normalizer/transporteur ambiguity for `\alpha_{i'}`, and the underlying assumptions on `\Gamma` all require verification; part of the connecting prose is itself uncertain.

### R8 — Scan 54, approximately lines 14–19 and lower-left margin
**Kind: unproved claim.** The compatibility formula `f^0_{i'}=(d_{i'}\chi(\beta_{i'}))id` is followed by the assertion that the positive integer is “évidemment uniquement déterminé.” The margin also claims invariance under a change of the indexed conjugating element, although its verb phrase is partly unreadable. Verification should track the change in both `f^0_{i'}` and `\beta_{i'}` and identify what makes the integer coefficient unique, without assuming the unreadable marginal scalar belongs to the unit group.

### R9 — Scans 51–54
**Kind: own idea. Confidence: medium. Reason:** the displayed transformations supply concrete equations, but several domain/codomain annotations remain doubtful. Test simultaneous conjugation and residual stabilizers in explicitly chosen finite-group analogues, keeping global and local subgroups distinct. Such tests could detect a convention or handedness error in a proposed interpretation; they would not prove a statement about the profinite objects of the manuscript.

### R10 — Scan 55, approximately lines 1–15
**Kind: unproved claim.** The “Réflexion taxonomique” identifies the dimension-zero case by injectivity of `p` and the dimension-one case by its failure. The scope of this criterion must be checked against the earlier definition of admissible objects, especially any restrictions incorporated into the curve data. It should not be detached from that setting and asserted for every scheme or every curve.

### R11 — Scan 56, approximately lines 9–21
**Kind: unproved claim.** After describing pairs `(f,\alpha)` and their equivalence, Grothendieck says the resulting “corr. 1-1” with the field/scheme Hom-set “est clair.” A detailed proof should fix the transporter convention, verify the effect of replacing `\alpha` by `\mu\alpha`, and show that this is exactly equality of the corresponding field homomorphisms. The final letter of the preceding compatibility formula is a textual doubt, not a license to rewrite it.

### R12 — Scan 57, approximately lines 1–9
**Kind: unpursued implication.** The condition `I'=\varnothing` in case II is explained using `f^{-1}(S)=S'`, alongside the nonexistence of a map from a nonempty `I'` to the empty `I`. This is an obstruction attached to the chosen definition of morphisms of marked pairs, not merely to the dimensions of the underlying schemes. The final page's proposed modification should therefore be checked against this earlier obstruction.

### R13 — Scan 57, approximately lines 10–20
**Kind: unproved claim.** The text associates the open image `\Gamma'_0` with `L'` and then uses the fixed isomorphism `L'\simeq H^0(X',\mathcal O_{X'})`. To justify the reduction to `L→L'`, one must identify the assumptions making the global-section object the stated field and explain the compatibility with the chosen Galois data. These hypotheses cannot be supplied just from the phrase “Les choix faits.”

### R14 — Scan 58, approximately lines 10–19
**Kind: unproved claim.** The transporter quotient is used to conclude “la pleine fidélité.” A proof should verify both the quotient's side of action and the passage from subgroup transporters to field transporters. The first quotient line is overwritten and partly doubtful, so a mathematical reconstruction must remain separate from the diplomatic reading.

### R15 — Scans 56 and 58, transporter calculations
**Kind: own idea. Confidence: medium. Reason:** these pages explicitly give representatives, an equivalence relation, and a field interpretation, but not all conventions. Write two independently defined maps, one from conjugating elements to field homomorphisms and one back to equivalence classes, and test their compositions before simplifying either quotient. This is a way of checking the intended formula without using the expected answer to decide what the pen strokes say.

### R16 — Scan 59, approximately lines 5–12
**Kind: unproved claim — cancelled draft.** The diagonally cancelled passage begins “Il suffit que `X'` soit un schéma connexe quasi-compact quasi-séparé” and reduces morphisms to homomorphisms `L→A'`. It is worth determining whether the cancellation rejects a hypothesis, an argument, or only the choice of presentation. The cancelled wording must not be represented as an unqualified final theorem of this extract.

### R17 — Scan 59, approximately lines 13–20; scan 60, lines 1–4
**Kind: unpursued implication.** The live replacement uses “Sections” of the finite étale pullback and a category of finite local systems, then returns to the common description of cases I and II. This suggests that the relevant data for maps to the particular dimension-zero target can be read at the level of finite étale objects rather than all geometric morphisms. The visible reversal of arguments in the displayed Hom-expression is retained in the transcript and requires separate interpretation before a proof is formalized.

### R18 — Scan 60, approximately lines 1–4
**Kind: unproved claim.** “Sans aucune hyp. autre … que … la 0-connexité” is a substantial relaxation of the earlier assumptions, but it belongs to the continuing discussion of cases I and II. A proof should state exactly what 0-connectedness means in the author's conventions and why the groupoid over the base can be described by the single homomorphism displayed here. It does not by itself establish a full-faithfulness claim for arbitrary targets.

### R19 — Scan 60, approximately lines 6–10
**Kind: conjecture.** The parenthetical “il suffirait `=` que …” appears to propose a weaker condition involving failure of local disconnection, with an inserted étale qualification. Because the wording is partly doubtful, the first research task is textual verification, followed by a precise formulation of the local condition and its connectedness consequence. No standard named condition is substituted for the manuscript's words.

### R20 — Scan 60, approximately lines 13–16
**Kind: unproved claim.** The equality `Hom_K(X'-S',X)=Hom_K(X',X)` is asserted under normality of `X'` at the points of `S'`. A proof should establish both extension and uniqueness in the current dimension-zero-target setting and specify the role of the local hypotheses near the removed subset. The equality should not be generalized here to unspecified targets.

### R21 — Scan 60, approximately lines 17–20
**Kind: conjecture / proposed change of definition.** “Ceci encourage à modifier la définition” explicitly proposes changing where the inverse-image condition on the marked set is imposed. The sentence ends after “la réunion des comp. irréd. de `X'`,” before the relevant components are characterized. The next scan is indispensable for stating the proposal completely; this extract does not authorize a completed definition.

### R22 — Scans 57 and 60, old obstruction versus proposed definition
**Kind: own idea. Confidence: low. Reason:** the motivation is visible, but the proposed selection of components is outside the supplied extract. Once the continuation is available, test the revised condition on sources with several components, including components whose maps have different image dimensions. Compare precisely which morphisms are excluded by `f^{-1}(S)_{réd}=S'` and which the proposed componentwise condition would admit; this is a proposed test, not a claim about what the completed manuscript says.

## Source reference

Grothendieck, A. (1981). *La Longue Marche à travers la théorie de Galois* [Unpublished handwritten manuscript; cote 140-2, supplied scans 51–60 of the archive file described as pages 1–292]. Université de Montpellier.

Bibliographic identification is taken from the supplied task brief. There are no external references or claims of literature verification in these notes.

# LM_P07 — transcription notes

## 1. Source, pagination, and scope

The transcription is based on the ten supplied JPEGs, `LM_P07_scan061.jpg` through `LM_P07_scan070.jpg`. The author, date, title, and archive identification below are supplied by the task brief; they have not been independently verified against an edition or catalogue. No published transcription has been used to complete the handwriting, and no OCR has been used.

The French fragment is separate from all interpretation. Its uncertainty markers are substantive: an attractive mathematical reconstruction is not evidence that the corresponding letters are present. In particular, the spelling **bordélique**, the prose shorthand **=**, the direction `f:X→X'` on scan 65, the order of the arguments of `Hom` on scan 67, and the displayed local-conjugacy equation on scan 70 have not been silently regularized.

Two handwritten numbering systems are visible. The upper numerals 29–33 are represented by `\mspage`. The lower-left handwritten numerals 60–69 are reproduced as the **last `\margin` entry on each scan**: the images alone do not establish that these are the author's own pagination. The printed institutional footer, its URL, and its printed page number are archive overlays and are not included in the manuscript fragment.

The approximate line references below count handwritten body lines from the top, including headings and legible cancellations, but not pagination or the printed footer. Floating additions and the very crowded bottom portions make exact line numbering unreliable; identifying words and positions are therefore also supplied. Roman case numerals with upper and lower rules are represented by an overline and `\uline`. Script capitals have been rendered as `\mathcal D` and `\mathcal E`; a particular printed calligraphic typeface cannot be recovered from handwriting. Paragraph-internal lineation has not been preserved.

An em dash in the alternatives column means that no responsible competing reading is offered. Counts in `\illegible{n}` are estimates, especially inside cancellations; a dense mathematical group may be counted as one unit.

## 4. Legibility and reliability

The continuous prose is often recoverable, especially where the same hand and terminology recur. The brief formulas involving `pf`, inner automorphisms, and the normalization `α=1` are generally easier to follow than the boundary-indexed formulas. Enlarged crops helped distinguish strokes, but did not recover writing obliterated by cancellation.

The least reliable portions are the overwritten opening on scan 61; the auxiliary parenthesis on scan 62; the source of the bottom map on scan 63; the homology argument and cross-reference on scan 64; the canceled block at the bottom of scan 65; the canceled marginal formula on scan 69; and the conjugation exponents, local correction group, and final transporter expression on scan 70. The diagonal correction on scan 64 is clearer at its beginning (“faux tel quel”) than at its end. Some cancellation boundaries and insertion attachment points remain uncertain even where individual words are readable.

This is an explicitly qualified diplomatic transcription, not a verified critical edition. An unmarked mathematical statement has not thereby been proved or checked against the literature. In particular, the Mordell–Weil sentence, the claims of bijectivity, and the final conjugacy equations have not been “repaired” to fit an expected theorem. The table should be consulted before quoting any of the low-confidence passages.

## 5. Research notes — separate from the transcription

These are source-based research leads, not statements of the current status of any conjecture. Brief French quotations retain the author's wording where legible; a quotation involving a doubtful reading is explicitly qualified. “Unproved claim” means that a proof is not given in these ten scans, not that no proof exists elsewhere. Own ideas are marked separately, with confidence and a reason; none is asserted to be new or established.

### R01 — Scan 62, approximately lines 5–8
**Kind: unproved claim.** “Sauf erreur, une réduction facile” introduces the passage to `X'=Spec K` and `E'=Γ`. A proof should explain the change of base field from a general zero-dimensional source and show that the relevant geometric morphisms, diagram morphisms, and their equivalence classes are preserved.

### R02 — Scan 62, approximately lines 14–17
**Kind: conjecture / expectation.** The paragraph says that knowledge of `f`, hence of `pf`, determines `α`, and associates this with “Cent(Γ)={1}”; the status of the nearby canceled “sans doute” is uncertain. The precise hypotheses on the field and on Γ need to be recovered before treating this as a general centre-triviality assertion.

### R03 — Scan 63, approximately lines 1–10
**Kind: unproved claim.** “On peut tjrs décrire un morphisme … avec α=1” is followed by the description in terms of sections modulo `π=Ker p`. The calculation should be checked on representatives: choose a lift of `α^{-1}`, normalize, and then verify that equivalence between normalized representatives uses kernel-valued conjugation rather than unrestricted conjugation in E.

### R04 — Scans 63–64, bottom display and opening
**Kind: conjecture.** The displayed geometric-to-section map is asserted to be “bijective” for an “anabélien” pair, but the next page carries the explicit correction “faux tel quel”. The qualification belongs to the claim: it must not be removed when extracting a section-type conjecture from these pages.

### R05 — Scan 64, upper-left margin
**Kind: conjecture / question.** The note calls for “des conditions supplémentaires sur les f” and refers to “n° 9 ci-dessous”; its closing words are read, less securely, as “Peut-être vrai si I=∅”. The later number 9 is outside the supplied excerpt, so the additional conditions and the scope of the tentative empty-boundary exception remain open here.

### R06 — Scan 64, approximately lines 3–10
**Kind: unproved claim.** “Je sais en tous cas que cette application est injective” is justified by an embedding of U into an algebraic group that is an extension of an abelian variety by a torus, together with the stated Mordell–Weil input. A proof needs the exact field hypotheses, an account of the embedding, and the point-separation step linking the group-theoretic invariant to a rational point.

### R07 — Scan 64, middle paragraph
**Kind: unproved claim.** “Il suffit de connaître la classe de splittage” asserts that an abelianized extension already determines the point. One should identify the map from geometric sections to splittings of this quotient extension and prove the required injectivity, without presuming that injectivity answers the earlier surjectivity problem.

### R08 — Scan 64, last paragraph
**Kind: unpursued implication.** The author explicitly says the faithfulness statement holds under “des hyp. moins draconiennes” than the anabelian hypothesis and writes a numerical condition read as `g=0 ⇒ n≥2` (n/ν remains uncertain). The exact class of curves allowed by this weaker condition, and its dependence on the uncertain preceding cross-reference, should be compared with the hypotheses of the conjectural full correspondence.

### R09 — Scan 64, the sentence about G(K)
**Kind: own idea. Confidence: high for the elementary test; conditional on the scope of the manuscript sentence.** Test the visibly stated finite-generation assertion with `G=G_m` and `K=Q`: distinct rational primes give infinitely many multiplicatively independent elements of `Q×`, so the full rational-point group in that example is not finitely generated. This is a reason to recover the exact earlier hypotheses or the intended meaning of “absolu”, not a license to alter the sentence or to conclude that the intended argument has no valid formulation.

### R10 — Scan 65, opening paragraph
**Kind: conjecture / research program.** “Ceci suggère une approche de la conjecture de Mordell” proposes studying finiteness of π-conjugacy classes of splittings for `g≥2`. A realization of this program must both justify the geometric-to-section comparison and prove the proposed finiteness on the group-extension side; the observation alone supplies neither proof.

### R11 — Scan 65, Fermat paragraph
**Kind: conjecture / research program.** “Idem pour une approche du th. de Fermat” points to an extension involving the displayed punctured-projective-line fundamental group, whose small labels are marked uncertain in the transcription. These scans do not explain how solutions would be encoded, what distinguished sections or maps would be selected, or what property of the extension would exclude the relevant solutions.

### R12 — Scans 65–66, case IV(a)
**Kind: unproved claim.** Constant applications are explicitly allowed, and the continuation describes them by maps “qui sont nuls sur π'”. The claimed full-faithfulness description of this constant part should be derived by the appropriate factorization through a zero-dimensional object, checking the uncertain case numeral rather than silently substituting the expected one.

### R13 — Scan 66, case IV(b), final sentence
**Kind: conjecture / explicit research question.** “Il faudrait essayer de vérifier ce point directement” asks for the group-theoretic triviality on π' of the compatible maps in this boundary case. The manuscript says that this, together with case III, would establish the corresponding bordélique claim; the desired vanishing must therefore be distinguished from the geometric observation that the relevant geometric maps are constant.

### R14 — Scan 66, same vanishing problem
**Kind: own idea. Confidence: medium, because this isolates a necessary test but not the full obstruction.** Try first to identify the map induced on the abelianizations and to show that it vanishes under the exact geometric and field hypotheses. Even a successful abelianized calculation would leave a nonabelian step: it does not by itself rule out an image contained in a commutator subgroup.

### R15 — Scan 67, case IV(d)
**Kind: unproved claim.** The assertion “dominant et fini, génériquement étale” is made after excluding constant maps. A proof should identify which assumptions on the curves imply finiteness and which characteristic or separability assumptions imply generic étaleness, since those hypotheses are not restated at this point.

### R16 — Scan 67, last paragraph, continuing onto scan 68
**Kind: unproved claim / open reconstruction problem.** Faithfulness “résulte aisément” from case III, whereas full faithfulness is called “chose mystérieuse”. The distinction suggests two different proof obligations: detect equality of already existing geometric morphisms, and construct a geometric morphism from the groupoid/extension data; the second is not supplied by the first.

### R17 — Scan 68, first reduction paragraph
**Kind: unproved claim.** “Par des ext. finies des corps K” is used to reduce to surjective structure maps to Γ. The reduction should be formulated with its exact open subgroups and descent data, rather than treating passage to an arbitrary finite extension as automatically reversible for all morphism classes.

### R18 — Scan 68, the sentence beginning “Sauf erreur”
**Kind: unproved claim.** The author tentatively says that case III for extensions “de t.f. de K” implies the general case over K. This is a stronger and different passage from the immediately preceding finite-base-extension reduction; a proof must explain why a section over the relevant finitely generated field supplies a map and why that map extends with the required boundary behavior.

### R19 — Scan 68, same reduction
**Kind: own idea. Confidence: medium, because a generic-point construction gives a natural route but does not solve extension or compatibility.** Apply the case-III description over a source function field to obtain a candidate geometric map at the generic point. Then isolate, as separate steps, extension across the source, agreement of the induced fundamental-group data, and compatibility with the marked boundary; none of these steps should be silently assumed from the existence of the generic point alone.

### R20 — Scan 69, paragraph beginning “Il se pourrait”
**Kind: conjecture.** “Il se pourrait … que … la classe de π-conj. de f suffise” asks whether the underlying outer extension map determines its entire bordélique enhancement when `I,I'≠∅`. This is a reconstruction question for the extra indexing and local data, not merely a choice of representative for f.

### R21 — Scan 69, transporter paragraph
**Kind: conjecture / question.** The explicit “question de savoir si” concerns whether `Trans_E(L_i^d,L_i)` is reduced to `E_i` for every positive d. The convention for the transporter and the meaning of powers of the subgroup should be fixed from earlier definitions before comparing this with any normalizer or commensurator statement.

### R22 — Scan 69, paragraph beginning “Dans ce cas”
**Kind: unpursued implication.** Conditional on the transporter assertion, `E_i` “serait connu” from `L_i⊂π`, and even from the subgroup ray or the homomorphism ray defined by κ_i. A proof should establish that these progressively weaker data really recover the same boundary subgroup, and not just one of its finite-index subgroups or its conjugacy class.

### R23 — Scan 69, transporter problem
**Kind: own idea. Confidence: medium, because this separates two logically distinct requirements.** Analyze d=1 first, then ask whether replacing L_i by a proper power introduces additional transporter elements. This separates a self-transporter problem from stability under passage to the specified powers; it is a diagnostic plan, not a proposed proof of the universal assertion.

### R24 — Scan 70, initial normalization
**Kind: unproved claim.** The surjectivity of each local `E_i→Γ` is used to arrange that the local α-data lie in π as well as to retain the global normalization `α=1`. The simultaneous normalization should be checked against the global and local equivalence relation, particularly because several components of that relation are revised on this page.

### R25 — Scan 70, multiplication condition
**Kind: unpursued implication.** The required local map is characterized by “l'hom de multiplication par d_i”. Recovering the earlier definition of d_i and of `T(bar K)` would clarify which multiplicities are permitted and what geometric information they retain; no positivity, nonvanishing, or ramification theorem is added to the transcription on the strength of expected notation.

### R26 — Scan 70, final paragraph
**Kind: unproved claim.** For fixed `(f,τ)`, the α-data are said to be determined modulo right multiplication by elements of the displayed transporter. This links the ambiguity of local enhancements to the preceding transporter problem, but the heavily revised final formula must be reread before making a precise torsor or uniqueness statement.

### R27 — Scan 70, displayed equivalence formulas
**Kind: own idea. Confidence: medium, because the proposed verification is elementary once the missing conventions and uncertain signs are settled.** Substitute the displayed transformations into the local inclusion and multiplication conditions, keeping track of both global μ and local μ_{i'}. In particular, the visible equation `α'_{i'}=μ_{i'}α_{i'}` has not been supplied with an additional μ or μ⁻¹; any suspected discrepancy belongs in the verification and in a future critical apparatus, not in this diplomatic text.

## Source identification

Grothendieck, A. (1981). *La Longue Marche à travers la théorie de Galois* [Handwritten manuscript; supplied scans 61–70 of the archive file identified in the task as cote 140-2, pages 1–292]. Université de Montpellier.

This identification follows the task brief. All research notes above refer to the supplied excerpt; no claim is made that the questions remain unanswered elsewhere in the manuscript or in later literature.

## 3. Legibility and reliability

The continuous prose is substantially more legible than the revisions and compact formula annotations. The least reliable passages are the nested cancellations spanning scans 74–75, the overwritten fraction-field letters and finite/limit indices of the paired formulas on scans 75–76, the projection-functor label and added base-object identification on scan 72, and the final subgroup/commutation notation on scan 80. Small primes, bars, subscripts, and abbreviated superscripts remain a particular risk. The resulting reading is provisional, not a certified transcription.

The source base was the ten supplied JPEGs, inspected directly and in enlarged crops; no OCR, published transcription, or external mathematical source was used to supply words. The cancelled passage continuing from scan 74 into scan 75 has separate `\deleted{…}` spans on the two pages; nested deletions record visible local cancellations without claiming a secure chronology. The visible `t/s`, `K/L`, singular/plural, and coefficient/subscript discrepancies have not been harmonized. In particular, the unit-change formula on scan 76 has been retained as read, not repaired by algebraic expectation.

The LaTeX file is a fragment, not a standalone document. A temporary wrapper defining the requested editorial macros and loading standard mathematics, underlining, and `tikz-cd` support was used to check its syntax; that wrapper and its PDF are not part of the archive.

## 4. Research notes — separate from the transcription

These notes concern only what this batch supports or leaves to check. The definitions of the earlier numbered cases and the full hypotheses preceding scan 71 are not included. Short quotations below retain the relevant wording; uncertain readings are identified rather than treated as established statements.

1. **Scan 71, approximately lines 6–10 — conjecture.** “Je présume qu’un peu de sueur permettrait de prouver que cela marche encore dans le cas général” explicitly proposes removing the assumption that the images `Γ_i` equal `Γ`. A proof would have to identify exactly which boundary data can still be recovered from the stated outer-conjugacy class when these images are proper subgroups; the preceding definitions are outside this batch.

2. **Scans 72–73, the section/groupoid comparison — unproved claim.** The assertion that “les classes d’isomorphismes des couples … correspondent aux classes de … conjugaison” identifies categorical section data with conjugacy classes of splittings. The added choice of an isomorphism involving the lifted object is part of the argument, not disposable notation. A complete verification should track natural isomorphisms, the compatibility with the projection functor, and dependence on the chosen universal-cover object.

3. **Scan 73, approximately lines 8–11 — unproved claim.** The parenthesis “s’il y en a, et on sait qu’il y en a…” asserts nonemptiness of the splitting torsor at this point without giving the construction there. The local construction on scans 75–76 subsequently addresses existence, so this should be cross-checked against that construction rather than treated as an entirely abandoned question. Its precise hypotheses still need to be matched with those of the extension on scan 73.

4. **Scan 73, the cohomology/inverse-limit displays — unproved claim.** The displayed identification with `lim H¹(K_i, μ_n)` needs a justification for passing from the inverse system of finite coefficients to the cohomology group on the left. The Kummer sequence identifies the finite-level terms but does not by itself specify all topology, transition-map, or inverse-limit hypotheses. The small subscript on the earlier coefficient is itself uncertain, and must be resolved before formulating the exact statement.

5. **Scan 74, approximately lines 3–7 — unproved claim.** “Comme K_i est de t.f. sur Q, cet hom est injectif” asserts that the kernel described by membership in every power subgroup is trivial. A proof should isolate the role of finite generation and address both valuation data and elements in the constant field, including torsion. No corresponding assertion for arbitrary fields should be inferred from this wording.

6. **Scan 74, approximately lines 8–11 — unpursued implication.** The proposed “sous-K_i*-torseur” is also described as “un élément de Σ_i/K_i*”. This suggests that the intrinsic datum is an orbit, not a preferred splitting. It is worth tracing how that orbit behaves under changing the universal-cover object and the identification of its automorphism group, choices used on the preceding pages.

7. **Scans 74–75, the large cancelled paragraph — unproved claim.** The deleted argument says that the completed local ring “contient canoniquement le sous-corps K_i” and relates a uniformizer to a power-series isomorphism. The intended canonicity, its hypotheses, and its compatibility with the chosen algebraic closures merit checking. Because this passage is cancelled, it must not be quoted as an unqualified assertion adopted by the surviving argument.

8. **Scan 76, approximately lines 3–9 — unproved claim.** The phrase “des iso can.” introduces base-change identifications for the root-adjoining rings and their fraction fields. A proof needs to establish compatibility of the finite-level maps, passage to the direct limit, and the claim that the indicated fraction-field tensor products have the required field interpretation for the allowed extensions. The overwritten first indices should be resolved before deciding whether the neighbouring displays distinguish finite and limit levels or are revised versions of one formula.

9. **Scan 76, approximately lines 10–15 — unproved claim.** “On sait que L′(∞,t) est alors alg. clos” is the crucial assertion supporting the construction of a section. Its proof would need to show that, under the stated characteristic-zero henselian conditions and after making the residue field algebraically closed, the indicated root tower accounts for the relevant finite extensions. The following identification of the algebraic closure of the residue field inside that tower also needs to be made functorial to obtain the claimed section of groupoids.

10. **Scan 76, approximately lines 16–20 — own idea.** Check the unit-change display literally before using it: with the readings `s′ = s u^n` and `T̄_n ↦ u T̄_n` for an arrow from the quotient with relation `T_n^n − s` to the one with relation `T_n^n − s′`, the image of the source relation is `u^n s′ − s`, which does not vanish formally from that displayed equality alone. The exponent, direction, and interpretation of the chosen unit therefore need a separate check; this is not a licence to reverse the arrow or change the exponent in the transcription. **Confidence: medium**, because the polynomial calculation is direct but the manuscript’s compact exponent and conventions could resolve the apparent discrepancy.

11. **Scan 76, approximately lines 21–23 — unproved claim.** “D’où par passage à la limite” requires more than the existence of an isolated finite-level isomorphism. One should write down the compatibility equations between the unit changes at different levels and the transition maps of scan 75. In particular, determine whether the notation `u` represents one unit or a compatible system of roots, without inserting that interpretation into the diplomatic text.

12. **Scan 77, approximately lines 3–6 — unproved claim.** The principal-unit group is described as “un s.-groupe divisible de O*”, and sections arising from the corresponding changes of parameter are said to be isomorphic. A proof should verify root existence in `1 + m` under the actual henselian and characteristic assumptions and establish the compatibility needed for the entire tower. It should also distinguish isomorphism of sections from literal equality of chosen representatives.

13. **Scan 77, the paragraph beginning “On constate aussitôt” — unproved claim.** “Cette application est compatible avec l’hom” Θ asserts equivariance of the map from bases of `m/m²` to section classes. An explicit check should compare the cocycle measuring a change of parameter with the image under Θ of its residue-field scaling factor. The action conventions and any inverse/sign introduced by the root-tower maps are part of that verification.

14. **Scan 77, the source `(m/m²)*` of the displayed map — own idea.** Compare the torsor of bases of `m/m²` with the torsor of nonzero vectors in its dual, without renaming either one in the transcription. The two scalar actions may help diagnose a reciprocal factor in a change-of-parameter convention. **Confidence: medium**, because the comparison is elementary once the actions are fixed, but these pages do not explicitly make that duality convention.

15. **Scan 78, approximately lines 10–13 — unpursued implication.** “Il faudrait expliciter le comportement de cette structure suppl. relativement à des morphismes” is an explicit request for a functorial description. A useful next step is to compute the induced map on the distinguished sub-torsors for a local morphism, keeping the residue-field map and the leading term of the parameter change visible. The sentence limits attention to morphisms “provenant de situations géométriques”, a qualification that should be retained.

16. **Scan 78, approximately lines 14–16 — unpursued implication.** The requested “relation avec la norme des 1-formes différentielles” is not worked out on these pages. One should determine the precise operation called the norm, its source and target, and how its effect on the relevant one-dimensional spaces compares with the cohomological torsor map. The author explicitly postpones the exposition in “j’ai la flemme de l’expliciter en long et en large !”.

17. **Scans 78–79, the “transcendantes” / “géométriques” distinction — unpursued implication.** The text describes a large family parametrized by a cohomological torsor and a smaller family described as “géométriques”, parametrized by a multiplicative-group torsor. A careful formulation should keep the repeated qualification “plus précisément” and avoid choosing an origin implicitly. The terminology alone does not settle whether the final proposed exhaustion concerns the entire larger family, only the smaller family, or another precisely defined union.

18. **Scan 79, approximately lines 7–13 — unproved claim.** “On vérifie que” asserts separation of the classes obtained from distinct indices, even after passing to an abelianized extension, while “je ne fais pas le détail des vérifications, via Mordell-Weil” explicitly withholds the argument. A proof should identify the exact abelianized extension and the invariant distinguishing the indices, then state the hypotheses under which the indicated arithmetic input applies. The mere mention of Mordell–Weil is not a proof of the separation statement.

19. **Scan 79, approximately lines 13–14 — unproved claim.** The boundary-related section classes are also said to be “distincts des scindages associés aux pts. rat/K de X\S”. This is a separate separation assertion from the comparison between two boundary indices. It would be useful to identify an invariant that distinguishes these two sources of sections and to check whether the same abelianized quotient suffices.

20. **Scan 79, final paragraph — conjecture.** The proposed exhaustion is expressly hedged by “sous toutes réserves, encore !” and “peut-être”, before “pas d’autres scindages … que ceux-ci”. Both the case numeral, provisionally read as III with IV as an alternative, and the antecedent of “ceux-ci” must be settled from the wider manuscript before restating a formal conjecture. This is an expectation to investigate, not an established classification on these pages.

21. **Scan 80, approximately lines 2–8 — unproved claim.** In broadening the morphisms of open pairs, the text says they are “se prolongeant bien sûr” to the compactified objects. The exact geometric hypotheses making that extension assertion valid are not all restated here. Any reconstruction of the revised category should recover those hypotheses from the preceding manuscript rather than assume the phrase holds for arbitrary varieties and compactifications.

22. **Scan 80, the subset `I′_f` and partial boundary map — own idea.** Work out composition for the revised morphism data by tracking which source boundary components remain in the boundary through each successive map, and restrict the commutation data accordingly. This tests whether the proposed enlargement really defines a category and whether the additional torsor structure survives the enlargement. **Confidence: medium**, because the partial indexing is explicit but the complete commutation and triviality conditions continue beyond the supplied page.

23. **Scan 80, approximately lines 20–22 — own idea.** Resolve the subgroup letter in the clause “est trivial sur” before interpreting its mathematics: it may not be the same object as the earlier extension group. A literal requirement that a homomorphism over a fixed nontrivial Γ kill an entire subgroup that projects nontrivially to Γ would be incompatible with that projection, which gives a conditional diagnostic for the reading rather than an emendation. **Confidence: medium**, because that algebraic implication is straightforward but the subgroup glyph and the full surrounding hypotheses remain uncertain.

## Source identification

Attribution and dating are those supplied in `LM_P08_prompt.md`:

Grothendieck, A. (1981). *La Longue Marche à travers la théorie de Galois* [Handwritten manuscript, cote 140-2, archive file “pages 1–292”, supplied scans 71–80]. Université de Montpellier.

The reference identifies the supplied source; no independent catalogue or published transcription was consulted.


# ===== LM_P09 (first-pass notes) =====

# LM_P09 — Transcription notes

## Scope and location conventions

**Source attribution supplied with the task (not independently verified):** Grothendieck, A. (1981). *La Longue Marche à travers la théorie de Galois* [Handwritten manuscript, Montpellier archive, cote 140-2, supplied scan pages 81–90]. Université de Montpellier.

The transcription is based only on the ten supplied images, read directly with enlarged details where useful. No OCR, published transcription, or external mathematical source was used to reconstruct the text. The files are ordered by their scan filenames, 81 through 90, rather than by the order in which the attachments were displayed.

The small lower-left handwritten numerals are 80–89; the printed footer repeats them. Their authorship is not established here, so the handwritten numerals are preserved with `\margin{…}` and the printed archival footer is omitted. The separate upper numerals are encoded with `\mspage{…}` as follows.

| Scan | Lower-left numeral | Upper numeral |
|---|---:|---:|
| 81 | 80 | — |
| 82 | 81 | 39 |
| 83 | 82 | — |
| 84 | 83 | 40 |
| 85 | 84 | — |
| 86 | 85 | 41 |
| 87 | 86 | 42 |
| 88 | 87 | — |
| 89 | 88 | 43 |
| 90 | 89 | — |

Line references below are approximate main-text lines on the image, counting headings and displayed formula lines, not lines of the generated TeX file. Interlinear and marginal material is located relative to the main text. A table row may cover several associated uncertainty marks. “High” means a preferred but still flagged reading, not a claim of certainty.

The large cancellation beginning near the bottom of scan 86 continues through the proposition at the top of scan 87. It is split into separate `\deleted{…}` blocks at the scan boundary. Smaller readable cancellations and insertions are retained inside or alongside that material without attempting to date successive revisions. Some unmatched source brackets, parentheses, and quotation marks are intentionally left unmatched; they are not supplied from grammar or mathematical expectation. The equals-like shorthand is retained rather than expanded to *même*.
