## 3. Legibility assessment

Much of the larger prose is readable, but these are rapid working pages, not a clean fair copy. Primes, subscripts, abbreviated words, fine accents, and punctuation are appreciably less secure than the main sentences. The least trustworthy passages are the opening and cancelled margin of scan 81; the heading and final multiplicative-group expression on scan 82; the interlinear base-change explanation on scan 83; the group qualifier in the cancelled proposition and the heavily revised bottom of scan 87; the first orientation-twist formula and local-groupoid revision on scan 89; and the compressed category and choice-independence wording on scan 90.

The long diagonal cancellations are clear as cancellations, but the precise attachment and order of some nearby small revisions are not. Illegible counts are estimates of words or, for a mathematical erasure, of the corresponding lost item. This is a provisional diplomatic transcription with explicit unresolved readings, not an independently collated edition. In particular, the manuscript’s complete mathematical assertions must not be inferred from the cleaner-looking parts of a damaged or revised sentence.

## 4. Research notes — English; separate from the transcription

These notes identify questions and proof obligations raised by this excerpt. The category “conjecture” also includes explicit expectations and deferred tasks; it does not imply that every item was stated as a formal mathematical conjecture. “Unproved claim” means that a proof is not supplied in these ten scans; it does **not** mean the statement is unknown in the literature or absent later in the manuscript. Brief quotations follow the provisional reading. Cancelled material is identified as cancelled. Proposed implications and my own ideas are not used to alter the transcription, are not claimed to be new, and have not been checked against later pages or external literature.

### R01. Filling punctures and quotienting

**Location:** Scan 81, approximately 3–10. **Kind:** unproved claim.

The description beginning « boucher les trous » identifies the operation with quotienting by the invariant subgroup generated by the corresponding L_{i'}. A proof would need to specify the relevant topology and the meaning of invariant generation, and to show that the remaining local groups map to the stated images. The excerpt begins mid-argument, so the hypotheses inherited from the preceding page are unavailable.

### R02. A groupoid-level formulation is requested

**Location:** Scan 81, approximately 11–13. **Kind:** conjecture.

« il faudrait exprimer ces opérations aussi au niveau des diagrammes de groupoïdes » explicitly leaves a reformulation to be supplied. It would require compatible quotient or filling operations on all three objects and both connecting maps, not only a description at the level of one group.

### R03. Comparison of the two viewpoints

**Location:** Scan 81, approximately 14–19. **Kind:** unproved claim.

The claim « on s'est ramené à la situation envisagée au début » is followed by a judgment about the relative simplicity of the earlier viewpoint. The actual comparison would need the earlier construction and a precise statement of what data are preserved; neither is fully present in this excerpt.

### R04. Abelian local kernels and their images

**Location:** Scan 82, approximately 3–10. **Kind:** unproved claim.

« les groupes noyaux … sont abéliens » and the subsequent statement about their images are recalled rather than proved here. A proof would have to identify each local kernel, construct the stated Kummer identification, and check the image condition for both displayed groupoid maps; the adjective read as ouverts is not entirely secure.

### R05. Admissibility of sections remains to be defined

**Location:** Scan 82, approximately 13–18. **Kind:** conjecture.

The phrase « en un sens qui sera défini par la suite » explicitly defers the meaning of "section admissible". The stated purpose is to exclude the "triviales" sections coming from the local groups; the present pages do not supply a complete criterion.

### R06. Splitting after passing to an open subgroup

**Location:** Scan 82, approximately 13–18. **Kind:** unproved claim.

Condition a) asserts openness of the image and the existence of a section above an open subgroup. Establishing this in the geometric setting would require the hypotheses on the base and geometric object that make such a section available, as well as compatibility with the deferred admissibility condition.

### R07. Algebraic splittings of the local extensions

**Location:** Scan 82, approximately 19–22. **Kind:** unproved claim.

Condition b) calls the local extension « triviale » and distinguishes a privileged class of « scindages, dits "algébriques" ». The torsor description and its Kummer-theoretic comparison require proof; the final displayed expression is too uncertain to identify its target with a specific completion.

### R08. A deliberately discarded refinement

**Location:** Scan 83, approximately 1–2. **Kind:** unpursued implication.

« on va pas considérer … cet élément de structure supplémentaire » sets aside the algebraic-splitting refinement discussed on the preceding page. It remains to determine which subsequent conditions use only the local extensions and which would change if the privileged class of splittings were retained.

### R09. Reduction by finite extension of the base field

**Location:** Scan 83, approximately 9–21. **Kind:** unproved claim.

« Le cas général s'en déduit par extension finie du corps de base » asserts a reduction to the case where the displayed image subgroups equal Γ. A proof would need to track splitting of punctures, the indicated indices, the connected components of X_{K′}, and the outer homomorphisms under base extension; several interlinear words in this explanation remain uncertain.

### R10. The base-change operation should be expressed for groupoids

**Location:** Scan 83, approximately 21–24. **Kind:** conjecture.

« j'ai la flemme d'expliciter comme il faudrait » concerns the formulation using groupoids rather than profinite groups. The omitted construction should identify the new objects and morphisms and explain how choosing a component recovers the group-theoretic description.

### R11. Check the two index counts simultaneously

**Location:** Scan 83, approximately 15–21. **Kind:** own idea.

Own idea — confidence: medium. A useful consistency test is a finite base change with both more than one geometric component and at least one non-rational puncture, checking the two displayed counts (Γ:Σ) and (Γ:Σ_i) in the same example. The reason for this test is that the text tracks both phenomena but the groupoid construction that should coordinate them is deferred; this is a proposed test, not an established result or a novelty claim.

### R12. Invariance under base extension

**Location:** Scan 84, approximately 1–2. **Kind:** unproved claim.

« seront invariantes par extension du corps de base » states the intended stability of the forthcoming conditions. A proof must check each condition separately, including the local conjugacy classes and the outer action, rather than only the underlying abstract group.

### R13. The conjugating element must lie in the geometric kernel

**Location:** Scan 84, approximately 4–13. **Kind:** unpursued implication.

The emphatic « pas seulement α∈E ! » distinguishes conjugation by the kernel π from conjugation in the full extension. This distinction is essential to the actual outer-equivariance condition; replacing π by E would weaken the requirement and is not justified by these pages.

### R14. A tentative normalizer characterization

**Location:** Scan 84, approximately 14–19. **Kind:** conjecture.

The word « probablement » qualifies the inference from normalizing L_i to belonging to E_i. Provided the groups are embedded as the notation suggests, this points toward the assertion N_E(L_i)=E_i, but that formulation is an interpretation and the claim is not proved here. A proof would have to rule out additional normalizing elements, including exceptional cases allowed by the preceding hypotheses.

### R15. From local action to cyclotomic equivariance

**Location:** Scan 84, approximately 16–22. **Kind:** unproved claim.

The paragraph gives part of an argument by choosing β with the same image as g and setting α=gβ⁻¹. To complete it one must justify the identification of the local kernel with T and the asserted action through χ; the text continues on the following scan.

### R16. Separate equivariance from normalizer equality

**Location:** Scan 84, approximately 14–19. **Kind:** own idea.

Own idea — confidence: high as a methodological suggestion. In an attempted proof, isolate the choice of β provided by surjectivity from the separate assertion about the full normalizer of L_i. The reason is that the manuscript explicitly marks the normalizer statement as probable while presenting the choice-of-lift argument directly; treating them as one lemma could accidentally turn the tentative claim into an assumption.

### R17. Strong nontriviality of the outer action

**Location:** Scan 85, approximately 5–15. **Kind:** unproved claim.

« Elle implique que l'opération de Γ sur π est très non triviale » is justified here only by reference to the open image of χ. A precise proof would have to specify what degree of nontriviality is meant and show that the cyclotomic effect on the local maps survives passage to outer automorphisms; the text does not formulate that conclusion as a separate theorem.

### R18. Arithmetic existence versus abstract presentation

**Location:** Scan 85, approximately 11–15. **Kind:** unpursued implication.

The remark « sans raisons arithmétiques profondes ! » emphasizes that existence of these actions is not evident from the forthcoming structure of π alone. This suggests separating the abstract group-presentation conditions from the additional realizability problem for an action with the required arithmetic behavior.

### R19. A further Weil-type condition is announced

**Location:** Scan 85, approximately 16–21. **Kind:** conjecture.

« une condition de non-trivialité à la Weil » announces an additional requirement rather than establishing it at this point. The later Frobenius condition should be read as addressing this announcement, while the intervening condition d) concerns generators and relations.

### R20. The profinite presentation

**Location:** Scan 86, approximately 1–7. **Kind:** unproved claim.

Condition d) requires the displayed generators and relation to present π as a profinite group, not merely to give a relation satisfied in π. A geometric proof would need to establish topological generation and that the closed relation subgroup has precisely the required normal generation.

### R21. Recovering the genus from an abelian quotient

**Location:** Scan 86, approximately 8–12. **Kind:** unproved claim.

« g est uniquement déterminé » is supported by a rank statement for the abelianization modulo the local images. To justify the argument one must compute that quotient from the profinite presentation and specify the treatment of the local images and their closure.

### R22. Check the case of no punctures before using the rank formula

**Location:** Scan 86, approximately 8–12. **Kind:** own idea.

Own idea — confidence: high for the conditional calculation; the earlier empty-set reading is also now preferred after enlargement. If ν=0 is permitted, the displayed commutator relation becomes trivial on abelianization and suggests rank 2g, whereas the written formula 2g+ν−1 gives 2g−1. The reason for flagging this is the apparent allowance of I=∅ on scan 82; verify the intended scope rather than correcting the formula.

### R23. Admissible choices of η and the cyclotomic image

**Location:** Scan 86, approximately 13–17. **Kind:** unproved claim.

The statement « si η convient » is extended to χ(α)η after taking conjugates. A proof should distinguish invariance under χ(Γ) from a precise torsor or orbit assertion; the compound read as sous-torseur is itself uncertain.

### R24. A cancelled argument involving simultaneous powers

**Location:** Scan 86, approximately 18–23; continues on 87. **Kind:** unpursued implication.

The paragraph beginning « Supposons pour simplifier que g=0 » is crossed out and continues into a cancelled proposition on the next scan. Its cancellation is mathematically relevant: the following surviving discussion distinguishes the original generators from separately conjugated powered generators, so the deleted argument must not be reported as a settled assertion.

### R25. A direct diagnostic for the deleted power assertion

**Location:** Scan 87, approximately 1–8, cancelled. **Kind:** own idea.

Own idea — confidence: high for the elementary word calculation. With three puncture generators and ℓ₃=(ℓ₁ℓ₂)⁻¹, the un-conjugated product at ρ=−1 becomes ℓ₁⁻¹ℓ₂⁻¹ℓ₁ℓ₂, so the deleted assertion would force this commutator to vanish. This explains why taking separately chosen conjugates can matter; it does not prove the surviving general assertion.

### R26. Powered conjugacy classes in a privileged generating system

**Location:** Scan 87, approximately 9–18. **Kind:** unproved claim.

The surviving claim says « on peut trouver des conjugués » of the powered local generators that can again be included in a privileged generating system. A proof must produce the conjugations and the remaining generators and establish the full presentation condition, rather than merely preserve each cyclic subgroup.

### R27. The rational-point existence assumption

**Location:** Scan 87, approximately 10–12. **Kind:** unproved claim.

The phrase « admettant qu'il existe » explicitly assumes a smooth projective geometrically connected curve over Q with the stated genus and ν rational points. No construction is supplied here; the exact range of genus and puncture number for which the argument uses this assumption must be checked before treating the resulting statement as general.

### R28. Even the inverse case is singled out

**Location:** Scan 87, approximately 18–19. **Kind:** conjecture.

« Même pour ρ=−1 ce n'est pas entièrement trivial » singles out a concrete case for further work. An explicit construction for inversion would be a useful test of the required ordering and individual conjugations, without resolving the assertion for all profinite units.

### R29. Recovering a lacet structure on an open subgroup

**Location:** Scan 88, approximately 6–12. **Kind:** conjecture.

« Avec un peu de travail, on doit pouvoir retrouver » leaves the local structure on E′→Γ to be constructed. The construction must identify the new local groups and their images in the abelianized ℓ-part, including the meaning and action on the inserted « la somme ». The marginal assertion that this is elegant in the groupoid setting supplies a proposed framework, not the construction itself.

### R30. The quotient called the VA part

**Location:** Scan 88, approximately 13–15. **Kind:** unpursued implication.

The quotient by the local-image submodules is called « la partie "VA" ». A fuller treatment should identify the quotient intrinsically, check that the indicated action descends, and explain the geometric meaning of this designation; those details are not supplied here.

### R31. Purity, integrality, and independence of ℓ

**Location:** Scan 88, approximately 16–20. **Kind:** unproved claim.

Condition e) requires the representation to be « pure de poids 1 » and the Frobenius characteristic polynomials to have integer coefficients and be « indépendants » of ℓ. A precise formulation needs to specify the Frobenius elements under consideration, the meaning of purity, and how the objects for different ℓ are compared. The manuscript states this as a condition on the system, rather than proving it on these pages.

### R32. Canonical orientation after twisting

**Location:** Scan 89, approximately 2–12. **Kind:** unproved claim.

The construction is followed by « X sera donc canoniquement T-orienté ». A proof should define the orientation set, its action, and the contracted product precisely, then establish the claimed canonical element without choosing one of the two orientations. The first formula is too uncertain to use as an authoritative complete definition.

### R33. The local groupoid of punctured germs

**Location:** Scan 89, approximately 13–20. **Kind:** unpursued implication.

The parenthetical description invokes « des germes de rev. universels ». A fuller construction needs explicit objects and arrows, the functor to Π_U, and the additional structure supplied by a T-orientation; the cramped interlinear revision makes it especially important not to treat the current transcription as an unambiguous definition.

### R34. Orientation and the local fundamental-group system

**Location:** Scan 90, approximately 1–5. **Kind:** unproved claim.

The local system of fundamental groups is said to be « can. isom. à T ». A proof should construct the identifications at punctured germs and establish their compatibility with all arrows in the local groupoid, not merely choose a generator at each puncture.

### R35. The proposed 2-equivalence

**Location:** Scan 90, approximately 6–12. **Kind:** unproved claim.

« on trouve une 2-équivalence » states a classification claim between the topological and groupoid descriptions. A proof must specify objects, 1-morphisms, and 2-morphisms on both sides and establish essential surjectivity and the corresponding equivalences on morphisms. The list of conditions is incomplete in the supplied scans, and the overwritten Π_X/Π_U notation should not be silently resolved in formulating the claim.

### R36. Independence of choices in the outer-group formulation

**Location:** Scan 90, approximately 13–20. **Kind:** unpursued implication.

« le tout dépend de choix, mais π est intrinsèque comme groupe extérieur » asserts that the group presentation retains a choice-independent meaning. A fuller treatment should describe changes of objects or base points, the resulting inner conjugations, and the induced equivalence class of the lacet data; the final condition continues beyond the supplied page.

### R37. Test the low-complexity objects before formalizing the equivalence

**Location:** Scan 90, approximately 6–20. **Kind:** own idea.

Own idea — confidence: medium as a proposed verification step. Test the asserted correspondence first on a sphere with zero, one, and two marked points, explicitly recording the local-to-global groupoid map and the intended 2-morphisms. The reason is that these examples can expose whether unstated exclusions or conventions are needed; this is a suggested check, not a claim that the proposed equivalence fails.


# ===== LM_P10 (first-pass notes) =====

# LM_P10 — Editorial and research notes

## 1. Scope, pagination, and limits

This is a working diplomatic transcription of the ten supplied images, not a definitive critical edition. The French text has not been translated, mathematically corrected, or completed from another transcription. The research notes below are separate from the transcription: they do not authorize replacing an uncertain reading with a mathematically expected one.

The bibliographic identification supplied with the task is Grothendieck’s *La Longue Marche à travers la théorie de Galois* (1981), Montpellier archive, cote 140-2, file described as pages 1–292. That identification has not been independently verified. Only the ten supplied images were used to read the handwriting; no OCR or published transcription was used.

| Scan / filename ending | Printed archive page | Top handwritten number | Lower-left handwritten numeral |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 91 / scan091 | 90 | 44 | 90 |
| 92 / scan092 | 91 | — | 91 |
| 93 / scan093 | 92 | 45 | 92 |
| 94 / scan094 | 93 | — | 93 |
| 95 / scan095 | 94 | 46 | 94 |
| 96 / scan096 | 95 | — | 95 |
| 97 / scan097 | 96 | 47 | 96 |
| 98 / scan098 | 97 | — | 97 |
| 99 / scan099 | 98 | 48 | 98 |
| 100 / scan100 | 99 | — | 99 |

The top numbers are represented by `\mspage`; the lower-left numerals by `\margin`, without assuming that the latter were added by the same hand. The printed archive identification, URL, and institutional footer are not part of the manuscript transcription. No manuscript page number has been supplied where none is visible at the top.

### Legibility and editorial limits

The main prose is appreciably easier to read than the small indices and corrections. The least reliable passages are the diagonal marginal material on scan 91, the marginal note on scan 93, the revised lower part of scan 95, the overwritten stabilizer notation on scan 96, the partly clipped bottom caption on scan 96, and the revised formulas and cancellation boundaries on scans 98–100. In those places the transcription intentionally leaves words, alphabets, indices, or correction attachments unresolved.

Repeated `\unsure{\mathcal E}` marks concern the alphabet of the curly extension-group symbol, not a claim that every occurrence is a different group. Repeated κ/k alternatives similarly preserve uncertainty between Greek κ and Latin k/K; the writing does not consistently establish case. These provisional letterforms must not be treated as independent identifications established from the surrounding mathematics.

Parentheses, grammatical fragments, and apparently surprising formulas have not been silently repaired. In particular, the sphere exceptions on scans 91 and 97, the center assertion on scan 98, the changed s/s′ indices on scan 98, and the revised inclusion wording on scan 99 require contextual study rather than editorial correction. The endpoint of the excerpt is not the endpoint of the discussion in the larger manuscript.

The supplied `.tex` file is deliberately a fragment. It uses the requested editorial macros, ordinary mathematical commands, and `tikz-cd`; their definitions and the document preamble belong to the receiving edition. Diagram ampersands use `ampersand replacement=\&` so that the diagrams can occur inside the `\diagram` argument. A temporary wrapper was used to check LaTeX syntax; its styling is not part of this delivery.

## 2. Table of doubts

Approximate manuscript lines are counted from the first body line on each scan, excluding the page numeral and printed footer; a displayed formula or diagram is counted as one body line. Marginal positions are described separately. Because the original baselines and additions are irregular, each location also gives the exact **line within that scan’s LaTeX section**, counting the initial `\scanpage` line as line 1; these are not line numbers in the complete concatenated file. Repeated identical markers on one page are grouped, and “×2” indicates two occurrences on the same LaTeX line.

Confidence describes confidence in the proposed visual reading, not confidence in the mathematical statement. “Low” means that the candidate should not be relied upon without returning to the image; “medium” means that the visible strokes favor it but leave a material ambiguity. For an illegible span, confidence is low in recovery and the word count is only an estimate. An alternative listed here is also provisional, not an emendation.

The fragment contains 149 `\unsure` occurrences and 22 `\illegible` occurrences. These counts include repetitions of the same uncertain notation and cancelled matter; they are not counts of independent textual errors.

## 4. Research notes — separate from the transcription

These notes concern only what is visible or argued in the supplied excerpt (Grothendieck, 1981, scans 91–100). “Unproved claim” means that the supporting proof is not supplied on these pages, not that the claim is false or unproved elsewhere. “Conjecture” is qualified when the source is a question, tentative recollection, or programme rather than a formal conjecture. No claim of novelty or literature coverage is made.

### R01 — Scan 91, ~1–7 — Kind: unproved claim

The presentation is described as “une relation de définition de π,” with conjugated peripheral elements and an ordered product. A proof would need to establish both generation and completeness of the single relation, with the precise choices of the peripheral representatives and their ordering. The uncertain indices in the first three lines prevent using this transcription alone as a definitive statement of those choices.

### R02 — Scan 91, ~8–13 and diagonal margin — Kind: unpursued implication

For “I=∅,” the passage replaces the visible peripheral-homomorphism data by a displayed cohomological identification involving H²(π,Z) and T. It would be useful to formulate exactly how this datum is transported by an outer automorphism and how it compares with the later χ-twisted local systems. The direction and label of the cohomological identification, and the detailed wording of the marginal instruction, should first be checked against the image.

### R03 — Scans 91, ~18–20; 97, ~10–15 — Kind: conjecture (tentative recollection, not a formal conjecture)

The words “Si je me rappelle bien” qualify the statement about not excluding the once-marked sphere on scan 91, whereas scan 97 excludes the sphere with zero or one marked point when asserting injectivity. These need not be contradictory: the two passages may be imposing different requirements on the objects and on their peripheral maps. A useful check is to separate admissibility of an object from injectivity of its lacet homomorphisms, without altering either passage.

### R04 — Scans 92, ~8–20; 93, ~1–2 — Kind: conjecture (research programme / expectation)

The proposed enlargement from homeomorphisms to more general maps “demanderait” an “étude soigneuse.” The missing work includes specifying the permitted ramification, the role of S′⊃f⁻¹(S), and the equivalence relation whose name is only provisionally read as a form of isotopie. The mention of Ladegaillerie is the manuscript’s attribution, not an independently checked description of that thesis.

### R05 — Scan 93, ~8–16 — Kind: unproved claim

The passage says that changing to “l’orientation opposée” leaves the groupoid map in place while inverting the lacet datum, and in the T=Z description replaces l_i by l_i⁻¹. A proof should make the correspondence between local orientation, the chosen T-parameter, and the peripheral maps explicit. It should also account for changes of representatives by conjugation, rather than treating the l_i as canonically chosen elements.

### R06 — Scans 93, ~17–20; 94, ~1–7 — Kind: unproved claim

The mixed groupoids are introduced “par la construction bien connue,” followed by a chain of groupoids and inverse-direction covering functors. The construction is not supplied here in enough detail to identify its objects and arrows solely from these pages. A verification should recover the stated Γ-torsor base category and check the variance of each functor, keeping the groupoids distinct from the later hatted topoi.

### R07 — Scans 94, ~12–18; 95, ~1–9 — Kind: unpursued implication

The reduction to a component and its stabilizer is followed by the warning that the “cas arithmétique” makes that reduction “inopportune.” This suggests separating equivalence of the underlying connected description from retention of the original map to the full Γ. These pages do not identify a particular arithmetic field or Galois group, so no such specialization should be read into the warning.

### R08 — Scan 95, ~7–9 — Kind: unproved claim

The text says that “(X,S,Γ) se récupère” from the component data and displays an induced-space expression whose product symbol is overwritten. A proof should explicitly construct the component copies indexed by cosets, their Γ-action, and the marked subset, and show independence of representatives. The exact notation for the construction must remain open until the overwritten symbol has been securely read.

### R09 — Scan 95, ~16–19 — Kind: unproved claim

The surjectivity discussion is revised to the phrase “U connexe,” together with Γ/Σ≃π₀(U). A verification should identify the image of the mixed fundamental group in Γ with the stabilizer of the chosen component, and then derive the asserted criterion. The deleted transitivity clause should be treated as a revision, not silently reinstated as the final statement.

### R10 — Scan 96, ~2–8 — Kind: unproved claim

The local finite faithful stabilizer is said to be “un groupe cyclique” or the following word provisionally transcribed as dihédral/dihedral. The missing justification is a classification of the relevant finite actions near a marked point, including the orientation-reversing case and the local regularity hypotheses. The insertion “est fini et” matters: the earlier allowance of a Γ that is not necessarily finite should not be replaced by a global finiteness assumption.

### R11 — Scans 96, ~11–18; 97, ~1–9 — Kind: unproved claim

The requirement that an element preserve or reverse orientation according to χ is followed by “Ceci implique” the relative local-system identification and the equivariance of κ_i. A proof should identify the conjugation action on the kernel L_i and compare it with the action of χp_i on T. It should also state exactly which changes of κ_i are absorbed by inner automorphisms, because the concluding clause on scan 97 is not fully legible.

### R12 — Scan 97, ~10–15 — Kind: unproved claim

After the displayed sphere exceptions, the text states that the peripheral maps are “injectifs” and that the mixed local groups can be viewed as “sous-groupes.” A proof should first establish injectivity of each geometric peripheral map and then show how the mixed extension maps inherit it. The statement should be checked against all standing hypotheses in the preceding manuscript, not generalized from the short list of exceptions on this page alone.

### R13 — Scan 97, ~16–20 — Kind: unpursued implication

The author calls it “particulièrement contre-indiqué” to require the point stabilizers all to equal Γ. A useful development would retain the action on the whole marked-point set, rather than replacing it by a family of Γ-fixed punctures. The parenthetical phrase about connectedness is too uncertain to support a stronger conclusion about which reductions are being assumed at this point.

### R14 — Scan 98, ~1–10 — Kind: unproved claim

The centerless-kernel discussion asserts that an extension is determined “à … unique près” by its outer action, with reconstruction as “l’image inverse” of the displayed automorphism extension. A proof needs to specify the equivalence relation on extensions, establish the kernel identification, and explain the sense in which uniqueness is asserted. The source’s cohomological or categorical hypotheses must be checked before applying this outside its intended class of groups.

### R15 — Scans 91, ~1–7; 98, ~1–3 — Kind: own idea

As a scope test for the phrase “le centre de π est réduit à 1,” specialize the earlier presentation formally to g=1 and I=∅: ⟨x,y | [x,y]=1⟩ is Z², whose center is not trivial. This is not an emendation or a claim that the manuscript is mistaken; an earlier hypothesis may exclude that specialization. **Confidence: high in the algebraic test, unresolved in its applicability**, because the omitted preceding pages may impose additional restrictions.

### R16 — Scan 98, ~4–10 — Kind: own idea

A concrete way to check the indicated pullback is to write E_ρ={(a,σ)∈Aut(π)×Σ : [a]=ρ(σ)} for the stated outer action ρ. Under the explicit assumption that π has trivial center, identify the kernel with π through inner automorphisms and then compare an arbitrary extension inducing ρ with this construction. **Confidence: high for this conditional construction**, because it is an explicit group pullback; the source’s uniqueness convention still needs to be specified.

### R17 — Scan 98, ~11–18 — Kind: unproved claim

The inserted assertion “s′ unique !” is important for recovering an actual permutation of the marked-point labels from the action on lacets. Its proof must establish the required distinction between the relevant peripheral morphism classes, with the χ-sign convention and the exceptional cases made explicit. The changed s/s′ index in the second displayed formula is not secure enough to resolve that issue editorially.

### R18 — Scans 98, ~19; 99, ~1–2 — Kind: conjecture (crossed-out question)

The cancelled sequence includes a proposed normalizer conclusion and then “donc sans doute” followed by the question whether the adjusted element belongs to the local mixed group. This is a deleted exploratory step, not an uncancelled conjecture to attribute to the final text. It is worth comparing with the normalizer reconstruction developed immediately afterwards, especially the distinction between moving a puncture and stabilizing it.

### R19 — Scan 99, ~8–20 — Kind: unproved claim

“Peut-on reconstituer” is answered by a normalizer argument culminating in the displayed identification of E_i with Norm_E(L_s). The key unsupported input on these pages is Norm_π(L_s)=L_s, together with the identification of the normalizer’s image with the point stabilizer. A proof must establish both statements for the intended class of peripheral groups and state their exceptions, rather than using them as general properties of arbitrary subgroups.

### R20 — Scan 99, ~14–20 — Kind: own idea

The final group-theoretic step can be isolated as follows: if H⊂N⊂E, p(H)=p(N), and H∩ker(p)=N∩ker(p), then H=N. For n∈N choose h∈H with p(h)=p(n); then h⁻¹n lies in the common kernel intersection and hence in H. **Confidence: high**, because this is a direct conditional argument; it does not establish the source’s separate self-normalizer and image claims.

### R21 — Scans 91, ~1–7; 98, ~1–3; 99, ~14–20 — Kind: own idea

The two-puncture sphere is a useful stress test: the formal presentation g=0, ν=2 gives l₁l₂=1, so the two peripheral subgroup images coincide with the cyclic group they generate. Thus unlabelled normalizer data alone need not distinguish the two marked points in that specialization, even though parameterized lacet maps can retain more information. **Confidence: high for the presentation calculation and moderate for its precise application to this reconstruction**, because the source explicitly singles out this exceptional case and the equivalence conventions need clarification.

### R22 — Scan 100, ~1–19 — Kind: unproved claim

The inverse reconstruction “redonne la structure à lacets,” and the subsequent data are said to be equivalent. Turning this into a theorem requires specifying objects, morphisms, conjugacy identifications, the role of the subgroup Σ⊂Γ, and the compatibility of both sign data. The overwritten choice of acting group in the conjugate formula should be resolved from the manuscript before writing the precise equivalence.

### R23 — Scan 100, ~20–22 — Kind: unpursued implication

The endpoint says that the construction defines π only as a “groupe extérieur,” with the T-lacet qualification inserted above the line. This invites a formulation that distinguishes a genuinely outer action from a chosen action by actual automorphisms, and records the dependence of any lift on choices. These pages do not supply a general strictification result, so such a result should not be inferred from the final sentence.

## 5. Source reference

Grothendieck, A. (1981). *La Longue Marche à travers la théorie de Galois* [Handwritten manuscript, cote 140-2, supplied scans 91–100; printed archive pages 90–99]. Université de Montpellier.

The title, date, author attribution, and archive designation in this reference are the metadata supplied with the task. The observations and quotations above are grounded in the attached images; no independent bibliographic or literature verification is claimed.


# ===== LM_P011 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P011 (scans 101–110): research notes, checked against the corrected transcription

Each first-pass note (R01–R21) is checked against `LM_P011_checked.tex` and kept, corrected or dropped, with the
reason. Notes added in the second pass are labelled S01–S07 ("second pass, own"). Kinds: conjecture/question, claim
without proof, implication not pursued, own idea. Literature mentioned below is a lead for later reading; it was
not checked against the corpus in this pass.

## First-pass notes

**R01 — scan 101 — claim without proof — KEEP, quote corrected.**
The author expects that full faithfulness of the functor "ne fasse pas de difficultés essentielles" (not "ne fera
pas"), using the case Γ = 1. The margin note beside these lines, misread in the first pass, says "Ça vaudrait
drôlement le coup de le faire très soigneusement…": the author himself marks this proof as worth writing out with
care. No proof is given here.

**R02 — scan 101 — implication not pursued — KEEP, reading corrected.**
The margin reads "Attention au cas S = ∅ dans la définition des structures : … (donnée de H²(π,Z) ≃^κ T); il faut
bien sûr tenir compte lors de l'action de Σ sur H²(π,Z) (via son action ext. sur π) et sur T (via χ) et exiger la
compatibilité", with some words still doubtful. With the corrected source category ("X surface compacte T
orientée"), the orientation datum lives in T, so the required compatibility is between the action on H²(π,Z) and
the χ-twisted action on T. The precise equivariance condition is not written out.

**R03 — scans 101–102 — conjecture/question — KEEP.**
Essential surjectivity is called "un problème ouvert sur lequel les gens sèchent": whether every finite subgroup of
the extended Teichmüller group is realized on (X₀,S₀), "de façon essentiellement unique". The question has an
existence part (lifting to A) and a uniqueness part (conjugacy by A⁰, "isotopie au sens strict"). See S02 for the
later literature.

**R04 — scan 102 — claim without proof — KEEP, quote corrected.**
"A⁰ est contractile, dans les cas anabéliens" (plural in the ink). The topology on the homeomorphism or
diffeomorphism group and the treatment of the marked points are not specified, and no proof is given. See S02.

**R05 — scan 102 — own idea (first pass) — KEEP.**
Separating the lifting problem (a splitting problem for the preimage extension) from the conjugacy problem
(simultaneous conjugacy of homomorphisms) is sound and matches the two clauses of the text.

**R06 — scan 103 — implication not pursued — KEEP.**
The aside distinguishes T^{(⊗−1)} ≃ H²(π,Z) from the "iso. canonique T^{⊗−1} ≃ T i.e. T^{⊗2} ≃ Z"; which data make
this identification canonical is not said, and its compatibility with the later T(K̄) is not discussed.

**R07 — scan 103 — conjecture/reconstruction question — KEEP.**
The programme is to recover the diagram Π_{D*,Γ} → Π_{U,Γ} → Π_{e,Γ} "à équivalence définie à isom unique près"
from the lacet data. The pages that follow carry it out for Π_{U,Γ} and Π_{D*}, and stop at the equivariant step
(R17).

**R08 — scan 104 — own idea (first pass) — KEEP, context corrected.**
Checking i_{u'',u'}∘i_{u',u} = i_{u'',u} by multiplying the unique conjugating elements is the right verification
of the "syst. transitif d'iso". In the corrected text the restriction functor u* "définit donc, avec u_* =
(u⁻¹)*, une équivalence des Topos B_{π'} → B_π" (labelled u_•), so the coherence has to be stated for these
equivalences.

**R09 — scan 105 — claim without proof (with a proposed route) — KEEP.**
"Comme Centr(π) = 1, l'opération de Σ sur π définit une ext. ℰ de Σ par π": the pull-back of Aut(π) → Out(π)
along Σ → Out(π) gives it, using Inn(π) ≅ π. In the profinite case continuity must be checked.

**R10 — scans 105–106 — question / incompletely checked claim — KEEP, notation corrected.**
The subscripts are Γ in the ink: B_{π,Γ} = B_ℰ, B_{π,Γ} → B_Γ, Θ_Γ : B_{π',Γ} → B_{π,Γ}, u_Γ, u'_Γ = int_ℰ(g)∘u_Γ.
The extension map ℰ' → ℰ is said to lie "au-dessus(?) de u" (the word is still doubtful). Compatibility with
composition of exterior homomorphisms is not written out.

**R11 — scan 105 — own idea (first pass) — KEEP, scope corrected.**
The corrected margin restricts the remark to anabelian loop groups: "marche pour les [groupes à] lacets
anabéliens, et les hom. de tels dont l'image soit d'indice fini… (car le centralisateur dans un tel groupe π d'un
sous-groupe d'indice fini est encore réduit à e)". The infinite dihedral group (centre trivial, but ⟨r⟩ of index 2
centralizes itself) shows why that restriction is needed; it is not a counterexample to the stated remark.

**R12 — scan 106 — own idea (first pass) — CORRECT.**
The margin says only "L'injectivité dans le cas qui nous intéresse résulte de l'hyp. anabélienne"; the
"(infinité des lacets)" read by the first pass is "(initiale des "lacets")", written under Λ and explaining the
letter. The substantive point stands: Aut(κ) = Centr_π(κ) = κ(T) uses self-centralization, and the identification
with T uses injectivity.

**R13 — scan 107 — own idea (first pass) — KEEP.**
With f_π = id, T = Z and κ injective and self-centralizing, Im(κ∘d) is centralized by κ(Z), so the hypothesis
"κ ∈ Λ ⇒ Centr_π κ = Im κ" forces d = ±1. The hypothesis is therefore a real restriction on d. See S05 for what this
suggests about the formula.

**R14 — scans 107–108 — own idea (first pass) — KEEP, ink confirmed.**
The ink confirms the readings the first pass left open: on scan 107 "u∘κ∘(d.id_T)" with an unprimed κ (the object
is κ'); on scan 108 the prose "u_Λ : Π_Λ → Π_{Λ'}" against the square Π_{Λ'} → Π_Λ, and "Λ_i = Λ_{i'}∘Θ∘(d_{i'}.id)"
with the prime on the index only. As written the last composition does not type-check (Θ : π' → π after a map into
π'); these are slips to be read, not silently repaired.

**R15 — scans 108–109 — claim without proof — KEEP, quote corrected.**
"si I → Homext(T,π) injectif, ces conditions montrent (not "impliquent") que τ est déterminé par Θ, et si de plus Z
opère fidèlement dans T, p.ex. si T ≃ Z, alors (d_{i'}) est également unique". Uniqueness has to be checked on
exterior classes, i.e. modulo conjugation (R16, S06).

**R16 — scans 108–109 — own idea (first pass) — KEEP.**
In the infinite dihedral group, conjugation by s identifies κ with κ∘(−id), so d = 1 and d = −1 give the same exterior
class although Z acts faithfully on T: faithfulness alone does not give uniqueness of d. S06 explains why this does
not happen for anabelian surface groups.

**R17 — scan 109 — claim without proof — KEEP, quote corrected.**
"Supposons maintenant (ouf !) que Σ opère sur groupes extérieurs à lacets… Je déclare forfait — il est évident que
tout marche bien !": the equivariant version of the whole construction is asserted without proof.

**R18 — scan 109 — conjecture/expectation — CORRECT.**
Over a field of finite type over F_p one should restrict to fundamental groups "premiers à p", "à cel' près nos
développements pourraient se faire quand même": the author expects the developments to carry over unchanged to
the prime-to-p quotients (the first pass left this clause unread). Which quotient, and whether injectivity and
the centralizer conditions survive in it, is not discussed.

**R19 — scan 110 — implication not pursued — KEEP.**
Σ is "remplacé par un conjugué, quand on change Ū₀"; the compatible change of the exterior action, of the lacet
data and of the topos diagram is not checked.

**R20 — scan 110 — claim without proof — CORRECT.**
The vertical sign under S(K̄) is ≃ turned through 90°, so the text asserts S(K̄) ≃ S₀(K̄) ∧_Σ Γ, with I = S₀(K̄) ⊂
S(K̄) "vide ssi(?) S = ∅". The claim follows from the transitivity of Γ on the components of X̄ (X connected) and
the disjointness of the components, but no argument is given. The IUT witness prints "S ≠ ∅", which is wrong; the
ink and CSG have "=".

**R21 — scan 110 — own idea (first pass) — KEEP.**
The "T(K̄)-structure à lacets, avec(?) comme ens d'indices I" carries a Σ-action on T(K̄) through the cyclotomic
character as well as a permutation of I; both have to enter the equivariance condition on each lacet map.

## Second-pass notes (own)

**S01 — scan 101 — own idea (gap in a stated condition).**
The source condition "si X₀ ≃ S², on ait card S₀ ≥ 3" does not exclude a torus component with S₀ = ∅, whose π = Z²
is commutative, while the target requires "π non commutatif"; the gloss "(i.e. π ≠ 0, Z)" also omits Z². The
anabelian condition 2g − 2 + ν > 0 excludes exactly the sphere with at most two points and the unpunctured torus.
Unless the torus is excluded on an earlier page, the stated source condition is incomplete. Confidence: high
(elementary).

**S02 — scans 101–102 — status lead for R03/R04.**
The existence part of R03 is the Nielsen realization problem, solved for finite subgroups of the mapping class
group by Kerckhoff (Ann. of Math. 117, 1983), after this text; whether that covers the extended group with marked
points and the uniqueness up to A⁰ still has to be checked. For R04, contractibility of the identity component is
classically due to Earle–Eells (1969, diffeomorphisms) and Hamstrom (1966, homeomorphisms); read the exact
hypotheses before citing either.

**S03 — scans 103 and 105 — implication not pursued.**
The corrected scan 103 makes the functoriality of B_π explicit: "de façon "fonctorielle" (pour Iso)", i.e. for
conjugacy classes of isomorphisms. The NB on scan 105 then extends the construction to every exterior homomorphism
with trivial centralizer, and the margin to finite-index homomorphisms between anabelian loop groups. The text
does not check that these extensions compose.

**S04 — scan 105 — own check of a claim without proof.**
With the corrected "(ce qui pour un épi se réduit à l'hyp Centr(π) = 1)" the parenthesis is immediate: if u is
surjective, the centralizer of u(π') is the centre of π. The first-pass "iso" would have stated a special case only.

**S05 — scans 107–108 — own idea.**
For a finite-index inclusion f_π : π' → π of surface groups (a covering ramified with index e at a puncture), one
has f_π∘κ' ~ κ∘(e·id_T) with κ self-centralizing. Under the stated hypothesis "κ ∈ Λ ⇒ Centr_π κ = Im κ", the
formula Λ = f_π∘Λ'∘(d.id_T) then forces e·d = ±1, which excludes ramification. The geometric situation suggests the
intended relation is f_π∘Λ' = Λ∘(d.id_T) (and on scan 108, Θ∘Λ'_{i'} = Λ_i∘(d_{i'}.id)). This is a proposed reading
of the intended mathematics, not a correction of the ink. Confidence: medium.

**S06 — scans 108–109 — own idea.**
In a torsion-free hyperbolic group, and so in a discrete anabelian surface group, no nontrivial element is
conjugate to its inverse: if hgh⁻¹ = g⁻¹, then h lies in the maximal elementary subgroup of g, which is infinite
cyclic, so h commutes with g and g² = 1. So d = ±1 are distinguished there, unlike in the dihedral test of R16. The
profinite case needs the corresponding statement for the normalizers of cuspidal inertia subgroups, which should
be looked up rather than assumed.

**S07 — scan 110 — claim without proof.**
"Alors Σ opère sur le schéma Ū₀ (or X̄₀), donc opère extérieurement sur son π₁": the passage from an action on the
scheme to an exterior action on the (profinite) fundamental group is stated without argument. The package ends
here; according to the CSG draft, the next page asserts the compatibility of this action with the T(K̄)-lacet
structure and with χ|Σ, which should be checked on that page's scan.


# ===== LM_P012 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P012 (scans 111–120): research notes, checked against the corrected text

The first pass's research notes R01–R27 are checked here against `LM_P012_checked.tex`. Each note gives its verdict (keep, correct or drop) with a reason, the note itself, and its scan and kind. "Unproved claim" means that these ten scans give no proof; it says nothing about the rest of the manuscript. The notes labelled **P** are the second pass's own.

## First-pass notes

**R01 — scan 111 — unproved claim — KEEP (minor correction).** Grothendieck says that the operation of Σ on π determines its action on the index set I, and hence "on récupère donc le K-schéma étale S" through S(K̄) ≃ (·) ∧_Σ Γ. The first factor is written over itself (I₀ or S̄₀). CSG reads S̄₀(K̄), which matches the identification I = S̄₀(K̄) on the previous page, so the claim does not depend on how the symbol is read. A proof needs the induced-set construction from the Σ-set I to a Γ-set.

**R02 — scan 111 — unproved claim — KEEP (corrected).** "Mais mieux, on récupère tout le diagramme Π_{D̄₀*} → [Π_{Ū₀} →] Π_Ū → Π_e" with the Σ-action, and from it a "diagramme des topos". The qualifier of "topos" is unresolved: the main line reads "des revêt.(?)", and a cancelled addition above it may read "classifiants". The claim covers the whole diagram with its equivariance data, not only the boundary set.

**R03 — scans 111–112 — unproved claim — KEEP (corrected).** Morphisms (X',S') → (X,S) "se décrivent simplement" by outer homomorphisms π' → π. These are compatible with Σ' ⊂ Σ and with the anabelian lacet structures. The compatibility is expressed by a Σ'-compatible map τ : I' ⊂ S̄'₀ → I = S̄₀ and by "un système d'entiers naturels (d_i)_{i∈I'}". The first pass had cancelled "⊂ S̄'₀" and read "une application d'entiers naturels"; both readings were wrong. The claim that these data suffice, and not merely follow, is unproved.

**R04 — scan 112 — unproved claim — KEEP (corrected).** For I = ∅ (S = ∅), the lacet structure is completed by a Kummer isomorphism κ : T^{⊗−1} ≃ H²(π,Ẑ), that is Ẑ ≃ H²(π,T), compatible with Σ. The bracket reads "en caractéristique p ≥ 0" (not "p > 0"): the restriction to l-adic components with l ≠ p is stated uniformly for every characteristic and is empty in characteristic 0. The equivariance of κ is asserted, not proved.

**R05 — scans 112–113 — unproved claim — KEEP (corrected).** The degree d (∈ ℕ) is "défini de façon unique par cette condition" (the commutative square), and the text also links it to "l'ordre de Coker f*" ("comme" or "ou aussi"; unresolved). Given the two identifications κ and κ', f* is multiplication by d on Ẑ, so Coker f* = Ẑ/dẐ has order d when d ≠ 0. That the index equals the geometric degree is not shown. On the direction "π → π'", see P02.

**R06 — scan 113 — conjecture — KEEP (corrected).** "Quand S(*?) = ∅, (S' ≠ ∅), il devrait y avoir encore […] une compatibilité pour les données kummériennes." The first pass had misread "kummériennes" as "harmoniciennes". The Kummer data have different natures on the two sides: lacets on π', and the purely cohomological κ on π. The required compatibility is announced, not formulated.

**R07 — scans 113–114 — question — KEEP (corrected).** "La question équivaut sans doute à celle de décrire une structure kummérienne “cohomologique” sur le groupe π̃'", where π̃' is obtained from π' by dividing out the lacets. Grothendieck adds that the question is the same in the topological and arithmetic settings ("il me faudra revenir dessus"). He also asks that π̃' → π̃ respect the lacet structure for a suitable degree. The first pass's sentence about the "uncertain adjective harmonicienne" is dropped: the word is "kummérienne".

**R08 — scan 114 — conjecture — CORRECT.** For pairs (X̄,S̄) defined over K̄, the datum is an outer group with T-lacets "muni d'une action (non de Γ mais des) noyau de groupe profini défini par Γ". The word is settled as "noyau", not "germe", and recurs in quotation marks as “noyau” on l. 19. The status of G stays open in the text: "(pro-fini ???)" and "(“discret” ???)". One must still decide which topology G and G → Γ carry.

**R09 — scan 114 — conjecture — KEEP (corrected).** G₀, the outer lacet automorphisms commuting with the action of the “noyau”, is "conjecturalement par la “conj. bordélique”" isomorphic to the finite group Aut_K̄(X̄,S̄). The epithet "bordélique" is legible. It refers to the manuscript's own earlier "conjecture bordélique" (CK witness: "Conjecture bordélique (7.1)", §7), not to an external result. Both a realisation statement and a faithfulness statement would be needed.

**R10 — scan 115 — conjecture — KEEP.** "La “restriction de (X̄,S̄) au corps K”" (the quotation covers the whole phrase) is expressed by a splitting Γ → G of G → Γ in 1 → G₀ → G → Γ. This is "on l'espère, de façon pleinement fidèle, si la conj. bordélique est valable". The hope covers morphisms, not only the existence of sections.

**R11 — scans 115–116 — unproved claim — CORRECT.** The relation ends in "= 0", and on these pages Grothendieck writes the trivial group as 0 ("π=0", "π≠0"), so this is his convention rather than an error. The bound "g=0, ν≤3" is in the ink, as in all the witnesses, but it is a slip (see P03). The freeness and rank claims of a) are standard consequences of the presentation and are not proved here.

**R12 — scan 116 — unproved claim — KEEP.** The canonical exact sequence 0 → T_π → ∏ L_i → π_ab → π̃_ab → 0 and the isomorphisms T_π → L_i hold under the added hypothesis "pour I ≠ ∅, π ≠ 0". The two margin notes treat the exception: π = 0 exactly when g = 0 and ν = 0 or 1, and otherwise the L_i are ≃ ℤ. Canonicity, meaning independence of the chosen representatives L_i, is taken up on scan 117.

**R13 — scan 116 — unproved claim — KEEP.** For I = ∅ and g ≠ 0, T_π is defined as H²(π,ℤ)^{⊗−1}. Grothendieck promises "on établira plus bas une relation entre les deux définitions de T_π"; the comparison is not on these pages.

**R14 — scan 116 — unpursued implication — KEEP (corrected).** "(donc g est uniquement déterminé par π et ν = card I." The phrase replaces the cancelled "la famille des ([L_i])": Grothendieck reduced the needed datum from the whole family to its cardinality. π alone does not determine (g,ν) when ν ≥ 1 (see R26).

**R15 — scan 117 — unproved claim — KEEP.** Apart from the abelian cases g = 0, ν = 1, 2, the conjugacy classes of the L_i are distinct. Every element normalising some L_i centralises it. The text calls this evident only for ν ≥ 2, where L_i → π_ab is injective. It follows that the members of one class are canonically isomorphic, which makes ∏ L_i and T_π → L_i intrinsic.

**R16 — scan 117 — open check — KEEP.** The margin note "voir à part le cas ν=1" points to the normaliser, in the free group on the x_α, y_α, of the subgroup generated by ∏[x_α,y_α]. It is a check left for later, not a proof.

**R17 — scans 117–118 — unproved claim — KEEP.** An action of Σ on the lacet structure induces an action on the invertible ℤ-module T_π, hence a character χ : Σ → ℤ* = {±1}. Conversely, one may speak of an action "compatible avec χ". The cancelled continuation about a T-structure à lacets ("et si Σ opère sur T (… χ) on dira qu'une action est compatible") is not a settled definition.

**R18 — scan 118 — conjecture — CORRECT.** The profinite variants are "(ou profinies premières à p, si p est premier donné…)": the prime-to-p variants are an alternative, not a special case. The first check, Norm_π(L_i) = Centr_π(L_i) = L_i, is qualified "dans le cas ν=1, sinon pas de pb", so the stated difficulty concerns only ν = 1. The discrete argument does not transfer automatically to the profinite case.

**R19 — scan 118 — conjecture — KEEP.** The second check is Centr(π) = (1) "(cas anabélien)", and more generally that the centraliser of every open subgroup of π is trivial. The margin says that this is not proved "pour le moment dans aucun cas anabélien profini !". This records the author's state of knowledge when he wrote; the second pass has not checked the present status.

**R20 — scan 119 — unproved claim — CORRECT.** "Par le topos|tapis d'Artin sur les ouverts du topos" (the noun is unresolved; the addition reads "du topos"), the constructible sheaves of sets on X that are locally constant on U correspond to triples (F_U, F_S, φ) with φ : F_S → j*i_*(F_U). This is Artin's gluing for an open-closed decomposition. The correspondence for all étale sheaves, constructibility apart, is asserted, not proved.

**R21 — scans 119–120 — unproved claim — KEEP.** The restriction is to F with F_U coming from the fundamental topos B_{Π₁(U)}, and "il n'y a pas lieu de supposer F_U à fibres finies". The admitted class must not be silently reduced to finite locally constant sheaves.

**R22 — scan 120 — unproved claim — KEEP (corrected).** The henselian disks D_s = Spec O_s ("disque en s") and D_s* = D_s − {s} = Spec K_s ("disque pointé") give the commutative diagram U ← D* → D → X with S (j₀) → D. The local hypothesis "dim_s S = 1" on scan 119 is in the ink but is inconsistent with S being discrete (see P04).

**R23 — scan 120 — unproved claim — CORRECT.** The sheaves on D are "“ess^t loc. constant”", that is essentiellement localement constants, not "ind loc. constant". The NB states that S → D induces an equivalence between "ces faisceaux" on D and the étale sheaves on S. For a henselian local ring this is the identification of the fundamental groups of the disk and of its closed point (see P05). The statement concerns only that class, not all étale sheaves on D.

**R24 — scan 120 — unproved claim — KEEP.** The gluing datum becomes F_D → σ_*ρ^*F_U ("ou encore", where the page ends). Comparing it with φ : F_S → j*i_*F_U needs base change and adjunction, and compatibility with morphisms of triples.

**R25 — scans 113, 116, 119–120 — first-pass model's idea — KEEP (clarified).** The idea is a relative cohomology of a group with its peripheral subgroups, to compare the kernel definition of T_π with the cohomological one. With the corrected reading "structure kummérienne “cohomologique”", this comparison is exactly the question Grothendieck poses on scan 113. It remains a research direction, not a result.

**R26 — scans 115–117 — first-pass model's idea — KEEP.** The types (0,3) and (1,1) both give a free π of rank 2g+ν−1 = 2. So π alone does not determine (g,ν), which agrees with "g est uniquement déterminé par π et ν = card I". The peripheral data must therefore be part of any reconstruction.

**R27 — scans 119–120 — first-pass model's idea — KEEP (minor correction).** The proposal is to express the local gluing datum through the punctured henselian disks, with F_D essentially locally constant ("ess^t", not "ind"). The page shows the start of this ("ou encore …"), and CSG continues with the description by the fundamental topoi B_{Π₁D*}, B_{Π₁U} and B_{Π₁D}. Finite fibres must not be assumed.

## Second-pass notes (own)

**P01 — scan 113 — Grothendieck's question (own note).** The corrected passage makes the problem explicit. When S = ∅ but S' ≠ ∅, a morphism must be compatible with the Kummer data even though one side has lacets (π') and the other only the cohomological κ (π). Grothendieck reduces this, "sans doute", to defining a cohomological Kummer structure on π̃' = π'/⟨⟨lacets⟩⟩, the "bouchage des trous" of the margin and the subject of §14. He leaves it open ("il me faudra revenir dessus").

**P02 — scans 112–113 — apparent slip (own note).** "que l'hom π → π' induit un diagramme commutatif": the morphism of pairs gives π' → π (scan 112, l. 1), and f* : H²(π,T) → H²(π',T) is the map induced contravariantly by π' → π. "π → π'" is therefore apparently a slip for π' → π. The first pass and IUT keep "π → π'".

**P03 — scan 116 — error in the manuscript (own check).** For ν ≥ 1, π is free of rank 2g+ν−1 and so abelian iff g = 0 and ν ≤ 2. For ν = 0 it is abelian iff g ≤ 1 (trivial or ℤ²). The bracket "π ≃ π_{g,ν} est abélien ssi g=0, ν≤3 ou g=1, ν≤0" should therefore read "ν≤2": π_{0,3} is free of rank 2. The corrected bound agrees with a) "sauf si g=0, ν≤2" and with the exclusion "g=0, ν≤2" on scan 117. All the witnesses reproduce "ν ≤ 3".

**P04 — scan 119 — slip in a hypothesis (own check).** As written, "S un sous-schéma fermé réduit discret tel que ∀s∈S, dim_s S = 1" is contradictory, since a discrete S has dim_s S = 0. The consequence drawn, "(donc S défini par une partie fermée discrète de X)", suggests that the intended condition is dim O_{X,s} = 1 (codimension 1 in X), which with dim X = 1 makes S discrete. The later addition "réduit discret" makes the condition redundant in any case.

**P05 — scan 120 — claim without proof, standard argument (own note).** The NB "l'inclusion S → D définit une équivalence" for essentially locally constant sheaves is the henselian property. For O_s henselian, the finite étale covers of D_s are those of the closed point, so π₁(D_s) ≅ π₁(s) and the locally constant sheaves agree. "ess^t loc. constant" is not defined on this page. CSG's continuation identifies these sheaves with the objects of the fundamental (multigaloisian) topoi.

**P06 — scan 114 — interpretation (own idea, low confidence).** "(non de Γ mais des) noyau de groupe profini défini par Γ": a pair (X̄,S̄) over K̄ is defined over some finite extension, so only open subgroups of Γ act, and only up to shrinking. The quoted word “noyau” seems to be a provisional name for this system of open subgroups, and G → Γ has open image. That would explain why Grothendieck hesitates over whether G is "pro-fini ???" or "discret ???".

**P07 — scan 115 — possible cancellation (own observation).** In the definition of a lacet structure, "∀i∈I" before "un L_i ∈ [L_i]" may be cancelled (a stroke through "∈ I" and an overwritten ∀). If so, the quantifier over i must be read from the context ("un L_i ∈ [L_i], un générateur l_i ∈ L_i"). The mathematics does not change, because a representative L_i and a generator l_i are chosen for every i in any case.

**P08 — scan 118 — status of the two checks (own note).** The two profinite checks have different scopes. The first, Norm = Centr = L_i, is said to be a problem only for ν = 1. The second asks for trivial centralisers of all open subgroups, which is much stronger than a trivial centre. The margin's "dans aucun cas anabélien profini" applies to the whole list. The second pass has not checked which of these statements were later proved, or by whom.


# ===== LM_P013 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P013 (scans 121–130): research notes, checked against the corrected text

Each first-pass note (R01–R23, O01–O05 in `answers/unzipped/LM_P013/LM_P013_notes.md`) is checked against `second_pass/LM_P013_checked.tex`. A verdict follows each note (keep, correct or drop), with a reason. Notes added in this pass are labelled **[second pass]**. "Kind" uses the categories of the task: conjecture or question, claim without proof, implication not pursued, own idea.

## First-pass notes

**R01. Scan 121. Claim without proof. Correct.** The fundamental topos $B_{\pi_1,X}$ is said to be the amalgamated sum of the diagram (*). The iso case $\varphi$ is identified with sheaves on $X$ that are "loc. ess. constants". The corrected text concerns *essentially* locally constant sheaves ("ess$^{t}$ loc. constants"), not "ét. loc. constants". The universal property, and its 2-categorical sense, still need to be stated and proved.

**R02. Scans 122–123. Claim without proof. Correct.** The text says the quotient formula follows "aussitôt" (not "d'où résulte"): $\pi_1(X)=\mathcal E=\pi_1(U)/$(invariant subgroup generated by the $\rho_i(\Pi_i)$). The construction uses $\rho_!(\widetilde D_i^{*})$ and path classes to Ũ. In the profinite setting the generated subgroup must be read as the closed normal subgroup. The text does not say so, and the transcription does not add a closure sign.

**R03. Scan 123. Conjecture/programme. Keep.** "Notre propos est celui d'un calcul galoisien (si possible)" is a qualified programme, not a theorem.

**R04. Scan 124. Question. Correct (wording).** The text reads "On se pose la question" (singular) and calls $B_{X,U}$ "le topos abracadabra". Question a) asks for an explicit calculation of its cohomology in terms of profinite group cohomology. It notes that the cohomology of $B_{\pi_1,X}$ is the profinite group cohomology of $\pi_1(X)=\mathcal E/\ldots$. The rest of the note stands.

**R05. Scan 124. Question. Correct (detail).** The question is whether $H^{*}(B_{X,U},F)\to H^{*}(X_{\mathrm{ét}},f^{*}F)$ is an isomorphism, for every torsion sheaf $F$ on $B_{X,U}$ and in all degrees at once (the superscript is a star, not q). Any answer must keep the torsion hypothesis.

**R06. Scan 124. Claim without proof. Keep.** Up to this point, only "S partie fermée discrète, X connexe" has been used, not dim X = 1 or noetherianity. The later local steps (scans 128–129) use dim$_s X=1$ explicitly.

**R07. Scan 125. Claim without proof. Correct.** The hypothesis reads "les $D_s$ (or $O_s$) sont des *jauges* à corps résiduel $k(s)$ de car. 0". "jauges" is the ink and the witnesses' reading, where a henselian trait is the expected notion. The first pass dropped a condition: the isomorphisms $T_{\mathcal E}\simeq T_i$ are $\mathcal E_i$-isomorphisms (equivariant). The canonical Kummer isomorphism $T_i\simeq\Pi_i$ is announced in the future tense ("on aura"). Residue characteristic 0 is what makes the whole inertia tame, so $\Pi_i$ is identified with $\widehat{\mathbb Z}(1)$.

**R08. Scan 125. Conjecture (wish). Keep.** "On aimerait pouvoir paraphraser, sur $B_{X,U}$" the localization sequence. Scans 126–127 carry it out ("On trouve donc une suite exacte").

**R09. Scan 126. Claim without proof. Keep.** Finite projective limits and arbitrary inductive limits in $B_{X,U}$ are said to be computed "terme à terme". That requires $\sigma^{*}$ and $\rho^{*}$ to commute with them, and this is not checked here.

**R10. Scans 126–127. Implication not pursued. Keep.** The open object "U" of $B_{X,U}$ is $(e_U,\varnothing_D,\varphi)$, and the residual topos is $B_{\pi_1,D}$. Writing out the six functors of this open/closed decomposition remains to be done.

**R11. Scan 127. Claim without proof. Keep (wording corrected).** The gluing functor is $\sigma_*\rho^*$: inverse image on $B_{\pi_1,D}$ of the direct image of $F$ on $B_{\pi_1,U}$. This recovers Artin's description of a topos "déduit par recollement" (not "décrit") from an open and its closed complement.

**R12. Scan 127. Claim without proof. Keep.** The five-lemma reduction needs a morphism of long exact sequences, compatible with the boundary maps. That compatibility is not shown.

**R13. Scan 128. Claim without proof / formula to check. Keep.** The ink confirms the coefficient letter E in $H^0_S(B_{X,U},E)$ while the prose speaks of sections of $F$. The display $\prod_i(\operatorname{Ker}(E_i\to E_U)$ shows no $\Gamma_i$-invariants, although the prose requires the $x_i$ to be $\Gamma_i$-invariant.

**R14. Scan 128. Claim without proof. Correct (details).** $H^0_S$ factors through the analogous "inclusion" $B_{\pi_1,D^{*}}\to B_{D,D^{*}}$, where $\pi_1,D^{*}$ is marked "remplaçant $\pi_1,U$". The $H^i_S$ "sur $D_{X,U}$" (sic, for $B_{X,U}$) are computed as in the local situation $B_{D,D^{*}}$. That calculation is the "calcul habituel" for $\mathcal E_i\to\Gamma_i$, "que j'ai un peu oublié !". It is still to be written.

**R15. Scans 128–129. Claim without proof. Correct.** dim$_s X=1$ is said to imply that the localized topos situation $D^{*}\hookrightarrow D$ is "déjà entièrement définie en termes des topos fondamentaux", so that $B_{D,D^{*}}$ is equivalent to $D_{\mathrm{ét}}$. The arrow is an inclusion, and the index is a-shaped in the ink (the witnesses read ét). The reason is that $D_s^{*}$ is the spectrum of a field and $D_s$ is henselian. This needs to be stated with the hypotheses of scan 120.

**R16. Scan 129. Claim without proof. Keep.** The Proposition is conditional on $H^i(\mathcal E,F_{\mathcal E})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^i(U_{\mathrm{ét}},F_U)$ for all i. Here $F_U$ is a sheaf (probably "étale") on $U_{\mathrm{ét}}$ defined by an $\mathcal E$-group $F_{\mathcal E}$.

**R17. Scan 129. Claim without proof. Correct.** The example reads "Exemple : OK Si U est une courbe affine sur k alg. clos, F premier à car". The word "torsion" was a cancelled word, and "OK" is part of the sentence, not a margin note. The justification follows: "OK pour i=0,1", and for i=2 both members vanish because "le groupe fondamental premier à p est libre". As noted, the text writes i=2, not i ≥ 2 (see S03).

**R18. Scan 129. Claim without proof. Keep.** "d'où on déduit le cas analogue pour U [or X; one letter is written over the other] affine sur k quelconque" gives no descent argument.

**R19. Scan 129. Question. Keep.** "puis même si U n'est pas affine mais simplement quasi-projective ?" is a question.

**R20. Scan 129, margin. Conjecture withdrawn. Keep.** The corollary "la cohomologie de X à coefficients dans un $F_X$ loc. const. se calcule également galoisiennement ?" is rejected: "Non, à cause du genre 0 !!!". It adds that for smooth projective curves of genus ≠ 0 "il faut un argument spécial". The word "galoisiennement", missing from both witnesses, is in the ink.

**R21. Scan 130. Claim without proof. Correct.** For S = ∅ the text says that "la cohomologie de U à (coefficients dans les syst. locaux) est celle de π". It is not about "fixing all local coefficients". It then gives a canonical $H^2(\pi,T)\simeq H^2(X,T)\,(\simeq\widehat{\mathbb Z})$ "avec grain(s) de sel en car. p>0". See S04 for the implicit genus hypothesis.

**R22. Scan 130. Claim without proof. Correct (the clause is now legible).** "b) Si S ≠ ∅, les $H^i_!(U,-)$" (cohomology with proper supports) are determined [or described] and computed by Galois means, "en termes des groupes à lacets". The construction rebuilds the diagram with $B_{\pi_1,D}$ as the discrete topos on S = I, "en postulant que le composant $B_{\pi_1,D_i^{*}}$ n'est autre que $B_{L_i}$ (avec les notations du n° précédent)". The input is thus a group with loops, and the $L_i$ are its loop subgroups.

**R23. Scan 130. Claim without proof. Correct (detail).** The canonical isomorphisms come "par calculs locaux" (not "classes locales"). The comparison with $H^2(X,\widehat{\mathbb Z})\simeq H^2(\pi,\widehat{\mathbb Z})$ is explicitly qualified "(si g ≠ 0)". The combined isomorphisms $L_i\simeq H^2(\pi,\widehat{\mathbb Z})^{\otimes-1}$ still need their normalization and their compatibility across i.

**O01. Scans 122–123. Own idea (first pass). Keep, sharpened.** Changing the path classes from $\rho_!(\widetilde D_i^{*})$ to Ũ conjugates $\rho_i$ by an element of $\mathcal E$. Hence the invariant subgroup generated by the $\rho_i(\Pi_i)$, and so $\pi_1(X)$, does not depend on the choices. What remains open is the comparison of whole diagrams (with the $\sigma_i$ and the $\mathcal E_i$-sets).

**O02. Scans 124–128. Own idea (first pass). Keep.** A derived or mapping-cone model of $B_{X,U}$-cohomology is still a reasonable check of the open/closed sequence.

**O03. Scan 128. Own idea (first pass). Keep.** The display still shows no invariants. The example ($C_2$ acting on $\mathbb Z/3$ by −1, $E_U=0$) separates the kernel from its $\Gamma_i$-invariants.

**O04. Scans 129–130. Own idea (first pass). Correct.** The margin of scan 130 states that $H^2_!(B_{\pi_1,U},\widehat{\mathbb Z})\simeq\widehat T_\pi^{\otimes-1}$ canonically in all cases, "même si g=0", provided ν ≠ 0. The genus-0 failure therefore concerns the case without punctures (projective X, S = ∅). The diagnostic examples should be chosen there (g = 0, ν = 0) and compared with g = 0, ν ≥ 1.

**O05. Scan 130. Own idea (first pass). Keep.** Check that automorphisms of the group with loops act compatibly on all the $L_i$ through the local-calculation isomorphisms. The independence of i is claimed in the text that follows (CSG/IUT: "les composés $T_\pi\to L_i\to H^2(\pi,\widehat{\mathbb Z})^{\otimes-1}$ ne dépendent pas du choix de i").

## Notes added in the second pass

**S01. Scan 121. Implication not pursued [second pass].** The reduction to fundamental topoi needs $\sigma^{*}$ and $\rho^{*}$ to send essentially locally constant sheaves to essentially locally constant sheaves. It also needs $F_D$ to be automatically of that kind, which scan 120 asserts through the equivalence with étale sheaves on S. Then the triples $(F_U,F_D,\varphi)$ live entirely in $B_{\pi_1,U}$, $B_{\pi_1,D}$ and $B_{\pi_1,D^{*}}$. Neither step is argued on these pages.

**S02. Scan 125. Implication not pursued [second pass].** The equivariance restored in the transcription has a consequence. Because $T_{\mathcal E}\simeq T_i$ are $\mathcal E_i$-isomorphisms and $\Pi_i$ acts trivially on the $\Gamma_i$-module $T_i$, every inertia group $\rho_i(\Pi_i)$ acts trivially on $T_{\mathcal E}$. So $T_{\mathcal E}$ descends to a local system $T_X$ with an action of $\mathcal E$ modulo the invariant subgroup generated by the $\rho_i(\Pi_i)$, that is of $\pi_1(X)$ by scan 123. This is exactly the parenthesis "(i.e. un $T_X$ sur lequel opère $\widetilde{\mathcal E}=\mathcal E/\ldots$)".

**S03. Scan 129. Claim without proof [second pass].** In the affine-curve example the Proposition needs the comparison in every degree. Both sides vanish for all i ≥ 2, not only for i = 2, provided both have cohomological dimension ≤ 1 at the primes dividing the order of F. The text's reason ("le groupe fondamental premier à p est libre") concerns only the maximal prime-to-p quotient of $\mathcal E$. It still has to be shown that $H^i(\mathcal E,F_{\mathcal E})$ with prime-to-p coefficients agrees with the cohomology of that quotient, or that $\mathcal E$ itself has ℓ-cohomological dimension ≤ 1 for ℓ ≠ p.

**S04. Scan 130. Claim with an implicit hypothesis [second pass].** Case a) (S = ∅) asserts $H^2(\pi,T)\simeq H^2(X,T)\,(\simeq\widehat{\mathbb Z})$ for a connected projective curve over an algebraically closed field. For g = 0 this fails: π is trivial, so $H^2(\pi,T)=0$, while $H^2(\mathbb P^1,T)\neq0$. That is the obstruction of the scan 129 margin ("Non, à cause du genre 0 !!!"). Case a) therefore needs g ≠ 0, which the text states only in case b).

**S05. Scan 130, margin. Claim without proof [second pass].** "Mais dans tous les cas (même si g = 0, du moment qu'on n'a pas ν = 0)" means as soon as there is at least one puncture. In that case $H^2_!(B_{\pi_1,U},\widehat{\mathbb Z})\simeq\widehat T_\pi^{\otimes-1}$ canonically, "d'où une description cohomologique des modules d'orientation, commune au cas sans trous et avec trous". The first pass had reversed the condition ("voire pour ν = 0") and lost the proper supports. The excluded case g = 0, ν = 0 is exactly the one in which π is trivial.

**S06. Scans 126 and 130. Claim without proof [second pass].** $B_{\pi_1,D}\approx B_{\pi_1,S}$ (scan 126) rests on each $D_s$ being henselian, so that it has the fundamental group of its closed point. Over an algebraically closed k each $\Gamma_i$ is trivial. This is why scan 130 can describe $B_{\pi_1,D}$ as "topos discret défini par S = I".

**S07. Scans 128 and 130. Implication not pursued [second pass].** The "calcul habituel … que j'ai un peu oublié" can be written down in the case used on scan 130. The local situation $B_{D,D^{*}}$ is $B_{X,U}$ with (D, D*) in place of (X, U), so its recollement gives a long exact sequence $\cdots\to H^q_{s_i}\to H^q(\Gamma_i,\cdot)\to H^q(\mathcal E_i,\cdot)\to H^{q+1}_{s_i}\to\cdots$. Over an algebraically closed k, $\Gamma_i=1$ and $\mathcal E_i=\Pi_i$. With coefficients $\widehat{\mathbb Z}$ (as a limit over $\mathbb Z/n$) this gives $H^2_{s_i}\simeq H^1(\Pi_i,\widehat{\mathbb Z})=\operatorname{Hom}(\Pi_i,\widehat{\mathbb Z})$. That is $L_i^{\otimes-1}$ once $\Pi_i$ is identified with $L_i$, which is the displayed isomorphism "$H^2_{s_i}(B_{U,X},\widehat{\mathbb Z})\simeq L_i^{\otimes-1}$".


# ===== LM_P014 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P014 (scans 131–140): research notes, checked against the corrected transcription

Each first-pass note (R01–R25) is kept, corrected or dropped against `LM_P014_checked.tex`; notes added in the second pass are marked "Own (second pass)". Scan numbers are the file numbers.

## First-pass notes

**R01 — scan 131 — unproved claim — keep, corrected.** The exponent is now read ⊗−1, so the claim is: the composites T_π ≅ L_i → H²(π, Ẑ)^{⊗−1} do not depend on i, hence T_π is identified with the dual of H²(π, Ẑ) when g ≠ 0. The exponent doubt that the first pass gave as a block to formalisation is settled; the independence of i still needs the preceding pages' definitions.

**R02 — scans 131–132 — conjecture / proposed construction — keep.** "On voudrait en déduire" a diagram of topoi (horizontal links drawn without heads) and a trace homomorphism inducing an isomorphism H²_!(U′, Ẑ) → H²_!(U, Ẑ) that should be the contragredient of the orientation isomorphism attached to f. The degree H²_! is now settled; the construction remains a wish, not a result.

**R03 — scans 132–133 — unproved claim — keep, corrected.** The section is about "morphismes mixtes" (not "non stricts"), and the datum k identifies the local system of π₁ on B_{D*} with "le système loc. constant T", a locally constant system, not a constant one. This is what is said to define B_D (≃ B_S) and B_{X,U} with complementary open and closed subtopoi; the reconstruction of (X, S) from these data is still asserted, not proved.

**R04 — scan 133 — unpursued implication — keep, corrected.** The ink reads "On aura S′ = S′₀ ⨿ S′₁ (S′₀ = f⁻¹(S)), donc D′ = D′₀ ⨿ D′₁": the splitting of D′ is a consequence of that of S′, not a further datum. The point stands that B_{X′,U′} → B_{X,U} needs data beyond f_π: π′ → π.

**R05 — scan 134 — unproved claim — keep.** The passage from a family of universal coverings of U indexed by S′₁ = I′₁ to a family of π-torsors (P_{i′}) after choosing Ũ is asserted; the action conventions and the change of Ũ remain to be tracked.

**R06 — scan 134 — proof deferred — keep, corrected.** The ink has "l'hom de topos B_{X′,U′} → B_{X,U}" (a cancelled "un" replaced by the definite article): the author expects the square on the 0-part and B_{D′₁*} → B_{D′₁} → B_U to determine the morphism. "Je passe sur le détail de la description" leaves this unproved.

**R07 — scan 134, margin — conjecture — keep, corrected.** Reading settled: "Il semble qu'on soit en droit de faire la description des morphismes de topos B_{X′,U′} → B_{X,U} qui induisent U′ → U et qui sur D′₁* (= D′*|U …) se factorisent par D′₁* → D′₁" (the witnesses' "en train de" is not the ink). It is a characterisation claim without proof; the parenthesis identifying D′₁* with the part of D′* over U is not fully secure.

**R08 — scan 135 — unproved claim — keep, corrected.** In the equivariant version the operator group at the head of the paragraph is Γ written over Σ, the source topos is B_{D′₁,Σ} = B_{I′₁,Σ(?)}, and the target B_{U,Γ} is Γ written over Σ. The author is passing from Σ to Γ in mid-paragraph, so the groups in this display must not be silently unified. The required datum is, for each orbit, i.e. for each i′ ("tt i′"), a Γ_{s′}-object of the category of universal coverings of (U, Γ).

**R09 — scans 135–136 — unproved claim — keep, corrected.** The ink says the datum B_{Γ_{s′}} → B_{X,U} "revient sauf erreur à" a right π-torsor P and a splitting of ℰ^P → Γ over Γ_{s′}: the author marks the equivalence as tentative.
