ℰ is "une extension de Σ ⊂ Γ par π" (the inclusion was dropped by the first pass). Changes of representative and of torsor still need checking.

**R10 — scan 136 — unproved claim — keep.** An automorphism of (P, q) corresponds to α ∈ π centralising Γ_{s′}, "i.e. qui soit fixé par Γ_{s′} opérant sur π"; the action of Γ_{s′} on π is the one given by the splitting. Under π^{Γ_{s′}} = (1) the object is rigid ("défini à isom unique près") by its isomorphism class.

**R11 — scan 136 — conjecture — keep, corrected.** The parenthesis reads "(dont l'opération extérieure est draconienne !)", not "dite canonique". The expectation that π^{Γ_{s′}} = (1) always holds in the arithmetic case thus rests explicitly on the strength of the outer Galois action on π; it is not proved here (see own note N4).

**R12 — scan 137 — conjecture — keep, corrected.** The formerly illegible phrase reads "et permet de paradigmer ce qu'un tel pt rationnel permettrait d'obtenir": a splitting class should serve as the model for what a rational point of U over K provides. It remains a programmatic statement, not a bijection theorem.

**R13 — scan 137 — unproved claim — keep, corrected.** The points to check are labelled "cas anabéliens" (not "cas anodins"). The justification reads: known in abstract algebraic geometry "du moment qu'on admet que u conserve [or préserve] une structure complexe — il en est justement ainsi si on admet qu'il n'y a pas de sauvagerie". So the claim concerns the anabelian case, and it rests on the automorphism u preserving a complex structure, not on "U possède une structure complexe".

**R14 — scan 138 — unproved claim — keep, corrected.** A structure on Y = X/Γ⁰ gives one on X stable by Γ⁰, and one stable by Γ "si on choisit celle de Y invariée par l'élément non trivial de Γ/Γ⁰". The regularity hypothesis ("pas de sauvagerie") and the treatment of branching in the quotient are not supplied.

**R15 — scan 138 — unproved claim — keep, corrected.** The insertion reads approximately "si Γ⁰ ≠ 1, [i.e. Γ pas réduit à une … anti-involution …)": U^Γ is finite as soon as Γ is not just an anti-holomorphic involution together with the identity. The finiteness is asserted, not proved; the insertion is still partly unread.

**R16 — scan 138, NB — unproved claim — corrected.** The group is Γ⁰ (small superscript circle visible): "U^Γ ≠ ∅ implique que Γ⁰ est cyclique". So read, it is the standard fact that a finite group of orientation-preserving automorphisms fixing a point acts faithfully by rotations on the tangent line, hence is cyclic. The witnesses' "Γ est cyclique" would fail for dihedral stabilisers (R23).

**R17 — scan 139 — explicit question — keep.** "Cette application est-elle bijective ??", with the inserted condition Γ⁰ ≠ (1); injectivity and surjectivity are separate problems.

**R18 — scan 139 — explicit question — keep.** For Γ⁰ = {1}, Γ/Γ⁰ ≃ {±1}, the source becomes π₀(X^Γ − S^Γ) and "la question analogue de bijectivité se pose"; one must first show the splitting class is constant along a component.

**R19 — scan 139 — proof obligation — corrected.** The word the first pass took as a cancelled "Obs" is "OPS" (on peut supposer), not cancelled: the author reduces injectivity to Γ = Γ⁰ ≅ ℤ/pℤ, p prime, without giving the reason (see own note N3). "Il faut prouver que u_x, u_y ne sont pas conjugués sous π" remains open in these scans.

**R20 — scan 140 — construction to justify — keep, corrected wording.** The statement opens "Soient U … espace topologique connexe (…, par arcs)" and the extension is "opérant fidèlement sur Ũ". The lifting of the Γ-action to the pointed covering R_x and the change of splitting by inner automorphisms under change of ℓ are still compressed.

**R21 — scans 131–136 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Keeping torsors and splittings as groupoids of choices fits the text, which itself computes automorphisms of (P, q) on scan 136.

**R22 — scan 137 — own idea (first pass) — keep, corrected.** The torus translation of order n is homotopic to the identity, but the ink restricts the checks to "cas anabéliens"; the torus (g = 1, no puncture) is outside that scope. The diagnostic confirms that the anabelian hypothesis is needed rather than revealing an omitted one.

**R23 — scan 138 — own idea (first pass) — keep, corrected.** The dihedral stabiliser test is now settled by the ink: the statement is about Γ⁰, for which it holds; for Γ it would fail. Keep it as a consistency check of the corrected reading.

**R24 — scan 140 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Cancelling "fidèlement" for the Γ-action on U while keeping "opérant fidèlement sur Ũ" for ℰ is a real tension: if a nontrivial γ acts trivially on U, the pair (γ, id_Ũ) lies in ℰ and acts trivially on Ũ.

**R25 — scans 138–140 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Comparing a splitting subgroup Γ′ ⊂ ℰ with its fixed set in Ũ is the route the text takes next ("classes de π-conj. de ss-grpes sections Γ′ de ℰ"); existence and connectedness of the fixed sets need hypotheses.

## Notes added in the second pass

**N1 — scan 136 — Own (second pass), implication not pursued.** "Une extension ℰ de Σ ⊂ Γ par π", together with the Σ → Γ overwritings on scan 135, shows that a subgroup Σ ⊂ Γ (probably the group acting on I′₁, or a stabiliser) was in play and was then replaced by Γ. Before formalising, decide whether the extension is restricted to Σ or taken over all of Γ.

**N2 — scan 136 — Own (second pass), claim flagged by the author.** "Revient sauf erreur à" marks the torsor-plus-splitting description of B_{Γ_{s′}} → B_{X,U} as unverified by Grothendieck himself. Any use of it should state it as a claim to prove, not as part of the construction.

**N3 — scan 139 — Own (second pass), own idea.** The reduction "OPS Γ = Γ⁰ ≅ ℤ/pℤ" can be justified for injectivity: if Γ⁰ ≠ 1 choose Γ′ ⊂ Γ⁰ of prime order; for x ∈ U^Γ ⊂ U^{Γ′} the splitting class attached to x restricts to the class attached to x for ℰ′ = p⁻¹(Γ′), so equal classes for Γ give equal classes for Γ′, and injectivity for Γ′ gives x = y. Confidence: high for the formal reduction, assuming the fixed-point map is compatible with restriction to subgroups; it says nothing about surjectivity.

**N4 — scan 136 — Own (second pass), unproved claim and possible route.** For the conjecture π^{Γ_{s′}} = (1): in the pro-ℓ completion, a weight argument (the graded pieces of the lower central series have nonzero Frobenius weights) excludes nontrivial fixed elements when the base field is finitely generated over ℚ. This does not by itself give the profinite statement, because π does not inject into the product of its pro-ℓ completions. Confidence: medium; not checked against the literature here.

**N5 — scan 134, margin — Own (second pass), implication.** If "(= D′*|U)" is read correctly, D′₁* is the part of the punctured neighbourhoods D′* lying over U, i.e. around the points of S′₁ sent into U. The condition that the topos morphism factor through D′₁* → D′₁ then says that these punctured discs extend across the puncture in B_{X,U}, which is what "mixte" requires.

**N6 — scan 137, margin — Own (second pass), implication.** The marginal title "Structure des Γ-orbites critiques en termes d'extension…" announces the theme of § 15: orbits with nontrivial stabilisers are to be described by splittings of ℰ. This matches the author's later table of contents ("orbites critiques des scindages d'extensions").

**N7 — scan 132 — Own (second pass), definition implicit in the text.** "Morphismes mixtes" are the f: (X′, S′) → (X, S) with f(X′ ∖ S′) ⊂ X ∖ S but S′ ≠ f⁻¹(S), so that S′₁ = S′ ∖ f⁻¹(S) is sent into U. For them the open-part datum f_π: π′ → π does not determine B_{X′,U′} → B_{X,U}; the missing datum is the family of torsors indexed by S′₁ (scan 134).


# ===== LM_P015 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P015 (scans 141–150): research notes, checked against the corrected transcription

Each first-pass note (R01–R26) is kept, corrected or dropped against `LM_P015_checked.tex`; notes added in the second pass are marked "Own (second pass)". Scan numbers are the file numbers; the manuscript pages are 70–74 (upper numbering), inside § 15 of the author's later table of contents ("Retour sur le cas topologique: orbites critiques des scindages d'extensions", pp. 67–78).

## First-pass notes

**R01 — scan 141 — unproved claim — keep, corrected.** The set is named in an insertion: "l'ens. Ũ^{Γ′}_x des x̃ ∈ Ũ^{Γ′} au dessus de x" is a torsor under π^{Γ′}, since α.x̃ ∈ Ũ^{Γ′} ⇔ α ∈ π^{Γ′}, "vérification triviale, comme du reste toutes les assertions précédentes". The parenthesis is "(≠∅ hyp. sur a/x)", opening before ≠∅, so it is not a reference to condition a): the natural sense is that the fibre is non-empty because x lies in the image. The first pass's worry about the scope of "hyp. sur a)" is dropped; the one-line check (two Γ′-fixed lifts differ by a deck transformation commuting with Γ′) is still not written.

**R02 — scans 141–142 — unproved claim — keep, corrected.** The conjugacy statement is now legible: "il existe un homéomorphisme D ≃ ℂ tel que [le groupe] d'homéomorphismes de ℂ transformé de Γ soit le groupe des homothéties par μ_n(ℂ) … soit le groupe diédral associé". The proof that follows does not reach it in full: d) assumes the topological part, and the bracket on scan 148 leaves the anti-involution case open.

**R03 — scan 143 — unproved claim — keep.** "Un argument standard" (now secure) leads from an invariant C² structure to an invariant conformal structure; it is not given. Because the opening "Supposons d'abord Γ = Γ°" on scan 142 is cancelled (see N3), part a) is meant for any Γ, so the argument must also handle orientation-reversing elements (anticonformal maps).

**R04 — scans 143–144 — unproved claim — keep, corrected.** The ink writes Sl(2, ℝ)~ (first letter overwritten, perhaps Gl) and {u ∈ Gl(2, ℝ) | det u = ±1} with lower-case l, and "tt sous-groupe compact est contenu dans un conjugué du ss-groupe compact maximal". The missing pieces are as the first pass says: the kernel of the matrix realisation and the conjugacy of compact subgroups.

**R05 — scans 142–144 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** With τ the complex conjugation, τ^ε for ε = ±1 and τ^{det u} for det u = ±1 both give τ, so the exponent does not separate the two cosets; an exponent in {0, 1} (or (1 − det u)/2) is what the formulas need. The reading itself is not in doubt; this is a remark on the formula, not a reason to change the text.

**R06 — scan 143 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Θ_{−u} = Θ_u, so the displayed set of matrices maps onto the conformal group with kernel {±1}; "≃" needs that quotient.

**R07 — scan 144 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** F(z) = z/(1 − |z|), with inverse w/(1 + |w|), is a radial homeomorphism of the open disc onto ℂ commuting with rotations and conjugation, which is what "un homéom. [0,1[ → [0,+∞[" is used for.

**R08 — scan 145 — unproved claim — keep, corrected.** The ink names "les ss-groupes de stabilité Γ_x" and writes the quotient as D^Γ = U (superscript, no slash), the projection as D → U (overwritten). D^Γ is empty under the assumption being refuted, so the symbol must stand for the quotient (see N2). The point stands that the quotient charts have to come from the induction hypothesis, and the check for circularity remains to be done.

**R09 — scan 145 — unproved claim — keep, corrected.** The word struck under the inserted "conforme" (in quotes) is more probably "conforme" than "holomorphe": the author put the word in quotes and glossed it "(hol. ou anti-holomorphe)". The point about oriented and unoriented conformal structures stands.

**R10 — scans 145–146 — unpursued implication — keep.** "Γ y opère trivialement (car Γ = Γ°) donc Γ × ℤ opère sur D̃*": the lifted action fixing x̃ and the triviality on π₁(D*) ≅ ℤ are the two things to check.

**R11 — scans 146–147 — unproved claim — keep, corrected: the lemma as written is false.** "Soit u un homéomorphisme d'ordre fini de ℂ, commutant à z ↦ z+1, et laissant invariant l'origine. Alors u = id": u(z) = z̄ satisfies every stated hypothesis and is not the identity (N4). The application has Γ = Γ°, so what is needed is the lemma for orientation-preserving u; N5 gives a route.

**R12 — scan 147 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** The reduction "OPS ∃ nb premier p tel que u^p = id" is justified by replacing u of order m by u^{m/p}, which keeps both hypotheses (and the orientation, in the corrected lemma).

**R13 — scan 147 — unpursued implication — keep.** "Tous les pᵗˢ de ℤ ⊂ ℂ sont fixes par Γ" (inserted) and the accumulation of fixed points at ∞ on Ĉ ≃ S² diagnose what a counterexample would look like; "décidément très sauvage" is not yet a contradiction.

**R14 — scan 147 — conjecture — keep, corrected.** The sentence reads "On doit pouvoir terminer par la suite spectrale d'Adams... je n'entre pas dans ces dédales..." ("dédales", not "détails"). No construction is given; which spectral sequence was meant is open.

**R15 — scan 147 — unproved claim — keep, corrected.** d) opens "La partie purement top. étant [admise? / ainsi] supposée prouvée, on en conclut ainsi …": the topological part is explicitly assumed. The general claim that follows ("si Γ groupe fini opère sur une surface paracompacte U, les Γ_x respectant l'orientation, alors U/Γ = V est une surface") is then applied "pour le cas U = D" (scan 148), so it must cover the plane: the witnesses' "compacte" cannot be right.

**R16 — scans 147–148 — conjecture — keep, corrected.** The bracket reads "il faudrait prouver qu'il existe tjrs structure conforme invariante, [two struck words] Γ gpe fini opère sur surfaces paracompactes — donc U/Γ est une surface à bord". "À bord" is now secure, and the restored "gpe fini" is the finiteness hypothesis the first pass lost.

**R17 — scan 148 — unproved claim — keep.** "Ici ce qui manque, c'est l'analyse de l'action d'une anti-involution d'une surface au voisinage d'un pᵗ fixe": the local model near a fixed point of an orientation-reversing involution is the missing step.

**R18 — scan 148, margin — conjecture — keep, corrected.** "Il faudrait prouver que si τ est un anti-auto. involutif de D, alors ∃ isom D ≃ ℂ tel que τ devienne z ↦ z̄ (donc D^τ ≃ ℝ !)". The sentence goes on "ce qui doit permettre de prouver, si …": the straightening is the means to the statement of R19, not a remark that it "doit pouvoir se prouver".

**R19 — scan 148, margin — conjecture (was: unpursued implication) — keep, corrected.** The statement is now readable: "si Γ ≃ ℤ/2ℤ opère par anti-autom. sur U (orient. U ≠ S²) que π₀(U^Γ) ⟹ Classes de π-conj. de sections de ℰ sur Γ est bij." It is a component-level analogue of the theorem's bijection for exactly the case its hypothesis Γ° ≠ (1) excludes. The reading of "orient.", the arrow and some words of the codomain stay doubtful; N6 tests it.

**R20 — scan 149 — unpursued implication — keep, corrected.** c) ends "(et dans le cas U orienté, Γ° est cyclique)" (witnesses "ouvert"). "Orienté" fits the mathematics: for orientable U a section Γ′ ≅ Γ has Γ′° ≅ Γ°, which is cyclic by the lemma's b). The obstruction to splitting (ℰ → Γ splits only if Γ is cyclic or dihedral) holds under the theorem's hypotheses and inherits its open proof obligations.

**R21 — scan 149 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** π^{Γ′} = (1) says that π acts freely on each π-conjugacy class of sections, so each class is a π-torsor; this is what scan 150 uses ("ce torseur").

**R22 — scans 148–149 — own idea (first pass) — keep, corrected.** The example U = ℂ, Γ = {1, conj} (U^Γ = ℝ, one splitting class) was offered as a diagnostic for an "unreadable" marginal statement. The margin is now read as precisely the component-level statement π₀(U^Γ) → classes, and the example agrees with it (π₀(ℝ) is one point). N6 adds a test with two components.

**R23 — scan 150 — unproved claim — keep, corrected.** The set is U^! (an exclamation mark, not U′): "Soit U^! l'ens des x ∈ U tels que Γ_x ≠ (1) (qui est donc une partie discrète de U)", under "Supposons U orientable, Γ conservant l'orientation". Maximality of the stabiliser sections and the bijection b) are asserted here; the proofs follow on the next page (witness text), not in this package.

**R24 — scan 150, margin — unproved claim — keep.** "NB Cette application commute aux actions naturelles de Γ !": the Γ-action on π-conjugacy classes of partial sections (conjugation by lifts in ℰ) has to be defined and shown independent of the lift.

**R25 — scan 150 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** An equivariant bijection U^! → J gives a bijection of orbit sets U^!/Γ → J/Γ, and J/Γ is the set of ℰ-conjugacy classes of maximal partial sections ≠ (1).

**R26 — scan 150 — unproved claim — keep, corrected.** The last lines read "il y a un isom. unique [commutant à l'action de Γ_x] entre ce torseur, et le torseur déduit de Ũ en tordant par le π-torseur [struck word] P_x des Γ″ dans la classe [Γ′]…" (P_x medium; Γ″ and [Γ′] doubtful). So the twist is by P_x, the set of sections Γ″ in the class [Γ′], which is a π-torsor by π^{Γ′} = (1). A natural candidate for the comparison is Γ″ ↦ its unique fixed point in Ũ, a π-equivariant map from P_x to the fibre Ũ_x (own suggestion, not in the text).

## Own notes (second pass)

**N1 — scan 142 — Own (second pass), own idea (consistency check).** The ink says Γ° is "d'indice 0 ou 1" in Γ; Γ° is the kernel of the orientation character Γ → {±1}, so its index is 1 or 2. The witnesses print "1 ou 2" without comment. The diplomatic text keeps "0 ou 1"; a reading edition should note the slip.

**N2 — scans 141, 142, 145, 146 — Own (second pass), own idea (consistency check).** Four slips of the pen are kept as written: "l'image de U^{Γ′} dans U" (Γ′ acts on Ũ, so Ũ^{Γ′} is meant); "où σ est la conjugaison complexe" under a formula written with τ; D^Γ = U for the quotient D/Γ; and the covering "D̃* → D̃ donné par exp 2iπz", which is ℂ = D̃* → D* = ℂ*. None changes the argument once read as intended.

**N3 — scans 142–145 — Own (second pass), implication (structure of the proof).** With "Supposons d'abord Γ = Γ°, i.e. Γ conserve l'orientation. … On procède" cancelled on scan 142 and its continuation ("Par induction sur l'ordre de Γ", "a) D^Γ ≠ ∅", "a) Cas Γ d'ordre premier") struck on scan 143, the proof runs: a) the conformal argument for any Γ with an invariant C² structure; then, inserted on scan 145, "du moins pour Γ°. Supposons d'abord Γ = Γ°" for the induction b)–c); d) passes to Γ ≠ Γ°. The orientation assumption therefore governs only the "non-sauvagerie" part.

**N4 — scans 146–147 — Own (second pass), counterexample to the stated lemma.** u(z) = z̄ is a homeomorphism of ℂ of order 2, commutes with z ↦ z + 1 (conj(z + 1) = z̄ + 1) and fixes 0, yet u ≠ id. So the lemma as written ("Soit u un homéomorphisme d'ordre fini de ℂ, commutant à z ↦ z+1, et laissant invariant l'origine. Alors u = id") is false; it needs "u conserve l'orientation", which holds where it is used (Γ = Γ°, scan 146). The sentence "Il reste à prouver que si un groupe fini Γ opère sur ℂ en commutant à l'action de ℤ …" has the same gap. Confidence: high (direct check).

**N5 — scan 147 — Own (second pass), own idea (route for the orientation-preserving lemma).** Descend u to the cylinder ℂ/ℤ ≅ ℂ* (z ↦ e^{2iπz}): ū has finite order, fixes the image 1 of ℤ, and acts trivially on π₁(ℂ*) because u carries the segment [0, 1] to a path from 0 to 1; being orientation-preserving it therefore fixes both ends, and extends to S² with three fixed points 0, 1, ∞. By the classical theorem that a finite-order orientation-preserving homeomorphism of S² is conjugate to a rotation (Brouwer 1919, Kerékjártó 1919, Eilenberg 1934), ū = id; then u(z) = z + k with u(0) = 0, so u = id. Confidence: high for the reduction, conditional on that theorem (not checked against the corpus in this pass); it does not repair the manuscript's internal logic, since the lemma fondamental is the plane case of the same theorem.

**N6 — scan 148, margin — Own (second pass), own idea (test of Grothendieck's conjecture).** Take U = ℂ* (orientable, ≠ S²) and Γ = {1, conj}. With the covering z ↦ e^{2iπz}, the lifts of conj are s_n(z) = −z̄ + n (n ∈ ℤ), all involutions, and T_m s_n T_{−m} = s_{n+2m}: two π-conjugacy classes of sections (n even, n odd), each with π^{Γ′} = (1). The fixed line Re z = n/2 of s_n maps to ℝ_{>0} for n even and to ℝ_{<0} for n odd, the two components of U^Γ = ℝ*. So π₀(U^Γ) → classes is bijective here, while the point-level map U^Γ → classes is not injective. Confidence: high (explicit computation).

**N7 — scan 149 — Own (second pass), own idea (check of the NB).** "Celles sur Γ [assurent] que Γ opérant sur D satisfait aux hyp. du lemme fondamental" holds: a section Γ′ ≅ Γ acts faithfully on Ũ ≃ D because ℰ does (Γ acts faithfully on U). If U is orientable, π preserves the orientation of Ũ, so Γ′° ≅ Γ° ≠ (1); if U is not orientable, Γ′° has index at most 2 in Γ′, so card Γ ≥ 3 gives Γ′° ≠ (1). Confidence: high.

**N8 — scan 150 — Own (second pass), implication.** The corrected notation U^! connects this corollary with its continuation (witness text after this package: "soit J (≃ U^!) l'ensemble des classes de π-conjugaison de sections partielles (≠ 1) maximales", and S′ = S ∪ U^!), where the critical points are added to S to reconstruct the loop group of X ∖ S′. Under the first pass's U′ this link was invisible.


# ===== LM_P016 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P016 (scans 151–160): research notes checked against the corrected text

Each first-pass note (R01–R38, from `answers/unzipped/LM_P016/LM_P016_notes.md`) is checked against `LM_P016_checked.tex` and kept, corrected or dropped, with the reason. Notes S01–S06 are the second pass's own and are labelled as such. Kinds: conjecture (an open question or expectation stated by Grothendieck), unproved claim (asserted without proof on these pages), unpursued implication, own idea.

## First-pass notes

**R01 — scan 151 — unproved claim — corrected.** The proof of a) cites "le th. précédent" for the unique fixed point y and asserts that y does not change when Γ₁ is replaced by a smaller nontrivial group "(et la section induite)" such as Γₓ. The first pass read "(cf la section précédente)"; the corrected text makes the claim one about restricting both the action and the section. The invariance is asserted, not proved.
*Reason:* the corrected reading changes what is asserted.

**R02 — scan 151 — unproved claim — corrected.** "D'autre part c) est clair." The statement of c) is on scan 150 (previous package): for each such maximal partial section π^{Γ′} = (1), with a unique isomorphism of torsors commuting with Γₓ. No proof of c) is given.
*Reason:* the statement is not absent; it is on the preceding scan.

**R03 — scan 152 — conjecture — corrected (kind changed).** With U = X∖S anabelian and the description of U by π "plt fidèle" (pleinement fidèle), Grothendieck states what he "voudrait se convaincre": that the outer action of Γ on π, "quand Γ conserve l'orientation (pour simplifier)", suffices to describe the object (U,Γ) in "le catégorie isotopique qui convient". This is an expectation, not an asserted result; the category and the notion of isomorphism are not specified.
*Reason:* first-pass readings "se doit de convaincre", missing Γ, "ple" corrected.

**R04 — scan 152, margin — conjecture — kept.** "il faudrait revenir sur le cas général" records that the orientation-reversing case remains to be treated.
*Reason:* reading unchanged.

**R05 — scan 152 — unproved claim — corrected.** J (≃ U!) is defined as "l'ens des classes de π-conjugaison" of maximal partial sections ≠ 1 of ℰ over Γ, and "pour tt j ∈ J, j est un π-torseur comme classe de π-conjugaison de sections". The reason given is one line: a priori the stabiliser of Γ′ under π-conjugation is π^{Γ′}, which is (1) by c) of scan 150. The freeness of the action is thus reduced to c), itself not proved here.
*Reason:* the first pass read "par des classes" and "on a le … torseur donné".

**R06 — scan 153 — unproved claim — kept.** The set of all maximal partial sections is an ℰ-set with free π-action, canonically identified with Ũ|U! = Ũ!. Canonicity and equivariance are asserted without proof.
*Reason:* reading unchanged.

**R07 — scan 153 — conjecture — corrected.** "il s'impose d'essayer de reconstituer" (now settled) the outer group with lacets of U′ = X∖S′, S′ = S ∪ U!, and "l'action extérieure" of Γ on it (written over a struck "l'extension"). A reconstruction problem, not a construction.
*Reason:* the verb is settled and the struck word is "extension".

**R08 — scan 153 — conjecture — kept.** "Mais il faudrait d'abord s'assurer du caractère intrinsèque de la définition de J … comme Γ-ensemble" in terms of the outer group π and the outer action alone. Scan 154 calls this "assez évident" via π-conjugacy classes of partial lifts of Γ → Autext(π) to Aut(π), without detail.
*Reason:* reading unchanged; the follow-up on scan 154 added.

**R09 — scan 154 — conjecture — corrected.** "on aimerait cependant aussi une description intrinsèque de Ũ|U!, en (ou?) un paradigme pour l'application de Γ-espaces U! → U": a morphism of topoi B_J → B_ℰ with Γ-action. The target is B_ℰ (settled; the first pass offered B_π); the arrow label remains unread.
*Reason:* "pro-légomène" was a misreading of "paradigme"; the target subscript is a script E.

**R10 — scan 154 — unproved claim — corrected.** The inverse-image functor B_ℰ → B_J (π-Ens → Ens. sur J) "n'est autre que le produit contracté sur π avec l'ens des sections partielles ≠ 1 maximales de ℰ sur Γ". The contracted product is with the whole set of maximal sections, which by scans 152–153 is a π-torsor over J; no representative is chosen. What a proof must supply is that this set is a π-torsor over J and that the functor is Γ-compatible.
*Reason:* the first pass read "l'une des sections".

**R11 — scan 154 — own idea (first pass) — dropped.** It proposed comparing the functors obtained from two π-conjugate representatives of one partial-section class.
*Reason:* its premise came from the misreading "l'une des"; the text uses the whole set, so there is no representative to vary.

**R12 — scan 155 — unproved claim — kept.** Under the "draconiennes !" conditions, with Γ assumed faithful "(Pour les formuler …)": every partial section is cyclic (if Γ′ = Γ′⁰) or dihedral (if Γ′ ≠ Γ′⁰), there are finitely many π-conjugacy classes of them, and each nontrivial one lies in a unique maximal one. No complete proof on these pages.
*Reason:* reading confirmed (the "!" after "draconiennes" added).

**R13 — scan 155 — conjecture — kept.** "(On l'a établi tt au moins dans le cas Γ = Γ⁰, … je pense que ça reste vrai tel quel, à vérifier…)": the general case, including orientation-reversing elements, is an open expectation. A vertical stroke in the margin marks the sentence.
*Reason:* "tt" now read in place of a deletion; the sense is unchanged.

**R14 — scan 155 — unpursued implication — kept.** The double-underlined "structure supplémentaire" μ on finite-order elements of ℰ with values in T ⊗ ℚ/ℤ distinguishes the bare extension from the geometric datum; whether the latter is determined by the former becomes the question of scan 156.
*Reason:* reading unchanged.

**R15 — scan 155, NB — conjecture — kept.** The NB requires knowing that a finite-order u ∉ ℰ⁰ has order exactly 2 before μ(u) can be defined. Its last words are not read.
*Reason:* reading unchanged ("savoir" or "avoir").

**R16 — scan 156 — unproved claim — corrected.** From u of order n one gets ℤ/nℤ ↪ ℰ with trivial intersection with π, hence an injection ℤ/nℤ ↪ Γ and a subgroup Γ₁ ⊂ Γ of order n; the lifted section gives x ∈ U^{Γ₁}, and u₁ "correspond donc au voisinage de x à un multiplicateur", a primitive n-th root of 1 "pour une orientation locale choisie", hence intrinsically an element of T ⊗ ℤ/nℤ. The transformation law under change of local orientation is asserted, not written out.
*Reason:* first-pass readings "ℤ/nℤ ≃ Γ′", "dans un voisinage" and "donnée" corrected.

**R17 — scan 156 — unproved claim — kept.** The "conditions évidentes": μ(αuα⁻¹) = μ(u) for α ∈ π (not for all of ℰ), μ(uⁿ) = nμ(u), and, written smaller under the brace, ordre μ(u) = ordre de u. Not proved.
*Reason:* reading unchanged (the witnesses omit the third line).

**R18 — scan 156 — conjecture — kept.** "J'ignore si cette application μ peut se définir intrinsèquement en termes de l'extension ℰ", or whether two geometrically realised extensions admit an isomorphism ℰ → ℰ′ not compatible with μ, μ′. The central open question of the passage.
*Reason:* reading unchanged.

**R19 — scan 156 — own idea (first pass) — kept.** Look, inside one geometrically realised extension, for an extension automorphism that does not preserve μ. This is the special case U′ = U of the isomorphisms Grothendieck allows, so a positive example would answer his question negatively.
*Reason:* consistent with the corrected text.

**R20 — scan 157 — unproved claim — kept.** For π ≃ ℤ (card I = 2) a faithful action requires Γ = Γ⁰ cyclic and ℰ ≃ ℤ, so ℰ has no nontrivial element of finite order and "la question ne se pose pas". Stated without proof; the sentence was rewritten in the manuscript (the word before "doit avoir" is uncertain).
*Reason:* reading of the corrections only partly settled; the claim is unchanged.

**R21 — scan 157 — unproved claim — kept.** From 0 → π → π⊗ℝ → X₀(π) → 0 and H^i(Γ, π⊗ℝ) = 0 for i > 0 one gets H²(Γ,π) ← H¹(Γ, X₀(π)) (arrow pointing left, marked ∼): extension classes correspond to classes of X₀(π)-torsors with compatible Γ-action. The vanishing uses Γ finite, which is implicit.
*Reason:* reading unchanged.

**R22 — scan 157 — conjecture — corrected.** The extension is "essentiellement" a Γ-X₀(π)-torsor X, "(n-tore inhomogène sur lequel Γ opère …)". "S'il était vrai pour n = 2 que tte opération de Γ sur une surface torique (≃ S¹×S¹) est isomorphe à une telle action standard", μ would be intrinsic in this case. The hypothesis is stated conditionally, not proved.
*Reason:* "espace homogène" corrected to "n-tore inhomogène"; "pour n = 2" confirmed (CSG and IUT print "n = 1").

**R23 — scan 157 — own idea (first pass) — kept.** Fix the linear Γ-action on the lattice and vary the class in H¹(Γ, X₀(π)), tracking fixed points and μ, to separate the dependence on the extension class from the geometric realisation.
*Reason:* consistent with the corrected text.

**R24 — scan 158 — conjecture — kept.** The intrinsic character of μ in the torus case would follow from standardness of all actions on the torus; this is not proved here.
*Reason:* reading unchanged.

**R25 — scan 158 — conjecture — kept.** "La question revient à celle-ci": for a split extension of ℤ/nℤ (generator u) by π, given by θ with θⁿ = id_π, describe an element of T ⊗ ℚ/ℤ. T ≃ Λ²_ℤ π is part of the data, as R25 says.
*Reason:* reading unchanged.

**R26 — scan 158 — unproved claim — corrected.** Answer: the standard model X = X₀(π) with 0 fixed; if u reverses orientation "il est d'ordre 1 ou 2" (or "i.e. 2"; not settled), otherwise u is a rotation of order 2, 3, 4 or 6, located by an element of T ⊗ ℤ/νℤ "(si ν est d'ordre / l'ordre)", i.e. ν is the order. The list 2, 3, 4, 6 is the crystallographic restriction for finite-order elements of GL₂(ℤ), used without proof.
*Reason:* "(où ν est d'ordre u)" and "i.e. 2" corrected or opened.

**R27 — scan 158 — unpursued implication — corrected.** Identifying T ⊗ ℤ/νℤ with μ_ν(ℂ*) by choosing T ≃ ℤ, "i.e. π orienté i.e. X₀(π) = X orienté", the element is "une des deux" eigenvalues of u ⊗ ℂ. The choice of orientation is what selects one eigenvalue.
*Reason:* the first pass read a deletion where the ink has "= X".

**R28 — scan 159 — unpursued implication — corrected.** For an elliptic curve X = X₀ over an algebraically closed Ω and u of finite order, the "description paradigmatique" consists not only of π = π₁(X,0) (a ℤ̂-module free of rank 2) with the action of u, but also of one of the two roots "dans Ω" of T² + aT + b = 0, a = −Tr u, b = det u.
*Reason:* "pro-algébrique" was a misreading of "paradigmatique".

**R29 — scan 159 — unproved claim — kept.** "Il est clair que cette structure supplémentaire ne peut se déduire de la seule connaissance de l'action de u sur π." Marked by a double stroke in the margin; the marginal note relates it, for Ω = ℂ, to orientation(s). No proof written.
*Reason:* reading unchanged.

**R30 — scan 159 — conjecture — corrected.** Whether μ(u) ∈ μ_n(Ω) "peut se déduire de la connaissance au moins de l'action extérieure de u sur le groupe 'avec 1 lacet'", i.e. of an outer automorphism of order n of that group.
*Reason:* "au moins" restored.

**R31 — scan 159, margin — unproved claim — corrected.** The marginal answer "oui, il le peut, grâce à la considération des 'sous-groupes … de ℰ'" (CSG: "de ramification") which define extensions 1 → T → ℰᵢ → Γᵢ → 1, whence Γᵢ ≃ T/nᵢT. A positive answer is sketched, not proved; the subgroup name and labels are not fully read.
*Reason:* the CSG reading is recorded as a lead; the ink is still partly illegible.

**R32 — scans 159–160 — unpursued implication — corrected.** "Mais s'il en était bien ainsi, cela impliquerait d'autre part que la construction de u ∈ groupe (or: de ces groupes) à (1) lacet(s), avec l'op. de Γ dessus, ne peut se faire non plus à l'aide de la seule connaissance de l'action de Γ (= ℤ/nℤ) sur π." One conditional argument across the page break.
*Reason:* "lacet(s)" settles the bracket the first pass called unmatched; the object of "construction" is not settled.

**R33 — scan 160 — conjecture — kept.** "C'est cette question de 'forage de trous' qu'il faut donc … à la fin des fins, attaquer !": reconstruction together with the Γ-action.
*Reason:* reading unchanged.

**R34 — scan 160 — conjecture — kept.** In the anabelian case, whether μ: ₍∞₎ℰ → T ⊗ ℚ/ℤ is deducible from the action of Γ on π "[si elle est fidèle, …]" or is extra data; "la question reste entière".
*Reason:* reading unchanged ("si elle", not "si celle-ci").

**R35 — scan 160 — conjecture — corrected.** If the action is faithful, ℰ is a subgroup of Autext_ℓ(π), "et on peut se demander si μ n'est pas alors définissable sur cet tt entier": on the whole group, not only on ℰ. Which group "cet" refers to (Autext_ℓ(π), or ₍∞₎ℰ as CSG prints) is not settled.
*Reason:* "tt" settled; the referent remains open.

**R36 — scan 160, margin — unproved claim — kept.** "cela semble bien ainsi, compte tenu … que pour Γ résoluble … on sait … que tte action de Γ sur π … réaliser géométriquement": geometric realisation of outer actions of solvable Γ is invoked as known. See S05.
*Reason:* reading unchanged; middle words still unread.

**R37 — scan 160 — unproved claim — corrected.** §16 opens with a situation B_{D*} → B_U "(de topos multigaloisiens)"; the NB says that such a toposic situation "décrit" (or "reflète") 2-fidèlement the topological situations (X,S) or U, provided no irreducible component of U is ≃ S². Asserted without proof; the discussion continues beyond this package.
*Reason:* the source subscript is D*, not J*; the inserted "toposique décrit" placed.

**R38 — scan 160 — own idea (first pass) — kept, low confidence.** Compare the extensions 1 → T → ℰᵢ → Γᵢ → 1 of the scan 159 margin with the local data at the punctured discs D* in B_{D*} → B_U, to see what "bouchage" and "forage" must preserve.
*Reason:* consistent with the corrected text (J* corrected to D*); still speculative.

## Second-pass notes (own)

**S01 — scan 154 — unproved claim (own note).** The left object of the second diagram is B_ℰ, yet the annotation under it is "π-Ens". The identification only makes sense with the Γ-action kept ("compatible avec action de Γ"): an ℰ-set is a π-set with a compatible Γ-action twisted by the extension. The text uses this identification without stating it.

**S02 — scan 155 — unpursued implication (own note).** With the corrected ℤ/nℤ ↪ Γ, the construction of μ(u) needs only that π is torsion-free: the image Γ₁ of u then has the same order n, and its lifted section has a fixed point x. The NB's caveat matters because an orientation-reversing element has no rotation multiplier at its fixed point.

**S03 — scan 158 — own idea.** An orientation-reversing finite-order automorphism of π ≅ ℤ² has determinant −1, hence eigenvalues 1 and −1, hence order exactly 2. The reading "d'ordre 1 ou 2" (the witnesses) therefore contains a vacuous case, while "i.e. 2" would state the fact; the ink does not decide between them.

**S04 — scan 158 — own idea.** T ≃ Λ²_ℤ π is canonically H₂(π,ℤ), dual to H²(π,ℤ) ≃ Λ²H¹(π,ℤ) with H¹(π,ℤ) = π^∨. This supports the reading "dual de" against the witnesses' "dim 2 dans" on mathematical grounds only.

**S05 — scan 160, margin — unproved claim (own context note).** The claim that every outer action of a solvable Γ on π is geometrically realised matches the classical partial Nielsen realisation results (cyclic groups: Nielsen; solvable groups: Fenchel); the general finite case was proved by Kerckhoff in 1983, after this manuscript. This attribution is from general knowledge and was not checked against the literature corpus in this pass.

**S06 — scan 159 — own idea.** For an elliptic curve over Ω and u of finite order fixing 0, the root "dans Ω" that Grothendieck adds is naturally the scalar by which u acts on the one-dimensional tangent space at 0. Over ℂ the two complex eigenvalues of u ⊗ ℂ are exchanged by the choice of orientation of π ⊗ ℝ, which agrees with the marginal "pour Ω = ℂ … orientation(s)". This interpretation is not written in the manuscript.


# ===== LM_P017 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P017 (scans 161–170): research notes checked against the corrected text

Each first-pass note (R01–R23, from `answers/unzipped/LM_P017/LM_P017_notes.md`) is checked against `LM_P017_checked.tex`. Each is kept, corrected or dropped, with the reason. Notes S01–S07 are the second pass's own and are labelled as such. The kinds are:
- conjecture or question: an open question or expectation stated by Grothendieck;
- unproved claim: asserted without proof on these pages;
- unpursued implication;
- own idea.

## First-pass notes

**R01 — scan 161 — unproved claim, cancelled — kept, with a correction.** A boxed and struck passage starts ", donnons-nous un sous topos (néc^t. ouvert) B_{D₁\*} de B_{D\*}". It sets B_{D\*} ≃ B_{D₀\*} ⊔ B_{D₁\*}, gives the analogous decomposition of B_D, defines B_{π₀(D\*)} = B_S and forms a "somme amalgamée" B_U′ over B_{D₁\*}. This is an abandoned first attempt at a partial filling. The same idea returns, uncancelled, on scans 169–170, where B_{D₀\*} is "un sommande direct".
*Reason:* the reading is confirmed (apart from the opening comma), and the link to scans 169–170 is added.

**R02 — scans 161–162 — unproved claim (kind changed) — corrected.** The corrected text is negative: "dans ce cas S_ét [or X_ét, overwritten] ne peut se reconstituer à partir de B_{D\*} comme B_I avec I = π₀(B_{D\*}), il faut tenir compte des corps résiduels k(s) … i.e. des groupes de Galois Gal(k̄(s)/k(s))". The first pass read "on peut". This is the stated reason for the new starting datum B_U ← B_{D\*} → B_S, with σ inducing an isomorphism on Π₀ and surjective on Hom. No proof is given. For S_ét the point is that ∐_s B_{Gal(k(s)ˢ/k(s))} is a discrete topos B_I only when the residue fields are separably closed.
*Reason:* the negation is restored, and the note now states the claim rather than an open comparison.

**R03 — scans 162–163 — unproved claim — corrected.** The choices are:
- universal coverings D̃ᵢ\* of the components Dᵢ\*;
- a universal covering Ũ of B_U;
- isomorphisms ρ_!(D̃ᵢ\*) ≃ Ũ.

With these choices, and B_U connected, the situation "s'explicite par la donnée d'un groupe ℰ (ou Π) (=Aut Ũ), ℰᵢ (ou Πᵢ) (=Aut D̃ᵢ\*) (i∈I), Γᵢ (i∈I)", together with ρᵢ: ℰᵢ → ℰ and σᵢ: ℰᵢ → Γᵢ, σᵢ surjective. The group names are settled: the diagram labels are ℰᵢ and ℰ written over Πᵢ and Π, matching the insertions on scan 162. The text does not prove the equivalence between the topos datum and this group datum, or its dependence on the choices.
*Reason:* the letters are settled, and ρ_! (not ρ_{i!}) is confirmed.

**R04 — scan 163 — unpursued implication — kept, with a correction.** Over a base field: e = Spec K, B_e = the étale topos e_ét of e = B_Γ with Γ = Gal(K̄/K). The diagram (\*) is put into a square over B_e "avec donnée de commutativité pour le carré". The first pass's insertion "fét" is "e_ét". The text does not say whether the later gluings and amalgamations are to be formed over B_e.
*Reason:* the insertion is corrected; the implication stands.

**R05 — scans 163–164 — unproved claim — kept.** Gluing B_U and B_S along σ_\*ρ^\* gives "un topos mixte, qui n'est plus en général multigaloisien". It was formerly B_{X,U} and is renamed B_{X;S} "[pour rappeler qu'il s'agit de faisceaux sur X, mais n'ayant de singularités que sur S]". The construction and the failure of the multigaloisian property are asserted, and no criterion is given.
*Reason:* the content is unaffected. Only the renaming clause changes, to "et que \[je préfère\] maintenant noter", which stays doubtful.

**R06 — scan 164 — unpursued implication — kept.** The comparison α_X: σ^\*ψ^\* → ρ^\*φ^\* is "un morphisme de foncteurs sans plus", "pas un iso", and the arrow orientation is confirmed. A universal property for the glued topos must therefore allow a non-invertible 2-cell.
*Reason:* the reading is confirmed.

**R07 — scan 164 — unproved claim — kept, with a correction.** In the geometric model, B_X corresponds to sheaves on X that are "\[ess.\] loc. constants sur U". That is, on U they "proviennent par l'image inverse par U → B_{Π₁U} d'un faisceau sur B_{Π₁U}". B_{D;S} corresponds to sheaves on D = ∐ Spec(𝒪ᵢ = hensélisé …) that are locally constant on D\* = D − S. Then "avec l'hyp. de dim faite — en fait B_{D;S} ≃ D_ét (Dᵢ n'a que 2 pts, Dᵢ\* = {ηᵢ})". These identifications are not proved.
*Reason:* the inserted "par" is added and the "on a" read by the first pass is not in the ink. See also S03 on the name B_X here.

**R08 — scan 165 — unproved claim — corrected.** The canonical morphism B_{D\*} → B_{D;S} is φ_D, "et non ψ_Dσ". Geometrically, φ_D corresponds to the inclusion D\* ⊃ D∖S → D and ψ_D to S → D, "alors que σ ni ψ_Dσ ne correspondent en général à des morphismes de schémas". The margin says that the three topoi of the triangle are étale and that φ_D and ψ_D "correspondent à des morph. … mais non … de topos)". The row order is doubtful, and CSG has "à des morphismes de topos, mais non σ". The first pass's "inclusions de topos" is not in the ink. None of this is proved here.
*Reason:* the margin and the display are corrected.

**R09 — scan 166 — conjecture (qualified) — kept; reading settled.** "Sauf erreur les deux carrés sont [non seulement ess^t. comm. mais aussi] 2-cartésiens (dans la 2-cat. des topos)." This is to be checked as a 2-pullback statement for each square of (\*\*). The neighbouring warning that the triangle is "pas ess. commutatif" must stay in that formulation.
*Reason:* "ess^t." is settled; the rest is unchanged.

**R10 — scan 166 — unproved claim — corrected (notation).** Passing to the B_{Π₁} of the topoi gives "un topos B_X (= B_{Π₁B_{X;S}})" as an amalgamated sum, and "ce carré est bel et bien essentiellement commutatif". The Π₁ of the lower vertex is hatched out. Grothendieck renames B_{Π₁X} as B_X, as announced on scan 162 (see S02). The universal property, and which locally constant objects are meant, are not established here.
*Reason:* the notation is corrected; the claim is unchanged.

**R11 — scan 166 — unproved claim — kept; reading settled.** "un B_D, qui est cependant (par B_S → B_D) isom. (plutôt: équiv.) à B_S". In the ink, Grothendieck himself corrects isomorphism to equivalence. The equivalence induced by B_S → B_D is asserted, not proved.
*Reason:* "cependant" is settled.

**R12 — scan 167 — conjecture — corrected.** The morphism (\*\*) → (\*\*\*) is described "pour les flèches qui (ne) sont pas ess. des (semi-)identités, B_{D;S} → B_D et B_{X;S} → B_X". These two components "ont une nette tendance d'être acycliques i.e. d'induire des iso. sur la cohomologie (il faudrait voir … ce point)". The expectation therefore concerns exactly the two components that are not identities, and "acyclic" is defined by the "i.e.". CSG and IUT read "qui sont par essence des identités … ou à induire", which reverses the selection and weakens the definition.
*Reason:* the selection clause and the "i.e." are fixed on the ink, against both witnesses.

**R13 — scan 168 — unproved claim — corrected.** "Ici (\*\*\*\*) s'envoie dans le diagramme homologue (\*\*)". This induces isomorphisms of topoi for D\*, D, S. For U_ét → B_U and X_ét → X it induces morphisms "qui ont moins tendance d'être acycliques, mais qui le sont quand même dans des cas importants, rappelés dans une section antérieure…". These pages do not identify the earlier section.
*Reason:* "moins tendance" (not "moins de chances"), the singular "le diagramme homologue" and "s'envoie" (CSG: "s'insère") are corrected.

**R14 — scan 168 — unproved claim — kept.** The constructions of (\*\*) and (\*\*\*) are "purement formelles, et indépendantes de tte hypothèse". The formal existence of the glued and amalgamated topoi must be kept apart from the geometric identifications and the acyclicity expectation.
*Reason:* the reading is unchanged.

**R15 — scan 169 — unproved claim — corrected.** In the computational setting, B_X is the classifying topos B_{π_X}. Here π_X = π₁(X; X̃) is computed "comme quotient de π par le ss-groupe invariant engendré par les ρᵢ(Lᵢ), où Lᵢ = Ker σᵢ ⊂ ℰᵢ": a van Kampen statement for filling the holes. The letters are corrected: ℰᵢ and π (not Ξᵢ and Π), "ss-groupe", and ⊂. The object described is B_X (CSG: "BU"). The statement is not proved, and the closure question of R19 applies.
*Reason:* the letters are corrected.

**R16 — scans 169–170 — unpursued implication — corrected.** A "sommande direct" B_{D₀\*} of B_{D\*} (first pass: a "sous-monde/sous-ensemble ouvert") corresponds to a part I₀ ⊂ I. It gives the partial amalgamation of B_U and B_{S₀} under B_{D₀\*}, written "B_{U∪S₁} ← B_U". The filling is along S₀, and on scan 170 the object is B_{(U∪S₀)} with a brace U₁, where U₁ = X∖S₁ = U∪S₀. The S₁ on scan 169 is therefore probably a slip, kept as written. The family of partial fillings indexed by the subsets of I, with its transition maps, is not pursued.
*Reason:* "sommande direct" (a union of components, open and closed) is stronger than the first pass's "ouvert", and the index discrepancy is recorded.

**R17 — scan 170 — unproved claim — kept, with a correction.** The diagrams are a "hom de diagrammes cocartésiens" (CSG and IUT: "cartésiens"). The second amalgamation uses B_{D₁\*} → B_U → B_{U₁} and B_{D₁\*} → B_{S₁}. "Il est évident que celle-ci est can. équivalente à B_X", the full amalgamation; this is asserted, not proved. The sentence continues beyond this package.
*Reason:* "cocartésiens" is confirmed, and the composite is spelled out.

**R18 — scans 163–165 — own idea (first pass) — kept.** Test the gluing with sets, identity maps and a non-bijective connecting map, to see the role of a non-invertible comparison.
*Reason:* still apt, since α_X and α_D are confirmed as non-invertible.

**R19 — scan 169 — own idea (first pass) — kept.** Ask whether "ss-groupe invariant engendré" means algebraic or closed normal generation. In a profinite setting the closed normal subgroup is needed.
*Reason:* it applies directly to R15.

**R20 — scans 169–170 — own idea (first pass) — kept.** For J ⊂ I, take the quotient by the normal subgroup generated by the ρᵢ(Lᵢ), i ∈ J. This models the partial fillings and their transition maps.
*Reason:* it fits the corrected R16.

**R21 — scan 170 — own idea (first pass) — corrected.** Take a discrete toy model with π = F(a,b), two groups ℰᵢ = ℤ (first pass: "Ξᵢ") and trivial Γᵢ, so that Lᵢ = ℰᵢ. Filling one component kills a or b, and filling both kills both, in either order.
*Reason:* the group letter is corrected; the calculation stands.

**R22 — scans 167–168 — own idea (first pass) — kept.** Test acyclicity with declared coefficients and degrees, not only constant coefficients.
*Reason:* it now targets the two components named in R12.

**R23 — scans 167–168 — own idea (first pass) — kept.** Keep a ledger of scheme morphisms, topos morphisms and inverse-image functors to diagnose two things:
- the bottom arrow of (\*\*\*\*), which points from X_ét to U_ét;
- the target "X" in "X_ét → X" on scan 168.

*Reason:* both readings are confirmed on the images; see also S06.

## Second-pass notes (own)

**S01 — scan 161 — unproved claim (second pass).** This is the end of the marginal note begun on scan 160 ("une telle situation toposique décrit 2-fidèlement les situations topologiques (X,S) … pourvu qu'aucune composante irréductible de U ne soit ≃ S²"). Because these situations stay "très proches du langage et de l'intuition topologique", Grothendieck rates the toposic description above the "groupes à lacets" point of view, "qui correspond à l'appr. du calculatoire plutôt". The 2-faithfulness and its S² exception are asserted, not proved.

**S02 — scans 162, 166–170 — own idea, notation (second pass).** The warning "on écrit B_S, non B_D, qui aura un autre sens" (scan 162) is carried out on scans 166–170. B_D and B_X arise by hatching out the Π₁ in B_{Π₁D}, B_{Π₁X}, B_{Π₁(U∪S₀)}, B_{Π₁(U∪S)} and B_{Π₁U₁}, while B_{Π₁S}, B_{Π₁D\*}, B_{Π₁U} and B_{Π₁U₁} (scan 170, l. 2) are left as written. A reading edition should not restore the Π₁ silently, and should not confuse B_D and B_X with the glued B_{D;S} and B_{X;S}.

**S03 — scan 164 — own idea, possible slip (second pass).** "B_X correspond aux faisceaux sur X qui sont \[ess.\] loc. constants sur U (et sur S …)" describes the glued topos of the previous paragraph. Its parallel "et de m̄ B_{D;S} correspond aux faisceaux sur D" suggests that B_{X;S} is meant. On scans 166–170, B_X is the Π₁-topos instead (S02). The ink (an over-inked B with subscript X) and both witnesses read "B_X". This is recorded, not emended.

**S04 — scan 167 — question (second pass).** The ink and both witnesses read "le triangle sup. est bien ess. surjectif (c'est bien comme ça qu'on a défini σ, dans le cas géométrique!)". It contrasts with (\*\*), where the triangle "n'est pas ess. commutatif", and with the margin of (\*\*\*\*), "triangle sup. pas ess. comm.". This suggests that essential commutativity of the upper triangle of (\*\*\*) may be meant. On the other hand, σ was defined on scan 162 as surjective on Hom, which fits "surjectif". The two readings should be compared before the sentence is used.

**S05 — scan 167 — unproved claim (second pass).** The margin of (\*\*\*\*) states that in the étale diagram the upper triangle, D\*_ét → S_ét → D_ét against D\*_ét → D_ét, is "pas ess. comm." This is the étale counterpart of the warning beside (\*\*). No proof or comparison 2-cell is given.

**S06 — scan 168 — question (second pass).** "Toutes les flèches sauf D\*_ét → S_ét sont induites par des morphismes de topos": the ink (and both witnesses) reads "topos". But every arrow of a diagram of topoi is a morphism of topoi, and scan 165 says that σ does not come from a morphism of schemes. The intended contrast is probably with morphisms of schemes. This is recorded, not emended.

**S07 — scan 168 — unproved claim (second pass).** "La construction d'un B_X multigaloisien, comme somme amalgamée, peut être interprété comme la traduction (au niveau des groupoïdes fondamentaux) d'une opération de 'bouchage de trous'." The precise content would be that B_{Π₁}X is a 2-pushout of B_U ← B_{D\*} → B_S among multigaloisian topoi. This is a van Kampen theorem for filling punctures, whose group form is the quotient of R15. It is stated as an interpretation, without proof.


# ===== LM_P018 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P018 (scans 171–180): research notes checked against the corrected text

Each first-pass note (R01–R20) is checked against `LM_P018_checked.tex` and kept, corrected or dropped. Notes added in this pass are labelled "second pass" (S01–S06). Quotations follow the corrected transcription; doubtful readings keep their alternatives.

## First-pass notes

**R01 — scan 171 — implication not pursued — corrected.** The cancelled critique contrasts $B_U$, the classifying topos $B_{\Pi_1,U}$, with $B_X$, "un topos nettement plus gros". The corrected reading has the letters $U,X$ "(suggérant, en l'espèce, des schémas $U,X$)". It proposes $B_{X;S}$ "(pour suggérer les faisceaux sur $X$ ens.|ess. constants sur $X\setminus S$ et sur $S$)" and, as an interlinear replacement, "et de même $B_{D;S}$". Plain $B_D$, $B_X$ are kept for the classifying topoi. The whole passage is cancelled, so it records a notation that was considered and then dropped.

**R02 — scan 171 — claim without proof — corrected.** The text says the gluing operations are "essentiellement triviales et sans mystère, et indépendantes de ttes [toutes] hypothèses spéciales du type 'groupes à lacets'". The claim is independence from every loop-group hypothesis, not from one "hypothèse spéciale" as the first-pass wording suggested. A proof needs the amalgamated sums defined on scans 168–170.

**R03 — scans 172–173 — claim without proof — kept; quotation corrected.** The quotient topos is written $\mathcal X=(B/\Gamma)$. The text states that an action of $\Gamma$ on $B$ amounts to presenting $B$ as a Galois covering, with group $\Gamma$, of an essentially unique topos $(B,\Gamma)$, but gives no proof. A precise version must fix the 2-categorical data: actions up to coherent isomorphism, the torsor $U$, and the equivalence $B\simeq\mathcal X_{/U}$.

**R04 — scan 173 — claim without proof — kept.** The square $B_{D^*}\to B_U$ over $B_{D^*,\Gamma}\to B_{U,\Gamma}$ is asserted to be 2-cartesian, with $\Gamma$-galoisian vertical arrows. A proof must identify the 2-fibre product. In the profinite case of scan 172, it also needs a continuity convention.

**R05 — scan 174 — claim without proof — kept and sharpened.** The ink reads "on voit que $(B,\Gamma)$ est aussi multigaloisien". This is a claim, not the requirement "on veut que … soit" printed by CSG and IUT. The proof is not given. It needs the definition of a multigaloisian topos, and a description of $(B,\Gamma)$ through the fundamental groupoid of $B$ extended by $\Gamma$.

**R06 — scan 174 — implication not pursued — kept.** "$B_{U,\Gamma}$ est 0-conn. ssi $\operatorname{card}\pi_0(B_U)/\Gamma=1$, i.e. $B_U$ non vide et $\Gamma$ transitif sur $\pi_0(B_U)$." The author states non-emptiness explicitly. The induced action on $\pi_0$ is the only obstruction to connectedness of the quotient.

**R07 — scan 175 — implication not pursued — kept, with an addition.** The arithmetic situation is read as the geometric one, $(\overline X,\overline S)$ over $\overline K$ with $B_{\overline D^*}\to B_{\overline U}$, together with the $\Gamma$-action. Under $B_\Gamma$ the author wrote "$e_{\text{ét}}$". This identifies $B_\Gamma$, $\Gamma=\operatorname{Gal}(\overline K/K)$, with the étale topos of the base point $e$ (the notation of scan 163).

**R08 — scans 175–176 — claim without proof — kept.** $B_{(I,\Gamma)}$ is the topos of $\Gamma$-sets over $I$, "un topos induit de $B_\Gamma$". Its connected components correspond to the orbits, and for a transitive action it is $B_{\Gamma_i}$. The underlying fact is standard: a transitive $\Gamma$-set is $\Gamma/\Gamma_i$, and $\Gamma$-sets over it are $\Gamma_i$-sets. In the profinite case, continuity conventions are still needed.

**R09 — scan 176 — claim without proof — kept; quotation corrected.** The corrected reading is "le morphisme de groupoïdes qui le décrit soit injectif sur les flèches". Only "injectif" is underlined. Both directions are asserted without proof: discrete $B$ gives an injective groupoid morphism, and $I=\coprod_j\Gamma/\Gamma_j$ is reconstructed from the injective $\Gamma_j\to\Gamma$.

**R10 — scan 177 — claim without proof — corrected.** The quotient is taken "dans|des chaque composante connexe de $\Pi_1B_{D^*,\Gamma}$" (low-confidence reading) by the kernels of the induced maps. The factorization is "(bi (pour objets), épi (pour hom)) suivi de mono (pour hom)". The witnesses' "épimorphique" in the second factor is a misreading (see S03).

**R11 — scan 178 — claim without proof — corrected.** The ink reads "passage de [$B_{D^*}\to B_U$, with $B_S$] à un $B_{X,S}$, via un $B_X$ ('bouchage des trous')", not "voire un $B_X$". $B_X$ is named as a stage of the passage, not as a further alternative target. The claimed commutation of $B\mapsto(B,\Gamma)$ with these operations is stated as an impression ("J'ai l'impression d'avoir à peu près compris"), without proof.

**R12 — scans 178–179 — conjecture / programme — kept.** "Le temps semble donc mûr enfin pour s'expliquer sur|avec l'opération inverse hypothétique de 'forage de|des trous'." This announces a construction that is not yet available.

**R13 — scan 179 — claim without proof — kept, with additions.** "Conditions équivalentes" a)–d) for a finite group acting on a surface. The labels are written in the order b), b'), c), d), with a) added last at the foot over a d). The heading reads "(Complément au §7|§15)". The author's table and the proof on scan 180, which uses "le th. du n° 15", point to §15. The marginal notes restrict the theorem to orientation-preserving actions (S01).

**R14 — scan 179, M02 — question — corrected.** "Il est prudent de supposer que $\Gamma$ opère en conservant une orientation de $U$ (supposé orientable), sinon on a des ennuis, p. ex. avec $z\to\overline z^{-1}$ … Il faudrait éclaircir|tirer au clair la situation générale (cas abélien) dans le th. … cas d'une anti-involution)." This is the author's open question about the abelian case without orientation preservation. The first pass dropped "Il".

**R15 — scan 179, M01 — conjecture — corrected.** "Ça doit être le seul contre-exemple (dans le cas … renvers. d'une orientation …) En tous cas un contre-exemple doit être tel que (si $\Gamma$ fidèle, $\Gamma\simeq(\pm1)$), opère par anti-involution…" There are two statements. The first is a uniqueness expectation. The second is a necessary condition on any counterexample ("un", not "ce"): a faithful $\Gamma$ has order 2 and acts by an orientation-reversing involution.

**R16 — scan 179, M02 — first pass's own idea — kept and completed.** In polar coordinates, $z\mapsto 1/\overline z$ is $(r,\theta)\mapsto(1/r,\theta)$. This is an orientation-reversing involution of $\mathbb C^*$ that fixes the unit circle and acts trivially on $\pi_1=\mathbb Z$. It is homotopic to the identity, so b′) and c) hold. It is not isotopic to the identity, because isotopies preserve orientation, so b) fails. The lifts $w\mapsto-\overline w+2\pi ik$ commute with $w\mapsto w+2\pi i$, so $\mathcal E\simeq\mathbb Z\times\mathbb Z/2$ is central and d) holds. But a) fails: a translation of order 2 in a group structure on $\mathbb C^*$ has no fixed point, while this map fixes a circle. So the map is a counterexample to d) ⇒ a) without orientation preservation, exactly as M01–M02 anticipate. Confidence: high.

**R17 — scan 180 — proof sketch — kept.** The case c) ⇒ d) is checked. With $\operatorname{Centre}(\pi)=1$, an extension of $\Gamma$ by $\pi$ is the pullback of $1\to\pi\to\operatorname{Aut}\pi\to\operatorname{Autext}\pi\to1$ along the outer action. A trivial outer action therefore gives $\pi\times\Gamma$. In the abelian case the outer action is the action itself, so c) makes $\pi$ central. The sketch is correct; the bracket "(et … ]" is unbalanced in the ink.

**R18 — scan 180 — claim depending on earlier material — kept; quotation corrected.** The corrected reading is "OPS [on peut supposer] que $\Gamma$ opère fidèlement … Si on avait|avec $\Gamma\ne1$, fixons un scindage de l'extension $\mathcal E$ … par le th. du n° 15 $\pi^\Gamma=|\simeq1$, or le scindage canonique de $\pi\times\Gamma$ sur $\Gamma$ donne $\pi^\Gamma=\pi$, absurde". The reduction to a faithful action replaces $\Gamma$ by its image. The contradiction needs $\pi\ne1$, which holds by $\pi_1(U)\ne(1)$, and the theorem of n° 15 for the splitting chosen. The witnesses' "Si on avait Γ = 1" inverts the hypothesis.

**R19 — scan 180 — claim the author marks unproved — kept; quotation corrected.** "Je dis qu'une action de $\Gamma$ sur $U$ est ici (à ~~isotopie~~ homéomorphisme|conjugaison près) définie par une action de $\Gamma|\pi$ sur le torseur", with "pas prouvé!" in the margin. $\pi$ acts trivially on $U_0=\pi\otimes\mathbb C/\pi$, so only $\Gamma$ gives the sentence a sense, even if the ink there is overwritten.

**R20 — scan 180 — first pass's own idea — kept and strengthened.** Crops at 2.2× confirm $\mathbb C$ in both torseur formulas, so the transcription keeps $U_0=\pi\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb C/\pi$ for $U\simeq S^1\times S^1$ as well. For $\pi\simeq\mathbb Z^2$ this group is $(\mathbb C^*)^2$, of real dimension 4, so a surface cannot be a torsor under it. The torus needs $\pi\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb R/\pi$. On the next page (CSG l. 3719–3721) the author writes "π ⊗Z C (ou π ⊗Z R)", so this is a slip in the manuscript, not a transcription error.

## Second-pass notes

**S01 — scan 179 — claim without proof (second pass).** The definition says "anabélienne si $\pi$ non abélien (auquel cas $\operatorname{Centre}(\pi)=1$), i.e.|et si $U\not\simeq\mathbb C^*,S^1\times S^1$". Both parts hold for orientable surfaces but fail without orientability. The Klein bottle group $\langle a,b\mid bab^{-1}=a^{-1}\rangle$ is non-abelian with $b^2$ central. The Möbius band ($\pi_1=\mathbb Z$) and $\mathbb{RP}^2$ ($\pi_1=\mathbb Z/2$) have abelian $\pi_1\ne1$ but are not in the list. The assumption "(supposé orientable)" in M02 is therefore needed already for the definition and for c) ⇒ d). Confidence: high.

**S02 — scan 179 — claim without proof (second pass).** In a), the existence of a topological group structure on $U$ is followed by "(donc $U\simeq\mathbb C^*$ ou $S^1\times S^1$)". This needs the fact that a locally Euclidean group is a Lie group (Gleason–Montgomery–Zippin). The connected 2-dimensional Lie groups with $\pi_1\ne1$ are then, up to homeomorphism, $\mathbb R\times S^1\simeq\mathbb C^*$ and $T^2$. The step is correct but not argued.

**S03 — scan 177 — check by the second pass.** The factorization of $\Pi_1B_{D^*,\Gamma}\to\Pi_1B_\Gamma$ into (bijective on objects, surjective on Hom) followed by (injective on Hom) produces $B_{I,\Gamma}$. Its vertex groups are the stabilizers $\Gamma_i\subset\Gamma$. By the NB of scan 176, $B_{I,\Gamma}\to B_\Gamma$ must be injective on arrows, which confirms the ink "mono" against the witnesses' "épimorphique". Confidence: high.

**S04 — scan 179 — textual question with mathematical weight (second pass).** If the heading reads "Complément au §15", as the author's table has it, §17 completes the theorem on finite groups acting on surfaces in §15. The proof on scan 180 indeed rests on "le th. du n° 15". If the ink "§7" is right, it points to the groupoid set-up of §7 recalled on scan 178 instead. The ink is ambiguous (see the changes file, change 27).

**S05 — scan 178 — implication not pursued (second pass).** The upward-drawn sign under $B_{D^*}$ is more likely the arrow $B_{D^*}\to B_S$ of the gluing datum $B_U\leftarrow B_{D^*}\to B_S$, whose amalgamated sum is $B_{X,S}$. The sentence then states that $B\mapsto(B,\Gamma)$ commutes with forming this amalgamated sum and with "bouchage des trous" ($B_X$). That is the property §18 needs before inverting it as "forage de trous".

**S06 — scan 180 — claim without proof (second pass).** The abelian case of d) ⇒ a) is left open at the foot of the page. The author marks the reduction to a $\Gamma$-action on the torsor "pas prouvé!". The witnesses' continuation ("Corollaire-Scholie … pas tout à fait démontré faute d'avoir établi la classification topologique des opérations d'un groupe fini sur $\mathbb C^*$ ou sur $S^1\times S^1$") shows that the author left this gap open. By R16, without orientation preservation the abelian statement is false for $\mathbb C^*$, so any proof must use orientation preservation.


# ===== LM_P019 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P019 (scans 181–190): research notes checked against the corrected transcription

Each first-pass note (R01–R19) is checked against `LM_P019_checked.tex` and kept, corrected or dropped, with the reason. The notes S01–S06 are the second pass's own, labelled as such. Kinds: conjecture or question; claim without proof; implication not pursued; own idea.

## First-pass notes

**R01. Scan 181. Claim without proof. Corrected.**
Grothendieck says the abelian case "n'est pas tout à fait démontré, faute d'avoir établi la classification top. des opérations d'un groupe fini sur ℂ* ou sur S¹×S¹". The missing piece is a topological classification of finite group actions on ℂ* and on the torus, enough to show that such an action comes from an action on the U₀-torsor.
Reason for the correction: the first pass read "étudié" where the ink has "établi", and left the exponent of ℂ open; it is ℂ*.

**R02. Scan 181. Claim without proof. Corrected.**
Under the extra hypothesis U^Γ ≠ ∅, "il est encore vrai que" trivial operation on π is equivalent to trivial action on U. The argument reduces to a faithful Γ and refers to the anabelian argument "plus haut", which is outside this package.
Reason: the first pass read "il en résulte aussi que". That presents the statement as a consequence of the struck conditions a) b) c); the ink states that the equivalence remains true under the new hypothesis.

**R03. Scan 182. Claim without proof. Kept, with a correction.**
Three correspondences are stated "à isom unique près" without proof: between realisations of the outer group, objects of Π₁B_{U'}, and pairs (Π', L'ᵢ) ↔ objects D̃ᵢ'* of Π₁B_{D'ᵢ*}. Scan 183 makes the last one precise through ρ_!: a subgroup Lᵢ corresponds to a pair (D̃ᵢ, λ), where λ is an isomorphism ρ_!(D̃ᵢ) =: Ũ → Ũ₀. The margin note "Je vais provisoirement laisser tomber les "primes" !" (first pass: "voir précisément dans les points") explains the change of notation from Π', L'ᵢ to π, Lᵢ on scans 183–184. The first pass's remark that the passage is unreadable no longer applies to the margin.

**R04. Scans 183–184. Claim without proof. Kept.**
The surjection Isom_{Π₁Vᵢ}(a,bᵢ) ≃ Isom_{Π₁U}(a,bᵢ) → {Lᵢ in class i} and its compatibility with the action of π are asserted; the fibres are not computed. The first pass's warning not to strengthen surjectivity to bijectivity is right: two paths differing by a loop round sᵢ give the same Lᵢ.

**R05. Scans 184–185. Implication not pursued. Kept; quotation corrected.**
The independence of π = π'/⟨⟨L'ᵢ⟩⟩ from the chosen object D̃ᵢ* is reduced ("tt revient à voir", not "il revient") to the triviality of the action of Aut(D̃ᵢ*) ≃ T ≃ Lᵢ. That triviality holds because u ∈ Lᵢ acts by int(u) and u becomes 1 in the quotient. The argument is present. What is left implicit is why a functor on a connected groupoid whose automorphism actions are trivial is "constant", i.e. factors through a point up to unique isomorphism.

**R06. Scan 185. Claim without proof. Kept; detail corrected.**
The functor "bouchage du trou i", (π', i) ↦ β(π', i), is said to land in realised loop groups of type (g, ν) and to give Aut ext(π', i) → Aut π. Here Aut ext(π', i) is defined as the "ens. des autom cat. de π' respectant la structure à lacets et fixant la classe de lacets i" (first pass: "gr."). That the quotient inherits a loop structure (the images of the remaining ν classes, and T) is not argued. The struck "+1" in the target type agrees with filling one of ν+1 holes.

**R07. Scan 186. Claim without proof. Corrected.**
The partial amalgamated sum B_{U*} of B_{U'} and B_{sᵢ} over B_{D'ᵢ*} is asserted to exist. The map B_{sᵢ} → B_{U*} "fournit un pt géom. de B_{U*}", whence a canonical universal covering and a canonical realised group π of type (g, ν−1).
Reason: the first pass read "plongement" and called the noun doubtful. The ink and both witnesses give a geometric point, which is what fixes a fibre functor and hence a group rather than an outer group.

**R08. Scans 186–187. Claim without proof. Corrected.**
The theorem: if the loop groups of types (g, ν) and (g, ν−1) are anabelian, i.e. 2g+ν ≥ 4 [donc g ≥ 2, ou g ≥ 1 (or = 1), ν ≥ 2, ou g = 0, ν ≥ 4], then Aut ext(π', i) → Aut(π) is an isomorphism. The proof plan has two steps. First, the exact sequence 1 → π → Aut ext(π', i) → Aut ext(π) → 1 "on va définir": it is still to be defined, not already defined as the first pass read. Second, a morphism of extensions into 1 → π → Aut π → Aut ext π → 1, which "sera nécessairement un isomorphisme". The relation sign in "2g+ν … 4" is overwritten in the ink; only ≥ agrees with the bracket.

**R09. Scan 188. Claim without proof. Corrected.**
"OPS T = ℤ" (one may suppose T = ℤ) is a claimed reduction, not the case distinction "Si T = ℤ" of the first pass; the reduction is not justified on these pages. Grothendieck then cites as known ("on sait que"), for 2g+ν ≥ 3, that 𝒯_{g,ν} is the fundamental group of the complex modular topos M_{g,ν}. Here 𝒯_{g,ν} = Aut ext_lac(Π_{g,ν}) inducing the identity on I and on T.

**R10. Scan 188. Claim without proof. Kept.**
The margin asserts that the universal covering of M_{g,ν} is contractible, citing Teichmüller. This is what removes π₂ on the left of the homotopy sequence.

**R11. Scan 188. Implication not pursued. Corrected.**
The homotopy sequence of the universal curve M_{g,ν+1} → M_{g,ν} is compared with 0 → Π_{g,ν} → 𝒯^{00}_{g,ν+1} → 𝒯^{00}_{g,ν} → 1. The first two identifications are equalities; π₁(M_{g,ν}) ≃ 𝒯^{00}_{g,ν} is an isomorphism, written as a vertical ≃ (the first pass drew it as an equality). Left exactness uses π₂(M_{g,ν}) = 0 (R10); right exactness uses the connectedness of the fibre U. The annotation after M_{g,ν} in the π₂ term is still unread.

**R12. Scan 189. Claim without proof (explicit deferral). Kept.**
"(à passage à un sous-groupe d'indice "2ν! près) cette structure d'extension annoncée. & les vérifications de compatibilité sont laissées …". The comparison of the moduli extension with the automorphism extension of scan 187 is explicitly left undone. Two exact sequences of the same shape do not by themselves give it.

**R13. Scan 189. Claim without proof. Corrected.**
The quasi-inverse is given on objects only. From a system B_{D*} → B_U of type (g, ν) (2g+ν ≥ 3) "et un "pt" de B_U", one builds canonically a connected T-groupoid B_{Dᵢ*} and a system of type (g, ν+1); filling the new hole gives back the data. The action on morphisms and the comparison isomorphisms are not given.
Reason: the first pass omitted "et", which made the point read as an apposition to the system; they are two separate data.

**R14. Scan 190. Implication not pursued. Kept.**
An action of Γ on π of type (g, ν) "pas seulement extérieurement" gives an outer action on π' of type (g, ν+1) fixing the class i. The reverse passage, from such outer actions to honest ones, is what the isomorphism Aut ext(π', i) ≃ Aut(π) of the theorem supplies. These lines state only the forward construction.

**R15. Scan 190. Claim without proof. Corrected.**
The normaliser ℰ'ᵢ of Lᵢ in ℰ' gives 1 → L'ᵢ (≃ T) → ℰ'ᵢ → Γ → 1 ("on trouve"). Two facts are used without proof. First, ℰ'ᵢ → Γ is onto: Γ fixes the class i, so any lift can be corrected by an element of π'. Second, ℰ'ᵢ ∩ π' = N_{π'}(L'ᵢ) = L'ᵢ: a loop subgroup is self-normalising in a non-abelian loop group. The annotation ≃ T under L'ᵢ is legible (a vertical ≃ over T), so the first pass's doubt about it is removed.

**R16. Scans 183–185. The first pass's own idea. Kept.**
Test the path description on a free group of rank 2 with a chosen cyclic peripheral subgroup, computing which conjugating elements give the same subgroup. This is a cheap check of the fibres in R04 and of the self-normalising property used in R15.

**R17. Scans 181, 187. The first pass's own idea. Kept, made concrete.**
Treat the cases outside 2g+ν ≥ 4 separately. For (g, ν) = (1, 1) or (0, 3), the filled group of type (1, 0) or (0, 2) is ℤ² or ℤ. Its centre is then non-trivial, so 1 → π → Aut π → Aut ext π → 1 is not exact and the proof plan of R08 fails at its first step.

**R18. Scan 190. The first pass's own idea. Kept.**
Whether 1 → L'ᵢ → ℰ'ᵢ → Γ → 1 splits, for small finite Γ and a given action on T, is a further question; the text only displays the extension.

**R19. Scans 182, 188–190. The first pass's own idea. Corrected.**
Separate what holds for an arbitrary orientation module T from what uses T = ℤ, and ask what survives in a profinite setting. The manuscript introduces T = ℤ by "OPS", i.e. as a reduction it claims. The question is therefore how that reduction is justified, not only which steps were stated under T = ℤ.

## Second-pass notes (mine)

**S01. Scan 182. Own idea (an inconsistency in the text).**
"card I' = ν" is written for a group of type (g, ν+1), which has ν+1 classes of loop subgroups (the witnesses print ν+1). It is probably a slip of the pen, but the count matters for the index "2ν!" on scan 189 (see S04).

**S02. Scan 182. Implication not pursued.**
The struck parenthesis gives a uniform criterion: a loop group of type (g, ν+1) is non-abelian iff 2g+ν ≥ 2, i.e. iff 2−2g (= EP, the Euler–Poincaré characteristic of the compact surface) ≤ ν. Equivalently, χ of the punctured surface is negative. The case form kept in the text, "si g = 0 on a ν ≥ 2", is equivalent, since for g ≥ 1 the group (free of rank 2g+ν) is always non-abelian.

**S03. Scans 187–188. Claim without proof.**
"qui sera nécessairement un isomorphisme" is the five-lemma step. A morphism between two extensions of Aut ext(π) by π that is the identity on the kernel and on the quotient is an isomorphism. What has to be checked is exactly that the map Aut ext(π', i) → Aut(π) restricts to the identity of π (inner automorphisms) and induces the identity on Aut ext(π). This is the content of the "vérifications de compatibilité" left undone on scan 189.

**S04. Scan 189. Own idea. Confidence: low.**
The index "2ν!" plausibly measures the two rigidifications built into 𝒯^{00}: acting trivially on T (index at most 2, the orientation) and fixing each of the ν loop classes (index at most ν!, their permutations). The text does not say this; it is an interpretation to be checked against the definition of 𝒯^{00}_{g,ν} on scan 188.

**S05. Scan 184. Question.**
The base point is written with an index, Ũ₀ = Ũ(aᵢ) and Ũ(aᵢ) ⥲ ρ_!(D̃ᵢ*(bᵢ)), while the paths run "de a vers bᵢ" and Ũ₀ was defined from a single base point a ∈ U on scan 183. The relation between a and aᵢ (one base point per hole, or a slip) is not stated.

**S06. Scan 181. Own idea. Confidence: medium.**
In the abelian case with U^Γ ≠ ∅, a fixed point identifies the U₀-torsor U with U₀ itself, Γ-equivariantly. If Γ acts by torsor (affine) maps, it then acts by group automorphisms of U₀ = π⊗ℝ/π, determined by its action on π, so trivial action on π gives trivial action on U. The fixed point removes the translation part only. It does not remove the need for the claim that an action on U comes from an action on the torsor, which is the classification gap of R01.


# ===== LM_P020 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P020 (scans 191–200): research notes, checked

The first pass's research notes R01–R39 are checked here against the corrected transcription `LM_P020_checked.tex`; its doubt table and abbreviation list are superseded by that file and by `LM_P020_changes.md`. Each note gives the scan, the kind and a verdict (keep, correct or drop) with the reason. Notes O1–O9 are mine (Claude, second pass), labelled "own". Mathematical statements are Grothendieck's unless a note is marked "own"; none of his claims here is proved in this extract.

## A. First-pass notes

**R01 — scan 191 — claim without proof — keep.** The construction of Eᵢ with 1 → Lᵢ → Eᵢ → Γ → 1 is said to work without a finiteness hypothesis on Γ ("s.s hyp. de finitude", most likely the abbreviation of "sans"; CSG/IUT "sans"), the universal case being the Teichmüller group of π′ fixing i, Autext_lac(π′;i) ≃ Aut_lac(π). No proof is given here.

**R02 — scans 191–195 — claim without proof — correct.** The theorem (finite Γ acting faithfully and fixing i is cyclic if Γ = Γ°, dihedral otherwise, with Eᵢ° ≃ ℤ and Γ° ≃ T/nT canonically) is reduced to the lemme fondamental, which is then admitted. The corrected margin (CSG/IUT footnote 60) says "il y a équivalence, si dans le th, on suppose le type (g,ν) anabélien — sinon le th. est un peu plus général", not "euclidien … donc": the restated theorem is equivalent to the preceding statement in the anabelian case and slightly more general otherwise.

**R03 — scans 191–192 — claim without proof — keep.** "(évidemment indépendant du choix de Lᵢ …)" is asserted. A proof must compare the identifications Γ° ≃ Eᵢ°/Lᵢ ≃ T/nT for Lᵢ and its π-conjugates.

**R04 — scan 192 — claim without proof — keep.** The extension of Γ° by T is identified, through the cohomology sequence of 0 → T → T⊗ℚ → T⊗ℚ/ℤ → 0, with the pull-back ("image inverse", which the witnesses drop) of 0 → T → T → Tₙ → 0 along a homomorphism Γ° → Tₙ. The argument uses the trivial action on T stated on the page; the connecting maps are not written out.

**R05 — scan 192 — implication not pursued — correct (resolved by the ink).** "sans torsion" is an insertion after "T" on the next line ("opère trivialemᵗ sur T sans torsion"), so it qualifies T, not the extension. Torsion-freeness of T ≃ ℤ is what makes T → T⊗ℚ injective in the coefficient sequence; nothing is asserted about torsion in the extension at this point.

**R06 — scan 193 — claim without proof — correct.** The step is "OPS" (on peut supposer), not "OBS": replacing Γ° by the kernel of the homomorphism to Tₙ, one may suppose it is zero. The needed implication (zero homomorphism ⇒ trivial action) is the content of the lemme fondamental.

**R07 — scan 193 — claim without proof — correct.** With the inserted word the lemma reads "Tout automorphisme direct extérieur u d'ordre fini n d'un groupe à lacets π, qui fixe une classe de lacets i et est tel que l'extension de ℤ/nℤ par Lᵢ ∈ i définie par u soit triviale, est trivial." The hypothesis "direct" (orientation-preserving) is part of the statement (CSG/IUT agree); the first pass had misread the inserted word as a margin "évident!". The lemma is admitted on scan 196, not proved.

**R08 — scans 193–194 — claim without proof — correct.** The reformulation is headed "Corollaire", not "Lemme": "Tout autom direct d'ordre fini u₀ d'un groupe à lacets π, qui soit direct et qui normalise un sous-groupe à lacets Lᵢ (i.e. qui centralise Lᵢ), est réduit à l'identité." "direct" is inserted twice (above "autom" and as "qui soit direct et"); one insertion may be meant to replace the other. See O1 for why the hypothesis is needed for the parenthesis.
