**R09 — scan 194 — claim without proof — correct.** The text says "on est ramené aussitôt au cas où u₀ est tel que u₀ᵖ = id, où p est un nb premier"; the first pass's inserted "(cas ν≥1)" is a misreading of "aussitôt". The reduction is sound (own check): if u₀ ≠ id has finite order m and p | m, then u₀^{m/p} has order p, is still direct and still centralises Lᵢ, so excluding non-trivial elements with uᵖ = id excludes u₀.

**R10 — scan 194, upper-left margin — conjecture — correct.** The NB reads: g = 0 with ν+1 = 1 or 2 is trivial; ν+1 = 3 ((g,ν) = (0,2) abélien!) "n'est pas trivial par contre", nor g = 1, ν+1 = 1 ((g,ν) = (1,0) abélien); "Pourtant le résultat doit être valable aussi dans ce cas". The first pass's "(0,2) doit l'être!" is not in the ink. The conjecture is that the lemma or its corollary also holds in the two abelian cases.

**R11 — scan 194, cancelled block — claim without proof (cancelled) — keep.** The cancelled lines describe the lifts of the outer action to a true action through the fixed points of Γ in U and choose a fixed point a with π = π₁(U,a). They are replaced by the margin "… sᵢ de S correspondant à un pt fixe de Γ op. sur U" and by scan 195.

**R12 — scan 195 — claim without proof — keep.** "on sait par ailleurs dans le cas géom. et op. fidèle qu'elle n'est pas triviale !": for a faithful geometric action fixing the puncture sᵢ, the extension of Γ by Lᵢ ≃ T is non-trivial. It is cited as known, and it must be the same extension as the one assumed trivial in the lemma.

**R13 — scan 195 — claim without proof — correct.** The ink has no "Nielsen": it says "Or sauf erreur ce résultat est (bien) connu (m̄ pour les groupes résolubles …)", with the margin "comme th. d'exist. de pts fixes d'un groupe … de Teichmüller". So the realizability of finite cyclic outer actions, even of solvable ones, is cited as known without a name; the reduction to prime order ("il suffit m̄ de le savoir pour un groupe cyclique d'ordre premier") is stated, not argued. For the literature behind it, see O3.

**R14 — scan 195 — question (doubt) — correct.** "J'ai seulement un doute s'il est démontré dans le cas général d'un (g,ν), ou seulement pour g ≥ 2, ν = 0": the interlinear "g ≥ 2" after "(g,ν)" is struck, so the alternatives are the general case and closed surfaces of genus at least 2. The first pass's reading kept "g ≥ 2" in both alternatives.

**R15 — scan 196 — conjecture — keep.** "(pour g ≥ 2 tout au moins) on s'en tire par dévissage pour le cas ν quelconque" announces a reduction from ν = 0 to arbitrary ν that is not carried out here.

**R16 — scan 196 — implication not pursued — keep, word corrected.** "le cas g = 0, 1 demanderait aussi un traitement à part" ("aussi", not "encore"). Scan 197 says only that the abelian cases (0,2) and (1,0) have essentially been treated directly; the other low-genus cases remain open in this extract.

**R17 — scan 196 — dependency marker — keep.** "préfère pour l'instant admettre le “lemme fondamental”": a), b) and everything after them on these pages depend on the admitted lemma.

**R18 — scan 196 — claim without proof (conditional) — correct.** b) reads "pour tte classe de [π-conjugaison] de tels scindages partiels sur Γ′ ⊂ Γ°, on a un iso. can. correspondant Γ′ ≃ Tₙ (= T/nT), où n = card Γ′" ("tte classe", singular, and "correspondant", both missed by the first pass). The margin (CSG/IUT footnote 62) restates it: "on a établi l'existence d'une applic. can. … des éléments d'ordre fini de Aut_lac(π) dans T⊗ℚ/T, satisfaisant les conditions examinées précédemment"; the first pass's "identification … tangentielles" is not in the ink.

**R19 — scan 197 — claim without proof — keep, reading corrected.** The restriction to the anabelian case is dropped as soon as π ≠ 0, "ce qui inclut les cas abéliens g = 0, ν = 2 et g = 1, ν = 0" ("abéliens" is an insertion, not a margin note), which are said to have essentially been treated directly, extensions of Γ by π ≃ ℤ or ℤ² being always realizable. No construction is given here.

**R20 — scan 197 — claim (replacement hypothesis) — correct.** The inserted phrase is "une extension produit", not "une extension scindée": in the abelian cases faithfulness is replaced by "l'extension n'est une extension produit sur (or sous) aucun sous-groupe Γ′ ⊂ Γ° avec Γ′ ≠ 1". This is a different, weaker condition than non-splitting; see O5.

**R21 — scan 197 — question — correct.** c) reads "Si Γ est un sous-groupe fini de Aut_lac(π) i.e. un grpe fini opérant fidèlᵗ sur π, a-t-on π^Γ = {1} ?" with the label "c)" and without the "d'ordre n" of the first pass (those words belong to the margin's "d'ordre 2"). The margin reduces it: "OPS Γ = Γ° donc Γ engendré par un autom. direct u ou Γ engendré par un anti-autom. d'ordre 2" (see O6). Γ = 1 must be excluded (R33).

**R22 — scan 198 — question with warning — correct.** d) asks whether Γ′, Γ″ with Γ′ ∩ Γ″ ≠ (1) are "contenus dans le π-conjugué d'un ss-gpe fini Γ‴ de E", restated as "tt sous-groupe section ≠ 1 est contenu dans un unique sous-groupe section maximal, mod. π-conjugaison" ("section", not "fini"; a section is a subgroup meeting π trivially, i.e. a partial splitting). The margin NB is "Deviendrait faux en se plaçant dans le groupe Aut_lac(π) […]", not "Deuxièmement d) faux": the warning concerns the analogue in Aut_lac(π), not d) itself (see O7). The witnesses continue after scan 200 with "Démontrons d)", outside this package.

**R23 — scan 198 — question and conditional claims — correct.** e) asks whether the set of π-conjugacy classes of lifts of Γ′ to E is finite. The inserted paragraph says "Dans le cas c), OPS Γ cyclique d'ordre premier et c'est OK s'il est acquis qu'une op. extérieure d'un tel groupe sur un π_{g,ν} est réalisable" (conditional; the first pass read "c'est là où il est acquis", and the item letter, c) or e), is not fully settled). The margin adds "De m̄ e) est établi si on admet que les op. ext. de groupes cycliques sur un π ≃ π_{g,ν} sont réalisables": both c) and e) are reduced to the realizability of cyclic outer actions, not established.

**R24 — scan 198 — claim without proof — correct.** The Théorème (for finite Γ₀ ≠ 1 in A = Aut_lac(π)⁰ the normaliser equals the centraliser Z, whose finite-order elements form a cyclic group, the largest finite subgroup containing Γ₀, central in Z) is marked "Ultra fort!", and an oblique stroke crosses its first lines. With the cancelled proof on 199–200 and the warnings "pas vrai!" and "faux!", it reads as an abandoned attempt; neither witness contains it.

**R25 — scan 199 — claim without proof — keep, reading corrected.** The claim that the finite-order elements of Z form a cyclic group, maximal among finite subgroups containing Γ₀ and central in Z, is not proved; the inserted name of that group is read "F" (not Γ). The page is cancelled by an oblique stroke of uncertain scope.

**R26 — scan 199 — claim without proof — keep, with a reading doubt.** The NB has "et normalise|centralise Γ₀": the ink is closer to "centralise", the sense needs "normalise". With "normalise" it says an orientation-reversing g normalising Γ₀ induces σ ↦ σ⁻¹ on Γ₀ (via the canonical Γ₀ ≃ T_{n₀} and the action −1 on T); with "centralise" the same argument would force Γ₀ to have exponent at most 2.

**R27 — scan 199 — claim without proof — keep.** Putting u and Γ₀ in a finite commutative, hence cyclic, subgroup Γ′ uses "un ss-groupe fini de A est cyclique" (from the admitted lemma), and the next step needs a realization of Γ′ extending the chosen realization of Γ₀; that is exactly where scan 200 fails.

**R28 — scan 200 — claim without proof — keep.** "le relèvement de l'action extérieure de Γ₀ en une action est décrit par un unique pt a ∈ U" is asserted; the correspondence between lifts, fixed points and their conjugacy classes is not proved here.

**R29 — scan 200 — claim explicitly rejected — keep.** "de façon unique" (double underline) carries the margin "pas vrai!" (double underline); the rejected step is the uniqueness of the realization of Γ′ extending that of Γ₀ and fixing a.

**R30 — scan 200 — lemma explicitly rejected — correct.** The hypotheses read: u₀ of finite order n₀ ≠ 1 of an orientable surface (the struck "supposée X^{u₀} ≠ ∅", i.e. u₀ has a fixed point, replaced by "U = X−S, X (compacte, …, et) laissant fixe un pt a ∈ U"), d ∈ ℕ*; if u of (X,S) satisfies u^d = u₀ and u(a) = a, then u is determined by its action on π₁(U,a), and ⟨u⟩ is determined "(il est d'ordre nd)". The margin says "faux!".

**R31 — scans 192–193 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** The model E_χ = {(q,γ) : q mod T = χ(γ)} is the pull-back extension, and for γ ∈ ker χ the element (0,γ) is a non-trivial torsion element. This correctly isolates what must be excluded (torsion in Eᵢ° over ker χ) to make χ injective.

**R32 — scans 193–194 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** A strategy, not a proof; it should now start from the corrected statement (a "Corollaire" with the hypothesis "direct").

**R33 — scans 197–198 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** For Γ = 1, π^Γ = π, so c) needs Γ ≠ 1, which the margin's reduction to a cyclic Γ generated by a non-trivial element implicitly assumes.

**R34 — scans 198–199 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** ℚ/ℤ shows that "every finite subgroup is cyclic" does not give a largest finite subgroup; this tests the inference in R25, not the specific claim.

**R35 — scans 198–200 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Separating points, orbits and conjugacy classes of lifts is the right bookkeeping for e) and for R28.

**R36 — scan 199 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** The naturality square for Γ₀ ≃ T_{n₀} is also the way to settle the normalise/centralise reading of R26: it gives inversion exactly for orientation-reversing normalising elements.

**R37 — scan 200 — own idea (first pass) — keep, with a restriction.** The algebra is right: if h commutes with u₀ and is isotopic to the identity relative to a, then v = huh⁻¹ satisfies v^d = u₀ and induces the same automorphism of π₁(U,a). Such an h exists only among homeomorphisms; see O9 for the conformal case.

**R38 — scans 195–200 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Existence of a realization, extension of a given realization to a larger group, and uniqueness of that extension are separate obligations; the "pas vrai!" and "faux!" both sit at the uniqueness step.

**R39 — scan 200 — own idea (first pass) — keep, reading settled.** The insertion reads "(il est d'ordre nd)", with no subscript, and n is not defined in the lemma (only n₀). With n = n₀ the claim fails in general: if u has order m and u^d = u₀ has order n₀, then m = n₀·gcd(m,d), which equals n₀d only when d | m (m = 3, d = 2 gives order 3, not 6).

## B. Own notes (Claude, second pass)

**O1 — scans 193–194 — own idea.** The inserted "direct" is what makes the Corollaire's "(i.e. qui centralise Lᵢ)" right. An automorphism normalising Lᵢ ≃ ℤ acts on it by ±1. An orientation-preserving one that fixes the class i preserves the orientation of the loop and so centralises Lᵢ, while a reflection fixing the puncture normalises Lᵢ and inverts its generator. Without "direct" the equivalence "normalise ⇔ centralise" fails.

**O2 — scan 195 — claim checked (own, against the corrected text).** The removal of "Nielsen" matters for attribution: the ink cites no author. The text claims only that the result is known "sauf erreur", even for solvable groups, as a fixed-point theorem on Teichmüller space.

**O3 — scan 195 — own note (literature lead, from memory, to be checked against the corpus).** The claim matches the state of the Nielsen realization problem around 1981: Nielsen (Acta Math. 75, 1943) for finite cyclic groups, Fenchel (1948 and 1950) for finite solvable groups ("même pour les groupes résolubles"). The general finite case was proved by Kerckhoff (Ann. of Math. 117, 1983) through fixed points of the action on Teichmüller space, which is the margin's formulation. Whether these statements cover punctured surfaces and peripheral data, as needed for type (g,ν+1), is exactly Grothendieck's doubt in R14.

**O4 — scan 196 — implication not pursued (Grothendieck's, made explicit).** Combined with the margin, b) amounts to a map from the finite-order elements of Aut_lac(π) to T⊗ℚ/T. It is defined conditionally on the lemma, and its "conditions examinées précédemment" refer to pages before this package.

**O5 — scan 197 — own idea.** "Produit" is the right replacement for faithfulness in the abelian cases. Over Γ′ the extension is a product exactly when Γ′ acts trivially on π (here inner automorphisms are trivial) and the extension splits. So "never a product over Γ′ ≠ 1" still allows a Γ′ acting trivially with a non-split extension, which is the rotation about a puncture in the geometric case. With "scindée" the condition would instead exclude split extensions, which faithful semidirect products are, and the replacement would be wrong.

**O6 — scans 197–198 — own idea (check).** The reductions for c) are valid: π^Γ ⊂ π^{Γ′} for Γ′ ⊂ Γ, so one may pass to any non-trivial subgroup, first a cyclic subgroup of Γ° if Γ° ≠ 1 (otherwise Γ has order 2, generated by an orientation-reversing element, as the margin says), then one of prime order ("OPS Γ cyclique d'ordre premier"). This needs Γ ≠ 1 (R33).

**O7 — scan 198 — own idea (a test of the NB, to be written out).** In genus 2 the hyperelliptic involution ι is central in the mapping class group, so for a finite subgroup G ∋ ι and any mapping class h, the subgroups G and hGh⁻¹ meet in at least ⟨ι⟩. By Kerckhoff's fixed-point theorem a finite subgroup containing both would fix a point of Fix(G) ∩ h·Fix(G) in Teichmüller space. For G with an isolated fixed point p (e.g. the cyclic group of order 10 generated by ι and an element of order 5 of y² = x⁵ − 1) and h with h·p ≠ p, these sets are disjoint, so the analogue of d) fails in Aut_lac(π_{2,0}), consistent with the NB. The details (choice of G and h) still have to be checked.

**O8 — scans 198–200 — own note (status).** The Théorème on 198, its cancelled proof on 199–200, the "pas vrai!" and the "faux!" form one abandoned unit. A downstream edition should not cite the Théorème's statement as Grothendieck's result without that status; the witnesses omit the whole unit.

**O9 — scan 200 — own idea.** For conformal automorphisms of a hyperbolic Riemann surface of finite type, an automorphism fixing a and acting trivially on π₁(U,a) is isotopic to the identity, hence the identity (Hurwitz). So the first assertion of the lemma (u determined by its action on π₁(U,a)) holds there, and the construction of R37 needs homeomorphisms. The "faux!" therefore fits the topological reading and, independently, the order claim "nd" (R39).


# ===== LM_P021 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P021 (scans 201–210): research notes, checked

This file checks the first pass's research notes R01–R19 against the corrected transcription `LM_P021_checked.tex`. That file and `LM_P021_changes.md` replace the first pass's doubt table and abbreviation list. Each note gives the scan, the kind and a verdict (keep, correct or drop) with the reason. Notes O1–O6 are mine (Claude, second pass) and are labelled "own". The statements of questions c) and d) precede these pages; where they are used, they are quoted from the CSG witness (lines 4036–4045) and are marked as such.

## A. First-pass notes

**R01 — scans 201–203 — proof resting on results not given here — correct.** The passage is a complete argument for question d), ending in "cqfd". It is not an unsupported claim. Question d) itself is stated on the preceding page. According to CSG, it concerns finite subgroups Γ′, Γ″ of the extension Ɛ of the finite group Γ by π, which belongs to a faithful, orientation-preserving outer action. The argument's inputs that are not proved on these pages are listed in O1 and O2. The first pass is right not to reconstruct the hypotheses from this excerpt.

**R02 — scan 201, cancelled paragraph — claim without proof — keep.** The cancelled route cites "le résultat (b)" (the letter is still uncertain) on the existence and uniqueness of realisations in the cyclic case. Its continuation on scan 202 is also cancelled. It records an abandoned strategy, not the final proof.

**R03 — scan 202, cancelled passage — question — correct (kind).** The passage is a doubt, not a conjecture: "Mais il n'est pas clair à priori que les deux actions … soient compatibles en le sens préc. [que le ss-groupe engendré … ⊂ Aut top(U)] soit aussi fini." The doubt concerns the abandoned topological route. The surviving proof avoids it with the centraliser Γ̃.

**R04 — scans 201–202 — claim without proof — correct.** In the ink, Ɛ! is defined by two conditions joined by "et": int(g).Γ₀ is π-conjugate to Γ₀, and the image of g in Γ centralises Γ₀. It is not one condition followed by an "i.e." gloss. With this definition, Ɛ! is a subgroup containing π and is the preimage of Γ!. The step on scan 202 from "g normalise Γ₀" (read "i.e.", written over "donc") to "g centralise Γ₀" is valid: Γ₀ is central in Γ, and Γ₀ → Γ is injective. The remaining inputs are π^{Γ₀} = (1) (see O1) and the invariance hypothesis obtained by passing to Γ!.

**R05 — scan 203 — claim without proof — correct.** The cancellation strokes start on the line "belle jambe", so "– on peut en trouver, p.ex. isom. à Autext(π,i) tout entier, (…)" is part of the cancelled text. The retained NB ends at "belle jambe". The retained claim is unproved here: without finiteness of Γ, the groups Γ′, Γ″ still generate a Γ̃ ⊂ Aut(π) with Γ̃ ∩ π = (1). The cancelled example was apparently meant to show that such a Γ̃ can be too large to be useful.

**R06 — scans 204–205 — own idea (first pass) — keep, with evidence added.** The sequence line on 204 is indexed by ℕ* and starts at a_{g,1}, while (1), (7) and the margin of 205 are indexed from 0. The ink shows the renumbering. In (7), the "0" of "[0" and the "ν−1" are written heavily over earlier figures. In the margin of 205, "a_{g,0}" and "a_{g,1}" are written over earlier subscripts. The bracketed sequence line on 204 was not updated; whether its brackets mark it for revision is my conjecture. The letter in (1) is still x/a.

**R07 — scan 204, (5) and lower-left margin — conjecture (hedged) — correct.** The margin remark is explicitly tentative. "ss doute" (CSG and IUT have "sans doute") is inserted before "un isom.", so the topological isomorphism is offered as probable, not asserted. The topologies named are the one induced from A_g on A_{g,ν}, and on Aut(U_{g,ν}) "la top. habituelle de la conv. comp. de u et son inverse" ("comp." still uncertain). The proof obligations listed by the first pass, unique extension over the punctures and continuity both ways, remain.

**R08 — scans 204–205, (6)–(12) — claim without proof — keep, with a remark.** Surjectivity of (6) (every permutation of S_{g,ν} is realised by a homeomorphism) is asserted without proof. Once it is granted, (9) and (12) follow formally. A^!_{g,ν} is the kernel of a continuous homomorphism to the finite discrete group 𝔖_ν, hence open and closed in A_{g,ν}. So A_{g,ν} and A^!_{g,ν} have the same identity component, and A^!/A° → A/A° → A/A^! ≅ 𝔖_ν is exact (own check).

**R09 — scan 205, (13)–(14) — claim without proof — keep.** The corrected placement of the inserted symbols ("ouvert Sym^ν(X)* du …", "ouvert (X_g^ν)* de X_g^ν") does not change the claims. The ink writes "X" without an index in "Sym^ν(X)*" and in "Mon(I_ν, X)". The margin (read "les pts marqués des espaces homog. …") fixes the base points of the two homeomorphisms (see O4).

**R10 — scan 206, (16) — implication not pursued — keep.** The right-hand vertical map is the restriction along I_{ν′} ⊂ I_ν. No fibration statement is made on these pages.

**R11 — scans 205–206 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** The question of the fundamental groups of the configuration spaces in (13), (14), (16), and their braid-type groups, remains a proposal of the first pass. The manuscript does not assert it.

**R12 — scan 207, (19)–(22) — claim without proof — keep, with a correction.** The corrected sentence before (23) reads "on retrouve S en termes de A_{g,S} (ou de A^!_{g,S})". The first pass had the "!" on the other group. So the setwise stabiliser is named first. (23) recovers S from A^!_{g,S} by its fixed points, and (24) from A_{g,S} by finite orbits. "On voit de suite" in (18)–(19) is still unproved.

**R13 — scan 208, (23), (25), (26) — claim without proof — keep.** Recovering S from A°_{g,S} needs isotopies rel S that move every point outside S, and infinite A°_{g,S}-orbits off S. Both are plausible for surfaces but are not argued here.

**R14 — scan 208, last members of (24) and (26) — error in the text (own check) — keep.** Enlarged, both read "≠ X_g", with no complement written; IUT has ≠, and CSG prints "=". For ν ≥ 1, an orbit of a point of X_g − S stays in X_g − S, so {x | A_{g,S}.x ≠ X_g} is all of X_g, not S. For ν = 0 the equality holds trivially. The intended condition is presumably "≠ X_g − S", but that is an editorial hypothesis and the text has not been changed.

**R15 — scan 208, (27) — implication not pursued — keep.** The three equal normalisers recover S from the setwise stabiliser, the pointwise stabiliser and the identity component. The valid fixed-point descriptions, not the faulty last members, should be the starting point. In the first normaliser, A_{g,ν}/A_{g,x} is still unresolved.

**R16 — scan 209, (28) — claim without proof — correct.** In the ink, the target category of the claimed equivalence is "surfaces compactes orientées". CSG and IUT print "orientables … de genre g", and "de genre g" is not in the ink. The first pass's remarks (morphisms, topology on the class, full faithfulness) stand. See O5 on the orientation convention.

**R17 — scans 209–210 — claim without proof — keep.** "u est intérieur ssi il conserve la classe 𝒳" and "le centre de G est trivial" are asserted without proof. The trailing "i.e." on 210 (at the cut edge) is not followed by an argument in the supplied pages.

**R18 — scans 209–210 — own idea (first pass) — keep, with a pointer.** The question is whether the class of point stabilisers is characteristic in G ≅ A_g. A theorem of J. V. Whittaker (Ann. of Math. 78, 1963) says that an isomorphism between the homeomorphism groups of compact manifolds is induced by a homeomorphism. If it applies as recalled, every automorphism of A_g is inner and the criterion is unconditional. I recalled this and did not check it against the source in this pass.

**R19 — scan 210, (29) — claim without proof — correct.** The ink reads "et que l'homomorphisme can." ("application" is overwritten). The conditions speak of "surface orientée de type (g,ν)", but the conclusion of the same sentence group speaks of "surfaces orientables de type (g,ν)". (29) writes "G", without index and with a plain stroke for the arrow. The first pass is right that the identification of A_{g,ν} ∩ A_{g,ν+1} as the stabiliser of the next point a_{g,ν} is not argued here.

## B. Own notes (Claude, second pass)

**O1 — scans 201–202 — claim without proof (dependency) — own.** Injectivity of Γ̃ → Γ rests on "π^{Γ₀} = (1)". This is question c) applied to Γ₀, which according to CSG is settled on the preceding page only conditionally, on the realisability of cyclic outer actions. It also needs Γ₀ ≠ {1}, since π^{{1}} = π. That fits the hypothesis of d) if CSG's printed "Γ ∩ Γ = {1}" is a lost "≠". CSG drops ≠ elsewhere on these pages, e.g. in (24) and (26).

**O2 — scan 201 — gap — own.** "(car Ɛ! contient le centralisateur de Γ₀ dans Ɛ, donc Γ′ et Γ″)" needs Γ′ and Γ″, as subgroups of Ɛ, to centralise Γ₀. That means Γ₀ must be central in each of them, and the lifts of Γ₀ through Γ′ and through Γ″ must be the same subgroup of Ɛ. Neither point is argued on these pages, although Γ₀ = Γ′ ∩ Γ″ is formed after identifying Γ′, Γ″ with subgroups of Γ. Both hold if Γ′, Γ″ are abelian and Γ₀ is their intersection inside Ɛ. Without this, the passage to Γ! (Γ₀ central) is circular.

**O3 — scan 203 — claim withdrawn — own.** Grothendieck first concluded "Ainsi Γ̃ ≃ Γ est un ss-groupe fini de Aut(π), donc cyclique" and struck "donc cyclique". A finite group acting faithfully on a surface group need not be cyclic, so the deletion removes an overstatement. A dashed line under the struck words could be a restoration mark. If it were one, the claim would need Γ to be cyclic.

**O4 — scan 205, lower-left margin — clarification — own.** Read as "les pts marqués des espaces homog. des premiers membres de (13), (14) s'en vont vers les systèmes de pts {a_{g,0}, …, a_{g,ν−1}} ou (a_{g,0}, …, a_{g,ν−1})", the note fixes the normalisation of (13) and (14). The class of the identity goes to the base configuration, i.e. the maps are u ↦ u(S_{g,ν}) and u ↦ (u(a_{g,i}))_i. "dans/de" and "s'en vont vers" are still uncertain.

**O5 — scans 204, 209–210 — inconsistency to settle — own.** Scan 204 takes U_{g,ν} as "la surface (orientée, ou orientable) type" and A_g = Aut(X_g) without saying whether orientation must be preserved. The equivalence on 209 is with "surfaces compactes orientées", and the punctured analogue on 210 starts with "surfaces orientées" and "surface orientée de type (g,ν)" but ends with "surfaces orientables de type (g,ν)". If A_g is the full homeomorphism group, the pair (G, 𝒳) recovers the surface without an orientation, and the target should be orientable surfaces with all homeomorphisms. An equivalence with oriented surfaces needs A_g to be the orientation-preserving group.

**O6 — scan 209 — claim without proof — own.** "Il revient au m̄ de dire que …" asserts without proof that the two definitions of a Teichmüller pair agree. In the first, (G, 𝒳) ≅ (A_g, class of A_{g,1}). In the second, 𝒳 with its homogeneous-space topology is a compact orientable surface of genus g and G → Aut(𝒳) is a homeomorphism. One direction uses (17) and the self-normalisation (19), which makes the class a copy of X_g. The other direction needs the stabilisers of points of 𝒳 to be exactly the conjugates of G₁.


# ===== LM_P022 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P022 (scans 211–220): research notes checked against the corrected transcription

The first pass's notes R01–R18 are checked one by one against `LM_P022_checked.tex` (keep / correct / drop, with the reason). Notes C01–C09 are the second-pass checker's own, labelled as such. Kinds: conjecture or question, claim without proof, implication not pursued, own idea.

## First-pass notes

**R01 — scan 211 — claim without proof — keep, corrected.** The symbol is 𝒫_{ν'} (as in (25)–(26) on scans 207–208), so the claim is that G°_ν acts transitively on U_ν and also on the set 𝒫_{ν'}(U_ν) of ν'-element subsets, for every ν' ≥ 1. No proof is given; it is the multiple transitivity of the identity component of the homeomorphism group of a connected surface on finite sets of points.

**R02 — scans 211–212 — claim without proof — keep, corrected.** "Les deux carrés sont cartésiens" and the invariance of the horizontal inclusions remain unproved. The bracketed arrow of the second row of (31) ends in an inclusion hook against A_{g,(ν,ν+1)}: it is the inclusion of A_{g,(ν,ν+1)} into A_{g,ν+1}, the one inclusion the text exempts from invariance.

**R03 — scan 212 — implication not pursued — keep.** The equality B°_{g,ν} = (A^!_{g,ν+1})° = A°_{g,ν+1} is derived from (A^!_{g,ν+1})° ⊂ (A^!_{g,ν})° = A°_{g,ν}. An interlinear "= A°_{g,ν+1}" (doubtful sign) stands over the first inclusion of that display; see C02 for the missing justification of the identity-component equalities.

**R04 — scan 213 — claim without proof — correct.** The structure group is B_{g,ν} (no circle in the ink) and the torsor is called "can." (canonical); the first-pass doubt about a circle on B is settled and that part of the note is dropped. The fibre {u ∈ A°_{g,ν} : u(a_{g,ν}) = x} is a B_{g,ν}-torsor; its local triviality needs local sections of A°_{g,ν} → U_{g,ν}, which the text does not supply.

**R05 — scans 213–215 — claim without proof — correct.** The proof runs through (35)–(36) and the "Théorème bien connu" of scan 215, and the last line of scan 215 reads "et ils ont comme conséquence que Ũ_{g,ν} est simplement connexe dans les cas anabéliens" (not "il est connu classiquement"): simple connectivity is derived from the relations (37)–(38), and the unproved input is the "bien connu" theorem itself. The exclusions (1,0), (0,2) are inserted in the theorem only (see C03).

**R06 — scan 214 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Checked on the image: the quotient before (35) is written A°_{g,ν+1}/A°_{g,ν}, and the map after (35) π_1(A°_{g,ν}) → π_1(A°_{g,ν+1}); both are reversed relative to (33) and to (35) itself. CSG and IUT print silently corrected or garbled forms; the transcription keeps the ink.

**R07 — scan 214 — claim without proof — keep.** (36) carries the inserted hypothesis (g,ν) ≠ (0,0) and the bracketed remark [le conoyau est ≃ π_1(Ũ_{g,ν})]; bijectivity at i = 1 is the stated goal. The struck draft below it (titled "Théorème bien connu", not "déjà connu") is replaced by the theorem of scan 215 and proves nothing.

**R08 — scan 215 — claim without proof — keep, corrected.** The "Théorème bien connu" has no proof or reference. In d) the ink reads "A_{g,ν} est contractile" without the circle (a slip: the full group A_{g,ν} is not connected), and the relations are introduced by "Compte tenu de (36)", not (35). For the inserted (g,ν) ≠ (0,1) see C05.

**R09 — scans 216–217 — claim without proof — correct.** The ink reads "l'inclusion G ⊂ Aut°(U) ≃ A°_{g,ν} est une équivalence d'homotopie": G = Aut°_ℂ(U) is the finite-dimensional group of complex automorphisms, a subgroup of the topological group Aut°(U). The first-pass reading "G = Aut°(U)" would have made the claim empty; the claim is supported only by the diagonal note of scan 217 (R10, C06).

**R10 — scan 217 — claim without proof — correct.** The note says G is "simplement transitif" (not "k-plement transitif") on k-tuples of distinct points, which is exactly what the unique factorisation u = g·u′ needs; the first-pass objection rested on the misreading and is dropped. Still unproved in the note: dim G = k, the simple transitivity itself, and the contractibility ("∞-connexe" in the witnesses) of A°_{g,ν+k} in the anabelian case. C06 checks the first two case by case.

**R11 — scan 217 — implication not pursued — correct.** The ink reads "(donc à son sous-groupe cpt maximal SO(3,ℝ)/{±1})", a consequence and not an alternative ("ou"), and GP(1,ℂ) is legible. SO(3,ℝ) has trivial centre, so "SO(3,ℝ)/{±1}" is a slip, presumably for SU(2)/{±1} ≅ SO(3,ℝ) or O(3,ℝ)/{±1}; the stated π_1 ≅ ℤ/2ℤ and "n'est pas un K(π,1)" hold for SO(3,ℝ) ≅ ℝP³, whose π_3 is ℤ.

**R12 — scan 218 — implication not pursued — keep.** The comparison of the fibrations G_0 → G → U and B_{g,ν} → A°_{g,ν} → U_{g,ν} gives the equivalence of the two homotopy-equivalence statements; the text asserts it ("équivaut à celle que …") without the five-lemma argument on homotopy groups.

**R13 — scans 218–219 — own idea (first pass) — keep, corrected.** The corollary reads "(et dans ceux là seulement, bien sûr)": the converse is asserted as evident, not tentatively ("dirons nous"). It holds because in the anabelian cases B_{g,ν}/B°_{g,ν} ≅ Π_{g,ν} ≠ 1 by (34).

**R14 — scan 219 — expectation about scope — keep, corrected.** The wording is "moralement on travaille dans les cas anabéliens" (not "concrètement"), with the remaining abelian cases (0,1), (0,0) "sans intérêt pour ce qui va suivre, il me semble". It is a judgement about the plan, not a theorem.

**R15 — scans 219–220 — claim with an outlined derivation — correct.** The theorem is about the subgroup ("le s-groupe A_{g,(ν,ν+1)}/A°… = Γ'_{g,ν+1}", "formé des élts qui fixent a_{g,ν}") of Γ_{g,ν+1}, under the inserted hypothesis "Supp. (g,ν) ≠ (1,0) et (0,2)", and not about the whole group Γ_{g,ν+1}, whose top factor would be 𝔖_{ν+1}. The factors 𝔖_ν, Γ^!_{g,ν}, Π_{g,ν} fit Γ'_{g,ν+1}; the ink leaves the denominator (A°_{g,ν} or A°_{g,ν+1}) and the subscript of the first factor doubtful, while the mathematics requires A°_{g,ν+1} and 𝔖_ν.

**R16 — scan 220 — implication not pursued — keep.** The identification of the outer action of Γ^!_{g,ν} on Π_{g,ν} with the one induced by A^!_{g,ν} ⊂ Aut(U_{g,ν}) is asserted, not proved; base-point changes act by inner automorphisms, which is why only outer actions are well defined.

**R17 — scan 220 — own idea (first pass) — keep; carried out in C07.** The case table settles the question: the exclusion must be (1,0) and (0,2), so the written "(0,1)" is a slip of the manuscript (the ink and both witnesses agree on "(0,1)").

**R18 — scan 220 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Splitting of (41) and (43) is a separate question that the text does not raise; nothing in scans 211–220 bears on it.

## Checker's own notes

**C01 — scan 212 — implication not pursued (checker's note).** The margin of scan 212 proposes a simpler notation A•_{g,ν} for the stabiliser A_{g,(ν,ν+1)} of the marked point a_{g,ν} in A_{g,ν} = Aut(U_{g,ν}), the dot standing for the pointed object; it is distinct from the identity component A°_{g,ν}. The main text of scans 211–220 still writes A_{g,(ν,ν+1)}; the CSG reading edition substitutes A• throughout.

**C02 — scan 212 — own idea (checker's note).** The equality (A^!_{g,ν})° = A°_{g,ν} used in the display holds because A^!_{g,ν}, the kernel of A_{g,ν} → 𝔖_ν, is an open subgroup, and an open subgroup of a topological group contains its identity component. The same argument gives (A^!_{g,ν+1})° = A°_{g,ν+1}, the interlinear equality of the display.

**C03 — scan 213 — claim without proof, scope (checker's note).** The corollary (34), B_{g,ν}/B°_{g,ν} ≅ π_1(U_{g,ν}; a_{g,ν}) =: Π_{g,ν}, inherits the exclusions (1,0), (0,2) inserted in the theorem, although they are not repeated. In those two cases B_{g,ν} is connected (corollary of scan 219) while π_1(U_{1,0}) ≅ ℤ² and π_1(U_{0,2}) ≅ ℤ, so (34) fails there; in the cases (0,1) and (0,0) both sides are trivial.

**C04 — scan 215 — claim without proof (checker's note).** The hard parts of the theorem, the contractibility of A°_{g,ν} in the anabelian cases and the vanishing π_i(A°_{g,ν}) = 0 for i ≥ 2, (g,ν) ≠ (0,0), are classical results on homeomorphism groups of surfaces (Hamstrom, 1960s); the manuscript calls the whole theorem "bien connu" and gives no reference. The corrected reading of the last line of scan 215 makes the logical order explicit: from these relations follows the simple connectivity of Ũ_{g,ν}.

**C05 — scan 215 — implication not pursued (checker's note).** The inserted "(g,ν) ≠ (0,1)" in c) and d) is redundant: by (39), π_1(A°_{0,1}) ≅ ℤ, so c) and d) fail for (0,1) anyway, as a) and b) do. A possible explanation, offered as a guess only: the cancelled bracket under c) ("… A°_{g,ν} contract.") and the struck word before K(ℤ,1) in the (0,1) row of (39) suggest that A°_{0,1} was first taken to be contractible.

**C06 — scan 217 — own idea, check of the diagonal note (checker's note).** In the four abelian cases: (1,0), k = 1, G = U acting on itself by translations; (0,2), k = 1, G = ℂ*; (0,1), k = 2, G = Aff(1,ℂ), simply transitive on ordered pairs of distinct points of ℂ; (0,0), k = 3, G = PGL(2,ℂ) = GP(1,ℂ), simply transitive on ordered triples of distinct points of ℙ¹. In each case dim_ℂ G = k, so "dim G = k" is the complex dimension, and the simple transitivity claimed in the note holds.

**C07 — scan 220 — claim corrected by computation (checker's note).** The map (42) Γ'_{g,ν+1} → Γ_{g,ν} has kernel Π_{g,ν} exactly in the anabelian cases (the point-pushing sequence). In the abelian cases it is an isomorphism, as the margin NB says: (1,0) gives SL(2,ℤ) → SL(2,ℤ), kernel 1 ≠ Π_{1,0} ≅ ℤ²; (0,2) gives ℤ/2 → ℤ/2, kernel 1 ≠ Π_{0,2} ≅ ℤ; (0,1) and (0,0) give 1 → 1 with Π trivial. So the exclusion "(g,ν) ≠ (1,0), (0,1)" before (43) should read (1,0), (0,2), matching the hypothesis of the theorem on scan 219.

**C08 — scan 220 — claim corrected (checker's note).** The inclusion from which (42) is "déduit" is written A_{g,(ν,ν+1)} ↪ A_{g,ν+1} in the ink (both witnesses also have the target A_{g,ν+1}). That inclusion induces the inclusion Γ'_{g,ν+1} ⊂ Γ_{g,ν+1}; the homomorphism (42) comes from A_{g,(ν,ν+1)} ↪ A_{g,ν} together with A°_{g,ν+1} ⊂ A°_{g,ν}, so the target index is a slip.

**C09 — scan 216 — claim without proof (checker's note).** The second alternative of the corollary is "espace homogène sous un groupe topologique … par un ss-groupe connexe" (the condition had been lost in an illegible stretch of the first pass), with SO(3,ℝ) or probably GP(1,ℂ) for S². The connectedness of the isotropy group is what makes π_1(U) abelian through the homotopy sequence, as the NB at the top of scan 217 explains; the converse ("La réciproque … remarquable !") is stated without proof.


# ===== LM_P023 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P023 (scans 221–230): research notes checked against the corrected text

Each note gives its scan, kind and the decision taken. R01–R27 are the first pass's notes; M01–M09 are mine. "Own idea" marks a model's idea, not Grothendieck's.

## First-pass notes

**R01, scan 221, claim without proof: KEEP, corrected.** The canonical map from the surface-group extension to 1 → Π → Aut(Π) → Autext(Π) → 1 is justified only by "comme pour tte structure d'extension par un noyau de centre trivial". The ink says "noyau", not "groupe". The diagram is numbered (44), not (43); the theorem cites it as "par (44)", and (43) is the extension on scan 220. Injectivity of Π → Aut(Π) (trivial centre) and commutativity of the diagram are not written out.

**R02, scan 221, claim without proof: KEEP, corrected.** The "Théorème bien connu" (Γ_{g,ν} ≅ Autext_lac(Π_{g,ν}) and, by (44), Γ'_{g,ν+1} ≅ Aut_lac(Π_{g,ν})) is stated for the "cas anabélien" without proof. The margin note can now be read. It claims that the statement also holds for the couple (1,0) "(cf plus bas)", and even for (0,2) "si on définit ad hoc la notion de groupe à lacets (0,2)". The Conclusion commune on scans 229–230 supplies that ad hoc definition.

**R03, scan 222, claim without proof: KEEP, corrected.** The Corollary asserts two equivalences of categories, X ↦ π₁(X) and (X,s) ↦ π₁(X,s), from isotopic categories. The margin defines the arrows as "les classes d'isotopie d'homéomorph."; the first pass's "classes … à isotopie" is not in the ink. Essential surjectivity (every exterior group with loop structure of type (g,ν) comes from a surface) is not addressed.

**R04, scan 222, claim without proof: KEEP.** "Rappelons" recalls (45) Γ'_{g,ν+1} ≅ Γ_{g,ν} for 2g+ν ≤ 2. The source is the note on scan 220: the homomorphism is an isomorphism in the abelian cases. No proof is given on these pages.

**R05, scan 223, claim without proof: KEEP, corrected.** The torus statement, that a homeomorphism inducing the identity on π₁ is isotopic to the identity, is called "un résultat vrai (et connu)" and not proved. The struck "sur … pt marqué … a_{1,0}" shows a first formulation relative to the marked point. The surviving "le" leaves the corrected sentence ungrammatical, but the intended hypothesis is "induit l'identité sur π₁".

**R06, scan 223, claim without proof: KEEP, corrected.** (48) H × A_{g,ν+1} → A_{g,ν}, (g,u) ↦ gu, "est un homéomorphisme". The ink has no prime on A_{g,ν+1}, which is the stabiliser of 1_H. A proof needs continuity of φ ↦ (φ(1_H), φ(1_H)⁻¹·φ) in the compact-open topology; this holds because translations of a topological group are homeomorphisms.

**R07, scans 223–224, own idea (first pass): KEEP, confirmed on the ink.** (49) reads π_i(A_{g,ν+1}) ≃ π_i(A_{g,ν}) × π_i(H), with ν+1 on the left. But (48) gives A_{g,ν} ≅ H × A_{g,ν+1}, hence π_i(A_{g,ν}) ≅ π_i(H) × π_i(A_{g,ν+1}). The indices in (49) are swapped, an authorial slip that both witnesses copy; the next paragraph uses the correct direction, A°_{g,ν} ≃ H × A°_{g,ν+1}.

**R08, scan 224, claim without proof: KEEP, corrected.** The sentence reads "comme A°_{g,ν} ≃ H × A°_{g,ν+1} avec A°_{g,ν+1} contractile, on retrouve que H → A°_{g,ν} est un homotopisme". The first pass's "nous avons" was a misreading. The decomposition is a premise, and the contractibility of A°_{g,ν+1} in the anabelian case is assumed, not proved.

**R09, scan 224, claim without proof: CORRECT.** The ink says "structure de groupe ℂ-algébrique unique" and "variété algébrique X", not "analytique". My check of scope: for (0,2) and (0,1) the algebraic group structure (𝔾_m, 𝔾_a) lives on X = U_{g,ν}, not on X̂ = ℙ¹; only for (1,0) is X̂ = X, so "sur X̂" (if that is the reading) fits only that case. The statement that the identity component of Aut(X) consists of the translations holds for (1,0) and (0,2) but fails for (0,1), where Aut(𝔸¹) = Aff(1,ℂ) is connected and larger. That is why the text restricts it to "les cas limites".

**R10, scans 225–226, own idea (first pass): CORRECT; it becomes a definite finding (see M04).** The first pass suspected that the action on π₁ differs from the permutation of the ends. That is right, and (51) does not "s'identifier" with (51 bis) on all of Γ_{g,2}. Take s: z ↦ z⁻¹ and c: z ↦ z̄. On π₁(ℂ*) = ℤ, s and c both act by −1, so the kernel of (51) is {1, z ↦ 1/z̄}. The kernel of (51 bis) is Γ^!_{0,2} = {1, c}. The text's own example z ↦ z̄, given as a nontrivial element of Γ^!, therefore acts nontrivially on Π.

**R11, scan 226, claim without proof: KEEP.** The margin says "Cet hom. est tjrs surjectif" for χ and renames its kernel Γ^+ ("et non plus Γ^0!"). A proof needs an orientation-reversing homeomorphism of each type preserving the set of punctures; a reflection of a symmetric model works (see M08).

**R12, scan 226, Grothendieck's question/proposal: CORRECT.** The margin note can now be read: "Ceci suggère que pour une description isotopique de la cat. des surfaces (X) de type (0,2), il faut utiliser le couple de π₁(X) (≃ℤ) et de son module d'orientation T; itou plus bas à propos des cas des types (0,1), mais alors π = 0 et il suffit de T." The "(0,0)" in the first pass is not in the ink. The proposal is carried out in the Conclusion commune (scans 229–230).

**R13, scan 226, claim without proof: KEEP.** "Thm (bien connu!) Γ^{!+}_{0,2} = 1" is asserted without proof. It is equivalent to Γ^+_{0,2} ≅ 𝔖₂ via ρ^+ and to Γ^!_{0,2} ≅ {±1} via χ^!, which agrees with the computation in M04. The punctures must be kept as punctures, not as boundary circles fixed pointwise.

**R14, scans 226–227, implication not pursued: KEEP, sharpened by M05.** The bracketed "énoncé commun" (Γ^+ → Aut(Π) injective, with image the automorphisms of determinant 1) holds for (1,0), where Γ^+_{1,0} ≅ SL(2,ℤ). It fails as written for (0,2); see M05.

**R15, scan 227, claim without proof: KEEP.** Corollary (56): Γ^+_{0,3} ≅ 𝔖₃ via ρ^+_{0,3}, and Γ_{0,3} ≅ 𝔖₃ × {±1}. It is derived only through "modulo les isomorphismes Γ'_{g,3} ≃ Γ_{g,2}" and (55). The first subscript of Γ^+ remains doubtful (g|0).

**R16, scan 227, deleted claim: KEEP, corrected.** The paragraph struck with large crosses calls Γ_{0,3} an extension of {±1} (braces in the ink, not parentheses) by Γ^+_{0,3}, says it is split, and adds "Il en est ainsi de Γ_{g,ν} →χ {±1} quel que soit (g,ν)". This is a cancelled line of thought, not a retained theorem. The cancelled general claim is nevertheless true (M08).

**R17, scan 227, claim without proof: CORRECT.** The margin reads "cela redonne(?) bien que l'inclusion de G = Aff(1,ℂ) = Aut_ℂ(X)⁰ dans A_{g,1} est un homotopisme"; the first pass's "montre" is doubtful. As written the claim is too strong: (58) gives Γ_{0,1} ≅ {±1}, so A_{0,1} has two components, while Aff(1,ℂ) is connected (see R20).

**R18, scan 228, claim without proof: KEEP.** (58), Γ_{0,1} ≅ {±1} "par la signature", is stated as a corollary of Γ_{g,1} ≃ Γ'_{g,2} = Γ^!_{g,?} together with (55). See M07 on the doubtful subscript.

**R19, scans 228–229, claim without proof: CORRECT.** In both places "équivalence" is struck, so the claim is that (59) G ↪ A₀ "est un(e) … homotopisme". The argument uses simple transitivity on ordered triples, the homeomorphism (60) and the contractibility of A°_{0,3}. As in R20, it can hold only onto the identity component, because (61) gives Γ₀ ≅ {±1} while G ≅ GP(1,ℂ) is connected.

**R20, scans 227–229, own idea (first pass): KEEP, as a definite finding.** The inclusions Aff(1,ℂ) → A_{0,1} (margin of 227) and GP(1,ℂ) → A₀ (59) cannot be homotopy equivalences onto the whole groups, because π₀ is {±1} by (58) and (61) while the complex groups are connected. Read correctly, they are equivalences onto the identity components, as (50) states explicitly for A°_{g,2}.

**R21, scan 229, own idea (first pass): KEEP, sharpened.** The digit after "rg" looks like Grothendieck's 1. But type (1,0) has Π ≅ ℤ², and the same paragraph uses Λ²_ℤ Π, which is 0 in rank 1, and "c'est aussi son déterminant". Rank 2 is required, so this is probably an authorial slip; the witnesses print 2. The transcription keeps \unsure{1|2}.

**R22, scan 229, implication not pursued: KEEP, completed.** For type (0,2), automorphisms are "n'importe quel couple (u_Π, u_T)". With M04, g ↦ (action on π₁, χ) is an isomorphism Γ_{0,2} → Aut(Π) × Aut(T) = {±1}². This confirms the definition and explains why the action on Π alone, as in (51), does not suffice.

**R23, scan 230, implication not pursued: KEEP, corrected.** For types (0,1) and (0,0) the structure is the orientation module T alone. The margin requires the type in addition, "(sous-entendu g=0) le ν ∈ {0,1}"; the first pass's "le genre étant" is not in the ink. So Aut = Aut(T) = {±1} must be matched with (58) and (61).

**R24, scan 230, claim without proof: KEEP.** The Théorème (equivalence for every (g,ν) ∈ ℕ×ℕ, with arrows the isomorphism classes on both sides) is stated without proof. The proof would assemble the anabelian case (221–222) with the case-by-case computations (46)–(61).

**R25, scan 230, Grothendieck's reservation: KEEP, corrected.** The sentence breaks off at "récupérer" and continues on scan 231. The CSG witness gives the continuation, "l'extension de ce groupe par le dit π₁ !", which must be checked on that scan's ink. The reservation is explicit: "ce théorème n'est pas bien sûr pleinement satisfaisant".

**R26, scan 230, own idea (first pass): KEEP, low priority.** Testing the reservation with group actions through the translation group H is a reasonable example. According to the CSG witness, the author's own diagnosis follows on scan 231: the isotopic category neglects the homotopy type of the components of Homeo(X, X').

**R27, scan 230, own idea (first pass): DROP as an independent idea.** The proposed enrichment, keeping the topology of the homeomorphism groups, is what the author proposes on the next page according to the CSG witness: components seen as torsors under A°_{g,ν}, and an "∞-fidèle" description. It should be recorded as the author's line of thought when scan 231 is checked.

## My notes

**M01, scan 221, own idea (mine): check of a reference.** As read, "La structure d'extension (41) est déduite de (41) par image inverse par l'inclusion Γ^! → Γ" is circular. Pulling back along Γ^!_{g,ν} → Γ_{g,ν} must start from the extension of Γ_{g,ν}, which is (43), and both witnesses print (43). If the ink is (41), it is an authorial slip. The transcription marks \unsure{(41)|(43)}.

**M02, scan 222, claim without proof (Grothendieck).** The margin says "C'est même une équivalence au niveau des catégories ∞-isotopiques ce qui exprime seulement le fait que A°_{g,ν} et A°_{g,ν+1} sont contractiles". It asserts a homotopy-coherent strengthening of the Corollary. Its only input, the contractibility of the identity components in the anabelian range, is assumed, not proved.

**M03, scan 222, claim without proof (Grothendieck).** "(condition vide si ν = 0, d'ailleurs…)": for ν = 0 the loop structure imposes nothing, so the theorem gives Γ_{g,0} ≅ Autext(Π_g), all outer automorphisms, orientation-reversing ones included. This is the classical Dehn–Nielsen–Baer statement, used here without proof.

**M04, scans 225–226, own idea (mine): (51) and (51 bis) are different characters.** Γ_{0,2} ≅ {±1}² is generated by s: z ↦ z⁻¹ (swaps the ends, holomorphic) and c: z ↦ z̄ (fixes the ends, reverses orientation). Action on π₁(ℂ*) = ℤ: s ↦ −1, c ↦ −1. Permutation ρ of the ends: s ↦ −1, c ↦ +1. Orientation χ: s ↦ +1, c ↦ −1. So the π₁-action equals ρ·χ, and ker(51) = {1, z ↦ 1/z̄} ≠ Γ^!_{0,2} = ker(51 bis). The identification "(51) s'identifie à (51 bis)" holds only on Γ^+_{0,2}.

**M05, scans 226–227, own idea (mine): the "énoncé commun" fails for (0,2).** Γ^+_{0,2} = {1, z ↦ 1/z} maps isomorphically onto Aut(Π) = GL(1,ℤ) = {±1}, and its nontrivial element acts by −1, which has determinant −1. So "a comme image le groupe des autom. … qui sont de dét. 1" is false for rank 1. It would hold for Γ^{!+}_{0,2}, which is trivial by (55). The Conclusion on scan 229 treats (0,2) correctly, with T as extra data.

**M06, scan 226, own idea (mine): sign of the example.** If the ink really gives "(−1,−1)" as the image of z ↦ z⁻¹ under (ρ, χ), it is impossible: z ↦ z⁻¹ is holomorphic (χ = +1) and swaps the ends (ρ = −1), so the image is (−1, 1). The second example, z ↦ z̄ ↦ (1, −1), is consistent with ρ being the permutation character of (51 bis), not the π₁-action of (51), under which z̄ ↦ −1.

**M07, scan 228, own idea (mine): subscript in "On a ici".** In Γ_{g,1} ≃ Γ'_{g,2} = Γ^!_{g,?}: with two punctures, the stabiliser of one fixes the other, so Γ'_{g,2} = Γ^!_{g,2}. Only the subscript 2 is correct. The ink is doubtful (1|2), and the witnesses print Γ^!_{g,1}.

**M08, scan 227, own idea (mine): the cancelled splitting claim is true.** "Il en est ainsi de Γ_{g,ν} →χ {±1} quel que soit (g,ν)": embed the surface symmetrically in ℝ³ with the punctures on the mirror plane. The reflection is an orientation-reversing involution preserving the punctures, and its class splits χ. Grothendieck struck the paragraph, but the general claim in its NB stands.

**M09, scan 226, own idea (mine): "ρ et ρ".** The ink appears to read "mettant ensemble ρ et ρ". The homomorphism (54) that follows is g ↦ (ρ(g), χ(g)), so the second letter must mean χ. The transcription keeps \unsure{\rho|\chi}.


# ===== LM_P024 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P024 (scans 231–240): research notes, checked against the corrected transcription

The first pass's notes R01–R27 are checked one by one against `LM_P024_checked.tex`. Each has a verdict (keep / correct / drop) and a reason. Notes S01–S07 are the second-pass checker's own and are labelled as such. Kinds: conjecture or question, claim without proof, implication not pursued, own idea.

## First-pass notes

**R01 — scan 231 — implication not pursued — KEEP (reading corrected).**
Grothendieck complains that the isotopic equivalence keeps only the Π₀ of the homeomorphism spaces and “brutally” replaces Homéo(X, X′) by its Π₀. In doing so it forgets the homotopy structure of the components, which are homeomorphic to A°_{g,ν}. The interlinear gloss reads “(i.e. tient compte des Π₀ des espaces d'homéomorphismes)”, not “espaces d'isom.”. The overwritten isotopy digit (1 or 0) is still unresolved.

**R02 — scan 231 — claim without proof — KEEP.**
“C'est [justement] dans le cas anabélien, et dans celui-là seulement, que ce groupe est ∞-connexe” is asserted without proof. The case boundaries in (g, ν) belong to earlier pages.

**R03 — scan 231 — own idea (first pass) — KEEP.**
The note warns that “∞-connexe” (all homotopy groups vanish) should not be silently replaced by “contractible”. It is a methodological caution and is unaffected by the corrections.

**R04 — scan 231 — claim without proof — CORRECT.**
The first pass read “celles de Teichmüller” and “elles sont alors … ∞-fidèles”. The ink reads: in the abelian case the description “doit être remplacée par celle de Ladegaillerie en termes [del.] B_{D*} → B_u ou Π_{D*} → Π_u”, and “elle devient alors (dans tous les cas ≠ (0,0)) ∞-fidèle”. So the claim is that Ladegaillerie's description becomes ∞-faithful in every case except type (0,0); it rests on outside work and is not proved here. The case (0,0) is then called “entièrement réfractaire”.

**R05 — scan 232 — conjecture or question — CORRECT.**
“Il faudrait pour commencer expliciter le Gr.-catégorie 𝒢” asks for the group-category with Π₀ ≃ {±1} (via χ) and Π₁ ≃ Π₁(A°₀) ≃ ±1, “déduit de A₀ en tuant les groupes d'homotopie sup.” The 2-category of 2-isotopic spheres is then to be described by the 2-groupoid of 1-torsors under 𝒢. The margin, unread by the first pass, now largely reads “[O.p.s.] que 𝒢 n'a que deux objets correspondant à l'identité et la conjugaison complexe de la sphère de Riemann; en fait elle est scindable et m̄ canoniquement scindée…”. This is a further claim without proof (see S01).

**R06 — scan 232 — own idea (first pass) — CORRECT.**
The first pass asked whether the two groups determine 𝒢 or whether coherence data remain. For a group-category with Π₀ = Π₁ = ℤ/2 the action is necessarily trivial, and the remaining invariant is a class in H³(ℤ/2; ℤ/2) ≅ ℤ/2. The margin's “scindable … canoniquement scindée” is exactly the claim that this class vanishes, so the question now has a definite target (S01).

**R07 — scan 233 — claim without proof — CORRECT.**
(63) is introduced as “la suite exacte correspondante de ss groupes” (sub-groups), not “de ∞-groupes”: S̃l(2, ℂ) ⊂ Ã₀ and G̃P(1, ℂ) ⊂ A₀. The existence, group structure and canonicity of the covering Ã₀ in (62) are not established on this page. They are derived on scan 234 from (64) and the restriction equivalence, which is itself “sauf erreur”.

**R08 — scan 233 — claim without proof — CORRECT.**
The coordinate-free parenthesis is now legible. Independently of the cases, one takes a unimodular real vector space V and its complexification V_ℂ, with a real structure on Λ²V_ℂ and an invariant basis of it. One then considers the ρ-linear automorphisms (ρ ∈ Aut_ℝ ℂ) of V_ℂ that are id or complex conjugation on Λ²V_ℂ. The first pass's “ℝ-linéaires φ” was a misreading. Closure under composition and the kernel of (63) remain to be checked; S02 does this.

**R09 — scans 233–234 — own idea (first pass) — KEEP.**
Testing a lift of the non-trivial component and its square is the right check. S02 carries it out for S̃l(2, ℂ): entrywise conjugation is such a lift, and it squares to 1.

**R10 — scan 234 — claim without proof — CORRECT.**
After “On [choisit ici | fait agir sur] X₀ = P¹_ℂ”, the inclusion (64) G̃P(1, ℂ) → A₀ “est (par ce qui précède (59)) … une équivalence d'homotopie”. The parenthesis is unproblematic (one “(” before “par”, “(59))”). The proof is referred back to (59), outside this package.

**R11 — scan 234 — conjecture or question — CORRECT.**
The statement is about “la classification des extensions du groupe top. A₀ par un groupe [discret|disons] μ” (not “description”). Restriction to GP(1, ℂ) is claimed to be an equivalence, under “Sauf erreur … (sauf erreur)”, which yields Ã₀ up to unique isomorphism. Whether the ink says “discret” is unresolved. If it says “disons”, the discreteness needed for the restriction equivalence is first stated in the scan 237 margin (“on suppose μ discret”). As the first pass noted, the GP(1, ℂ) on this line has no tilde in the ink.

**R12 — scans 234–235 — claim without proof — CORRECT.**
The surface is “compacte orientée X” (not “orientable”). It yields 1°) the {±1}-torsor via χ and 2°) the groupoid of lifts to Ã₀, a gerbe bound by μ₂(ℂ) on which Tors(μ) ≃ Ens₂ acts. The asserted equivalence of 2-categories is not proved. Note the tension: if X is oriented, the torsor ω of its two orientations is canonically trivial, yet ω is used as an invariant. “orientée” is probably to be understood as “orientable”, but the text is not emended.

**R13 — scans 235–236 — implication not pursued — CORRECT.**
The parenthesis is now legible. “de ce point de vue on a envie de dire qu'elle est 3-fidèle”, but since essential surjectivity on objects is trivial “il vaut mieux l'appellation 2-fidèle”. Since one respected the Π₁ (besides the Π₀) of the components of the A₀-torsors Isom(X₀, X), “qui sont des A°₀-torseurs donc à Π₁ isom. à μ = {±1}”, it is better still to call it 1-faithful. “Mais en fait elle est m̄ 2-fidèle” because of (65), and not 3-faithful because of (66).

**R14 — scan 236 — conjecture or question — KEEP.**
“On cherche une description ∞-isotopique … de tous ordres (à déterminer !) … Au concours !” is an open request. Nothing on these pages answers it.

**R15 — scan 236 — own idea (first pass) — KEEP.**
Organising the requested description by Postnikov truncations of A°₀ ≃ S³/±1 is a sound way to make the request concrete. With the corrected reading, the torsor-plus-gerbe description is the 2-truncated stage (Π₂ = 0), and Π₃ = ℤ is the first obstruction.

**R16 — scan 237 — conjecture or question — KEEP.**
Grothendieck is “pas trop sûr” that the proposed 1- or 2-isotopic description is correct, because no relation has been imposed between the μ-torsor ω and the μ-groupoid ℛ. The general discussion that follows tries to settle this.

**R17 — scan 237 — claim without proof — KEEP.**
Extension classes with a given outer action form a torsor under H²(Γ, μ) “s'il n'est vide, ce [qui est | qu'on a] exclu par hypothèse de départ, en partant de G”. This is standard extension theory, stated without proof. Only the alternative reading of “qui est” is new.

**R18 — scan 238 — conjecture or question — CORRECT.**
The Gr.-catégorie “définie par Sinh via (69)” is “donc ici scindée par la donnée de l'extension G” (“ici”, not “même”). Grothendieck doubts that the order-2 action is inner and has “l'impression” that (69) Γ = {±1} → Autext(G°) is an isomorphism in both cases. S04 confirms this for the semilinear group.

**R19 — scan 238 — own idea (first pass) — DROP.**
The note's counterexample attacked a reading “μ = Centre(G)”, but the margin reads “μ ⊂ Centre(G)”. With μ = Centre(G°), G° acts trivially on μ, so the conjugation action of G on μ factors through Γ. Hence “Γ opère trivialement sur μ” is exactly equivalent to μ ⊂ Centre(G), and the margin is correct as written.

**R20 — scan 239 — conjecture or question — CORRECT.**
“On voudrait dégager des conditions sur G et sur S” for (70) to be bijective is a stated research question. The margin, misread by the first pass, says “ω ne dépend pas du choix de l'extension G̃ de ℋ par [?] ≃ Π₁(ℋ°); par contre ℛ en dépend (et il faudrait voir comment)”. So the second component of (70) depends on the chosen extension G, and Grothendieck leaves that dependence open.

**R21 — scan 239 — own idea (first pass) — KEEP.**
The injectivity argument lifts a class with zero obstruction to H¹(S, G) and then, if its Γ-image is trivial, to H¹(S, G°). That shows only that the fibre of (70) over the base point is trivial. In non-abelian cohomology this does not give injectivity without twisting, which is the device used on scan 240 for surjectivity.

**R22 — scan 239 — claim without proof — CORRECT.**
The ink reads “Par les marteaux-pilons d'homotopie, ça marche si ∃ n ∈ ℕ avec Πᵢ(G°) = 0 si i ≤ n (donc Πᵢ(B_{G°}) = 0 si i ≤ n+1) [et] S un CW-complexe de dim ≤ n+1”. The margin reads “dans le cas qui nous occupe, on peut prendre n = 2”. The sufficiency is standard obstruction theory and is not written out. The value n = 2 is checked in S05.

**R23 — scans 236, 239 — own idea (first pass) — KEEP (sharpened).**
The test S = S⁴ is valid and decisive. S⁴ is simply connected, so H¹(S⁴, ℋ) = [S⁴, Bℋ] = π₃(ℋ°)/Γ, which is infinite because π₃(ℋ°) = ℤ. Meanwhile H¹(S⁴, Γ) = H²(S⁴, μ) = 0. So (70) fails to be injective as soon as dim S = 4, which shows that the bound dim S ≤ n+1 = 3 is sharp in this sense.

**R24 — scans 238–239 — own idea (first pass) — KEEP.**
Without the margin hypothesis (Γ acting trivially on μ), the second invariant should live in H² with coefficients twisted by the Γ-torsor rather than in a fixed H²(S, μ). This is a sound extension of the question.

**R25 — scan 240 — conjecture or question — KEEP.**
The obstruction in H²(S, G) means expressing that “une certaine gerbe liée par G_S est neutre — brr!”. Grothendieck sets this route aside (“Mais partons plutôt…”), and the non-abelian obstruction is left unsolved.

**R26 — scan 240 — claim without proof (unfinished) — CORRECT.**
If the extension of Γ by ℋ° (ℋ° written over G°) splits, ω lifts to some ζ₀ ∈ H¹(S, ℋ_S) with obstruction ρ₀. “Il s'agit de corriger ζ₀ en ζ” so that the obstruction becomes a given ρ. “Utilisant ζ₀ pour tordre ℋ_S en ℋ′_S” and G_S “en G′_S”, one gets 1 → μ → G′_S → ℋ′_S → 1. The argument continues past scan 240.

**R27 — scan 240 — own idea (first pass) — KEEP.**
Twisting by a class in H¹(S, ℋ) through the conjugation action of ℋ = G/μ on G leaves the central kernel μ unchanged, because μ_S ⊂ centre G_S. The comparison of the two obstruction maps should be computed on cocycles, not read off the exact sequence.

## Second-pass notes

**S01 — scan 232 (margin) — claim without proof — second-pass note.**
The margin claims that 𝒢 can be taken with two objects (identity and complex conjugation) and is splittable, even canonically split. A splitting exists because A₀ contains orientation-reversing involutions, so A₀ → {±1} has a section. This matters because H³(ℤ/2; ℤ/2) ≅ ℤ/2, so splitting is not automatic for such a group-category. Canonicity needs a preferred involution up to isotopy. One candidate is the antipodal map, which is central in O(3) ⊂ A₀ = Homéo(S²) and so gives a product decomposition. Another is complex conjugation, once the Riemann-sphere structure X₀ = P¹_ℂ is fixed.

**S02 — scan 233 — claim check, (63) — second-pass note.**
Entrywise conjugation σ of ℂ² is τ-linear, and Λ²σ is conjugation on Λ²ℂ² ≅ ℂ (det σ(v, w) is the conjugate of det(v, w)). So σ ∈ S̃l(2, ℂ), σ² = 1, and S̃l(2, ℂ) = Sl(2, ℂ) ⋊ ⟨σ⟩. No antilinear map fixes every line of ℂ²: if f(e₁) = a·e₁ and f(e₂) = a·e₂, then f(i e₁ + e₂) = a(−i e₁ + e₂), which is not a multiple of i e₁ + e₂. So the kernel of S̃l(2, ℂ) → G̃P(1, ℂ) is the kernel ±1 of the linear part, as (63) states.

**S03 — scan 234 (margin) — claim check — second-pass note.**
An ℝ-algebra automorphism of M₂(ℂ) preserves the centre ℂ, acting on it by id or by conjugation. After composing with entrywise conjugation it is ℂ-linear, hence inner (Skolem–Noether). So Aut_{ℝ-alg}(M₂(ℂ)) = PGL(2, ℂ) ⋊ ℤ/2, which acts on P¹(ℂ) by Möbius and anti-Möbius maps; this is G̃P(1, ℂ), as the margin says. The margin's consequence holds in this sense: twisted forms of M₂(ℂ) correspond to G̃P(1, ℂ)-torsors, which by (64) correspond to sphere bundles. Their centre is a form of ℂ, i.e. a quadratic extension of ℝ with no chosen isomorphism to ℂ, which is the sense of “extension quadratique non précisée de ℂ”.

**S04 — scan 238 — claim check, (69) — second-pass note.**
For G = S̃l(2, ℂ), G° = Sl(2, ℂ). Every continuous automorphism of the real Lie algebra 𝔰𝔩(2, ℂ) is ℂ-linear or ℂ-antilinear. The ℂ-linear ones are inner, since transpose-inverse is conjugation by [[0, 1], [−1, 0]] on SL₂. So Out(Sl(2, ℂ)) ≅ ℤ/2, generated by conjugation, which is not inner because inner automorphisms are holomorphic. Hence (69) Γ → Autext(G°) is an isomorphism here, confirming Grothendieck's “impression” for the semilinear model. For Ã₀ the analogous statement would need rigidity theorems for homeomorphism groups, which are not checked here.

**S05 — scans 236, 239 — claim check — second-pass note.**
A°₀ ≃ SO(3) ≅ ℝP³ = S³/±1, with universal cover S³. So (65) and (66) hold: Π₂ = 0 and Πᵢ(A°₀) ≅ Πᵢ(S³) for i ≥ 2, with Π₃ = ℤ. For G° simply connected (67), Π₂(G°) = 0 as for any Lie group or covering of A°₀, and Π₃(G°) = ℤ ≠ 0. So n = 2 is the largest admissible value. Then B_{G°} is 3-connected and H¹(S, G°) = 1 for CW-complexes of dimension ≤ 3, which justifies the margin “on peut prendre n = 2”.

**S06 — scans 237–238 — implication not pursued (hypothesis check) — second-pass note.**
Grothendieck's examples of G are “(Ã₀, ou G̃P(1, ℂ))” on scan 237 and “les deux cas qui nous occupent (G̃P(1, ℂ) et Ã₀)” on scan 238. G̃P(1, ℂ) does not satisfy (67): its identity component PGL(2, ℂ) ≃ SO(3) has Π₁ = ℤ/2, and its centre is trivial, so μ would be 1. The group in the matrix model that satisfies (67) is S̃l(2, ℂ), whose quotient by μ = ±1 is G̃P(1, ℂ) = ℋ, just as Ã₀/μ₂ = A₀. So in these two places Grothendieck seems to name ℋ where G is meant. The text is left as written.

**S07 — scan 231 — claim without proof — second-pass note.**
Reading Ladegaillerie instead of Teichmüller changes the attribution of the replacement description. The ∞-faithfulness claim (all types except (0,0)) rests on outside work (Ladegaillerie's thesis, which the witness text cites elsewhere in the manuscript). The arrows B_{D*} → B_u and Π_{D*} → Π_u are legible only in part. The claim should therefore not be restated as a theorem from these pages alone.


# ===== LM_P025 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P025 (scans 241–250): research notes, checked against the corrected text

Each first-pass note (R01–R19, I01–I06) is kept, corrected or dropped against `LM_P025_checked.tex`; the note text that follows the verdict is the checked version. Notes C01–C07 are new, from the second pass, and are labelled as such. “Unproved claim” means that no proof is given on these ten scans.

## First-pass notes

**R01 — Scan 241, l. 4–17. Kind: claim without proof / question. Verdict: corrected.** Reason: the first pass read “Soit encore”; the ink has “Sauf erreur on a”, so the author himself flags the identity as unchecked.
Checked note: The identity δ′(ξ′) = δ(ξ′) − ρ₀, introduced by “Sauf erreur on a”, reduces the lifting problem to the surjectivity of H¹(S, ℋ′_S) → H²(S, µ), after which Grothendieck writes “je ne m'en tire pas” and proposes to consult Giraud or Larry Breen. The switch to a Gr-champ ℋ̿ with invariants π̲₀ ≃ Γ, π̲₁ ≃ µ is a change of framework, not a proof.

**R02 — Scan 241, l. 18–21. Kind: claim without proof. Verdict: kept.** Reason: the corrected text supports it unchanged.
Checked note: H¹(S, ℋ̿) is identified with classes of maps S → Bℋ̿, a connected space with π₁ ≃ Γ and π₂ = µ, and the Postnikov class in H³(Γ, µ) is said to be zero (“est nulle”). No model of ℋ̿ or proof of the vanishing is given on these pages.

**R03 — Scan 242, l. 1–5. Kind: question. Verdict: kept.** Reason: unchanged by the corrections (only H⁰ became H° and “ext.” lost its doubt mark).
Checked note: The bracket “mais peut-être n'y a-t-il pas lieu de trop se raccrocher à cette hypothèse” ties the vanishing to the existence of an extension 𝒢 of ℋ by π₁(ℋ°) restricting to the universal extension of ℋ° by π₁(ℋ°). Whether the argument needs that extension as an object, or only the vanishing class, is left open.

**R04 — Scan 242, l. 6–15. Kind: claim without proof. Verdict: kept.** Reason: the corrections there (sss, est donné, the struck B in the second display) do not change the claim.
Checked note: Bℋ → Bℋ̿ is said to be an isomorphism on π_i for i ≤ 2, and for i ≤ n exactly when π_i(ℋ) = 0 for 2 ≤ i ≤ n − 1, which with n = 3 for ℋ = A₀ gives Hot(S, Bℋ) ⥲ Hot(S, Bℋ̿) for CW complexes of dimension ≤ n. The bound at the top dimension needs the usual connectivity argument, which is not written out.

**R05 — Scans 242–243, l. 16–20 and 1–5. Kind: claim without proof. Verdict: corrected.** Reason: the first pass read “Postnikoff, donc K” and left the second factor as π|µ; the ink has “Postnikoff-Sinh”, “K(π, 2)” and “dépendant”.
Checked note: If the Postnikoff-Sinh invariant K|k ∈ H³(Γ, µ) vanishes (and the action of Γ on µ is trivial), Bℋ̿ is said, “sauf erreur”, to be a product K(Γ,1) × K(π,2), the “identification” depending on a choice in H²(π₁, π₂) ≃ H²(Γ, µ); hence formula (71), H¹(S, ℋ) ⥲ H¹(S, Γ) × H²(S, µ) for dim S ≤ n. The quotation marks and the dependence on a choice mean that the splitting is not canonical; the text should not be promoted to a canonical isomorphism.

**R06 — Scan 243, l. 6–15. Kind: claim without proof. Verdict: corrected.** Reason: the first pass read “approximation 2-isotopique|homotopique”; the ink has “description 2-isotopique (et non ∞-isotopique)”, the wording of the §20 title in the author's table of contents.
Checked note: The classification of topological sphere bundles over CW complexes of dimension ≤ 3 is said to “marche[r] bel et bien”, and the restriction to dim ≤ 3 is attributed to having only a 2-isotopic, not ∞-isotopic, description of the category of topological 2-spheres. A proof would separate the statement on isomorphism classes from the statements on morphisms and homotopies.

**R07 — Scans 243–244, l. 16–19 and 1–9. Kind: claim without proof. Verdict: kept, with a correction of detail.** Reason: the insertion is “(non précisée)”, not “en principe”, and the m̄ (même) is now read without doubt.
Checked note: The inclusion G̃P(1, ℂ) ↪ A₀ is asserted to be a homotopy equivalence, inducing a bijection on H¹ and even an ∞-equivalence of the ∞-categories of torsors (conformal sphere bundles, or projective-line bundles over an ℝ-algebra “(non précisée)” isomorphic to ℂ, against topological sphere bundles). The passage from a homotopy equivalence of groups to the equivalence of torsor ∞-categories is not argued here.

**R08 — Scan 244, l. 10–19 and margin. Kind: claim without proof. Verdict: corrected.** Reason: the first pass read “invariant numérique”, “système d'orientations tordues” and “la ℝ-algèbre ℂ”; the ink has “(à isom. non unique près)”, “système T d'entiers tordus” and “la ℝ-extension ℂ”.
Checked note: The first invariant of a G̃P-bundle is a torsor (η|T) under (ℤ/2ℤ)_S, determined up to non-unique isomorphism by χ ∈ H¹(S, ℤ/2ℤ); the margin says this is a double covering and that its datum amounts to a system T of twisted integers on S. The torsor twists the ℝ-extension ℂ through the faithful action of ℤ/2ℤ, giving a locally constant quadratic extension C and the sheaf C̲, and so GP(1, ℂ)_S into GP(1, C̲). Functoriality of this twisting is assumed, not proved.

**R09 — Scan 245, l. 1–10. Kind: claim without proof. Verdict: kept.** Reason: the corrections (“via C̲”, “de ces relèvements”, Sl, µ₂) do not change it.
Checked note: The text passes between rank-4 Azumaya algebras over C̲, projective-line bundles over C̲ and GP(1, C̲)-torsors, then uses 1 → µ₂S → Sl(2, C̲_S) → GP(1, C̲_S) → 1 to attach to a torsor the fibred category of its liftings to Sl(2, C̲_S), “une gerbe 𝒢 liée par µ₂”. The local existence of liftings and the identification of the band are not proved.

**R10 — Scan 245, l. 11–15. Kind: claim without proof. Verdict: kept.** Reason: unchanged.
Checked note: The gerbe is called the second complete invariant, finer than its equivalence class ξ ∈ H²(S, µ₂) = H²(S, {±1}), which plays the role of a Brauer group. “Complete” needs a precise target 2-category and a reconstruction statement.

**R11 — Scans 245–246, l. 16–20 and 1–8. Kind: claim without proof. Verdict: corrected.** Reason: the parenthesis opens with “d'où”, not “c.à.d.”.
Checked note: An isomorphism of bundles with invariants (T, 𝒢), (T′, 𝒢′) defines T ≃ T′ (whence C̲_T ≃ C̲_{T′}, whence GP(1, C̲_T) ≃ GP(1, C̲_{T′})) and an equivalence of gerbes; a homotopy h_t from f to g gives f_T = g_T and h_* : f_𝒢 ⥲ g_𝒢, depending only on the homotopy class of h. This is announced as something “il faudrait expliciter”; composition and coherence are not checked.

**R12 — Scan 246, l. 9–20, and 247, l. 1. Kind: claim without proof. Verdict: corrected.** Reason: the target 2-category consists of pairs (T, 𝒢) of a system of twisted integers (not “orientations”) and a µ₂(ℂ)-gerbe.
Checked note: The 2-functor from G̃P(1, ℂ)-torsors to pairs (T, 𝒢) is said, “sauf erreur”, to be 2-faithful for dim S ≤ 4 and 3-faithful for dim S ≤ 3, where 3-faithful means that the map H¹(S, G̃P) → H¹(S, ℤ/2ℤ) × H²(S, µ₂), called injective, is surjective. Injectivity is asserted, not proved.

**R13 — Scan 247, l. 1–3. Kind: conjecture. Verdict: kept.** Reason: unchanged except “qu'elle” for “qu'il”.
Checked note: “On s'attend qu'elle soit 1-fidèle dès que dim S ≤ 5, 0-fidèle dès que dim S ≤ 6”, with “??” in the margin beside a vertical stroke. These bounds conflict with those derived on scan 248 (see C05) and should be treated as provisional.

**R14 — Scans 247–248, l. 3–21 and 1–15. Kind: claim without proof. Verdict: corrected in detail.** Reason: the definition of 2-faithfulness reads “de plus, pour toute φ : T ≃ T′, ψ_𝒢 : 𝒢 ≈→ 𝒢′”, and “ci-dessus” belongs to “si ci-dessus on a deux homotopies”.
Checked note: The n-faithfulness conditions are defined cumulatively (0: h_* = h′_* implies h ≃ h′; 1: also every isomorphism f_𝒢 → g_𝒢 is some h_*; 2: also every pair (φ, ψ_𝒢) comes from a bundle isomorphism; 3: also every pair (T, 𝒢) comes from a bundle) and then read, “il semblerait”, as injectivity or bijectivity on π₂, π₁, π₀ of the map Cont(S, Bℋ) → Cont(S, Bℋ̿). Base points and components of the mapping spaces are not specified.

**R15 — Scan 248, l. 16–22. Kind: question. Verdict: corrected.** Reason: the formula is π_i(Cont(S, B)) ≃ π₀(S × S^i, B), not Ho/Hot(S × S^i, B).
Checked note: From π_i(Cont(S, B)) ≃ π₀(S × S^i, B) the author is led to the “draconian” hypotheses dim(S × S²) ≤ 4 for 0-faithfulness and dim(S × S²) ≤ 3, i.e. dim S ≤ 1, for 1-faithfulness, with a question mark, and notes that this would imply 3-faithfulness. The page leaves the conflict with scans 246–247 open.

**R16 — Scan 249, l. 1–15. Kind: question. Verdict: corrected.** Reason: the ink has “homotopismes de f à g” and the abbreviation “quelc.”.
Checked note: For forms of the projective line over a field of finite type K over ℚ (or over any scheme S), the author does not know how to make the groupoid into a 2-category: for two isomorphisms f, g (= f ∘ u) one would have to define “homotopismes” from f to g, i.e. “homotopies” from a section u of a form G of GP(1)_S to the identity, or rather a substitute for their homotopy classes. The arithmetic meaning of “homotopie” is the open point.

**R17 — Scan 249, l. 15–25. Kind: conjecture. Verdict: kept.** Reason: unchanged.
Checked note: The surviving sentence proposes that the substitute is “sans doute un relèvement de u en une section du revêtement simplement connexe G̃ de G !”. The cancelled block between its parts is abandoned working text and should not be read into the proposal.

**R18 — Scan 250, l. 1–8. Kind: claim without proof. Verdict: kept.** Reason: unchanged (Sl for SL).
Checked note: The resulting description is rejected as not even 0-faithful: all automorphisms of ℙ¹_K coming from Sl(2, K) “seront identifiés à l'identité”. This gives a concrete test of what the proposal loses.

**R19 — Scan 250, l. 9–15. Kind: research programme. Verdict: kept.** Reason: unchanged except the comma after “choses”.
Checked note: The conceptual work around the “Brauer” themes is said to be unfinished: the category of Azumaya algebras of fixed degree (“ici 4”) “devrait être une 2-catégorie”. Scan 245 has “de rang 4”; see C04.

**I01 — Scans 242–243 (first pass's own idea). Kind: own idea. Verdict: kept.** Reason: the coefficient is now settled as K(π, 2) and the verb as “dépendant”, which strengthens the point.
Checked note: Compare two choices of the product decomposition of Bℋ̿ and record how the coordinates on H¹(S, ℋ̿) change, instead of fixing one splitting. This turns the author's caveat on the “identification” into an explicit change-of-coordinates statement.

**I02 — Scans 245–247 (first pass's own idea). Kind: own idea. Verdict: kept.** Reason: still apt.
Checked note: Test the 2-functor on a point, a circle and a 2-sphere, computing objects, equivalences and automorphisms of equivalences separately, before any general dimension argument.

**I03 — Scan 248, l. 16 (first pass's own idea). Kind: own idea. Verdict: corrected.** Reason: the notation is π₀, not Ho/Hot, so the doubt about the symbol is gone; the base-point point stands.
Checked note: As written, π_i(Cont(S, B)) ≃ π₀(S × S^i, B) identifies a based homotopy group with free classes of maps S × S^i → B; the based version is the set of classes relative to S × {pt}. With S a point and B having non-abelian π₁ the two already differ, so the comparison should be made relative.

**I04 — Scans 247–248 (first pass's own idea). Kind: own idea. Verdict: kept.** Reason: still apt; C05 carries it out in the simplest case.
Checked note: Replace the counting of dimensions by the relative lifting problem for B_α and its homotopy fibre, tracking existence and uniqueness level by level, and match each level with one of the 0- to 3-faithfulness conditions.

**I05 — Scans 249–250 (first pass's own idea). Kind: own idea. Verdict: kept.** Reason: still apt; see C06.
Checked note: Treat the cover G̃ → G as a source of explicit 2-morphisms instead of identifying the corresponding automorphisms with the identity, and test this against the Sl(2, K) example of scan 250.

**I06 — Scans 245 and 250 (first pass's own idea). Kind: own idea. Verdict: kept.** Reason: still apt.
Checked note: Compare the full automorphism data of a fixed-size Azumaya algebra with those kept by its lifting gerbe and by the gerbe's class in H², keeping the three levels apart.

## New notes (second pass)

**C01 — Scan 241, l. 5–9. Kind: claim without proof (second pass).** The identity δ′(ξ′) = δ(ξ′) − ρ₀ is the behaviour of the connecting map of the central extension 1 → µ → 𝒢 → ℋ → 1 under twisting by the torsor ξ₀ whose obstruction is ρ₀. Grothendieck marks it “Sauf erreur”; its sign depends on the convention for twisting and should be fixed before the reduction to surjectivity is used.

**C02 — Scans 241–243. Kind: implication not pursued (second pass).** In the case at hand Γ = π₀ and µ = π₁ of the 2-type are both ℤ/2 (µ = µ₂ on scan 245), and Aut(ℤ/2) is trivial, so the condition “(ainsi que l'action de Γ sur µ)” holds automatically; the only content is the vanishing of the Postnikoff-Sinh class in H³(ℤ/2, ℤ/2) ≅ ℤ/2, which is not automatic. The splittings of Bℋ̿ as K(Γ,1) × K(µ,2) that induce the identity on π₁ and π₂ form a torsor under [K(Γ,1), K(µ,2)] = H²(Γ, µ) ≅ ℤ/2, which is the dependence on a choice that the author notes.

**C03 — Scan 244, margin. Kind: claim without proof, checked (second pass).** The margin's statement that a (ℤ/2ℤ)-torsor amounts to a system of twisted integers holds because Aut(ℤ) = {±1} ≅ ℤ/2: a ℤ/2-torsor η gives the local system ℤ ×^{ℤ/2} η of rank one, and conversely the sheaf of generators of such a local system is a ℤ/2-torsor. This is the orientation local system when χ is the orientation character.

**C04 — Scans 245 and 250. Kind: inconsistency noted (second pass).** “Algèbres d'Azumaya de rang 4” (245) and “de degré fixé (ici 4)” (250) disagree: an Azumaya algebra of rank 4 has degree 2, which matches Sl(2) and µ₂ in the exact sequence of scan 245. The “4” on scan 250 is therefore the rank, and “degré” there is either a slip or used for rank.

**C05 — Scans 246–248. Kind: own idea (second pass).** For ℋ = G̃P(1, ℂ) one has π₂(ℋ) = 0 and π₃(ℋ) ≅ ℤ, so B_α is an isomorphism on π_i for i ≤ 3 and only surjective on π₄.
