By the standard connectivity count, maps of a relative CW pair of relative dimension d then correspond bijectively for d ≤ 3 and only surjectively for d = 4; applied to (S × S^i, S × pt) this gives injectivity on π₂ of the mapping spaces only for dim S ≤ 1, not for dim(S × S²) ≤ 4 as written for 0-faithfulness, and bijectivity on π₀ for dim S ≤ 3. This would explain both the “??” of scan 247 and the remark that 1-faithfulness “impliquerait alors la 3-fidélité”; it is a heuristic count, not a proof of the optimal bounds.

**C06 — Scans 249–250. Kind: implication not pursued (second pass).** For a form G of GP(1) = PGL₂ the simply connected cover G̃ is the corresponding form of Sl(2), so “un relèvement de u en une section de G̃” is the arithmetic counterpart of the liftings to Sl(2, C̲_S) that define the µ₂-gerbe on scan 245. Every automorphism coming from Sl(2, K) has such a lift, which is why scan 250 concludes that they are all identified with the identity.

**C07 — Scan 241 (and scan 240). Kind: transcription question affecting notation (second pass).** The letter of ξ₀, ξ′ here is read ζ in LM_P024 for the same sentence; the mathematics does not decide between them, but the edition must use one letter for the class in H¹(S, ℋ_S) and its twist across both packages.


# ===== LM_P026 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P026 (scans 251–260): research notes checked against the corrected transcription

The first pass's notes R01–R20 are checked against `LM_P026_checked.tex`. Each is kept, corrected or dropped. Notes S01–S07 are the second pass's own and are labelled as such. "Claim without proof" means only that these ten scans give no proof.

## First-pass notes

**R01. Scan 251. Claim without proof. Keep.**
The step "comme les normalisateurs de A°_{g,ν} et A°_{g,ν+1} … sont A_{g,ν} et A_{g,ν+1}" is asserted, not proved, and it selects A•_{g,ν} as their intersection. The corrected text also identifies the isotopically trivial part as "le sous-groupe formé des éléments de A_{g,ν} dont l'opération sur U_{g,ν} est isotope à l'identité". That clause was an insertion the first pass missed.

**R02. Scans 251–252. Claim without proof. Keep, with a small correction.**
The quotient A°_{g,ν}/A°_{g,ν+1} ≃ Ũ_{g,ν} is called "la fibration" and identified with the universal cover without proof. The condition now reads "dans le cas anabélien 2g+ν ≥ 3" (singular). The divisor A_{g,ν+1} in the running sentence still shows no circle.

**R03. Scans 252–254. Implication not pursued. Keep.**
"On voudrait décrire le groupe de tous les autom. de la fibration" is answered by building an action of Ã_{g,ν}. The pages do not show that this is the full automorphism group of the fibration.

**R04. Scan 253 d). Claim without proof. Correct.**
In the corrected reading the inserted phrase is "a priori d'une telle op.", attached to "l'existence", not "pourvu qu'une telle op.". The claim is therefore: continuity together with a) determines the operation uniquely, and the a priori existence of such an operation follows from π₁(A°_{g,ν}) = 0. How a lifted continuous group action is obtained from simple connectivity is not argued.

**R05. Scans 253–254. First-pass model's own idea. Keep.**
Fixing left and right conventions remains useful. The text is internally consistent: b) asks for "translation à droite par v⁻¹", and the computation int(v)∘(left translation by v⁻¹) ends with x ↦ xv⁻¹ ("Ouf !").

**R06. Scan 255 margin. Claim without proof. Keep.**
"Structure C^{ν|r} avec … ≥ 2 suffirait" is unproved, and the exponent is still unresolved in the ink.

**R07. Scan 255. Claim without proof. Keep, sharpened.**
E_g is said to be ∞-connected as a quotient of an ∞-connected space by an ∞-connected group. The inserted "(un convexe)" gives the reason for the space of Riemannian structures. No argument is given for the quotient map, for instance as a principal fibration.

**R08. Scan 255. First-pass model's own idea. Keep.**
The proposal stands: normalise each conformal class by a fixed volume form and contract. It is labelled as the first-pass model's idea, not the author's.

**R09. Scans 255–256. Implication not pursued. Keep.**
The quotient by A_g gives curves "modulo passage à la complexe conjuguée", while (78) uses A_g⁺. The residual involution is not studied.

**R10. Scan 256. Implication not pursued. Keep.**
The coarse spaces M^♮ are "trop grossiers pour les usages géométriques les plus importants". The superscript sign is now read as ♮ or ♯ (both witnesses print ♮).

**R11. Scan 257. Claim without proof. Correct.**
The theorem is stated "(dans les cas anabélien 2g+ν ≥ 3)". The first pass's "de la condition" was a misreading. The δ-rule is a general statement, "et plus généralement δ augmente de 1 chaque fois …", not a phrase "cas plus généraux". The theorem gives a homeomorphism with ℂ^μ, not a biholomorphism.

**R12. Scans 257–258. First-pass model's own idea. Correct.**
The suggested case table is carried out in S01, and the displayed values, the δ-rule and the marginal ν′ formula agree. The text says "les seuls trois cas … avec le cas anabélien (0,3)". This is consistent if *trois* counts the listed non-anabelian cases. The witnesses' "4 cas" is an editorial change.

**R13. Scan 258. Claim without proof. Keep, with the corrected wording.**
"Dans le cas anabélien" (singular): freeness of A°_{g,ν} on E_g and the torsor structure are asserted. No local triviality or slice is given.

**R14. Scans 258–259. Claim without proof. Keep.**
(83) and the equivalence of ∞-connectedness of M̃_{g,ν} and of A°_{g,ν} need E_g contractible and the torsor to be locally trivial. The index of the right-hand π in (83) is still unresolved in the ink.

**R15. Scan 259. Development explicitly omitted. Keep.**
"Je n'insiste pas ici" sets aside the rigidification of Teichmüller and the complex-manifold structure.

**R16. Scan 259. Author's expectation, not a formal conjecture. Correct.**
The corrected closing reads: the simple connectedness "est bien profond, qui ne semble pas pouvoir rentrer dans le cadre de la topologie (ou de la topologie différentielle (C∞)) sans plus…" (the second closing parenthesis is not visible in the ink). So the expectation concerns topology, or differential topology, without further input, not "topologie différentiable pure". The parenthesis before it now reads "ce qui peut exprimer en interprétant M̃_{g,ν} comme rev. universel du topos modulaire M_{g,ν}".

**R17. Scan 259. First-pass model's own idea. Keep.**
Comparing with the action groupoid stays apt. The text says "rev. universel du topos modulaire M_{g,ν}", not of the coarse space M^♮. CSG/IUT misprint this as U_{g,ν}.

**R18. Scan 260. Claim without proof. Keep.**
(85) says "une fibration en fibre A°_{g,ν}/A°_{g,ν+1} ≃ Ũ_{g,ν}", for (g,ν) anabelian. The first pass read "de fibre". Local triviality of M̃_{g,ν+1} → M̃_{g,ν} is not proved.

**R19. Scans 259–260. Implication not pursued. Correct.**
The base cases are "M̃_g si g ≥ 2, M_{1,1} et M_{0,3} si g = 1 ou 0". In the ink a ">" is superposed on the "=" and crossed, and a *g* before "1,1" is cancelled. These are slips under correction, not new conditions. The reduction along the tower is formal once (85) is a fibration with contractible fibre.

**R20. Scan 260. Claim with a partial argument. Correct.**
The ink says the structures are *congrues* (not *conjuguées*) under A°_{g,ν}, and "elles sont = congrues" (= stands for *même(s)*) under A°_{g,ν+1}. The proof writes u = gv with g in the identity component of the complex automorphisms of U_{g,ν} for β. The existence of this factorisation, which amounts to G acting transitively on the fibre in the abelian case, is not justified.

## Second-pass notes (my own)

**S01. Scans 257–258. Own check.**
The case values agree with the stated rules. δ = dim_ℂ Aut°: (1,0) → 1, (0,2) → 1, (0,1) → 2, (0,0) → 3. With μ = 3g−3+(ν+δ) these give μ = 1, 0, 0, 0, matching the display. The rule "δ augmente de 1 … (g,ν−1) abélien" gives δ(0,1) = 2 and δ(0,0) = 3, and the marginal ν′ form gives the same μ.

**S02. Scan 258. Own remark on an internal tension.**
"À partir de … M̃_{g,0} avec g ≥ 1, quand ν augmente les dimensions complexes augmentent d'autant" is not true for the step (1,0) → (1,1), which is abelian. The very next parenthesis ("Par contre M̃_{1,0} ⟸ M̃_{1,1} est un homéomorphisme") makes that exception explicit. It agrees with (85), M̃_{g,ν+1} ≃ M̃_{g,ν} for abelian (g,ν).

**S03. Scan 253 margin. Claim without proof.**
"L'opération de A°_{g,ν}×1 se décrit le plus simplement par translations de A°_{g,ν} sur l'espace homogène Ũ_{g,ν} de A°_{g,ν}" uses (73), Ũ_{g,ν} ≃ A°_{g,ν}/A°_{g,ν+1}. It inherits the unproved identification of R02.

**S04. Scan 252 NB. Claim without proof.**
"Ã°_{g,ν} = A°_{g,ν} et π₀(Ã_{g,ν}) ≃ Γ′_{g,ν+1}" is read off the second extension in (75). It needs A°_{g,ν} connected and the topology on the fibre product (74). Neither is discussed on these pages.

**S05. Scan 252. Reading consistency.**
A tilde stands above the U in "Π_{g,ν} = π₁(U_{g,ν}, a_{g,ν})". Read literally as π₁(Ũ, a), the group would be trivial. So either the mark is stray or it is a slip, and the transcription keeps both readings under `\unsure`.

**S06. Scan 258. Implication not pursued.**
The margin note "i.e. sur surfaces (orientées?) sphériques à ν ≤ 3, toutes les structures complexes sont isotopes" is the geometric content of μ = 0 in genus 0. It is the basis of the later reduction to M_{0,3}. No argument is given here.

**S07. Scan 260. Claim without proof.**
Inside the bracket, "il est donc à fibre contractile" relies on Ũ_{g,ν} being contractible as the universal cover of a hyperbolic (anabelian) surface. This is used but not stated as a separate fact on these pages.


# ===== LM_P027 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P027 (scans 261–270): research notes, checked against the corrected transcription

Each first-pass note (R01–R24) is kept, corrected or dropped, with the reason. Notes S01–S08 are the second pass's own. Quotations follow `LM_P027_checked.tex`.

## First-pass notes

**R01. Scan 261. Claim without proof.** Keep, with additions. The author calls the cases g = 0, 1 "assez évident" and the case g ≥ 2 "profond". The genus-0 argument uses two unproved inputs: that A_0^0 consists exactly of the automorphisms of X_0 ≃ S² that preserve orientation, and the chain M̃_{3,0} ⥲ M̃_{2,0} ≃ M̃_{1,0} ≃ M̃_{00}. The ink gives the indices as (3,0), (2,0), (1,0), (0,0), although the conclusion concerns M_{0,i} (i ≤ 3). They should be read against the earlier definitions, not reversed silently.

**R02. Scans 261–262. Claim without proof.** Correct. The first pass dropped the hypothesis and the quantifier. The text says that *any* ("tout") ∞-connected space E on which A_g acts, with A_{g,ν}^0 acting freely, lets one interpret the canonical extension Γ'_{g,ν+1} of Γ_{g,ν} by Π_{g,ν} as a mixed fundamental group. It says this "(cas (g,ν) anabéliens)", not "considérés". The comparison with the earlier extension Ã_{g,ν} of Γ'_{g,ν+1} by A_{g,ν}^0, with Ã_{g,ν} → A_{g,ν} of kernel ≃ Π_{g,ν}, is announced but not carried out.

**R03. Scan 262. Claim without proof.** Keep. The twist of U_{g,ν} by the A_{g,ν}^0-torsor E → M̃ gives the bundle 𝔛 ≃ (U_{g,ν} × E)/A_{g,ν}^0 with diagonal action. Its local triviality and the residual Γ_{g,ν}-action are asserted, not constructed. So is the identification, for E = E_g, with the universal family with Teichmüller rigidification.

**R04. Scan 263. Claim without proof.** Keep. From "E et A_{g,ν}^0 sont ∞-connexes" the text infers that M̃ is ∞-connected and that the fibre inclusion is "un homotopisme". This needs E → M̃ to be a principal bundle, or at least a fibration, so that the homotopy exact sequences apply. As the first pass said, "∞-connexe" should not be replaced by "contractible" without checking the spaces.

**R05. Scan 263. Detail omitted by the author.** Keep, updated. The reading "c'est bien essentiellement Γ'_{g,ν}" is now settled ("inévitablement" is excluded), and the author writes "je me dispense de donner le détail". The index differs from Γ'_{g,ν+1} on scans 261–262. Whether this is a slip or a different object should be settled from the section where Γ'_{g,ν+1} is defined.

**R06. Scan 264. Claim without proof (attributed).** Correct. The 2-category of admissible surfaces is introduced "comme des|ds Ladegaillerie": the first pass left the name illegible, and it attributes the construction to Ladegaillerie's work. Its 2-morphisms are homotopy classes of paths from f to g in Isom(U,U'); the ink has "de f de|dans g", which the witnesses normalise to "de f à g". The components of Isom(U,U') are said to be torsors under products of groups A_{g,ν}^0 with (g,ν) ≠ (0,0), "donc ce sont des K(π,1)". See S03.

**R07. Scan 264. Implication not pursued.** Keep. Admissibility (U = X∖S, X compact, S finite, every genus-0 component of X meets S) is the author's own condition. It is exactly what rules out the case (0,0) in the K(π,1) assertion, so it should not be replaced by an outside stability inequality.

**R08. Scans 264–265. Claim without proof.** Keep. "On trouve un 2-foncteur" from admissible surfaces to morphisms B_{D*} → B_U of multigaloisian topoi, or of groupoids, is asserted. The construction of D* and of the functor on 1- and 2-morphisms is not given on these scans.

**R09. Scan 265. Claim without proof.** Keep, with additions. The 1-morphisms are "diagrammes ess.|en. comm. de morphismes de topos (ou de foncteurs de groupoïdes)". The curved arrow in the square is marked α ∼, so the commutativity datum is an isomorphism. The morphisms between such data are "définis ad hoc", so the coherence conditions are left open (see S04).

**R10. Scan 266. Claim without proof.** Keep, with one point settled by type. The cofibred groupoid Π → Δ_1 has Ob Π = Ob Π_{D*} ⨿ Ob Π_U, and its arrows from D̃* to Ũ are the isomorphisms ρ_!(D̃*) ⥲ Ũ. Their home is written over (Π_{B_U} or Π_{B_{D*}}), but only Π_U = Π_{B_U} fits, since Ũ ∈ Ob Π_U. Identities, composition and the claimed cocartesian property still have to be written out.

**R11. Scans 266–267. Implication not pursued.** Correct. The text reads "une cat. fibrée B telle que les cat. fibres soient des topos, et les foncteurs de ch^t de base soit exact à gauche et commutent aux lim→ quelconq."; the first pass had "sont … sont". The fibred description through the inverse images ρ^* must be matched with the cofibred groupoid description without reversing the direction of geometric morphisms. The lim-arrow reading remains doubtful (see S06).

**R12. Scan 267. Claim without proof.** Correct. The author writes "on sait décrire l'image 2-essentielle par la condition « structure à lacets »" (first pass: "On peut"). This presents the description as known rather than possible, and names a "sous-2-catégorie 2-pleine" ℳ_lac (objets admissibles). The intrinsic conditions on objects, 1-morphisms and 2-morphisms are not listed here.

**R13. Scan 267, margin. Excluded example.** Keep. "NB on exclut par exemple B_{D*} ≃ « topos vide », B_U ≃ « topos ponctuel », i.e. Π_{D*} = ∅, Π_U ≃ cat. ponctuelle": the closed sphere, with no punctures, is the excluded case. This matches the admissibility condition of scan 264.

**R14. Scan 268. Claim without proof.** Keep, with an addition. "On sait que le foncteur est 2-fidèle, donc induit une équivalence de 2-catégories 𝒮 → ℳ_lac" (not "2-équivalence" as in the witnesses). Being presented as known, the 2-fidelity most likely rests on the work cited on scan 264 (Ladegaillerie). The author's meaning of "2-fidèle" should be fixed before it is translated into modern terms.

**R15. Scan 268, diagonal margin. Implication not pursued.** Correct, since the note is now partly legible. When B_{D*} is the empty topos over B_U, no extra datum, "un iso. T ≃ H²(U_i, Z) pour tte composante connexe", has to be given, because one does not assume that one works with orientation-preserving automorphisms (reading "Automorph.|structures" … "orientation"). The author adds that in the arithmetic analogue this will no longer be so. The opening words and two cancelled lines remain unread (see S07).

**R16. Scan 268. Claim without proof.** Keep. A morphism of multigaloisian topoi with Γ-action "équivaut à celle d'un morphisme de topos multigaloisiens B_{D*,Γ} → B_{U,Γ} → B_Γ". The proof should say whether the action is strict or coherent, and should compare 1- and 2-morphisms, not only objects.

**R17. Scan 269. Implication not pursued.** Keep. Replacing Γ by a groupoid Π "jouant le rôle d'un groupoïde fondamental" is motivated by "quand on fait varier Γ". This invites a study of functoriality in the base. The margin note "Rigidité de « représentations » (de groupes|groupoïdes)" marks rigidity as the issue.

**R18. Scans 269–270. Claim without proof.** Correct the wording: the text has "la famille de ceux-ci" (first pass "l'ensemble"). The family of diagrams (**) with the actions of Γ_i = Aut_{B_{Π_i}} B̃_{Π_i} "permet de récupérer la situation complète". The descent argument, independence of the chosen universal coverings, and compatibility on morphisms are not given.

**R19. Scan 270. Definition with unverified properties.** Correct. Admissibility of (*) is required of "les ρ_i (or f_i) dans (**)", that is, of the arrows B_{D*_i} → B_{U_i}. The first pass read "fibres|fibrés" here. So ℳ_lac's notion of admissibility is applied fibrewise to the arrows, and the first pass's caveat about a doubtful noun no longer applies. Independence of choices and closure under isomorphism remain to be checked.

**R20. Scan 270. Conjecture (incomplete on these scans).** Keep, updated. The author restricts to the full sub-2-categories ("catégories pleines") of base groupoids Π with finite fundamental groups, proposes to study the fidelity properties of R_fS → R_fM̃_lac, and begins "Je conjecture que c'est une". The continuation is on scan 271. The witnesses read "une équivalence de 2-catégorie i.e. qu'il est 3-fidèle", which is not verified on these images.

**R21. Scans 261–263. First-pass model's idea.** Keep. Model the mixed fundamental group by pairs (element of the acting group, homotopy class of a path from a base point to its image), and compare with Ã_{g,ν} → A_{g,ν}. The test should be done under the anabelian hypothesis restored on scan 261, where A_{g,ν}^0 is contractible.

**R22. Scans 264 and 267. First-pass model's idea.** Keep. Test the admissibility conditions on arrows with empty source, the excluded example of the margin of 267, before using the "structure à lacets" axioms. The margin of 268 shows that this degenerate case is where orientation data would enter.

**R23. Scans 265–266 and 268. First-pass model's idea.** Keep, low confidence. Look for distinct equivariant comparison data α that become identified in the target diagrams, on a small groupoid example. The morphisms between data are only "définis ad hoc", so this is a test of the missing conventions, not yet an obstruction.

**R24. Scans 269–270. First-pass model's idea.** Keep. For the finite-base restriction, test with Π = [Z/n] acting trivially and non-trivially, and follow (**) and the deck actions. S08 identifies what the object-level part of the conjecture contains.

## Second-pass notes (mine)

**S01. Scan 261. Claim without proof (second pass).** "A_0^0 est simplement formé des autom. de X_0 ≃ S² qui conservent l'orientation" says that the group of orientation-preserving homeomorphisms of S² is connected. This is a classical theorem (Kneser: it deformation-retracts onto SO(3)), used here without reference. The isotopy of any two complex structures on S², and hence the conclusion that the M_{0,i} (i ≤ 3) are points, depends on it.

**S02. Scan 264. Claim attributed, not proved (second pass).** The words "comme des|ds Ladegaillerie" make Y. Ladegaillerie's work the source of the 2-category 𝒮, and probably of the 2-fidelity "on sait" on scan 268. In the witnesses' later text (CSG l. 5499) the "résultat déjà connu (Ladegaillerie)" is stated for Γ = 1. The equivariant statements of scans 268–270 therefore go beyond the cited result and should be marked as the author's extension.

**S03. Scan 264. Check of a claim (second pass).** "Donc ce sont des K(π,1)" needs each A_{g,ν}^0 with (g,ν) ≠ (0,0) to be aspherical. This holds in the anabelian cases (contractible identity components) and in the cases (0,1), (0,2), (1,0) (a circle, a circle, a torus), by classical results (Kneser, Hamstrom, Earle–Eells) to be checked against the literature. The exclusion (0,0) is necessary, since A_0^0 ≃ SO(3) is not a K(π,1). Admissibility is what removes (0,0).

**S04. Scan 265. Type check (second pass).** In α: ρ f_{D*} ≃ f_U ρ', we have f_{D*}: B_{D'*} → B_{D*} and f_U: B_{U'} → B_U, so both composites run B_{D'*} → B_U. The formula type-checks, and it fixes the direction of 1-morphisms (from the primed object to the unprimed one). The ∼ on the curved arrow makes α invertible, as a "donnée de commutativité" requires. What "définis ad hoc" leaves open is the 2-morphisms between pairs (f, α).

**S05. Scan 266. Type check (second pass).** The cross arrows D̃* → Ũ of the cofibred groupoid are isomorphisms ρ_!(D̃*) ⥲ Ũ, so they must live in Π_U. This decides the overwritten "Π_{B_U}" on mathematical grounds, although not on the ink. All arrows are cocartesian because the fibres are groupoids, as the text states.

**S06. Scan 267. Implication not pursued (second pass).** "Fibres topoi; change-of-base functors left exact and commuting with arbitrary colimits" characterises inverse-image functors of geometric morphisms, since such a functor between topoi has a right adjoint. The further left adjoint ρ_! in "ρ_! ρ^* ρ_*" is extra: it expresses that morphisms of multigaloisian topoi are essential. The fibred-topos description of 267 does not encode this by itself, so ℳ as "morphismes de topos multigaloisiens" needs it added.

**S07. Scan 268, margin. Implication not pursued (second pass).** With B_{D*} the empty topos, there are no punctures and each U_i is a closed surface with H²(U_i, Z) ≅ Z, so an iso T ≃ H²(U_i, Z) is an orientation. T is presumably the coefficient module of the earlier sections. In an arithmetic setting the corresponding H² carries a Tate twist (cyclotomic character), so the datum would no longer be a free choice; this is plausibly the meaning of "il n'en sera|est plus de m̄". The author does not pursue it.

**S08. Scans 269–270. Content of the conjecture (second pass).** For Π = [Γ] with Γ finite, essential surjectivity of R_fS → R_fM̃_lac on objects says that every admissible diagram B_{D*,Γ} → B_{U,Γ} → B_Γ comes from an action of Γ on an admissible surface. This is a group extension of Γ by a surface group with loop structure being realised geometrically: the Nielsen realisation problem for finite groups. It was solved in general by Kerckhoff (Ann. of Math. 1983), after this manuscript; Nielsen had treated the cyclic case and Fenchel the solvable case. The conjecture adds the 2-categorical fidelity on top of this, and that part is not covered by those results.


# ===== LM_P028 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P028 (scans 271–280): research notes checked against the corrected transcription

Each first-pass note (R01–R20) is checked against `LM_P028_checked.tex` and kept, corrected or dropped. Quotations follow the corrected text. Notes M01–M09 are the second pass's own, labelled as such. None of the first-pass notes is dropped.

## First-pass notes

### R01 — keep
**Scan 271 · claim to be proved.** The paragraph ends "A prouver que α et β sont homotopes": two paths α, β from f to g in Isom_Γ(U,U') that induce the same isomorphisms on the topossic diagram B_{D*,Γ} → B_{U,Γ} → B_Γ should be homotopic. Scan 272 reduces this to g = id, U = U_{g,ν}, f ∈ A^Γ_{g,ν}, and then to injectivity of π_1(A^Γ) → π_1(A). No proof is given in this package.

### R02 — correct (attribution now legible)
**Scan 272 · claim without proof.** The boxed "π_1(A^Γ) → π_1(A) est injectif" is derived from the case Γ = 1. The parenthesis attributes that case to Ladegaillerie (ink "Ladegallerie"); the first pass had it as illegible. Grothendieck then uses π_1(A) = 0 "(cas (g,ν) anabélien)" to reduce the claim to π_1(A^Γ) = 0. The exact statement of Ladegaillerie's result is not on the page and should be identified in the literature before the reduction is relied on.

### R03 — keep
**Scan 273 · provisional claim.** 1-faithfulness is stated as "∀ isomorphisme entre B_f et B_g provient d'un chemin de f à g". After the reductions it becomes an isomorphism of f* : (B_{D*} → B_U) → (B_{D*} → B_U) with the identity (the corrected text has "d'où f*" here). The sentence breaks off at "sauf erreur cette condition", and the rest of the page is blank. CSG and IUT silently end it at "l'identité."

### R04 — correct (notation)
**Scan 274 · claim without proof.** The Gr-category A̲ has the naive fundamental groupoid of A as underlying category and composition induced by A × A → A, with invariants π_0, π_1 and the action of π_0 on π_1. The page says that "l'associativité dans A̲ = ΠA est stricte"; the ink has Π without index, where the first pass read Π_1. Strictness holds because the product of points and of path classes is computed pointwise from the associative multiplication of A. The recovery of the π_0-action on π_1 is not written out.

### R05 — correct (quotation)
**Scan 274 · claim without proof.** Corrected quotation: "tout torseur sous A définit un 1-torseur sous la gr-catégorie A̲"; the first pass had "sur … sur". The relative version for a space S, or a topos locally a space, gives an A̲_S-champ which is a champ of A_S-torsors. No construction of the gluing or descent data is given.

### R06 — correct (reading of the levels)
**Scans 274–275 · claim without proof.** With the corrected reading the statement is: if π_i(A) = 0 for i ≥ 2, then for every n ∈ N, n ≥ 2, there is an n-équivalence (the n before "équivalence" is uncertain) between the n-category of torsors on S with group A_S and the n-category deduced from the 2-category of (gerbes?) A̲_S-torsors by extending it discretely. My reading in modern terms: A is a homotopy 1-type, so its torsors are classified by its Gr-category. The page gives no proof, and the inserted word read "gerbes" is unresolved.

### R07 — correct (formula)
**Scan 275 · claim without proof.** The display reads Transp_A(u,v) = {g ∈ A | v = int(g).u}; the first pass dropped the parentheses. The claim that the components of the 2-category correspond to conjugacy classes of homomorphisms Γ → A relies on every A-torsor over a point being trivial (see M05). Continuity of the action and the topology of Transp are not specified.

### R08 — keep
**Scan 276 · claim without proof.** The 0-isotopic and 1-isotopic categories replace Transp(u,v) by its π_0 and by its Π_1. Then Aut(u) ≃ Π_1 Trans(u), with Trans(u,u) = A^u the centraliser of u, and Isom(u,v) is empty "(si u,v ne sont conjugués)" or a torsor under Aut(u). The torsor claim comes from Transp(u,v), when non-empty, being a right A^u-torsor under multiplication. Compatibility of composition with paths and homotopies is not written out.

### R09 — correct (quotation)
**Scan 277 · question / programme.** Corrected quotation: "On aimerait encore exprimer des conditions pour que ce[t] 2-foncteur soit une 2-équivalence"; the first pass had "avoir exprimées". This is an explicit request for a criterion. Scans 278–280 answer it only in the simplified case π_1 = 0.

### R10 — keep
**Scans 277–278 · implication not pursued.** The text asks for the classification of equivalence classes of objects of the target 2-category, which are "de nature purement algébrique" in terms of A̲ and Γ. It identifies an action of Γ on the trivial A̲-torsor with a morphism of Gr-categories from the discrete Gr-category Γ to A̲. The classification beyond trivial torsors is not carried out.

### R11 — keep
**Scan 278 · open definition.** Hom(u,v) is to be a category, not only a set, defined as a sub-category Transp(u,v) of A̲ "à définir ad-hoc". The definition is left open on these pages.

### R12 — keep
**Scan 278 · simplification stated without proof.** Under π_1 = 0, A̲ "se réduit à π_0", and homomorphisms Γ → A̲ reduce to homomorphisms Γ → π_0. For a Gr-category with trivial π_1 this is an equivalence with the discrete Gr-category π_0. Everything on scans 279–280 is done under this hypothesis, as the insertion "si π_1(A^0) = 0" on scan 280 recalls (M08).

### R13 — keep
**Scan 279 · claim without proof.** 0-faithfulness holds if and only if π_1(A^u) = 0 for every u, and the NB rewrites the condition as π_1((A^0)^u) = 0 because A^u and (A^0)^u have the same identity component. The NB is valid when π_1 is taken at the identity, since π_1 of a topological group at 1 depends only on its identity path component. The superscript u of the first A^u is written over another letter.

### R14 — correct (scope of the insertion)
**Scan 279 · claim without proof.** 1-faithfulness: α, β ∈ Transp_A(u,v) with the same image in Transp_{π_0}(u̲,v̲) lie in the same component. Reducing to u = v gives injectivity of π_0(A^u) → π_0 and the boxed condition π_0((A^0)^u) = 0; M06 checks these steps. Correction: only "le sous-groupe ouvert" is interlinear, and the formula (A^u) ∩ A^0 = (A^0)^u stands in the line. The cancelled first draft of the argument remains largely illegible.

### R15 — correct (reading secured)
**Scan 280 · claim without proof.** 2-faithfulness: if u̲, v̲ are conjugate (or equal), then u, v are already conjugate, so a lift of u̲, if it exists, is unique up to conjugacy. The margin strengthens this to "Transp(u,v) → Transp(u̲,v̲) surjectif", equivalently "si u̲ = v̲, alors u et v sont conjugués par un élément de A^0". The reading is secure apart from the opening words; there is no "est" before "surjectif". M07 proves the stated equivalence, and M09 interprets the interlinear "(par un él^t de π̲_0)".

### R16 — keep
**Scan 280 · claim without proof.** Summary: if π_1(A^0) = 0, then 3-faithfulness holds if and only if every u̲ : Γ → π_0 = A/A^0 lifts to u : Γ → A, uniquely up to conjugacy, and (A^0)^u is then connected and simply connected. The inserted hypothesis belongs to the summary's scope. The sentence ends with a comma at the bottom of scan 280, so its continuation lies outside this package.

### R17 — keep
**Scans 278–280 · own idea (first pass).** Discrete A: A^0 = 1, π_0 = A, π_1 = 0. Every u̲ lifts uniquely and (A^0)^u = 1, so the criterion predicts a 2-equivalence, as it should. This is only a check of the indexing.

### R18 — keep, with a remark
**Scans 279–280 · own idea (first pass).** Take A = (R,+), Γ = Z and u_t(n) = nt. Then (A^0)^u = R is connected and simply connected, all u_t have the same image in π_0 = 1, and the u_t are pairwise non-conjugate. So 0- and 1-faithfulness hold and 2-faithfulness fails, in agreement with the written criterion. The example lies outside the intended case, where Γ is finite ("fini s'il le faut", scan 271) and A = A_{g,ν}.

### R19 — keep
**Scan 279 · own idea (first pass).** The ink confirms "le sous-groupe ouvert". For A = (Z/2)^N with the product topology, A^0 = {1} is not open, so openness of (A^u) ∩ A^0 in A^u needs a local hypothesis on A (local connectedness), which the page does not state. For the intended A_{g,ν} it should follow from the local contractibility of homeomorphism groups of compact manifolds (Černavskiĭ; Edwards–Kirby); the punctured case still has to be checked. As M06 shows, the connectedness criterion itself does not need openness.

### R20 — keep
**Scans 277–278 · own idea (first pass).** Compare a coherent action of Γ on an A̲-torsor, given by a morphism of Gr-categories Γ → A̲ with its coherence data, with the strict actions that come from homomorphisms Γ → A. This is the gap that "à définir ad-hoc" leaves open. The pages give no coherence equations and no obstruction classes.

## Second-pass notes (mine)

### M01 — conjecture (Grothendieck's), continued from scan 270
**Scan 271 · conjecture.** The package opens inside the conjecture of the preceding scan: the 2-functor from Γ-actions on surfaces (groupoids Π_f with finite fundamental groups) to the topossic/groupoid data is an "équivalence de 2-cat, i.e. qu'il est 3 fidèle". The 0-, 1-, 2- and 3-faithfulness analysis on scans 271–280 tests this conjecture, first for A_{g,ν} and then for an abstract topological group A.

### M02 — claim without proof
**Scan 272 · claim without proof.** "en fait on voit qu'elle est équivalente pour Γ, et pour son sous-groupe 1": the hypothesis on α, β (equality of the induced isomorphisms) is asserted to be the same with or without the Γ-action, with no argument. A proof would compare the natural transformations on the Γ-equivariant fundamental groupoids with their restrictions to the ordinary ones.

### M03 — claim without proof (literature to check)
**Scan 272 · claim without proof.** "Dans le cas π_1(A) = 0 (cas (g,ν) anabélien)": the vanishing of π_1 of the homeomorphism group A_{g,ν} in the anabelian case is used without proof. It agrees with the classical results on homeomorphism groups of hyperbolic surfaces (identity component contractible; Hamstrom). These should be cited for the punctured case that is actually used here.

### M04 — implication not pursued (own idea)
**Scans 271 and 274 · own idea.** The exclusion (g,ν) ≠ (0,0) on scan 271 fits the hypothesis π_i(A) = 0 for i ≥ 2 on scan 274. Homeo(S²) has the homotopy type of O(3) (Kneser), and π_3(O(3)) ≠ 0. For the other types, to my knowledge, the identity component is contractible or of the homotopy type of a circle or a torus. The pages do not say whether this is the reason for the exclusion, and the classical results should be checked before this is used.

### M05 — claim without proof, checked
**Scan 275 · claim without proof.** "Ces objets forment une 2-catégorie, dont les composantes connexes correspondent aux classes de conjugaison d'homomorphismes de Γ dans A". This holds because every A-torsor over a point is trivial, and an action of Γ on 1_A that commutes with the A-action is left multiplication through a homomorphism u. Morphisms (1_A,u) → (1_A,v) are then the elements of Transp(u,v). Continuity of u is part of the data and is not discussed.

### M06 — own check of the 1-faithfulness reduction
**Scan 279 · own idea.** If α ∈ Transp_A(u,v), left multiplication by α^{-1} maps Transp_A(u,v) homeomorphically onto A^u, compatibly with the maps to the π_0-transporters, so the case (u,v) reduces to (u,u) ("OPS u = v"). Each component of A^u lies in A^0 or misses it, so A^u ∩ A^0 is a union of components of A^u. The kernel of π_0(A^u) → π_0(A) is therefore trivial if and only if A^u ∩ A^0 = (A^0)^u is connected, which needs no openness. If π_0 means path components, as in the isotopic categories, the same argument runs with path components, and "connexe" should be read as path-connected.

### M07 — own check of the marginal equivalence
**Scan 280 · own idea.** The margin's equivalence holds when both statements are taken for all pairs (u,v). If every Transp(u,v) → Transp(u̲,v̲) is surjective and u̲ = v̲, a lift of 1 is an element of A^0 conjugating u to v. Conversely, given c ∈ Transp(u̲,v̲), pick a ∈ A over c: int(a).u and v have the same image in π_0, so v = int(g).int(a).u for some g ∈ A^0, and g·a lies over c.

### M08 — own check of the inserted hypothesis
**Scan 280 · own idea.** The inserted "si π_1(A^0) = 0" restates the standing assumption π_1 = 0 of scan 278, since π_1(A) at the identity equals π_1(A^0). The insertion thus marks the summary as proved only in the simplified case. According to the witnesses, the next scan turns to the general case.

### M09 — question on the interlinear "(par un él^t de π̲_0)"
**Scan 280 · question.** In "u,v sont déjà conjugués (par un él^t de π̲_0)", u and v take values in A, so they cannot be conjugated by an element of π_0. The addition presumably refers to the given conjugator of u̲, v̲ in π_0, which should lift to a conjugator of u, v. That is exactly the stronger formulation the margin then writes as surjectivity of Transp(u,v) → Transp(u̲,v̲). This is my interpretation; the page does not spell it out.


# ===== LM_P029 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P029 (scans 281–290): research notes checked against the corrected transcription

Basis: `second_pass/LM_P029_checked.tex`. FP-1 … FP-50 are the first pass's research notes, numbered as in
section 5 of `answers/unzipped/LM_P029/LM_P029_notes.md`. Each is kept, corrected or dropped, with a reason.
SP-1 … SP-7 are second-pass notes (my own). Kinds: conjecture/question, claim without proof, implication not
pursued, own idea. Literature names in the SP notes are leads only; they were not checked against the corpus
in this pass.

## First-pass notes

- **FP-1 · 281 (margin) · conjecture · corrected.** The main-text condition is now securely "Π1(A^{0u}) → Π1(A^0) un isomorphisme", where u is the homomorphism Γ → A. The margin calls this "Trop brutal !": the condition in question says Π1(A^{0Γ}) ⥲ Π1(A^0)^Γ. It adds that the whole set of conditions must be taken up again "avec soin et les modifier ad hoc" (cf. the inserted sheet). It remains open which hypotheses make the comparison with the invariants an isomorphism.
- **FP-2 · 281 · conjecture · kept.** "Le plus agréable serait que ce soit un homotopisme — c'est cela sans doute qui exprimerait qu'on a une ∞-équivalence" expresses a hoped-for homotopy-level strengthening of the π1 statement. The reading is secure.
- **FP-3 · 281 · conjecture (work plan) · corrected.** The plan is to prove 1°, 2°, 3° for A = A_{g,ν} and finite Γ, "en me limitant au besoin au cas anabélien (sans doute le plus dur en fait ! mais moins touffu conceptuellement)", by first determining A^{0u} and proving it contractible. The first-pass reading "combinatoire" was wrong. By Grothendieck's own assessment, the anabelian case is probably the hardest and conceptually the least tangled.
- **FP-4 · 282–283 · claim without proof · kept, detail corrected.** Two steps are asserted without proof: the passage from f ∈ A^Γ to an automorphism g of (X', S') preserving S'^!, and "la donnée de f équivaut à la donnée d'un autom du Γ-rev. V de V', induisant un isom de V' qui, prolongé à X', envoie S' dans lui-même". Scan 282 ends with "induisant un", not "au dess. d'un". The dots under V' are stains, not notation.
- **FP-5 · 283 · claim without proof · kept.** The lifting criterion is that "son composé avec φ est conjugué à|de φ, et que g̅ applique S'^! dans lui-même". It is stated without proof. The margin fixes base points a ∈ V and a' ∈ V' to make Π' explicit.
- **FP-6 · 283 · claim without proof · kept.** "g est défini par g̅ à isotopie près", except the degenerate (0,ν'), ν' = 0,1,2, "qu'il faudra examiner à part". By SP-2, these types cannot occur in the anabelian case.
- **FP-7 · 283 · claim without proof · corrected.** The ink reads "g étant choisi, f est défini modulo multiplication par un élément du centre de Γ". With "on sait déjà que A^0 ∩ Γ = (1)", restricting to "examiner des élts f ∈ A^{0Γ}" makes f unique. The intersection fact is quoted from earlier pages; SP-6 gives its hypothesis.
- **FP-8 · 281–283 · own idea (first pass) · dropped.** It names no definite statement to test. SP-2 to SP-6 give concrete checks of the same passages.
- **FP-9 · 284 · claim without proof · corrected.** The parenthesis reads "(ce qui exprime que g provient par passage au quotient d'un autom f de V, défini mod multiplication par un élément de z ∈ Centre Γ)", with ∈ secure. This is exactly the ambiguity of Γ-equivariant lifts. Two lifts commuting with Γ differ by a deck transformation that commutes with Γ, which is therefore central.
- **FP-10 · 284 · conjecture · kept.** "On veut exprimer que parmi les f qui remontent g, il y en a un (néc. unique !) qui est isot. à 1 dans Aut(U)". The "unique" follows from A^0 ∩ Γ = (1), as stated on scan 283.
- **FP-11 · 284 · claim without proof · kept.** "isot. à 1 dans Aut(U) — i.e. qui induit l'autom ext. trivial de Π1(U)" relies on the classical identification of isotopy with homotopy for surfaces of finite type with χ < 0 (Baer; Epstein; lead). The page does not argue it.
- **FP-12 · 284 · own idea (first pass) · kept.** Testing the lifting and uniqueness statements on a cyclic cover with one branch orbit is concrete, and it uses the stated nontriviality of L_{s'} → Γ.
- **FP-13 · 285 · conjecture · corrected.** The gloss reads "i.e. est dans Aut(V)^0 (⊂ Aut(V|U)^0) et pas seulement dans Aut(U)^0", with no Γ subscript. The wish is that a lift isotopic to 1 after filling S^! is already isotopic to 1 on the punctured surface V.
- **FP-14 · 285 · claim without proof · corrected.** The insertion "lorsque Aut(V')^0 est 1-connexe" sits between "Notons" and "que". It is therefore the hypothesis of the lifting "Aut(V')^0 → Aut(V)^{0Γ} ⊂ Aut(U)^{0Γ}", obtained "en vertu de principes généraux". Isotopies lift through the Γ-cover V → V', and simple connectivity makes the lift a homomorphism. See SP-2.
- **FP-15 · 285 · claim without proof · kept.** "Donc la condition énoncée sur g d'isotopie à 1 est certainement suffisante" claims sufficiency only. The converse is the open part.
- **FP-16 · 285 · claim without proof · kept.** "les résultats déjà obtenus impliquent que Aut(U)^Γ ∩ Aut(U)^0 = Aut(U)^{0Γ} induit l'identité sur S^!" relies on pages outside this package.
- **FP-17 · 285 · claim without proof · kept.** Aut(U)^0/B ≃ Mon(S^!,U) and B^0 = Aut(V)^0 are stated without argument. The proof would use the orbit map to the embedding space and the identity component of the stabiliser.
- **FP-18 · 285 · claim without proof · kept.** "un argument connu nous montre que B/B^0 ≃ Π1(Mon(S^!,U))". The fibration B → Aut(U)^0 → Mon(S^!,U) gives Π1(Aut(U)^0) → Π1(Mon) → Π0(B) → 1, so the isomorphism needs Π1(Aut(U)^0) = 1. That holds when Aut(U)^0 is contractible, i.e. when U is hyperbolic.
- **FP-19 · 285 · conjecture · kept.** The exact sequence ends with H^1(Γ, B^0), written over a vertical "=" with "?" and then 1. This is a stated but open vanishing.

- **FP-21 · 285 · own idea (first pass) · kept.** Comparing the Γ-fixed part of Π1 of the embedding space with Π1 of the fixed locus remains a sound diagnostic.
- **FP-22 · 286 · claim without proof · kept.** "la composante neutre de Aut(U)^{0Γ} est ≃ Aut(V)^0 (donc est ∞-connexe si V anabélien)". Its Π0 lies in (B/B^0)^Γ ≃ Π1(Mon(S^!,U))^Γ.
- **FP-23 · 286 · conjecture · kept.** "On voudrait prouver que le sous-groupe de ce Π1 des invariants sous Γ est réduit à 1", i.e. H^0(Γ, Π1(Mon(S^!,U))) = 1. On scan 288 this becomes exactly "La fidélité signifie justement que Π1^Γ = 1".

- **FP-26 · 286 · conjecture · corrected.** The ink reads "un homomorphisme ext de 'bouchage des trous de I'' Π' → Π", with a single prime followed by closing quotation marks. Yet on scan 289 only the holes of I'^! are filled ("défini par bouchage de I'^!"). The I' looks like a slip, and it is kept as written.

In the realisable case this gives Π' = Π1(V') and I'^! = S'^!, so the note adds no new construction.

This is the H^1 term struck on scan 286. Full faithfulness thus asks for H^0 = 1 (fidélité), H^1 = 1, and the surjectivity on scan 288.

## Second-pass notes (own)

- **SP-1 · 281 (margin) · implication not pursued.** The marginal condition Π1(A^{0Γ}) ⥲ Π1(A^0)^Γ is the H^0 step of a ladder in H^0, H^1 and H^2(Γ, Π1(A)). The CSG witness prints an "[Intercalaire]" directly after this page, stating the conditions "0-fidèle" to "3-fidèle" in these terms. That sheet is not in this package, so its text needs checking against its own scan before use.
- **SP-2 · 283–285 · implication not pursued.** In the anabelian case to which scan 281 restricts (χ(U) < 0), V → V' is an étale Γ-cover and V = U ∖ S^!. Hence χ(V') = (χ(U) − |S^!|)/|Γ| < 0, so V' is hyperbolic and the degenerate types (0,ν'), ν' ≤ 2, of scan 283 cannot occur. Aut(V')^0 is then contractible (Earle–Eells, Earle–Schatz; lead), so the hypothesis "lorsque Aut(V')^0 est 1-connexe" inserted on scan 285 holds automatically there. Confidence: high for the Euler-characteristic step, medium for the cited contractibility.
- **SP-3 · 288 · own idea (proof sketch).** The "fait" on scan 288 follows from Teichmüller's uniqueness theorem. First choose a complex structure J for which the finite group Γ acts holomorphically, as any finite group of orientation-preserving homeomorphisms allows. If a mapping class [h] commutes with the classes of Γ, let F be the unique extremal quasiconformal self-map of (S, J) in the class of h⁻¹. Then γFγ⁻¹ lies in the same class with the same dilatation, so γFγ⁻¹ = F for every γ ∈ Γ, and F⁻¹ is a homeomorphism in [h] commuting with Γ. Confidence medium, for finite-type surfaces with χ < 0. The argument is probably known but was not checked against the literature.
- **SP-4 · 290 c) · claim without proof, with an argument.** If I'^! = {i'} is Γ-stable, then Γ = Γ_{i'}. Each γ ∈ Γ lifts to E' normalising L_{i'} ≅ ℤ, and because the action is direct (Γ = Γ^+) the lift centralises it. So Z_{i'} maps onto Γ, with kernel the centraliser of L_{i'} in Π', which is L_{i'} for a peripheral subgroup of a surface group. Under 2° (E' torsion-free), Z_{i'} is torsion-free and virtually infinite cyclic, hence ≅ ℤ, so Γ = Z_{i'}/L_{i'} is cyclic. Realisability of finite cyclic actions is Nielsen's theorem (1942; lead). Confidence: medium-high, given 2° and Γ = Γ^+.
- **SP-5 · 286–290 · implication not pursued (lead).** Kerckhoff's solution of the Nielsen realisation problem (1983, after this manuscript) realises every finite subgroup of the mapping class group of a finite-type hyperbolic surface by isometries. Through Dehn–Nielsen–Baer, this covers faithful finite outer actions that preserve the loop structure. For such Π, the "cas réalisable" restrictions on scans 286–290 then exclude nothing, and what remains open in the manuscript's sense is uniqueness and full faithfulness. This was not checked against the corpus in this pass.
- **SP-6 · 283 · claim without proof (hypothesis).** "on sait déjà que A^0 ∩ Γ = (1)" needs U of hyperbolic type: on a torus or an annulus, finite rotations lie in the identity component. For χ(U) < 0, a periodic homeomorphism isotopic to the identity is the identity (classical, Hurwitz/Nielsen; lead). This gives the statement in the anabelian case of scan 281.
- **SP-7 · 289 · claim without proof (lead).** "on sait aussi qu'il est fini": if Π is a hyperbolic surface group, the extension 𝓔 has a torsion-free subgroup of finite index with a finite K(π,1). Groups of this kind have finitely many conjugacy classes of finite subgroups (K. S. Brown, *Cohomology of Groups*, ch. IX; lead not checked), so maximal nontrivial partial sections fall into finitely many Π-conjugacy classes. With realisation (SP-5), these classes correspond to the cone points of the quotient orbifold, which fits the name "pts fixes".


# ===== LM_P030 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P030 (scans 291–300): research notes checked against `LM_P030_checked.tex`

Notes 1–39 are the first pass's, in its order. Each has a verdict (keep, correct or drop) and a reason. Notes S1–S7 are new in the second pass and are labelled as such. Quotations follow the corrected transcription.

## First-pass notes

1. **Scan 291 — Grothendieck's conjecture — correct.** "La conjecture est que le foncteur … est une équivalence de catégories, i.e. 1°) pl. fidèle et 2°) ess. surjectif." The parenthesis reads "lui-même un peu conjectural, sauf si on se réduit aux situations de départ réalisables". So the functor is itself only conjecturally defined, because it rests on the bijection (∗) of scan 290, which is not proved outside realizable situations. *Reason:* "un pur engueulard" was a misreading, and the corrected words carry mathematical content.

2. **Scan 291 — claim without proof — keep.** Essential surjectivity is "clair" when both sides are restricted to realizable situations. Full faithfulness "n'est pas prouvé" even there. *Reason:* the readings are confirmed.

3. **Scan 291 — Grothendieck's expectation — keep.** "Je pense que si I' est réduit à un élément, alors les propriétés du foncteur "forage d'un trou" permettront de l'établir aisément." The condition is written with I', not I'!. It replaces a cancelled start about "fidélité" and a cancelled Autext display. *Reason:* the readings are confirmed.

4. **Scan 292 — Grothendieck's programme — correct.** The reductions and item I concern "la bijectivité des (∗)", i.e. of the map (∗) relating Φ(π',Γ)_{I'!} and Φ(π,Γ) from scan 290, where "c) Bijectivité de (∗)" is said not to be proved. They do not concern maps "α_s". Item II reduces full faithfulness to the case where I'! is one (singular) orbit of Γ in I'. A proof would have to say how morphisms over several orbits are assembled. *Reason:* "α_s" was a misreading of "(∗)".

5. **Scan 292 — Grothendieck's programme — keep.** Item III: if Φ(π,Γ)=∅, i.e. ℰ has no element of finite order ≠ 1, prove that ℰ with the ℰ-conjugacy classes of the centralizers of the L_i is a groupe à lacets. Torsion-freeness alone is not offered as a proof. *Reason:* only the singular "d'élément" changes, which has no mathematical effect.

6. **Scans 292–293 — Grothendieck's programme — correct.** The programme is first to be carried out for cyclic groups "opérant de façon directe", i.e. preserving orientation (Γ = Γ⁺, as on scan 289), and then "dans ce cas par dévissage". *Reason:* the first pass left the adjective illegible. It is a direct-action hypothesis; no prime-order condition appears.

7. **Scan 293 — conditional expectation — correct.** "Si le th. de classification complet (comme équivalence des 2-catégories –des opérations topologiques et des opérations isotopiques|homologiques–) était vrai", items I–III "devraient l'être aussi". The theorem invoked is a classification theorem. The adjective of the second 2-category is not settled by the ink; the witnesses read "isotopiques". *Reason:* "désinfection/description" was a misreading.

8. **Scan 293 — conditional expectation — keep.** Items I and II should hold as soon as the 2-functor is "2-fidèle (pas néc. 3-fidèle), du moins pour des situations réalisables". The cancelled "et la condition néc." is revision evidence, not a claim. *Reason:* unchanged.

9. **Scan 293 — implication not pursued — keep.** Admissibility combines Autext(π',Γ,I'!) ⥲ Aut(π,Γ,I'!) with a conjugacy condition on every other action giving the same action on π and the same map I'! → Φ(π,Γ). The two sides of the display are written "Autext" and "Aut". *Reason:* unchanged.

10. **Scan 293 — model's own idea (first pass) — keep.** Test the two admissibility requirements separately on a small cyclic action with explicit peripheral data. *Reason:* a sensible check that the corrections do not affect.

11. **Scan 294 — Grothendieck's conjecture — keep.** The criterion: a faithful exterior action of Γ on π is realizable if and only if it lifts, by "forage de trous" for I'! = Φ(π,Γ), to an admissible action on (π', I'!), and the corresponding extension ℰ' of Γ by π' is a groupe à lacets. *Reason:* unchanged. The extension carries a prime (ℰ'), as the extension by π'.

12. **Scan 294 — claim without proof — keep.** "par définition m̄, ce remontement serait alors ess. unique". The abbreviation remains doubtful. Uniqueness needs the equivalence relation on lifts to be specified. *Reason:* unchanged.

13. **Scan 294 — claim without proof — correct.** The section defines here ("On va définir", not a recall) SA_{g,ν} = {u ∈ A_g | u induise l'identité sur un voisinage de S_{g,ν}}. It asserts that, unlike the other subgroups of A_g, this one is not closed. *Reason:* the tense is corrected; the claim is unchanged.

14. **Scan 294 — model's own idea (first pass) — keep.** Take products of homeomorphisms supported in disjoint annuli shrinking to a puncture, with displacements tending to 0. The partial products lie in SA and converge uniformly to a homeomorphism that is not the identity near the puncture. *Reason:* this is a valid construction for the uniform (compact-open) topology on A_g; the intended topology should still be confirmed from §19.

15. **Scan 295 — implication not pursued — keep.** For compact surfaces with boundary, "il y aurait lieu de prendre le groupe des autom. qui induisent l'identité sur le bord". *Reason:* unchanged.

16. **Scan 295 — claim without proof — keep.** SA_{g,ν} is normal ("invariant") in A_{g,ν}. The quotient is isomorphic to the group of germs of automorphisms of X_g near S_{g,ν}, or of germs at infinity of automorphisms of U_{g,ν}. *Reason:* unchanged; only the abbreviation "ss-gr" is corrected.

17. **Scan 295 — claim without proof — correct.** "On aura évidemment … (SA_{g,ν})° ⊂ SA°_{g,ν} | A°_{g,ν}". The second term is not settled by the ink; the witnesses read A°_{g,ν}. That reading gives the inclusion needed for SA/(SA)° → A/A°, hence for the canonical map SΓ^! → Γ^{!+}. If SA° is read as (SA)°, the other reading is a tautology. *Reason:* the first pass gave SA° as the reading without doubt.

18. **Scan 295 — announced interpretation — keep.** "qu'on va interpréter de façon algébrique" announces the algebraic description of SΓ^! → Γ^{!+}, carried out on scans 296–299. *Reason:* unchanged.

19. **Scan 296 — claim without proof — keep.** Choosing L_i amounts to giving a point of B_{D_i^*} (≃ a universal cover D̃_i^*) and an isomorphism from its image in B_U to the reference point s. *Reason:* unchanged.

20. **Scan 296 — claim without proof — correct.** The g_i are determined by u "modulo multiplication à droite par un λ_i ∈ L_i" (λ, not ℓ). Uniqueness needs the centralizer of L_i in π to be L_i itself. This holds when L_i is generated by a puncture loop that is not a proper power in a free or surface group; the abelian low-complexity cases need separate checking. *Reason:* the letter is corrected and the justification added.

21. **Scan 296 — implication not pursued — keep.** The composition computation yields the rule (v,(h_i))·(u,(g_i)) = (vu, h_i v(g_i)) of scan 297. The struck term before "h_i v(g_i)" is a "(g_i)" term with u or v, an abandoned first version. *Reason:* unchanged; see S3 for the check.

22. **Scan 296 — model's own idea (first pass) — keep.** Compare two choices of the family L_i and their conjugators. *Reason:* the dependence on choices is explicit in the text ("une fois choisis les D̃_i^*", scan 298).

23. **Scan 297 — claim without proof — correct.** The group structure on Sℰ^! is that of a subgroup of the semidirect product of ℰ^! = Autext_{lac}(π, I) [id. sur I] by π^I. *Reason:* the first pass read "Autext_loc". The subscript is "lac" (lacets), as on scan 300. On "ext", see S4.

24. **Scan 297 — claim without proof — keep.** Sℰ^! → ℰ^! is surjective, with kernel "can. isom" to ∏_{i∈I} L_i ≃ T^I. The kernel consists of the pairs (id,(g_i)) with g_i in the centralizer of L_i, so it uses the same fact as note 20. *Reason:* unchanged.

25. **Scan 297 — implication not pursued — correct.** "(centrale)" is cancelled, and the margin says that ℰ acts on T^I "via son action sur T". Automorphisms in ℰ^! fix I and act on T by the orientation character χ = ±1. The extension is therefore central over ker χ, and not in general. *Reason:* the first pass left this open; the margin settles the action.

26. **Scan 297 — model's own idea (first pass) — keep.** Compute the cocycle of Sℰ^! from chosen lifts. *Reason:* unaffected by the corrections.

27. **Scan 298 — Grothendieck's wish (conjecture) — correct.** "Je voudrais interpréter cette extension comme l'image inverse d'une extension can de Γ^! par T^I (canonique en tous cas, une fois choisis les D̃_i^*)." Canonicity is claimed relative to the chosen universal covers. *Reason:* "au bon sens" was a misreading of "en tous cas".

28. **Scan 298 — claim without proof — correct.** The lifts of u̇ are the systems (u̇, γ_i), with γ_i : D̃_i^* → u̇(D̃_i^*) "[déterminé mod|par élt de T]". An isomorphism between two universal covers of a punctured disc over a given map is unique up to a deck transformation, and the deck group is T ≅ ℤ. So for each i the γ_i form a T-torsor, which accounts for the kernel T^I. *Reason:* the bracket was reread; this answers the first pass's torsor question.

29. **Scan 298 — claim without proof — keep.** Sℰ^! is equally described by the classes "d'isom." of automorphisms of (B_I → B_{D*} → B_U) inducing the identity on I. A comparison compatible with composition is still needed. *Reason:* unchanged; "d'isom." is now settled.

30. **Scan 298 — model's own idea (first pass) — keep.** Compare two choices of the covers D̃_i^* and the resulting extensions. *Reason:* supported by "une fois choisis les D̃_i^*".

31. **Scan 299 — announced proof — correct.** "On va définir un hom. de suites exactes … qui prouve que Sℰ^! est bien l'image inverse de l'extension SΓ^! de Γ^! par T^I." The homomorphism is announced and then sketched through classes of autoequivalences; it is not completed on the page. *Reason:* "On a défini" was a misreading.

32. **Scan 299 — claim without proof — correct.** The group of the square diagram maps "de façon naturelle" to that of "des 2-diagrammes" B_I → B_{D*} → B_U. The curved arrow labelled "isom. de comm." runs from the pt.s corner to the left side. The natural map forgets this commutation datum; which coherences survive is still to be specified. *Reason:* the arrow direction and the plural are settled.

33. **Scan 300 — claim — keep.** "il n'y avait aucunement lieu de se borner aux autom. de π (ext. ou totaux) induisant l'identité sur I". *Reason:* "besoin" → "lieu" does not change the content.

34. **Scan 300 — claim without proof — keep.** Sℰ is to be an extension of Γ by T^I × π. This needs the lift π → Sℰ drawn in the diagram, and π acting trivially on T^I by conjugation. The latter holds because inner automorphisms fix I and preserve orientation. Trivial intersection and normality of the product remain to be checked. *Reason:* unchanged; the check is made concrete.

35. **Scan 300 — implication not pursued — keep.** ℰ and SΓ are obtained "en divisant par T^I resp. par π". The exponent is still doubtful (I or S), so I is the mathematically required reading. *Reason:* unchanged.

36. **Scan 300 — claim without proof — keep.** ℰ_{g,ν} = Aut_{lac}(π_{g,ν}) ≃^{can} Γ_{g,ν+1} if (g,ν) ≠ (0,0),(0,1). The cancelled "i.e. π_{g,ν} ≠ 0" is not part of the final text. *Reason:* unchanged; see S6.

37. **Scan 300 — claim without proof — correct.** The last comparison reads Sℰ_{g,ν} ≃ SΓ'_{g,ν+1}/T_{s_ν}, with letter and subscript doubtful (the witnesses have Γ_{g,ν}). Here SΓ'_{g,ν+1} is the subgroup of SΓ_{g,ν+1} of elements fixing the last element s_ν. SΓ'_{g,ν+1} is an extension of the point stabilizer, identified with ℰ_{g,ν}, by T^{ν+1}; dividing by the T-factor of s_ν leaves an extension by T^I, as Sℰ_{g,ν} requires. "/Γ_{g,ν}" would not give a quotient. *Reason:* the denominator and "s_ν" were reread.

38. **Scan 300 — model's own idea (first pass) — keep.** Work out one explicitly punctured surface and both quotient maps from Sℰ. *Reason:* it tests notes 34 and 37 directly.

39. **Scans 299–300 — model's own idea (first pass) — keep.** Treat the commutation isomorphism of the square as extra data and ask whether forgetting it accounts for the kernel T^I. *Reason:* the arrow direction is now known, so the datum is identifiable. It is still a proposed check.

## Second-pass notes

S1. **Scans 290–292 — implication not pursued (second pass).** The conjectural status of the functor (note 1) and programme item I (note 4) are the same open point. Scan 290 says the bijectivity of (∗) is not proved, "sauf si la situation de départ est réalisable, ou si I'! réduit à un seul élément, mais alors Γ cyclique et la situation est réalisable". The cyclic case, where the programme is to start (note 6), is therefore the case Grothendieck regards as accessible.

S2. **Scan 293 — question (second pass).** If the second 2-category is "opérations isotopiques" (actions up to isotopy, i.e. homomorphisms to a Teichmüller group), the classification theorem is a 2-categorical form of the realization problem studied here. For finite Γ, Kerckhoff's theorem on the Nielsen realization problem (1983) is the evident point of comparison. It was not checked against the corpus in this pass, and it does not by itself give the stronger 2-categorical statement or items II–III.

S3. **Scan 296 — check of a computation (second pass).** From u(ℓ) = int(g_i^{-1})ℓ^α and v(ℓ) = int(h_i^{-1})ℓ^β one gets (vu)(ℓ) = int(v(g_i)^{-1}h_i^{-1})ℓ^{αβ} = int((h_i v(g_i))^{-1})ℓ^{αβ}, as written. The rule (v,(h_i))(u,(g_i)) = (vu, h_i v(g_i)) is associative, with inverse (u^{-1}, (u^{-1}(g_i^{-1}))). It is the semidirect-product law on Aut(π) ⋉ π^I. The witnesses' "(uv)" and "h_i u(g_i)" are errors.

S4. **Scan 297 — question (second pass).** ℰ^! is written "Autext_{lac}(π, I)", but the pairs (u,(g_i)) need u to be an automorphism of π, since u(ℓ) = int g_i^{-1}(ℓ^α) is an equation in π. The kernel ∏ L_i of Sℰ^! → ℰ^! also presupposes this. On scan 300 Grothendieck writes ℰ_{g,ν} = Aut_{lac} with "ext" cancelled and puts π as the kernel of ℰ → Γ. So the "ext" on scan 297 should be read against scan 300, either as a slip or as a convention that changed.

S5. **Scan 294 — implication (second pass).** An element of SA_{g,ν} is the identity near every point of S_{g,ν}. On a connected surface it therefore fixes S_{g,ν} pointwise and preserves orientation, so SA_{g,ν} ⊂ A^{!+}_{g,ν}. This explains why the canonical map on scan 295 lands in Γ^{!+}_{g,ν}. It may also explain the cancelled "!" on SA: the superscript was redundant (a hypothesis).

S6. **Scan 300 — question (second pass).** The exclusions (g,ν) ≠ (0,0),(0,1) are exactly the cases π_{g,ν} = 1, as the cancelled "i.e. π_{g,ν} ≠ 0" says. For (g,ν) = (0,2), π ≅ ℤ and Aut_{lac}(π) is very small. Whether ℰ_{0,2} ≃ Γ_{0,3} needs a further exclusion or a specific convention (orientation character, stabilizer of the new point) should be checked against the definitions of §19.

S7. **Scans 295 and 297 — implication not pursued (second pass).** The cancelled lines on scan 297 identify the kernel of SΓ^!_{g,ν} → Γ^!_{g,ν} with (SA ∩ A°)/(SA)°. Grothendieck then drops this topological description in favour of the pairs (u,(g_i)) and the classifying-object description of scans 298–299. Comparing (SA ∩ A°)/(SA)° with T^I would show that the topological SΓ^! of scan 295 and the algebraic one coincide. The surviving text does not do this.


# ===== LM_P031 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P031 (scans 301–310): research notes, checked against the corrected text

Each first-pass note (numbered as in `LM_P031_notes.md`, §4) is given its scan, its kind and a short statement, then a verdict: keep, correct or drop. Notes added in the second pass are labelled **[second pass]**. Quotations follow `LM_P031_checked.tex`.

## First-pass notes

1. **Scan 301 — claim without proof.** An automorphism u that is the identity near S_{g,ν} gives an element of SΓ^! “en prenant comme γ_i les identités”, using u_•(D̃_i) ≃ D̃_i^*. A proof must show that these choices define an element and a homomorphism. *Verdict: keep.* The disc formula is now read with a doubtful u_• in place of u_i.

2. **Scan 301 — claim without proof.** “Le fait que l'hom obtenu soit trivial sur (SA_{g,ν})^0 est ss doute trivial.” Grothendieck asserts the triviality on the identity component with a hedge (“sans doute”) and gives no argument. *Verdict: correct.* The first pass read “tt d'ordre formel”, which misreads the ink and overstates the claim.

3. **Scan 301 — claim without proof.** Surjectivity says that every orientation-preserving automorphism inducing the identity on S_{g,ν} is isotopic to one that is the identity near S_{g,ν}, “c'est facile”. The isotopy can be chosen, “si on veut”, to be the identity outside a small neighbourhood of S_{g,ν}. *Verdict: correct.* The two illegible words are “si on veut”, so the support condition is an option offered, not part of what must be proved.

4. **Scan 301 — expectation.** “L'injectivité peut-être plus délicate” announces the kernel computation of scans 302–304. *Verdict: keep;* the reading is now secure.

5. **Scan 302 — claim without proof.** The sequence 1 → SA′⁺ → A′^{!+} → ∏ Aut⁺(C_i) → 1 and its long exact homotopy sequence require restriction to the boundary to be a fibration. The annotation adds that Aut⁺(C_i) is homotopic to “le groupe circulaire standard 𝕌, tordu par T”, which supplies the vertical ≀ identifying ∏ π₁(Aut⁺(C_i)) with T^I. *Verdict: keep,* extended with the corrected annotation.

6. **Scan 302 — expectation.** The map A′⁰ → A is defined “par 'recollt des disques'” (gluing the discs back), and the induced maps “sont sûrement des équiv. d'homotopie”. A proof would extend automorphisms across the glued discs, e.g. by the Alexander trick, and check the comparison of the subgroups SA′ → SA. *Verdict: correct.* The first pass read “par 'oubli' des disques”, which reverses the construction.

7. **Scan 302 — implication not pursued.** By exactness, the kernel of π₀(SA′^{!+}) → Γ′^{!+} is the cokernel of π₁(A′⁰) → T^I; scans 303–304 compute exactly this. *Verdict: keep.*

8. **Scan 303 — claim without proof.** In the anabelian case π₁(A⁰) = 0 gives the boxed extension 1 → T^{S_{g,ν}} → π₀(SA) → Γ^{!+} → 1. The vanishing of π_i((SA)⁰) for i ≥ 2 uses π_i((SA)⁰) ≅ π_i(A⁰) and the contractibility of A⁰, which these pages do not prove. *Verdict: keep.*

9. **Scan 303 — implication not pursued.** The correction “quatre” → “deux” comes with the insertion “(sous-entendant que l'on ait I ≠ ∅ !) — on a tjrs g = 0, ν = 1 ou 2”. *Verdict: correct the wording.* “types” is “tjrs” (toujours). See second-pass note B.

10. **Scan 303 — claim without proof.** G ≈→ A⁰: the identity component of the complex automorphism group is asserted to be homotopy equivalent to A⁰. *Verdict: keep.*

11. **Scan 304 — claim without proof.** In case a) the two components κ_i of T → T^I are “symétriques”, so the image is the kernel of the sum map T^I → T. The sign convention for the two loops is not checked on the page. *Verdict: keep.*

12. **Scan 304 — claim without proof.** In case b), π₁(G) → T^I = T is an isomorphism, so π₀(SA_{0,1}) = 0 and π_i(SA⁰) = 0 for i ≥ 1. *Verdict: keep.* The “≀ 0” written below Γ^{!+}_{0,1} is probably not deleted (low confidence), so Γ^{!+}_{0,1} ≅ 0 is asserted as in case a).

13. **Scan 304 — theorem stated on the page.** “Supposons ν >|≥ 0. Si (g,ν) est anabélien, alors π₀(SA_{g,ν}) est canoniquement isomorphe à SΓ^{!+}_{g,ν}. Dans tous les cas, (SA_{g,ν})⁰ est ∞-connexe.” Its proof rests on the homotopy equivalences that scan 302 only expects (“sûrement”). *Verdict: keep;* the inserted sign stays doubtful.

14. **Scans 303–304 — first pass's own idea.** Check the two-component relation on an explicit annulus model with boundary rotations. *Verdict: keep,* as a model check, not as part of the text.

15. **Scan 304 — first pass's own idea.** Ask whether “∞-connexe” means contractible. *Verdict: keep.* Whitehead's theorem would give contractibility if the group has the homotopy type of a CW complex, which needs its own reference.

16. **Scan 305 — claim without proof.** For fixed i, (SΓ)_i is an extension of Γ_i by T^I, and pr_i: T^I → T yields an extension of Γ_i by T, “qu'on va décrire d'une autre façon”. *Verdict: keep.*

17. **Scan 305 — claim without proof.** Each u ∈ Γ has a realisation u_i ∈ Aut_lac(π_i) with u_i(L_i) ⊂ L_i, determined up to right multiplication by int(κ_i(α)), α ∈ T. *Verdict: keep.*

18. **Scan 306 — claim without proof.** SΓ is described as “l'image inverse de la diagonale dans l'hom. naturel de ce produit dans Autext_lac(π)^I, qui est bien extension de ladite diagonale … par T^I”. *Verdict: keep;* see second-pass note D on the condition needed for the kernel to be T^I.

19. **Scan 306 — claim without proof.** The projected extension of Γ_i by T is the group of lacet automorphisms of π_i that normalise L_i, an extension of Γ_i by L_i, “(car intersection avec π_i ⊂ Aut_lac(π_i) est|étant réduite à L_i)”. *Verdict: keep,* with the quotation corrected.

20. **Scan 306 — expectation.** The extension of Γ by T^I “a une nette tendance à ne pas être triviale”, because its stabiliser extensions are not trivial. *Verdict: keep.*

21. **Scan 306 — claim referred back (“on l'a vu”).** For a finite G ⊂ Γ_i^+ with G ≠ 1, the induced extension is never trivial; G is cyclic and its inverse image is ≃ T. *Verdict: keep.*

22. **Scans 305–306 — first pass's own idea.** Express the compatibility through H²(Γ, T^I), restriction to Γ_i and pr_i. *Verdict: keep.*

23. **Scan 307 — claim without proof (margin).** “Cas anabélien donc G|G_K fini”: the K-automorphism group of an anabelian curve is finite. *Verdict: correct.* “anabélien” is now secure, and the G may carry the subscript K.

24. **Scan 307 — claim without proof.** The action of Γ on G_K̄ = Aut_K̄(U), “de façon pas néc. triviale”, “décrit un groupe algébrique étale fini sur K”. *Verdict: keep;* the adjective is “triviale” (secure).

25. **Scan 307 — first pass's own idea.** Work out a concrete surface with finite G and a normalising Γ. *Verdict: keep.*

26. **Scan 308 — claim without proof.** “Si l'action de 𝒢 sur U mod isotopie est fidèle, alors 𝒢 ↪ Autext(π)”, and (1) is the pull-back of the Teichmüller extension. *Verdict: correct.* The insertion “mod isotopie” attaches after “sur U”, not after “est fidèle”, and its doubt mark is removed.

27. **Scan 308 — claim without proof.** The U-isomorphisms Ũ(P) ≅ Ũ(Q) correspond to path classes from P to Q. *Verdict: keep.*

28. **Scan 308 — claim without proof.** G_Q^+ ≠ 1 ⇒ “(Donc G_Q^+ est cyclique)”. *Verdict: keep.* The standard argument averages a metric so that the finite orientation-preserving stabiliser acts faithfully on the circle of directions at Q; this needs a faithful, locally linearisable action.

29. **Scan 309 — claim without proof.** Changing the path class “donnera un relèvement r′_{G_Q} qui sera conjugué de r par un unique élément de π = π₁(U,P)”, with uniqueness from π^{G_Q} = {1}. *Verdict: keep,* with the wording corrected.

30. **Scan 309 — claim without proof.** The lift r_{Γ_Q} “a m̄ la propté de normaliser r_{G_Q}”, i.e. it also normalises the G_Q-lift, compatibly with the given action of Γ on G. *Verdict: keep;* the reading is now firmer (the supposed deletion is the m̄ sign).

31. **Scan 309 — claim (margin NB).** The set of points Q is finite and 𝒢 acts on it, so 𝒢_Q has finite index in 𝒢, “et de m̄ Γ_Q dans Γ”. The finiteness of {Q : G_Q^+ ≠ 1} follows from G being finite, provided each element ≠ 1 has finitely many fixed points on X. *Verdict: keep.*

32. **Scans 309–310 — claim without proof.** Under 𝒢 = Γ × G, r_{Γ_Q}(Γ_Q) lies in the centraliser of r(G_Q). Since π^{r(G_Q)} = 1, Centr(r(G_Q)) → 𝒢 is injective, so the lift is unique. *Verdict: keep.*

33. **Scan 310 — claim without proof (margin NB).** “Indépendamment de tte|l' hyp. que Γ centralise G”, the normaliser of r(G_Q) in π equals its centraliser and is trivial, so Norm(r(G_Q)) → 𝒢 is injective. *Verdict: keep.*

34. **Scan 310 — implication not pursued.** “Le choix d'un relèvement du|des (petit) groupe G_Q, impose déjà le choix d'un relèvement du|des (grand) groupe Γ_Q, ou 𝒢_Q.” *Verdict: correct the quotation.* The ink is singular (“petit”, “grand”, “impose”), not “(petits) groupes”.

35. **Scan 310 — claim (“je dis que”).** The image in 𝒢 of the centraliser, and even of the normaliser, of r(G_Q) is 𝒢_Q itself. Proof: if g̃ normalises the action of G_Q, it “invarie” the fixed-point set of G_Q in Ũ(Q), which reduces to Q̃, so g ∈ 𝒢_Q (“et g̃ est le relèvement évident”). *Verdict: correct.* The verb is probably “invarie” (leaves invariant), not “envoie”, and “induit” is “évident”. The singleton fixed-point set still needs proof.

36. **Scans 309–310 — first pass's own idea.** Test uniqueness on a rotation and on a reflection. *Verdict: keep;* see second-pass note E.

37. **Scan 310 — first pass's own idea.** State the passage from G_Q to 𝒢_Q as a statement about the projection of the normaliser, with existence and uniqueness separated. *Verdict: keep.*

## Second-pass notes

A. **Scan 301 — claim without proof [second pass].** SΓ^{!+} is discrete, so “ss doute trivial” amounts to saying that the map SA → SΓ^{!+} is constant along isotopies through automorphisms that are the identity near S_{g,ν}. That needs the map to be locally constant for the topology on SA, and the page does not show this.

B. **Scan 303 — implication not pursued [second pass].** The non-anabelian types are (g,ν) = (0,0), (0,1), (0,2) and (1,0). Of these, only (0,1) and (0,2) have I = S_{g,ν} ≠ ∅, which explains both “quatre” → “deux” and “on a tjrs g = 0, ν = 1 ou 2”. The theorem's inserted “Supposons ν >|≥ 0” repeats this hypothesis, which would favour “>”, but the sign stays doubtful in the transcription.

C. **Scan 304 — implication not pursued [second pass].** In both abelian cases the boxed extension of scan 303 fails: T^I is replaced by the cokernel of π₁(G) → T^I. With Γ^{!+}_{0,2} = 0 this gives π₀(SA_{0,2}) ≅ T, and π₀(SA_{0,1}) = 0. The theorem limits the canonical isomorphism to the anabelian case accordingly, and keeps ∞-connectedness for all cases.

D. **Scans 305–306 — claim without proof [second pass].** The sentence “Bien entendu, SΓ est le ss-groupe des ∏ Aut_lac(π_i), l'image inverse de la diagonale …”, absent from both witnesses, presents SΓ as a fibre product over the diagonal Γ ⊂ Autext_lac(π)^I. The whole inverse image of the diagonal has kernel ∏ Int(π_i) ≅ π^I. The kernel is T^I only for tuples with u_i(L_i) ⊂ L_i (scan 305) and only because L_i is its own normaliser in π_i (“réduite à L_i”, scan 306).

E. **Scans 309–310 — own idea [second pass].** Three steps are one fixed-point argument: the uniqueness on scan 309 (π^{G_Q} = 1), the margin NB of scan 310 (trivial normaliser in π) and the closing “En effet” (g̃ preserves Fix(G_Q) = {Q̃}). An element normalising r(G_Q) preserves the unique fixed point Q̃ of the rotation group, and π acts freely on Ũ. Writing the argument once makes the role of G_Q^+ ≠ 1 explicit, since a reflection alone fixes a line rather than a point. Confidence: medium.

F. **Scan 310 — claim without proof [second pass].** With the restored “et”, the image of Centr(r(G_Q)) “contient 𝒢_Q, et m̄ le relèvement 𝒢_Q → E”. The centraliser would then carry a canonical lift of the whole stabiliser, which is what the next sentence (the small group's lift fixes the large group's lift) relies on. This rests on a low-confidence reading of the struck passage.

G. **Scan 302 — question [second pass].** The diagram leaves π₀(SA′⁺) “à calculer”, with an arrow pointing at it. Scan 303 answers the question only for SA, after the expected homotopy equivalences, so the computation for the bordered surfaces themselves is never written out.


# ===== LM_P032 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P032 (scans 311–320): research notes checked against the corrected text

Each first-pass note (numbered as in `LM_P032_notes.md`, §5) gets a verdict (keep, correct or drop) and a reason. Notes marked "second pass" are new. Quotations follow `LM_P032_checked.tex`.

## First-pass notes

1. **311 — claim without proof.** Keep. Two remarks say the computation reduces to orbit representatives: the centraliser of a lift is transported "par simple conjugaison", and "les cas à déterminer correspondent pratiquement aux orbites de 𝒢 dans U^!". The corrected reading "le relèvement correspondant de 𝒢_Q" (not 𝒢_k/𝒢_l) does not change the note.

2. **311 — claim without proof.** Correct. The verb before "Centr_{E′}(r) ∩ π = (1)" is uncertain: the ink fits "on aura", while CSG and IUT read "on a encore". With "encore" the triviality is a recall, not a new assertion: on the preceding page (CSG footnote 88), the normaliser of r(G_Q) in π equals its centraliser and is trivial. The point about the topology of the resulting section stands.

3. **311 — claim without proof.** Keep. The page does not prove "Cette image ne dépend que de Q … et est remplacée par un G-conjugué". The G here is the roman G of the text, not 𝒢.

4. **312 — claim without proof.** Correct. The intersection Γ^♮ is taken over Q ∈ U^!, the points with a non-trivial stabiliser in the finite group G⁺. That set is finite: it consists of fixed points of finitely many non-trivial automorphisms of a curve, and CSG footnote 87 on the preceding page records that "l'ensemble des points Q est fini". A finite intersection of finite-index subgroups has finite index, and it is normal because Γ permutes the Γ_Q by conjugation. The first pass's worry about an infinite family therefore does not arise.

5. **312 — conjecture or question.** Correct. The verb is "caractériser", not "construire": "se proposer s'il est possible de caractériser au moins le|un ss-groupe fini Γ^♮ de Γ comme Centr_{Γ′♮}(G), et d'en récupérer peut être Γ comme le normalisateur de Γ^♮ dans Centr_G(Γ)". The last centraliser is now settled as Centr_G(Γ). "ss-groupe fini" stands in the ink, although two lines earlier Γ^♮ is "d'indice fini" (see second-pass note A).

6. **312 — question.** Keep. The ink reads "cela n'empêche de faire des calculs", not the witnesses' "m'empêche". That supports the first pass's reading: the unverified injectivity of Γ → Autext_lac(π̂) does not stop exploratory computation.

7. **311 — first pass's own idea** (compact-to-Hausdorff continuity of the section). Keep, conditional as stated. The argument is the standard one: a continuous bijection from a compact space onto a Hausdorff space is a homeomorphism.

8. **313 — implication not pursued.** Correct the detail. The margin keeps "par un groupe fini G)", and "fini" is not cancelled. The mixed case is "𝒢 ext. d'un groupe de Galois Γ par un groupe fini G", ending with "Géom. Alg." (partly uncertain). The note's suggestion stands.

9. **314 — first pass's own idea** (study Norm(𝒯̂)/𝒯̂ through its conjugation action). Keep. It is a line of verification, not a result.

10. **315 — claim without proof.** Keep. The page does not prove that 𝚪_{0,3} "s'identifie au centralisateur" (corrected from "normalisateur") of 𝔖₃ in 𝒯̂̂_{0,3}. The margin note attributes the semidirect structure to 𝒯̂̂, not to 𝚪 as the witnesses' footnote 90 has it.

11. **315 — conjecture.** Correct. The insertion ends "et peut-être égal." The supposed final "?" or "à" is the hook of the insertion line. The conjectured equality is 𝚪_{0,3} = 𝒯̂̂^{!}_{03}; second-pass note B gives an exact criterion for it.

12. **315 — question.** Keep. Injectivity and surjectivity of 𝚪 → 𝚪_{03} (and of 𝚪 → 𝚪_{g,ν}) are left open: "dont j'ignore pour l'instant s'il est injectif, et encore plus s'il est surjectif".

13. **315 — conditional assumption.** Correct. The first exponent is ρ, not β. It is the same letter as in "un relèvement u qui commute à ρ" (316). On 312, ρ (or P) names the point exp(2iπ/6), and on 315–316 it acts as an automorphism. The relations π̂^ρ = π̂^{σ₀} = (1) remain hypotheses ("si on admet").
