14. **315, margin — claim without proof.** Keep. "𝒯̂̂ est ici produit ½ direct de 𝔖₃ = 𝒯⁺ par 𝒯^!" is stated without a splitting.

15. **315 — claim without proof.** Keep. The identification of 𝓔_{gν} with 𝒯′_{g,ν+1} for anabelian (g,ν) is given without proof.

16. **315 — claim without proof.** Keep. The page does not spell out how an outer action defines an extension by π or π̂, nor the centre-freeness this needs. On 318 these appear as the assumption (1).

17. **316 — implication not pursued.** Keep. "Donc 𝓔 a tendance à être une sous-extension d'une 𝔖": the letter is now settled as 𝔖.

18. **316 — conditional assumption.** Correct the quotation to "Admettant que 𝚪 → 𝚪_{0,3} est injectif, on aurait donc". The content is unchanged: the diagram rests on an assumption.

19. **316, margin — claim without proof.** Keep. 𝔖̂̂′_{g,ν} is defined as the normaliser of 𝔖̂⁺_{g,ν} in 𝔖̂̂_{g,ν} and called an "extension de 𝚪_{g,ν} par 𝔖̂⁺_{g,ν}" without proof.

20. **316 — claim without proof.** Keep. The necessary condition "il faut … un relèvement u qui commute à ρ, et un rel. qui commute à σ₀" allows two different lifts. "qu'il(s) admette(nt)" stays uncertain in the ink.

21. **317 — claim without proof.** Correct. The element is γ, not σ: "déjà que γ dans 𝒯̂̂_{0,3} commute à 𝒯⁺_{0,3} = 𝔖₃, i.e. qu'ils est dans 𝚪_{0,3}". Given note 10, the "i.e." is immediate: 𝚪_{0,3} is defined as that centraliser.

22. **317 — conjecture.** Keep. "Il se pourrait que tout élément de 𝚪_{0,3} ait déjà cette propriété, donc que cette condition ne pose|fasse pas de restriction véritable sur l'image de 𝚪". The witnesses drop "véritable".

23. **316–317 — first pass's own idea** (finite quotients to refute vacuity). Keep. Finite tests can refute the vacuity but cannot prove it.

24. **318 — assumption.** Keep. (1) and the stronger triviality of centralisers of open subgroups are admitted ("on admettra qu'alors").

25. **318 — claim without proof.** Keep. The exact sequence (2)/(2′) is asserted from (1) without a topological argument.

26. **318–319 — claim without proof.** Keep. The "bijection évidente" (5), Bases(π) ← Isom_lac(Π̂_{g,ν}, π), presupposes that (4) is a defining relation ("relation génératrice") and that the ℓ_j "engend^t les diff. groupes à lacets".

27. **319 — claim without proof.** Keep. Discrétifications are defined as the images u(Π_{g,ν}). In the corrected text the insertion reads "les ss-groupes de π qui peuvent s'obtenir ainsi".

28. **319 — claim without proof.** Keep. (7) needs an existence argument (an automorphism of the dense subgroup Π₀ extends) and a uniqueness argument, with the lacet condition on both sides.

29. **320 — claim without proof.** Keep. The page asserts the profinite structure on the bitorsor and the agreement of the quotient topologies without proof. "Bases" (with a capital B) is now settled.

30. **320 — claim without proof.** Correct. In the ink, the inserted "(NB 𝔖_{g,ν} est tjs infini)" belongs after "pas séparé": "Cet espace n'est pas séparé (NB 𝔖_{g,ν} est tjs infini), car le groupe 𝔖_{g,ν} ⊂ 𝔖̂̂_{g,ν} n'est pas fermé". It replaces the cancelled "Sauf dans les cas exceptionnels (g,ν) = (0,3) où 𝔖_{g,ν} …". Infinitude is given as the reason for non-closedness; second-pass note C supplies the missing step.

31. **320 — claim without proof.** Keep. The separated quotient is identified with Isom/𝔖̄_{g,ν}, with no argument that orbit closures are the 𝔖̄-orbits.

32. **320 — assumption.** Correct. "un hom surjectif évident" is clearly legible; the witnesses drop "surjectif". In the target symbol, a double hat is crossed by a heavy bar. With the bar, 𝔖̂_{g,ν} → 𝔖̄_{g,ν} is literally surjective; with the double hat, "surjectif" must mean onto the image 𝔖̄. Injectivity is admitted ("nous admettrons"), and (8) depends on it.

33. **320 — first pass's own idea** (record the kernel of 𝔖̂ → 𝔖̄ separately). Keep. See also second-pass note D.

34. **320 — first pass's own idea** (Baire argument for non-closedness). Keep and upgrade. Second-pass note C completes it; confidence high.

35. **318–320 — first pass's own idea** (small types). Correct and narrow it. The cancelled exception names exactly (g,ν) = (0,3). For that type the discrete 𝒯_{0,3} = 𝔖₃ × Z/2 is finite (diagram, 316), but 𝔖_{0,3} contains π_{0,3}, which is free of rank 2, so it is infinite. This explains the cancellation and the inserted "tjs infini". Medium: the reading of the motive is an interpretation.

36. **312, 315 — literature comparison** (Belyi 1980). Keep as a lead. Faithfulness of Gal(Q̄/Q) on π̂₁(P¹∖{0,1,∞}) is Belyi's theorem. This pass did not re-read the first pass's page references.

37. **320 — literature comparison** (Boggi, arXiv:1804.06322). Keep as a lead, not re-verified here. See second-pass note D.

## Second-pass notes

A. **312 — the model's own idea (an ink slip).** "le|un ss-groupe fini Γ^♮ de Γ" follows "Γ^♮ des Γ_Q, qui est un ss-groupe invariant d'indice fini de Γ". In the profinite case Γ^♮ has finite index and is infinite, so "fini" is probably short for "d'indice fini". The transcription keeps "fini" as written.

B. **315 — the model's own idea.** Read the margin "𝒯̂̂ est ici produit ½ direct de 𝔖₃ = 𝒯⁺ par 𝒯^!" with Grothendieck's convention: an extension of A by B has B normal. Then 𝒯̂̂^! is normal and 𝔖₃ is a complement. An element commuting with 𝔖₃ maps to a central element of 𝔖₃, which has trivial centre, so 𝚪_{0,3} = Centr(𝔖₃) ⊂ 𝒯̂̂^{!}_{03}: this is the containment asserted on 315. Equality ("et peut-être égal") holds exactly when 𝔖₃ centralises 𝒯̂̂^!, i.e. when the product is direct. Confidence: high, given the margin's decomposition. Whether it is direct depends on the definition of 𝒯^!, which this package does not contain.

C. **320 — the model's own idea (completes first-pass note 34).** The map 𝔖_{g,ν} = Aut_lac(Π_{g,ν}) → 𝔖̂̂_{g,ν} = Aut_lac(Π̂_{g,ν}) is injective. Surface and free groups are residually finite, so Π_{g,ν} ⊂ Π̂_{g,ν}, and an automorphism that is the identity on Π̂ is the identity on Π. The image is countable, and infinite because it contains Inn(Π_{g,ν}) ≅ Π_{g,ν} (trivial centre in the anabelian case): this is the inserted "tjs infini". A closed countable subgroup of a profinite group is a countable compact Hausdorff group. By Baire it has an isolated point, so it is discrete, so it is finite. Hence 𝔖_{g,ν} is never closed, which gives the "car … n'est pas fermé". Confidence: high.

D. **320 — implication not pursued.** The admitted injectivity of 𝔖̂_{g,ν} → 𝔖̂̂_{g,ν} says that the profinite completion of Aut_lac(Π_{g,ν}) equals its closure in Aut_lac(Π̂_{g,ν}). This is a form of the congruence subgroup problem for these groups. Its present status should be checked in the literature (Asada for genus 0 and 1, and Boggi, are the usual starting points) before any claim; this pass did not check it.

E. **312, 315–316 — the model's own idea.** The inserted value exp(2iπ/6) = e^{iπ/3} is a fixed point of the order-3 automorphisms z ↦ 1/(1−z) and z ↦ 1 − 1/z of P¹∖{0,1,∞}: the equation z(1−z) = 1 has roots e^{±iπ/3}. This explains how ρ can denote both the point (312) and the order-3 symmetry fixing it, or its lift at that base point (315–316: "commute à ρ"). Confidence: high for the computation, medium for the reading of the notation.

F. **316–317 — question (literature lead).** In Ihara's and Drinfeld's Grothendieck–Teichmüller group, the defining relations (I) and (II) express compatibility with the order-2 and order-3 symmetries of P¹∖{0,1,∞}. It is worth checking whether the lifting condition on 316 ("un relèvement qui commute à ρ, et un rel. qui commute à σ₀") corresponds to those relations, and so whether its vacuity on 𝚪_{0,3} (317) can be decided there. Confidence: low; this is a lead, not a checked correspondence.

G. **316 — implication not pursued (notation).** The new letter 𝔖 is the same glyph as 𝔖₃, and the diagram that introduces it uses both in one row ("𝔖_{03} — 𝔖₃ × Z/2"). The collision the notation change was meant to remove therefore reappears at once.


# ===== LM_P033 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P033 (scans 321–330): research notes, checked against the corrected text

Each first-pass note (§5 of `LM_P033_notes.md`, same numbering) is checked against `LM_P033_checked.tex`. The verdict is **keep**, **correct** or **drop**, with a reason. The checker's own notes follow, labelled **[checker]**. "Ŝ" stands for the extension-group letter, read 𝔖 or 𝒢 (see the changes file, row 10). References to pages outside this package (scans 316, 320, 331) come from the witnesses, as noted, not from the ink.

## First-pass notes

1. **Scan 321, claim without proof — keep, corrected.** The classes of discretifications form an "espace hom." under Ŝ̂(π). The stabiliser of π₀♮ "s'identifie à : l'adhérence" of Aut_lac(π₀), "ou encore : Ŝ(π₀)" (the colon is in the ink). No proof is given. The identification of that closure with Ŝ(π₀) depends on the injectivity of Ŝ_{g,ν} → Ŝ̂_{g,ν}, which the preceding page only *admits* ("nous admettrons qu'il est injectif", CSG, scan 320). Reason: the claim is located correctly; its dependency on an admitted assumption was missing.

2. **Scan 321, implication not pursued — keep.** "Celle-ci contient toujours π" and the inclusion (10) make the stabiliser the inverse image of T̂(π₀♮). Whether T̂(π₀♮) in turn determines the class is exactly the question raised on scan 322. Reason: accurate.

3. **Scan 321 margin, first-pass own idea — correct (re-label).** With the corrected reading, the margin itself says that the degree-2 map Isom ext/T̂⁺_{g,ν} → Isom ext/T̂_{g,ν} "permet pour tte|les clas. de discrétifications de définir ses deux orientations". The comparison of the two quotients is therefore Grothendieck's construction, not an independent idea. The unproved point, that each fibre has exactly two elements, follows from [T̂_{g,ν}:T̂⁺_{g,ν}] = 2 and the free right action of T̂̂_{g,ν} on Isom ext. Reason: the words the first pass marked illegible are now read.

4. **Scan 322, claim without proof — keep.** "On montre par voie arithmético-géométrique que l'inclusion T̂ → T̂̂ n'est jamais un iso." The argument is not on these pages, and its range in (g,ν) cannot be checked here. Reason: accurate.

5. **Scan 322, claim without proof — correct.** (11) is "évidemment compatible à l'action de Ŝ̂(π) (transport de structure)"; the first pass had "changement de structure". The compatibility is formal: transporting a class by an automorphism carries its stabiliser to the conjugate subgroup. Reason: misread phrase corrected; the remark that a proof is needed is reduced to this formal check.

6. **Scan 322, conjecture retracted in the margin — correct.** The main text proposes that T̂_{g,ν} be its own normaliser in T̂̂_{g,ν}, "équivalent à l'assertion que (11) est injective|bijective". The margin calls this "complètement déconnant et ultra-faux — un moment d'égarement !" (the first pass read "déraisonnant|dément"). It is a retracted conjecture, not a maintained one. Reason: the marginal adjective is corrected; the final word of the main clause remains unsettled under the margin ink.

7. **Scan 322 margin, question — keep.** The "question judicieuse" is whether T̂ is invariant in T̂̂, i.e. whether its normaliser is all of T̂̂. Reason: the reading is confirmed by both witnesses and is compatible with the ink.

8. **Scan 323, implication not pursued — keep.** χ (12) and the action on I(π) (13) define T̂̂^!, T̂̂^+, T̂̂^!+ "et de même pour T̂". Comparing the kernels would show what the multiplier and the permutation of the loop classes retain. The target of (13) has the same glyph as the extension letter (𝔖 or 𝒢). Reason: accurate.

9. **Scan 323, claim without proof — keep.** "χ ne prend sur T̂ que les valeurs ±1" is stated without argument; see [checker] C for a short proof. Reason: accurate.

10. **Scan 323, conjecture — correct.** "On supposerait plutôt que T̂⁺ est invariant dans T̂̂." The parenthesis calls T̂⁺ "un sous-groupe ouvert|invariant d'indice 2" of T̂ (the first pass read "invariant" without doubt). The conjecture concerns normality in T̂̂, not the index in T̂. Reason: reading corrected; see [checker] D.

11. **Scans 323–324, conjecture — keep, corrected.** "(peut-être n'y en a-t-il qu'un seul et unique!)": the strict geometric subgroups "s'appelleront" (first pass "s'appellent") those obtained from discretification classes. Their possible uniqueness is a separate claim from uniqueness of the classes. Reason: accurate apart from the verb.

12. **Scans 322–323, first-pass own idea — correct (superseded).** The stabiliser/normaliser mechanism the note proposed does more than suggest the equivalence: it proves it. See [checker] A. Reason: the idea is right but understated.

13. **Scans 324, 326, conjecture — keep, corrected.** "(peut-être tjrs égal à T̂̂ tt entier)" below (14) and "(peut-être égal à T̂̂_{g,ν} tt entier!)" below (18): N_Σ = T̂̂ would mean that Σ is normal in T̂̂. This is the expectation of scan 323, now phrased as a normaliser statement. Reason: abbreviations settled ("tjrs", "tt"); the content is unchanged.

14. **Scan 324, claim without proof — keep.** A strict subgroup is "un élément de structure nettement plus faible" than a discretification class "et même [=]" than a non-strict geometric subgroup. The involutions of scan 325 measure the second step. Reason: accurate.

15. **Scan 324, first-pass own idea — correct (re-label).** Studying N_Σ/Σ = Γ_Σ (a double-stemmed Γ in the ink, not Π) with its order-2 elements is the manuscript's own programme: the canonical involutions on 325 and, according to the witnesses (scan 331), the conjecture (27) that Gal(Q̄/Q) → Γ_Σ is an isomorphism. Reason: not independent of the text.

16. **Scan 325, claim without proof — keep.** "Il est clair en tous cas qu'on a une application injective" (16), with image stable under conjugation. The proof needs [T̂:Σ] = 2, and injectivity holds because T̂ is the full inverse image of T̂/Σ in N. See [checker] F for the index. Reason: accurate.

17. **Scan 325, implication not pursued — correct.** The text reads "Montrons que deux|les éléments de l'image sont conjugués par Γ_Σ". The argument shows that the image of (16) is one conjugacy class (the canonical involutions); it does not claim that every involution of Γ_Σ is canonical. Reason: wording updated to the corrected reading; the point stands.

18. **Scan 325, deferred interpretation — keep.** "On en donnera une interprétation conjecturale remarquable plus bas." According to the witnesses, the next page identifies the image of complex conjugation with the canonical involution and states the conjecture (27). Reason: accurate; the cross-reference is added.

19. **Scans 325–326, claim without proof — correct.** "L'ens. T̂̂/𝒩 s'identifie à l'ens des ss-groupes de Teichmüller géométriques stricts." The transitivity this needs is supplied by the text: geometric subgroups form one conjugacy class (322), and conjugation preserves ker χ ([checker] E). The stabiliser is 𝒩 by definition (14). Reason: the note asked for an ingredient that is already present.

20. **Scan 325, first-pass own idea — correct (re-label).** The comparison of canonical involutions with complex conjugation is Grothendieck's own next step (scan 331 per the witnesses). Scan 330 prepares it: changing the isomorphism by complex conjugation gives "la quasi-discrétification orientée opposée". Reason: not independent of the text.

21. **Scan 326, claim without proof — keep, corrected.** "Plus intrinsèquement, on aura" (first pass "on a") the bijection (17) with Isomext_lac(Π̂_{g,ν},π)/𝒩_{g,ν}. The ink has "Isomext" (CSG "Isomlac"); the subscript remains doubtful. Reason: accurate apart from the tense.

22. **Scan 326, implication not pursued — keep.** The arithmetic Teichmüller group is defined "relatif au choix de M̃_{g,ν,Q}", and that choice also fixes Q̄. Later comparisons must track both choices. Reason: accurate.

23. **Scan 326, first-pass own idea — drop.** The general caution about formalising "rev. universel" adds no question specific to these pages. The text uses the universal covering of a connected topos in its standard sense. Reason: generic.

24. **Scan 327, claim without proof — correct.** (20) π₁(M_{g,ν,Q̄}) ≃ T̂⁺_{g,ν} "(compactifié profini de T⁺_{g,ν})" (first pass "complété|complétion"), "par la théorie transcendante de Teichmüller". It is defined "mod opération induite par π₁(M_{g,ν}) sur π₁(M_{g,ν,Q̄})". The comparison of algebraic and topological fundamental groups and the identification of the latter with T⁺_{g,ν} are both assumed. Reason: misread term corrected.

25. **Scan 327, claim without proof — keep.** The exact sequence (21) and its map to (22) are asserted without justifying the injectivity of π, the conjugation action, or any centre-freeness hypothesis. Reason: accurate.

26. **Scan 328, question — correct.** (23) π ≃ Π̂_{g,ν} is an isomorphism "de groupes à lacets" (the first pass preferred "bouts"), "dont on voudrait déterminer l'indétermination de façon précise". Scan 329 answers it: once Q̄ ≃ Q̄₀ is chosen, the class of isomorphisms is defined modulo inner automorphisms of π₁(U_{g,ν}). Reason: the reading is corrected and the answer is located.

27. **Scan 328, claim without proof — keep.** "(§ 21)" gives M̃_{g,ν,C} = E_g/A°_{g,ν} in the margin and Ũ_{g,ν,C} = E_g/A°_{g,ν+1} in the text. The canonicity of the covering is taken from § 21, not proved here. Reason: accurate.

28. **Scan 328, claim without proof — keep.** "Par passage aux complétés profinis" the discrete sequence (24) gives (25). The preservation of the kernel and the compatibility with Aut_lac/Autext_lac are not argued. Reason: accurate.

29. **Scan 328, first-pass own idea — keep.** Comparing E_g/A°_{g,ν} and E_g/A°_{g,ν+1} through the map that forgets the extra point and computing the kernel Π_{g,ν} is a sensible verification route. Reason: specific and consistent with the corrected text.

30. **Scan 329, claim without proof — keep.** "Cette situation provient d'ailleurs canoniquement d'une situation analogue sur Q̄₀": the descent from C to Q̄₀ of the universal coverings is assumed. Reason: accurate.

31. **Scan 329, claim without proof — keep.** The two choices are separated: 1°) Q̄ ≃ Q̄₀, with indeterminacy in Gal(Q̄/Q); 2°) the isomorphism of coverings, with indeterminacy in π₁(M_{g,ν,Q̄}). The groupoid of comparison data is implicit. Reason: accurate.

32. **Scan 329, claim without proof — keep.** "… en exactement une classe de discrétifications orientées de π." See [checker] I for what this uniqueness requires. Reason: accurate.

33. **Scan 330, cancelled claim — correct.** The cancelled paragraph reads "… s'identifie à Ŝ(π₀♮), et π₁(M_{g,ν}) à T̂(π₀♮)" (the first pass had a spurious subscript 0). It is a discarded formulation; [checker] H explains why the text's own claims exclude it. Reason: reading corrected.

34. **Scan 330, claim without proof — keep.** Changing the isomorphism to its complex conjugate gives "la quasi-discrétification orientée opposée". The effect of conjugation on the orientation datum is asserted, not computed. Reason: accurate.

35. **Scan 330, claim with a proof indication — keep.** The image of π₁(M_{g,ν,Q̄}) in T̂̂(π) is "le T̂⁺ correspondant à une quelconque des classes …", independent of the class, because π₁(M_{g,ν,Q̄}) is invariant in the arithmetic group. The last group should be over Q; the ink is unclear. Reason: accurate.

36. **Scan 330, implication not pursued — keep.** Normality is asserted only inside the image of π₁(M_{g,ν}), not inside all of T̂̂(π). Comparing the two is the content of the conjectures on N_Σ (scans 324, 326). Reason: accurate.

37. **Scans 329–330, first-pass own idea — keep.** Organising the two comparison choices as a groupoid, and recording their actions on the oriented class and on T̂⁺ separately, would clarify why the orientation can change while T̂⁺ does not. Reason: specific to the corrected text.

38. **Scan 330, first-pass own idea — keep.** A concrete check of how complex conjugation acts on the orientation datum, for one marked complex curve, is a cheap test of "orientée opposée". Reason: specific; no claim of novelty.

## [checker] notes

A. **Scan 322, claim without proof — checked [checker].** The asserted equivalence holds with "injective". (11) is an Ŝ̂(π)-equivariant map out of a transitive set. The stabiliser of π₀♮ is the inverse image of T̂(π₀♮) (using π ⊂ Ŝ(π₀♮), scan 321), and the stabiliser of the subgroup T̂(π₀♮) under conjugation is the inverse image of its normaliser. Such a map is injective iff the two stabilisers coincide, i.e. iff T̂ is self-normalising; "bijective" is right only with the target restricted to the conjugacy class of geometric subgroups.

B. **Scans 322–323, conjecture (status) [checker].** The retraction in the margin of 322 was added later ("Cela apparaît clairement par la suite"). The main text of 323, "À l'opposé de ce qu'on peut conjecturer sur T̂ … on supposerait plutôt que T̂⁺ est invariant dans T̂̂", still presupposes the retracted conjecture. Under the margin's "question judicieuse" (T̂ invariant in T̂̂), T̂ and T̂⁺ would behave alike, so the contrast on 323 is not the author's final position.

C. **Scan 323, claim without proof — proof sketch [checker].** For χ(T̂) ⊂ {±1}: T̂(π₀♮) is the image of Ŝ(π₀♮), the closure of the discrete Aut_lac(π₀) (scan 321). Assume, as "de multiplicateur +1" suggests, that the multiplier is the exponent with which loop generators go to conjugates of loop generators. An automorphism of the discrete π₀ preserving the loop structure sends loop generators to conjugates of loop generators or of their inverses, so its multiplier is ±1. χ is continuous and {±1} is closed in Ẑ*, so the same holds on the closure.

D. **Scan 323, claim without proof — reading-independent [checker].** Σ = ker(χ|T̂) is open, as the kernel of a continuous character into a discrete group, and normal of index 2 provided χ|T̂ is onto {±1} (see F). So "ouvert" and "invariant" both describe true properties, and the unsettled reading changes no claim. "Invariant" would, however, use one word in two senses within the sentence (normal in T̂, then normal in T̂̂).

E. **Scan 325, implicit step [checker].** "… on aura|a donc T̂′⁺ = int_g T̂⁺" uses that χ is a homomorphism to the commutative group Ẑ*, so χ∘int(g) = χ and int(g) carries ker(χ|T̂) onto ker(χ|T̂′). With T̂′⁺ = T̂⁺ = Σ this gives g ∈ 𝒩, and the class of g in Γ_Σ conjugates T̂/Σ to T̂′/Σ. The step is valid but not stated.

F. **Scan 325, claim without proof — gap [checker].** For each T̂ in (16) to give an element of order exactly 2, χ|T̂ must take the value −1. This is the "d'indice 2" of 323, stated without proof. It holds if the discrete Aut_lac(π₀) contains an automorphism of multiplier −1, e.g. one induced by an orientation-reversing homeomorphism of the surface of type (g,ν) that preserves the punctures. That justification is mine and is not on these pages.

G. **Scan 328, unstated theorem [checker].** The lower row of (24) identifies Autext_lac(Π_{g,ν}) with T_{g,ν}. This is the Dehn–Nielsen–Baer theorem, in its version for punctured surfaces (outer automorphisms permuting the loop classes), used without mention. The upper row has T⁺_{g,ν} of index 2 in T_{g,ν}. So the "isom|hom. de structures d'extensions" can only be an isomorphism onto the sub-extension over T⁺_{g,ν}, not onto the lower row.

H. **Scan 330, implication not pursued [checker].** The cancelled sentence put the image of the arithmetic group π₁(M_{g,ν}) inside T̂(π₀♮), which the text's own claims exclude. By scan 323, χ takes only the values ±1 on T̂. The multiplier of the image of a Galois element should be its cyclotomic character, which takes other values; this standard fact is not stated on these pages. The replacement keeps only the geometric part π₁(M_{g,ν,Q̄}) → T̂⁺, with complex conjugation giving the opposite orientation.

I. **Scan 329, implication not pursued [checker].** "Exactement une classe de discrétifications orientées" requires the indeterminacy left after choosing Q̄ ≃ Q̄₀ to fix the image of the standard oriented class. That indeterminacy consists of inner automorphisms by π₁(U_{g,ν,Q̄}). Their image in Ŝ̂(π) must lie in the stabiliser of that oriented class. The upper row of (25), with π₁(U_{g,ν,C}) ≅ Ŝ⁺_{g,ν}, supplies this, but the text leaves the inference implicit.


# ===== LM_P034 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P034 (scans 331–340): research notes, checked against the corrected transcription

The first pass's research notes 1–47 (here R01–R47) are checked one by one against `LM_P034_checked.tex`. Each gets a verdict (keep / correct / drop) and a reason. Notes S01–S08 are the second-pass checker's own and are labelled as such. Kinds: conjecture or question, claim without proof, implication not pursued, own idea. The first pass's editorial tables (its sections 1–3) are superseded by `LM_P034_changes.md`.

## First-pass notes

**R01 — scan 331 — claim without proof — KEEP (reading updated).**
Diagram (26) is introduced as "un hom canonique d'extensions de groupes", but the three vertical maps are not constructed. The lower middle group is N_Σ = Norm_{T̂̂(π)}(Σ), and Σ is by definition the image of π₁(M_{g,ν,Q̄}), so the left arrow is surjective. The mark beside it (≀ or 2) and the unfinished insertion "isomorphe à" leave open whether it is also claimed to be injective (S01).

**R02 — scan 331 — claim without proof — KEEP.**
For a chosen Q̄ ≃ Q̄₀, complex conjugation goes to "l'élt d'ordre 2 de Γ_Σ associé au sous-groupe de Teichmüller géométrique". The element is attached to the quasi-discretification, "orientée, mais peu importe". Neither the element nor the comparison is constructed on the page.

**R03 — scan 331 — own idea (first pass) — CORRECT.**
Comparing kernels and images is the right approach, but the left arrow is surjective by the definition of Σ, so only its injectivity is open. If (27) is an isomorphism, the short five lemma for groups shows that the middle arrow π₁(M_{g,ν}) → N_Σ is surjective, and that it is injective exactly when the left arrow is.

**R04 — scan 332 — conjecture — KEEP.**
(27): "La conjecture naturelle ici" is that Gal(Q̄/Q) → Γ_Σ "soit un iso", Γ_Σ being the arithmetic outer automorphisms of (π, Σ).

**R05 — scan 332 — conjecture — KEEP.**
The "encore plus hardie" conjecture is that Σ is normal in T̂̂(π) (⟺ Σ_{g,ν} normal in T̂̂_{g,ν}), i.e. N_Σ = T̂̂, "ou encore" (28) π₁(M_{g,ν}) ⥲ T̂̂(π) = Aut ext_lac(π), marked "Conjectural!!". Passing from N_Σ = T̂̂ to (28) also uses (27) and the injectivity discussed in R03 and S01.

**R06 — scan 332 — implication not pursued — KEEP.**
Under (28), Gal(Q̄/Q) would be the "quotient 'arithmétique'" of the outer loop automorphisms of π. Recognising Σ intrinsically is left open (see R16).

**R07 — scan 332 — claim without proof — KEEP (reading updated).**
π(U) is "canoniquement isomorphe" to π once an isomorphism of universal covers is chosen. The continuation on 333 places the indeterminacy in π₁(𝒰_{g,ν,Q̄}) ≃ Ŝ⁺, not in T̂⁺ (R09).

**R08 — scan 332 — own idea (first pass) — KEEP.**
Testing normality of Σ in a small type by explicit normaliser computations is a sensible probe. It is unaffected by the corrections.

**R09 — scan 333 — claim without proof — CORRECT.**
The ink reads "π₁(𝒰_{g,ν,Q̄}) ≃ Ŝ⁺", the extended Teichmüller group (an extension of T̂⁺ by π), not T̂⁺. The isomorphisms between 𝒰̃_{g,ν} and the universal cover deduced from Ũ form a torsor under this group. On outer classes the indeterminacy therefore acts through T̂⁺ = Σ (S02).

**R10 — scan 333 — claim without proof — KEEP.**
After a choice of Q̄ ≃ Q̄₀, π₁(U) gets an oriented quasi-discretification, "évidente d'ailleurs à définir directt, par voie transcend.te". Complex conjugation changes it into its opposite. No construction is given here.

**R11 — scan 333 — claim without proof — CORRECT.**
The parenthesis reads "(orientée, peu nous chaut)", not "pour un instant". The claim is that the subgroup T̂⁺ ⊂ T̂̂(π) does not depend on the choice of Q̄ ≃ Q̄₀ and is the same for the oriented and the unoriented class, so it is canonically attached to π. This is consistent with 331, where complex conjugation acts through an element of order 2 normalising Σ.

**R12 — scan 333 — conjecture or question — KEEP (reading updated).**
"On pourrait prendre un corps alg clos de car. 0 quelconque, pas la peine que ce soit [un | sur] Q̄, cf plus bas". The inserted word is unsettled, but both readings make the same claim, deferred to later pages.

**R13 — scan 333 — own idea (first pass) — KEEP, with a pointer.**
For algebraically closed fields of characteristic 0 too large to embed in ℂ, the usual tool is the invariance of the étale fundamental group of a smooth curve under extension of algebraically closed base fields in characteristic 0. This is a standard consequence of SGA 1, exposé XIII; I have not rechecked the numbered statement. It avoids a single embedding of the whole field.

**R14 — scan 334 — claim without proof, cancelled — CORRECT.**
The cancelled passage concerns "une courbe algébrique U" (not "logistique") over a field K of finite type, defined by Spec K → M_{g,ν} as Q-multiplicités. It asserts a cartesian square of fundamental groupoids Π₁(U), Π₁(𝒰_{g,ν}), Π₁(K), Π₁(M_{g,ν}) (capital Π; the word "hom" is doubtful). The passage is struck through, so it is a discarded draft, not a surviving claim.

**R15 — scan 334 — conjecture — KEEP.**
"Si la conjecture sur Γ est vérifiée", the data (π, Σ) would be equivalent to an algebraically closed extension Q̄ of Q with Galois group N_{T̂̂}(Σ)/Σ = Γ_Σ. The torsor Isom(Q̄₀, Q̄₁) (indices doubtful) would be identified with a set of oriented quasi-rigidifications inside Isom(Π̂_{g,ν}, π)/T̂°_{g,ν}. The antecedent is conjectural.

**R16 — scan 334 — conjecture — KEEP.**
"Σ serait uniquement déterminé par π !" No intrinsic characterisation of Σ is offered.

**R17 — scan 334 — own idea (first pass) — KEEP.**
Testing uniqueness by moving Σ with automorphisms that preserve the loop structure is a necessary check for any intrinsic recovery.

**R18 — scan 335 — conjecture — KEEP.**
Compatibility of the oriented quasi-rigidification with Σ is a "condition peut être automatiquement satisfaite…". The condition is not formulated separately.

**R19 — scan 335 — claim without proof (margin) — KEEP.**
"Si on se donne seulement π comme groupe ext., avec Σ, ceci revient à la donnée d'un rev. universel de M_{g,ν}." The correspondence is stated, not constructed.

**R20 — scan 335 — claim without proof — CORRECT.**
The corrected text reads: the curve with universal cover "reviendrait à" the previous data plus a point, and "cette dernière donnée s'identifierait à un germe de scindage de l'extension 1 → π₁(M_{g,ν,Q̄}) → π₁(M_{g,ν}) → π₁(ℚ) → 1. L'interprétation profinie (en termes des groupes ext. à lacets π) est alors un (π,Σ), et un germe de scindage de 1 → Σ → N_Σ → Γ_Σ → 1". The first pass's "et leur interprétation sur (π,Σ)" is gone. The translation between the two extensions presupposes that (26) is an isomorphism (R03, R05, S01).

**R21 — scan 335 — implication not pursued (margin) — KEEP.**
"Si on renonce à choisir Ũ, ceci équivaut [| revient] à travailler avec des groupes à lacets extérieurs". Forgetting the universal cover lowers the level of structure from groups to outer groups.

**R22 — scan 335 — claim without proof — KEEP.**
A curve over a finite subextension K′ corresponds to a partial splitting Γ′ → N_Σ over an open subgroup Γ′ ⊂ Γ_Σ. Descent and the equivalence relation on such partial splittings are not discussed.

**R23 — scan 336 — conjecture or question (deferred) — CORRECT.**
The ink reads "si cette description des courbes algébriques anabéliennes est pleinement fidèle, elle n'est pas 2-fidèle, i.e. il faut des conditions sur un scindage, qu'il faudra examiner par la suite". The word is "anabéliennes", not "conditionnelle", and "2-fidèle" is confirmed. From the "i.e." it follows that not every splitting germ comes from a curve (S04).

**R24 — scan 336 — claim without proof (proposed description) — CORRECT.**
The base is "un corps fixé de type fini [car. 0 | sur ℚ] K quelconque", not a finite field. The proposed data are (π, Σ), an identification of Q̄ with the field defined by (π, Σ) (Galois group N_Σ/Σ, so Γ₀ ≃ N_Σ/Σ), and a lift of Γ → Γ₀ to an outer action Γ → N_Σ. The lift comes "avec des conditions qu'il faudra essayer de dégager (néc. et suff. conjecturalement)".

**R25 — scan 336 — own idea (first pass) — KEEP.**
Separating the existence of a continuous lift of Γ → Γ₀ from the geometric admissibility of a lift matches the structure of the passage.

**R26 — scan 337 — claim without proof — KEEP.**
Every embedding K̄ ⊂ ℂ defines on π = π₁(U_{K̄}) an oriented quasi-discretification, replaced by its opposite under complex conjugation.

**R27 — scan 337 — claim without proof — KEEP (reading settled).**
"Je dis que" these quasi-discretifications all define Σ as group of automorphisms, and that the one defined by K̄ ↪ ℂ depends only on "sa restriction en Q̄ ⊂ ℂ", i.e. on Q̄ ≃ Q̄₀. Both claims are announced, not proved.

**R28 — scan 337 — implication not pursued — KEEP.**
"Mais il y a lieu d'examiner aussi la façon d'obtenir des discrétifications" opens the construction problem that 338 develops.

**R29 — scan 337 — own idea (first pass) — KEEP.**
Comparing two complex embeddings of K̄ that agree on Q̄ is the right test of the "ne dépend que de sa restriction" claim.

**R30 — scan 338 — claim without proof — CORRECT.**
The germ of splitting "fait opérer ext. le noyau de groupe défini par Γ_Σ sur π". The ink has "noyau", not "germe" (S05). The rational points over Q̄₀ "correspondants aux classes de π-conj. des germes de relèvement de cette opération ext. en une vraie opération". This point–lift correspondence is asserted without proof (S03).

**R31 — scan 338 — claim without proof — CORRECT.**
The ink reads "(lui-m̄ uniques car π^Γ = (1) !)". It is the operation, "l'op. définie mod autom intérieurs", that is said to be unique, not the inner automorphisms ("eux-mêmes"). With trivial fixed points, two lifts of the outer action that are conjugate are conjugate by a unique element of π, which is the uniqueness used. The vanishing π^Γ = (1) is stated without proof (S07).

**R32 — scan 338 — conjecture or question — KEEP.**
"Il faudrait définir dans π (≃ π₁(U_P, P)) une discrétification orientée π₀ ⊂ π, et pas seulement une quasi-discrétification orientée !" No construction is given.

**R33 — scan 338 — implication not pursued — CORRECT.**
The ink reads "dans la classe qu'on s'est donnée d'avance de discrétifications orientées, qu'on avait justement notée π₀^{♮+}". The class is the datum fixed on 337–338, not one "qui serait notée". The first pass's suggested check stays useful: what happens to the chosen representative when a lifting germ is replaced by a π-conjugate.

**R34 — scan 338 — conjecture or question (deferred) — KEEP.**
"On y reviendra — et à la situation analogue sur un corps de base K de type fini quelconque…". CSG/IUT drop "fini"; the ink has it.

**R35 — scan 338 — own idea (first pass) — KEEP.**
Separating the existence of a discretification preserved by an actual action from the uniqueness of comparisons is consistent with the corrected R31.

**R36 — scan 339 — claim without proof — KEEP.**
For the π₁ of a variety over K̄ ⊂ ℂ "il y a une discrétification mod autom. intérieurs", named a "prédiscrétification stricte".

**R37 — scan 339 — claim without proof — KEEP.**
The homogeneous set (the struck "espace", "dans T̂̂(π)") of strict prediscretifications "s'identifie à" Isom_lac(Π̂_{g,ν}, π)/Π̂_{g,ν}·𝒯_{g,ν} ≃ Isom ext_lac(Π̂_{g,ν}, π)/𝒯_{g,ν}. No proof is given.

**R38 — scan 339 — claim without proof — KEEP (reading updated).**
"Cette notion égal[eme]t ne dépend que du gr. [pr] à lacets extérieur défini par Π_{g,ν}". The independence from the representative is stated without proof.

**R39 — scan 339 — claim without proof — KEEP (reading updated).**
A strict prediscretification is equivalent to a "base extérieure" of π modulo 𝒯_{g,ν}, "i.e. d'un iso. de π avec le Π̂_{g,ν} 'type'" ("avec", not "sur"). The map from exterior bases to prediscretifications makes the source a torsor, "relatif à droite", under 𝒯_{g,ν}. Freeness and transitivity are not checked.

**R40 — scan 339 — claim without proof — KEEP.**
A prediscretification of (π, Σ) "correspond à un choix d'un isom" of the algebraic closure Q̄_{(π,Σ)} associated with (π, Σ) with Q̄₀. This presupposes the conjectural reconstruction of the field (R15).

**R41 — scan 339 — claim without proof — CORRECT.**
The inserted word is "doit": "toute prédiscrétification doit donner naissance à une discrétification stricte". This is an expectation, not an assertion (S06). The final clause reads "et une discrétification équivaut en remontant un germe d'action ('admissible') de Γ_Σ sur π …" and is unfinished. There is no "en revanche à", so no equivalence between discretifications and action germs is stated on this page.

**R42 — scan 339 — own idea (first pass) — KEEP.**
Drawing the two quotient operations before attempting the torsor statement is a reasonable consistency check.

**R43 — scan 340 — conjecture — KEEP (reading updated).**
"On prévoit" that a prediscretification "— compatible avec Σ —", which "revient moralement au plongement (mod conj. complexe) dans ℂ" of the algebraic closure attached to (π, Σ), gives rise to a true discretification once the arithmetic outer action of Γ_Σ = N_Σ/Σ is lifted to a germ of action. "Itou [| Idem]" for oriented ones.

**R44 — scan 340 — implication not pursued (margin) — KEEP.**
"[Moralement], Σ = T̂ (mais il n'y a pas de T !)". CSG/IUT read "Σ = T̂⁺, mais il n'y a pas de T̂ !"; a + after the hat is possible in the rotated note but not settled. The point stands: Σ plays the role of T̂⁺ without a discrete group underneath.

**R45 — scan 340 — implication not pursued — CORRECT.**
The elements of N_Σ/Σ are named "automorphismes arithmétiquement extérieurs de π". The two-stage quotient is built into the name: outer means modulo inner automorphisms, and arithmetically outer means further modulo the geometric ones (those in Σ). This parallels "action arithmétiquement extérieure" on 336.

**R46 — scan 340 — claim without proof — KEEP.**
The multiplier χ : T̂̂(π) → Ẑ* is trivial on Σ "par construction de Σ" and descends to χ_Σ : Γ_Σ → Ẑ*. The vanishing is referred to the construction of Σ, which is not reproduced here.

**R47 — scan 340 — own idea (first pass) — KEEP.**
Comparing ker χ_Σ with an arithmetic kernel is reasonable. According to the witnesses, the next page (outside this package) states that if "notre conjecture fondamentale" holds, χ_Σ identifies (Γ_Σ)^ab with Ẑ*.

## Second-pass notes (mine)

**S01 — scan 331 — conjecture or question (mine).**
The unfinished insertion "isomorphe à" after "l'image de π₁(M_{g,ν,Q̄})", together with the ≀ or 2 beside the left arrow of (26), asks whether π₁(M_{g,ν,Q̄}) → T̂̂(π) is injective, i.e. whether Σ ≅ π₁(M_{g,ν,Q̄}). Since π₁(M_{g,ν,Q̄}) is the profinite completion of the mapping class group, this is the congruence subgroup problem for mapping class groups. It is known in low genus (Asada for genus 0 and 1, Boggi for genus 2); I have not checked the present state for higher genus. The identifications in (26)–(28) and on 335 depend on it (R03, R05).

**S02 — scan 333 — implication not pursued (mine).**
With Ŝ⁺ read correctly, there is an extension 1 → π̂_{g,ν} → Ŝ⁺ → T̂⁺ → 1, the orientation-preserving part of CSG §26 (2′). The indeterminacy in π(U) ≅ π acts through inner automorphisms (from π̂) and through T̂⁺. Hence Σ = T̂⁺ is canonical, while the oriented quasi-discretification needs the choice Q̄ ≃ Q̄₀; this is the content of R11.

**S03 — scans 335, 338 — implication not pursued (mine).**
Two correspondences of the same shape occur. On 335, curves over Q̄ (and, over finite subextensions, curves over number fields) correspond to germs of splittings of π₁(M_{g,ν}) → Gal(Q̄/Q). On 338, rational points of U correspond to π-conjugacy classes of lifts of an outer action. The second is an early form of what Grothendieck later proposed as the section conjecture (letter to Faltings, 1983); the first is its analogue for the moduli stack. The "conditions sur un scindage" of 336 are the question which splittings are geometric. These notes do not assess the present status of that conjecture.

**S04 — scan 336 — conjecture or question (mine).**
"Pleinement fidèle, … pas 2-fidèle, i.e. il faut des conditions sur un scindage": here "2-fidèle" seems to name the property that every splitting germ comes from a curve, which would usually be called essential surjectivity. Before normalising, the term should be compared with Grothendieck's other uses of "n-fidèle" in the manuscript.

**S05 — scan 338 — conjecture or question (mine).**
"Qui fait opérer ext. le noyau de groupe défini par Γ_Σ sur π": the ink has "noyau", and CSG/IUT read "le noyau du groupe". What acts is the germ of open subgroups of Γ_Σ on which the splitting is defined. "Noyau" in the sense of a kernel does not fit, so Grothendieck may use "noyau de groupe" here for a group germ. Other occurrences should be checked before this passage is paraphrased.

**S06 — scans 339–340 — implication not pursued (mine).**
With "doit" inserted, the last paragraph of 339 states the same expectation that opens §27 on 340: "on prévoit qu'une pré-discrétification … donne naissance … à une vraie discrétification". The strict prediscretification is expected from (π, Σ) plus a lifting germ, and the discretification from an admissible action germ. The two passages should be cited together as one expectation, not as a theorem and a conjecture.

**S07 — scan 338 — own idea (mine).**
The vanishing π^{Γ'} = (1) needed for uniqueness could be checked through abelianisations of open subgroups of π. For a curve over a number field, these are extensions of Tate modules of Jacobians by cuspidal parts, and open Galois subgroups have no invariants on them, by weights. This would make the uniqueness in R31 unconditional over number fields. I have not traced a published reference for this exact statement.

**S08 — scan 340 — implication not pursued (mine).**
The heading "27. Changement de type (g,ν) : a) bouchage de trous" places the change of terminology, the arithmetic Teichmüller group N_Σ/Σ and χ_Σ at the start of a programme of filling punctures, (g,ν) → (g,ν−1). Grothendieck's table continues with "28. … b) passage à un revêtement fini" and "32. Retour sur les aspects arithmétiques du bouchage de trous: relations entre Γ_{g,ν} et Γ_{g,ν−1}". Any such comparison of the Γ_Σ has to respect the multipliers χ_Σ introduced here.


# ===== LM_P035 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P035 (scans 341–350): research notes checked against the corrected text

Section A takes the first pass's research notes (its §5, numbered 1–41) in order. Each note is kept, corrected or dropped against `LM_P035_checked.tex`, with the reason. Section B adds notes from this second pass, labelled as such. "Kind" is one of: Grothendieck's conjecture or question, claim without proof, implication not pursued, own idea.

## A. First-pass notes

1. **Scan 341: conjecture. KEEP.** "Si notre conjecture fondamentale est vraie, ce caractère identifie (Γ_Σ)_ab à Ẑ^*". The conditional statement concerns the cyclotomic ("multiplicateur") character of Γ_Σ; the conjecture itself is stated on earlier pages. *Reason:* the reading stands; only *s'appelera* and *et|ou* changed in that sentence.

2. **Scan 341: claim without proof. CORRECT.** The margin, read again as "(et|car) Σ/Σ^! ⥲ N/N^! (≃ 𝔖_I)", gives the reason for bijectivity. N/Σ^! → N/N^! × N/Σ is injective because Σ ∩ N^! = Σ^!, and surjective because Σ/Σ^! ⥲ N/N^! means N = N^!·Σ. The only input used without proof on this page is the surjectivity of Σ → 𝔖_I ("induit un hom surjectif"). *Reason:* the first pass asked for an argument that the margin in fact supplies.

3. **Scans 341–342: implication. KEEP (tightened).** N^!/Σ^! ⥲ N/Σ = Γ_Σ follows from N = N^!Σ and N^! ∩ Σ = Σ^!. So Γ_Σ can be described by outer automorphisms "induisant l'identité sur I". *Reason:* this is consistent with the corrected text.

4. **Scan 342: claim without proof. CORRECT.** In type (0,3), Σ^! = (1) gives Σ ⥲ 𝔖_3. N^! centralizes Σ because Σ → 𝔖_I is then injective and N^! acts trivially on I. Conversely, a centralizing element has central image in 𝔖_3, hence trivial image, so N^! is exactly the centralizer, and N = N^! × Σ. The product statement therefore follows from note 2 together with Z(𝔖_3) = 1; only Σ^! = (1) is asserted without justification here. *Reason:* the first pass said the claim needed earlier definitions only.

5. **Scan 342: claim without proof. CORRECT.** "Elles forment un torseur ⊂ Isom_lac(π̂_{g,ν},π) sous le sous-groupe Ĝ̂_{g;(ν,ν')} … formé des u ∈ Ĝ̂_{g,ν} dont l'image dans 𝔖_I invarie l'ens des ν' premiers éléments." The adapted bases are defined by the first ν' loop generators l_i (not b_i) lying in loop subgroups L_{i'}. Freeness is inherited from the Isom torsor; what needs checking is transitivity on adapted bases. *Reason:* corrected letters (l_i, 𝔖_I, u).

6. **Scan 342: implication not pursued. KEEP.** "Le passage de π à π' se décrit par le chgt de groupe d'opérateurs Ĝ̂_{g,(ν,ν')} → Ĝ̂_{g,ν'}." Grothendieck does not discuss whether filling holes one at a time agrees with filling them all at once. *Reason:* as written.

7. **Scan 342, left margin: claim without proof, plus an open case. CORRECT.** The margin is largely legible: "D'où aussi : ∀ base, ∀ discrétification, discrétification orientée de π en définit une de π', d'où itou pour classes de (π,π') conjugaison, i.e. pour les prédiscrétifications (év. orientées) strictes, et aussi pour les adhérences des classes, i.e. les … prédiscrétifications et prédiscrétifications orientées. Il n'y a que le cas des arithmétis. qui demanderait une analyse plus fine…". So it asserts that all these structures pass from π to π', and singles out (pre)arithmetizations as the open case, which is the question of scan 343. *Reason:* the first pass labelled it a question with an illegible wording.

8. **Scan 343: Grothendieck's question. KEEP.** "En déduit-on une préarithmétisation de π' — i.e. (p.ex.) si deux prédiscrétifications de π … ont le m̄ groupe Σ ⊂ T̂̂(π) d'autom extérieurs, en est-il de m̄ de leurs images ?" Grothendieck answers it on scan 347, as a conjecture. *Reason:* the reading is confirmed.

9. **Scan 343: claim without proof. KEEP.** "Toute prédiscrétification de π en définit une de π', de m̄ pour les prédiscrétifications strictes, discrétifications, bases extérieures 'adaptées à I''." Well-definedness on the equivalence classes is not checked. *Reason:* as written.

10. **Scan 343, cancelled block: cancelled formulation. KEEP.** It is not a claim. *Reason:* the whole bracket is struck.

11. **Scan 343: claim without proof. KEEP.** A total order on I gives the sub-torsor Isom^!_lac(π̂_{g,ν},π) under Ĝ̂^!_{g,ν}. *Reason:* as written.

12. **Scan 343: own idea (first pass). KEEP.** It is still a sensible test and is not a claim of the manuscript.

13. **Scan 344: claim without proof. CORRECT.** The diagram identifies the following quotients:
    - discrétifications orientées ≃ Isom_lac/G^+ ≃ Isom^!_lac/G^{!+};
    - prédiscrétifications orientées strictes ≃ Isom_lac/G^+·π̂ ≃ Isom ext_lac/T^+ ≃ Isom^!/G^{!+}·π̂ ≃ Isom ext_lac/T^{!+};
    - prédiscrétifications orientées ≃ Isom_lac/Ĝ^+ ≃ … ;
    - préarithmétisations ≃ Isom_lac/M ≃ Isom ext_lac/N ≃ … .

    {±1} arrows lead to the bracketed, unoriented versions. Every identification needs a stabilizer computation, and none is given. *Reason:* the node names and lines are corrected. The central node is *pré*discrétifications, which the reading-edition witnesses lose.

14. **Scan 344: own idea (first pass), "pas séparé". KEEP, strengthened (confidence medium).** The two braces enclose exactly the nodes that are quotients by the discrete groups G^+_{g,ν} and G^+_{g,ν}·π̂_{g,ν}. These groups are not closed in the profinite group acting on the torsor, so their quotient spaces are not Hausdorff. The node quotiented by the closure Ĝ^+ has no brace. *Reason:* where the braces stand supports the reading.

15. **Scan 345: claim without proof. KEEP (argument supplied).** The isomorphism Γ_Σ ⥲ Γ_Σ' is obtained from any outer isomorphism à lacets u carrying α to α'; "ne dépend évidemment pas du choix de u". Two choices differ by an element s of Σ. For n ∈ N_Σ, sns^{-1} ∈ Σn, so conjugation by s is trivial on N_Σ/Σ, and this justifies "évidemment". *Reason:* the u is now confirmed in the ink.

16. **Scan 345: claim without proof. KEEP.** The pairs (π,Σ) form a connected groupoid Π_{g,ν}, with origin (π̂_{g,ν}, Σ_{g,ν}) and automorphism group Γ_{g,ν}. The capital Π here names the groupoid, which the hand draws differently from the group π̂_{g,ν}. *Reason:* as written.

17. **Scan 345: own idea (first pass). KEEP.** A different isomorphism between two choices of origin changes the identification by an element of Γ_{g,ν}. "À isom unique près" must therefore be read in a category where that ambiguity is removed, as in note 15. *Reason:* still a valid question about scope.

18. **Scan 346: claim without proof. CORRECT.** The text reads "mais pas nécess. sous Ĝ̂(π,I'), voire sous Ĝ̂^!(π)", not "pas même". The two base spaces are homogeneous under Ĝ̂(π) but *not necessarily* under the smaller groups. The other four spaces are quotients of the Ĝ̂^!_{g,ν}-torseur (double hat) Isom^!(π̂_{g,ν},π) by G^!, G^!·π̂, Ĝ^! and M^!. The stabilizers still need checking. *Reason:* the reading is corrected; the first pass made the negative claim stronger than the ink.

19. **Scan 346: claim without proof. KEEP.** For I' = {i_0,…,i_{ν'−1}}, a homomorphism Ĝ̂^!_{g,ν} → Ĝ̂^!_{g,ν'} sends G^! to G^!, π_{g,ν} to π_{g,ν'}, π̂ to π̂, "Ĝ^!_{g,ν} = Σ^!_{g,ν}" to "Ĝ^!_{g,ν'} = Σ^!_{g,ν'}", and G^!·π̂ to G^!·π̂. See note B1 on the written equalities Ĝ^! = Σ^!. *Reason:* as written.

20. **Scan 346: claim without proof (lemma). KEEP.** "Lemme. Le N^!_Σ = Norm_{T̂̂^!}(Σ^!)", with the margin "pas prouvé". The argument stops at "montrons qu'il normalise Σ", and scan 347 opens with "C'est pas clair." *Reason:* the reading is confirmed.

21. **Scan 346: own idea (first pass), functoriality of normalizers. KEEP.** *Reason:* the general caution is correct.

22. **Scan 347: conjecture. CORRECT (wording).** "J'ai pourtant envie de [prouver|prononcer] la conjecture: L'application canonique Prédiscrét^+(π) → Prédiscrét^+(π') est bijective." The verb is uncertain: *proposer* is excluded and *prouver* is the witnesses' reading. *Reason:* the verb reading.

23. **Scan 347: claim without proof. CORRECT.** The ink has "il faut et s[uffit] qu'il en soit ainsi pour leurs images". The equivalence Σ_α = Σ_β ⇔ Σ_α' = Σ_β' is asserted without proof, and it is exactly what lets the bijection on prédiscrétifications descend to a *bijection* Préarithm.(π) ⥲ Préarithm.(π'). The reading editions print only "il faut", which would give a map but not its injectivity. *Reason:* both directions are needed, and the ink states both.

24. **Scan 347: claim without proof. KEEP.** The bijections are compatible with T̂̂(π,I') → T̂̂(π'), and "a fortiori" with T̂̂^!(π) → T̂̂^!(π'). This gives N^!_Σ → N^!_Σ', and Γ_Σ = N^!_Σ/Σ^! → Γ_Σ' = N^!_Σ'/Σ'^! "est un isomorphisme". *Reason:* as written.

25. **Scan 347: inconsistency in the manuscript. KEEP, sharpened.** The text says "le cas où I' = {i}", with I' "réduit au dernier élément de I_ν" (reading uncertain). But the sequel fills the hole i: π' = π̂_{g,ν−1}, and automorphisms fixing i act on π'. With the convention of scan 342 (the retained set is the first ν' indices), the filled set I∖I' is {last element}, so ν' = ν−1 and I' ≠ {i}. *Reason:* the notation conflicts on the page; this is not a transcription problem.

26. **Scan 347: claim without proof. KEEP.** Outer automorphisms à lacets fixing i define actual automorphisms of π' ("pas seulement extérieurs"). This is the construction behind "Ĝ̂(π,i) → Ĝ̂(π') est trivial sur π" on scan 348; independence of representatives still needs proof. *Reason:* as written.

27. **Scan 348: admitted assumption. KEEP.** "J'admets, en analogie avec le cas discret, que cet hom. est un iso", that is, T̂̂(π,i) → Ĝ̂(π'). Everything that follows on 348–349 is conditional on it. *Reason:* only *en* replaces *par*.

28. **Scan 348: implication not pursued. CORRECT.** As written, the sequences are:
    - 1 → π' → T̂̂(π,i) → Ĝ̂(π',i) → 1;
    - 1 → π̂_{g,ν−1} → T̂̂_{g,(ν,ν−1)} → Ĝ̂_{g,ν−1} → 1, which contains the discrete 1 → π^*_{g,ν−1} → T_{g,(ν,ν−1)} → G_{g,ν−1} → 1 "comme sous-suite exacte".

    The three-row diagram has T^{!+}_{g,ν}, T̂^{!+}_{g,ν} and T̂̂^!_{g,ν} in the middle column and G-groups on the right. The first pass had G in the middle. *Reason:* the letters are corrected; see note B3 for the third terms.

29. **Scan 348: claim without proof. KEEP.** "On lit sur le diagramme" that T̂̂^!_{g,ν}/T̂^!_{g,ν} ≃ Ĝ̂^!_{g,ν−1}/Ĝ^{!+}_{g,ν−1}. The quotient on the left is written with T̂^! and the one on the right with Ĝ^{!+}; the transcription keeps that difference. *Reason:* as written.

30. **Scan 348: own idea (first pass), diagnostic in finite quotients. KEEP.** *Reason:* reasonable, and not a claim of the manuscript.

31. **Scan 349: claim. CORRECT.** The text reads "on sait *bien* qu'alors T/Σ ≃ T'/Σ', et que le normalisateur N de Σ dans T est l'image inverse du normalisateur N' de Σ' dans T'". Since π' is normal in T and π' ⊂ Σ ("Σ … entre π' et T"), both statements are the correspondence theorem. The step is therefore justified; the non-trivial inputs are the admitted identification T' ≅ Ĝ̂^!_{g,ν−1} and Σ' ≅ Ĝ^{!+}_{g,ν−1}. *Reason:* the first pass treated it as needing unstated hypotheses.

32. **Scan 349: corollary, conditional. KEEP.** Γ_{g,ν} ⥲ Γ_{g,ν−1} ⥲ …, hence Γ_{g,ν} ≃ Γ_{g,0} (g ≥ 2), Γ_{1,ν} ≃ Γ_{1,1}, Γ_{0,ν} ≃ Γ_{0,3}. It depends on the admitted fact. *Reason:* as written.

33. **Scan 349: Grothendieck's expressed doubt. CORRECT.** The doubted statements are surjectivity of T̂̂_{g,(ν,ν−1)} → [T̂̂|M̂̂]_{g,ν−1} and injectivity of T̂̂_{g,(ν,ν−1)} → Ĝ̂_{g,ν−1}, followed by "est peut-être tout à fait faux !". The target glyph has no M-shaped middle, and the witnesses read T̂̂_{g,ν−1}. That reading fits a doubt about surjectivity onto the outer group of type (g,ν−1). *Reason:* the target reading is corrected.

34. **Scan 349: reformulation. CORRECT.** "Le fait admis revient à un énoncé d'existence d'un foncteur en sens inverse 'forage des trous', qui en tout état de cause reste incompris." The first pass's "des étal. de courbes" misreads "tout état de cause", so the line contains no étale covers of curves. The reformulation adds no hypothesis; it says that the inverse construction is not understood. *Reason:* a misreading had added mathematical content to the line.

35. **Scan 349: own idea (first pass). KEEP.** *Reason:* a reasonable research strategy.

36. **Scan 350: claim without proof. CORRECT.** "Soit π un groupe profini à lacets de type (g,ν), anabélien comme tjrs, π' un ss-groupe d'indice fini. On sait ([ou on] vérifie, par passage au cas discret) que muni des traces [sur|sous] π des ss-groupes à lacets de π, π' est un groupe à lacets." The first pass lost the standing hypothesis *anabélien* (2g+ν ≥ 3), reading it as "considérons/considéré". *Reason:* a dropped condition is restored.

37. **Scan 350: claim without proof. KEEP.** The loop subgroups of π' are "[aussi|ainsi]" characterized by a) L' = Centr_π(L') ∩ π' and b) L = Centr_π(L') being a loop subgroup of π. Their centralizer behaviour relies on the anabelian hypothesis restored in note 36. *Reason:* as written.

38. **Scan 350: claim without proof. KEEP.** Formula (1): Σ_{i' ∈ I' sur i} d(i') = n, with n = [π:π']. *Reason:* as written.

39. **Scan 350: claim without proof. KEEP (tightened).** "On aura la formule de Hurwitz" (2): 2g'−2 = n(2g−2) + Σ_{i'}(d(i')−1). The overbrace n·card I − card I' follows from (1) by summing over i ∈ I; the genus relation itself is announced, not proved. *Reason:* the elementary part is separated from the announced part.

40. **Scan 350: own idea (first pass), orbits of the loop subgroup on π/π'. KEEP.** *Reason:* still the natural route to (1).

41. **Scans 349–350: own idea (first pass). KEEP.** *Reason:* the distinction between the two problems is still useful.

## B. Notes added in the second pass (Claude)

B1. **Scans 342 and 346: implication not pursued (Claude).** The inserted vertical relation reads Ĝ_{g,(ν,ν')} ⊂ Σ_{g,ν}, with "= Σ_{g,(ν,ν')}"; the same line has "Ĝ_{g,ν'} = Σ_{g,ν'}", and scan 346 has "Ĝ^!_{g,ν} = Σ^!_{g,ν}". These equate single-hatted *automorphism* groups with subgroups Σ of the *outer* group T̂̂ (scan 345: Σ_{g,ν} = T̂^+_{g,ν}). Making them precise needs the passage Aut → Out (quotient by π̂) and the orientation condition +. They should not be read literally. Confidence: medium.

B2. **Scan 346: own idea (Claude).** For 0 < ν' < ν, the "pas nécess." is in fact a "not". Ĝ̂(π,I') preserves which subset of I(π) a base sends the first ν' standard loops to, so it cannot act transitively on Bases(π). Likewise Ĝ̂^!(π) acts trivially on I, so it cannot change the bijection a base induces between {1,…,ν} and I(π) unless ν ≤ 1. Confidence: high, granted that a base induces that bijection (scan 342).

B3. **Scan 348: own idea, possible slip in the manuscript (Claude).** Suppose the admitted isomorphism T̂̂(π,i) ≅ Ĝ̂(π') holds and π' has trivial centre (anabelian case). Then the quotient of T̂̂(π,i) by π' (inner automorphisms) is T̂̂(π'), an *outer* group. The third terms written as Ĝ̂(π',i), Ĝ̂_{g,ν−1}, G_{g,ν−1} and G^{!+}_{g,ν−1} are therefore probably meant as the outer groups, and the NB on 349 doubts surjectivity onto T̂̂_{g,ν−1} if that glyph is T. The quotient sets used afterwards are unaffected, because Ĝ̂^!/Ĝ^{!+} and T̂̂^!/T̂^{!+} are the same set Prédiscrét^+(π'). This is a question about the manuscript; the transcription keeps the letters of the ink. Confidence: medium.

B4. **Scan 349: implication not pursued (Claude).** The corollary stops at the minimal types allowed by the standing hypothesis 2g+ν ≥ 3: (g,0) for g ≥ 2, (1,1) and (0,3). The excluded (1,0) and (0,2) are exactly the non-anabelian types. This matches the margin of 348 and the restored "anabélien" of 350. Confidence: high (arithmetic check).

B5. **Scan 350: implication not pursued (Claude).** Formula (2) with its overbrace gives 2g'−2+card I' = n(2g−2+card I). Since anabelian means 2g−2+card I > 0, π' is anabelian whenever π is, so "anabélien comme tjrs" is inherited by finite-index subgroups. Confidence: high (arithmetic check).

B6. **Scan 341: claim without proof (Claude).** The page leaves unproved the statement that T̂̂ → 𝔖_I "n'est pas trivial sur Σ, mais induit un hom surjectif Σ → 𝔖_I". It needs Σ to permute the punctures transitively in every way. That holds for the discrete oriented Teichmüller group, whose image is all of 𝔖_I, and passes to the closure Σ = T̂^+. Confidence: medium; it depends on the definition of Σ on earlier pages.


# ===== LM_P036 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P036 — research notes checked against the corrected transcription (scans 351–360)

Source: Grothendieck, *La Longue Marche à travers la théorie de Galois* (1981), Montpellier archive, cote 140-2, scans 351–360 (authorial folios 178–182). Quotations follow `LM_P036_checked.tex`; π stands for the doubtful Π/π group letter, and 𝔊 for the looped C/G letter.

Each first-pass note is given a verdict (keep, correct or drop) with the reason. Notes S1–S9 are new; they are labelled [2nd pass] and are the checking model's own.

## First-pass notes, checked

### Scan 351

1. **351 — implication not pursued (formula (3)).** Keep. The relation 2g′−2+card I′ = n(2g−2+card I) and the NB, which identifies 2g−2+card I as minus the compactly supported Euler–Poincaré characteristic, say that this invariant is multiplied by the index n on passing to π′. *Reason:* agrees with the corrected text; the hypotheses (n the index, I′ over I) are on the preceding scan.

2. **351 — claim without proof (orientations).** Correct. „De façon évidente, tte discrétification de π en définit une de π′” (the first pass read „en fournit”). For oriented discrétifications Grothendieck needs „un peu de cohomologie”: that H²_!(U,ℤ) → H²_!(U′,ℤ) has the form nθ⁻¹, with θ: T′ ⥲ T the trace isomorphism. This is stated „dans le cas discret p.ex.”, without proof. *Reason:* the verb has been corrected, and the insertion is not cancelled.

3. **351 — question.** Correct. „Il n'est pas clair pour moi à première vue” whether Discret(π) → Discret(π′) is surjective, or injective. The inserted quantifier „(pour tt π′ d'indice fini de π)” qualifies „L'injectivité”: injectivity for every finite-index π′ would mean that two commensurable discrétifications of π are equal. *Reason:* the first pass placed the insertion after the display, so that the quantifier applied to the map itself.

### Scan 352

4. **352 — conjecture.** Keep, with an addition. Under „Conjecture”, restriction Discret(π) → Discret(π′) for an invariant π′ is injective. Its image consists of the π′₀ for which G → Autext_lac(π′) ≃ 𝒯̂̂(π̂′₀) factors through 𝒯(π′₀) = Autext_lac(π′₀), „et en fait, m̄ par 𝒯(π′₀)⁺”. The ink defines „G = π′/π”, a slip for π/π′. *Reason:* the note agrees with the text; the slip is recorded (see S3).

5. **352 — claim without proof (right-hand note).** Keep. „En d'autres termes”: ∀g ∈ π ∃h ∈ π′ with int(hg)(π′₀) ⊂ π′₀, i.e. π′·π₀ = π, for π₀ the normaliser of π′₀ in π. The equivalence is stated without argument. *Reason:* unchanged. The first factor (π′ or π′₀) is still palaeographically doubtful, but only π′ makes the product meaningful, since π′₀ ⊂ π₀.

6. **352 — claim without proof (reconstruction).** Correct. π is recovered from the outer action of G on π̂′₀ because Centre(π̂′) = 1. Grothendieck identifies it as „le complété profini de l'extension π₀ de G par π′₀”; a first wording, „l'adhérence dans …”, is struck. Compatibility with the „structure à lacets de π” is explicitly left to check („mais il faut voir”). *Reason:* the cancelled words are now read; the substance is unchanged.

7. **352 — the first-pass model's own idea.** Keep. The suggestion is to separate the descent into an extension problem, a completion problem and a lacet-compatibility problem. *Reason:* still a sound plan, grounded in the manuscript's own distinction between profinite structure and lacet structure.

### Scan 353

8. **353 — claim without proof (centralisers).** Keep. The lacet structure of π is recovered from the lacet subgroups L′ of π′ and „leurs Centralisateurs dans π”. Doing the same for π₀ ⊃ π′₀, „on devrait trouver” a lacet structure on π₀ „donnant lieu aux m̄s dᵢ”. *Reason:* only the reading of „m̄s dᵢ” changed.

9. **353 — conjecture / expectation.** Correct. The ink reads: „ça ne doit pas être très vache à prouver — ce serait comme si on savait déjà que tte extension "sans torsion" d'un groupe fini par un groupe à lacets est (de la façon canonique) un groupe à lacets” (the first pass read „ce serait connu si”). Grothendieck presents the claim as amounting to that general statement, not merely as a consequence of it. *Reason:* the corrected word changes the logical relation between the claim and the general statement.

10. **353 — claim without proof (Corollaire).** Keep. „En tous cas”, Discret(π) → Discret(π′) „est injective”. This rests on the uniqueness half of the argument on scan 352 (π₀ recovered as an extension), not on the open lacet compatibility. *Reason:* unchanged.

11. **353 — conjecture (NB).** Keep, with the corrected reading „une action extérieure d'un G dans un π̂′₀”. Failure of bijectivity is expected from homomorphisms G → 𝒯̂̂(π̂′₀) that do not factor through 𝒯(π′₀) but are as good profinitely as those coming from a π and an invariant subgroup. *Reason:* only the determiner changed.

12. **353 — the first-pass model's own idea.** Keep. The test is whether failure to preserve an embedded discrete subgroup distinguishes outer actions with isomorphic profinite extensions. *Reason:* this is the construction Grothendieck attempts on scan 354.

### Scans 354–356

13. **R354.1 — 354, claim without proof.** Keep. Conjugating G by γ gives an extension π^♮ that is „isomorphe à π̂₀ (en tant qu'extension de G par π̂′₀)” and carries the induced lacet structure. Yet the chosen discrétification π₀ of π̂₀ „n'est pas induite par une de π”. Neither the identification nor the non-inducibility is proved. *Reason:* unchanged.

14. **R354.2 — 354, claim without proof.** Correct. The surviving criterion reads: „il suffit de trouver un élément γ ∈ 𝒯̂̂(π′₀) tel que int(γ).G ⊄ 𝒯, i.e. tel que G ne stabilise pas γ⁻¹(π′₀) ⊂ π̂′₀”. It replaces a struck „ne fixe” and a framed, cancelled version for a single element u of finite order (γuγ⁻¹ ∉ 𝒯(π′₀)). „Un tel γ existe tjrs....” is asserted without argument. *Reason:* the first pass left „ne stabilise pas” doubtful and missed the struck words and the frame.

15. **R354.3 — 354, the first-pass model's own idea.** Keep. For G = 1 the criterion int(γ).G ⊄ 𝒯 can never hold, so the construction needs G ≠ 1, i.e. π′₀ ≠ π₀. *Reason:* a correct formal check. In the manuscript G is the quotient of an actual situation π₀ ⊃ π′₀, so this is a hypothesis to state, not an objection.

16. **R355.1 — 355, conjecture.** Keep. „Je présume” that if π₀, π₁ are „adhérents” to one another (define the same prédiscrétification), the same holds for π′₀, π′₁. *Reason:* unchanged.

17. **R355.2 — 355, conjecture.** Correct. The expected bijection (4) is Prédiscrt⁺(π) → Prédiscrt⁺(π′). The + superscripts are settled (darker ink, apparently added in revision), so the statement concerns oriented prédiscrétifications. Injectivity and surjectivity remain separate requirements, and scan 360 doubts the property (b) that corresponds to surjectivity. *Reason:* the superscript is no longer doubtful; possible blotted subscripts are recorded in the transcription (S8).

18. **R355.3 — 355, conjecture.** Correct. The bijection (5) is written with the label „Préarith” (possibly „Préarithm”) and is the quotient of (4). It requires compatibility with a further equivalence relation, and scan 360 doubts the property (b) it would need. *Reason:* the label reading has been corrected.

19. **R355.4 — 355–356, conjecture.** Keep. For corresponding Σ, Σ′ one expects „un isom canonique” 𝕀Γ_Σ → 𝕀Γ_Σ′, compatible with the actions on P_Σ and P_Σ′. *Reason:* unchanged.

20. **R355.5 — 355, the first-pass model's own idea.** Keep. The factorisation π = π′·π₀ converts a π-conjugation into a π′-conjugation; scan 357 carries this out. *Reason:* confirmed by scan 357 (see S5 for why π = π′·π₀ holds).

21. **R356.1 — 356, claim without proof.** Keep. The sets of compatible prédiscrétifications are said to be „resp. des torseurs à g. sous 𝕀Γ_Σ, 𝕀Γ_Σ′”, which is not established on these pages. *Reason:* unchanged.

22. **R356.2 — 356, conjecture.** Correct. „On trouvera donc que Σ est unique pour π, i.e. invariant dans 𝒯̂̂(π), ssi Σ′ est unique dans π′, i.e. invariant dans 𝒯̂̂(π′)”. The word „unique” is settled. *Reason:* the reading is settled; the content is unchanged.

23. **R356.3 — 356, conjecture.** Keep. The „conjecture fondamentale”, which interprets the 𝕀Γ_{g,ν} as 𝕀Γ₀ = Gal(ℚ̄₀/ℚ), „aurait au moins comme conséquence” that Discr⁺(π) — Discr⁺(π′) passes to the quotient in two ways. *Reason:* unchanged.

24. **R356.4 — 356, implication not pursued.** Keep. Cartesianness of the lower square identifies P with A ×_{A′} P′. Grothendieck explicitly does not prejudge injectivity or bijectivity of A → A′. *Reason:* unchanged.

25. **R356.5 — 356, the first-pass model's own idea.** Keep. A two-point A over a one-point A′, with P = A × P′, gives a cartesian square with A → A′ not injective. *Reason:* the example is correct.

### Scans 357–358

26. **357, note 1 — claim without proof.** Correct. „On aura π = π′·π₀” is not a gap. π′ is open of finite index in π, and π₀ is dense in π (π̂₀ = π), so π₀ meets every coset of π′ and π′π₀ = π. *Reason:* the first pass said a justification was missing; it follows at once from the definitions (S5).

27. **357, note 2 — conjecture / expectation.** Keep. „Tâchons de procéder de m̄ … en écrivant si possible u = u′u₀”, with u′ in the completion of the group of automorphisms of π₀ that stabilise π′₀ (inserted qualifier „discrets”, doubtful) and u₀ ∈ 𝔊(π₀)⁺. The factorisation is explicitly tentative. *Reason:* unchanged.

28. **357–358, note 3 — claim without proof.** Keep. „Il n'y a aucun Pb si π′ est un ss-groupe caractéristique de π”: restriction is then a discrete homomorphism 𝔊(π₀) → 𝔊(π′₀) inducing 𝔊̂(π̂₀) → 𝔊̂(π̂′₀). The passage to the completion is not justified further. *Reason:* unchanged. The ink shows a blotted subscript under π′, but the surviving reading is π′.

29. **358, note 4 — claim without proof.** Keep, with a justification added [2nd pass]. The reduction uses that the open characteristic subgroups of π are cofinal. π is topologically finitely generated, so it has finitely many open subgroups of each index, and their intersection is open, characteristic and contained in π′. *Reason:* the first pass left the justification open; this standard argument closes it.

30. **358, note 5 — implication not pursued.** Correct. The circled (a), (b), (c) separate the inverse-image condition, the congruence condition and preservation of the normaliser M. Scan 360 calls (a) „assez plausible” and (b) „beaucoup plus problématique, voir douteuse”. *Reason:* the first pass quoted „voire douteuse”; the ink has „voir”.

31. **358, note 6 — claim without proof.** Keep. „NB il est immédiat que c'est injectif” is said of 𝔊̂̂(π₀) ↪ 𝔊̂̂(π′₀), without argument. *Reason:* unchanged.

32. **357–358, note 7 — the first-pass model's own idea.** Keep. The suggestion is to test the factorisation u = u′u₀ in finite quotients. *Reason:* a reasonable plan; unchanged.

### Scans 359–360

33. **359, note 1 — claim without proof.** Keep. Condition (d) (M(π) „exactement l'image inverse” of M(π′)) and condition (e) (congruence mod M(π′₀)) are the translations of injectivity and surjectivity of A(π) → A(π′). *Reason:* unchanged.

34. **359, note 2 — claim without proof.** Correct. Conditions (c), (a), and (b) with (d) are said to suffice to define the map Γ_{π₀} → Γ_{π′₀}, for its injectivity, and „(pour sa bijectivité|surjectivité”. The last word is overwritten and remains doubtful. S7 checks that (b) with (d) gives surjectivity, so the division is define / injective / surjective whichever word stands. *Reason:* the reading is now marked doubtful.

35. **359, note 3 — conjecture.** Keep. The bracketed downward arrow with a question mark between the two M's in (7), and „à charge de prouver a) b) … et c)”, show that these properties are still to be proved. *Reason:* unchanged.

36. **359, note 4 — claim without proof (cancelled).** Keep. The struck paragraph „D'ailleurs d) sera conséquence immédiate de a), et du fait (qui assurera c)) que tt élément de 𝔊̂⁺(π₀) est congru mod 𝔊…” is an abandoned derivation. *Reason:* „assurera” is now settled; the verdict is unchanged.

37. **360, note 5 — conjecture / question.** Correct. „Peut. être les propriétés a) b) c) sont elles tout à fait fausses”; (a) „semble assez plausible”, while (b) is „beaucoup plus problématique, voir douteuse”. *Reason:* the quotation is corrected to „voir” (not „voire”). The status of (b) is doubtful, not false: the witnesses' „voir fausse” is a misreading.

38. **360, note 6 — claim without proof.** Keep. 𝔊̂̂(π₀) can be „beaucoup plus petit” than 𝔊̂̂(π′₀). This is seen by quotienting by π̂′₀ = π′, which lies in the smallest group of (7) and is invariant in the largest. *Reason:* unchanged.

39. **360, note 7 — claim without proof.** Keep. The upper row of (8) consists of extensions of the corresponding groups for π₀ by the finite group G = π₀/π′₀ ≃ π/π′, marked with tildes. *Reason:* unchanged.

40. **359–360, note 8 — implication not pursued.** Keep. The second bracketed question mark, in (8), shows that the comparison of the middle groups survives the quotient. The conclusion after „qui montre que” lies beyond scan 360. *Reason:* unchanged.

41. **359, note 9 — the first-pass model's own idea.** Keep. The suggestion is a group-theoretic lemma separating existence, injectivity and surjectivity of the induced quotient map. *Reason:* unchanged; S6 and S7 carry out part of it.

42. **360, note 10 — the first-pass model's own idea.** Keep. The suggestion is to look for an invariant obstructing (b), starting from G and its normalisers. *Reason:* unchanged.

## Second-pass notes (new, the checking model's own)

**S1. 351 — implication not pursued [2nd pass].** By (3), −EP_!(U′) = n·(−EP_!(U)), so the anabelian condition −EP_! ≥ 1 passes to every finite covering, as the margin says. The integer that „mesure … le degré d'anabélianité” is itself multiplied by the degree. Along a tower of coverings, only its positivity, or its ratio to the index, is invariant.

**S2. 351 — claim without proof, argument supplied [2nd pass].** The „peu de cohomologie” is the degree formula. For a degree-n covering U′ → U of oriented surfaces, pull-back H²_!(U,ℤ) → H²_!(U′,ℤ), read through the trace isomorphisms, is multiplication by n. Since n > 0, a generator of T (an orientation) goes to a positive multiple of a generator of T′, so oriented discrétifications restrict to oriented ones.

**S3. 352 — note on the text [2nd pass].** The ink writes „posons G = π′/π”. π′ is the invariant subgroup, so the intended group is π/π′, as the later uses show („G = π₀/π′₀ ≃ π/π′”, scan 360). Both witnesses silently correct the slip.

**S4. 354 — claim without proof [2nd pass].** The equivalence „int(γ).G ⊄ 𝒯, i.e. G ne stabilise pas γ⁻¹(π′₀)” presupposes that 𝒯(π′₀) is the stabiliser, in 𝒯̂̂(π′₀), of the π̂′₀-conjugacy class of the discrete subgroup π′₀ ⊂ π̂′₀. Given that identification, int(γ)G ⊂ 𝒯(π′₀) holds exactly when G stabilises γ⁻¹(π′₀) up to conjugacy. The existence claim „Un tel γ existe tjrs” then needs G ≠ 1 and a γ that moves π′₀ off the G-stable discrétifications. Neither is argued on this page.

**S5. 357 — the checking model's own idea [2nd pass].** π = π′·π₀ holds whenever π′ is open in π and π₀ is dense: every coset gπ′ is open and therefore meets π₀. This settles the step the first pass called unjustified, and it is the only property of π′ that the conjugacy argument on scan 357 uses.

**S6. 359 — check of a claim [2nd pass].** The margin „plus faible que (b)” beside (e) is correct. M(π′₀) is the normaliser of 𝔊̂⁺(π′₀) and therefore contains it, so congruence modulo 𝔊̂⁺(π′₀) implies congruence modulo M(π′₀). Hence (b) implies (e), provided both refer to the image of the same group 𝔊̂̂(π₀).

**S7. 359 — check of a claim [2nd pass].** Write Γ = M/𝔊̂⁺; the denominators in the display are written 𝔊̂(π₀), 𝔊̂(π′₀). Given m′ ∈ M(π′₀), (b) gives m′ = y·k with y in the image of 𝔊̂̂(π₀) and k ∈ 𝔊̂⁺(π′₀) ⊂ M(π′₀). Then y ∈ M(π′₀), and by (d) its preimage lies in M(π₀): so (b) and (d) give surjectivity. (a) gives injectivity once (c) defines the map, so the word expected in „(pour sa …ectivité” is „surjectivité”, the witnesses' reading, even though the overwritten ink may show „bijectivité”.

**S8. 355, 357 — note on the text [2nd pass].** A revision in darker ink adds the + superscripts in (4). The same ink blots what look like subscripts 0 in all four arguments of (4) and (5), and under π′ in „si π′ est un ss-groupe” on scan 357. If the blots are cancelled subscripts, the comparisons were first written for π₀, π′₀ and then restated for π, π′ and oriented objects. Confidence: low, because the blots cannot be read.

**S9. 360 — question [2nd pass].** Grothendieck calls (b) „beaucoup plus problématique, voir douteuse”: doubtful, not false. He finds (a) plausible and leaves (b) open, which is why the page goes on to compare the groups in diagram (8). The witnesses' „voir fausse” would make the doubt about the surjectivity of P(π) → P(π′) stronger than the manuscript states.


# ===== LM_P037 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P037 (scans 361–370): research notes checked against the corrected text

Each first-pass note (R01–R41) is kept, corrected or dropped against `LM_P037_checked.tex`; the checker's own notes follow as C01–C07. Kinds: conjecture/question, claim without proof, implication not pursued, first-pass model's idea, checker's own note.

## First-pass notes

**R01 — scan 361 — claim without proof — keep.** Grothendieck writes that "on doit pouvoir montrer sans trop de mal" that the tilde groups of (8) are exactly the normalisers of $G$ in $\widehat{\mathcal T}^{+}(\pi'_0)$ and $\widehat{\widehat{\mathcal T}}(\pi'_0)$, which would imply a). No argument is given; both inclusions need proof. Reason: the reading ("on doit" replacing a struck phrase) is confirmed.

**R02 — scan 361 — conjecture (doubt) — correct.** He sees "aucune raison plausible" for b), which would mean that the "transcendance arithmétique" defined by the very large $\widehat{\widehat{\mathcal T}}(\pi'_0)$ *modulo* $\widehat{\mathcal T}(\pi'_0)^{+}$ is no more than that defined by the much smaller $\widetilde{\widehat{\widehat{\mathcal T}}}(\pi_0)=\operatorname{Norm}(G)$. Reason: the first pass read "(ou le $\widehat{\mathcal T}(\pi'_0)^{+}$)", an alternative group; the ink has "(mod …)", so the comparison is made modulo the geometric subgroup.

**R03 — scan 361 — implication not pursued — keep.** Conditions a) and b) are "pas impératives" for the coherence of the fundamental conjecture relating the $\pi_{g,\nu}$ (letter doubtful: $\pi$ or $\Pi$) to $\Gamma_0$; condition c) is then called indispensable. The logical dependencies of the conjecture on a), b), c) are left unstated.

**R04 — scan 362 — claim without proof — keep, with addition.** c) is needed to define $A(\pi)\to A(\pi')$ and $\Gamma_\Sigma\to\Gamma_{\Sigma'}$ when $\pi'$ is characteristic in $\pi$, and a) "donne les injectivités qu'il faut". On scan 363 he grades these hypotheses: the injectivities are "relativement anodines", c) is "pas anodine du tout".

**R05 — scan 362 — question — keep (reading settled).** Why should an element of $\widehat{\widehat{\mathcal T}}(\pi'_0)$ that lies in the normaliser $R$ of $G$, "donc aussi $R$" (so it normalises $R$), and normalises $R\cap\widehat{\mathcal T}^{+}(\pi'_0)$, normalise $\widehat{\mathcal T}^{+}(\pi'_0)$ itself? The heavily revised sentence now reads without doubt in this form.

**R06 — scan 362 — conjecture (expectation) — keep.** The drastic hypotheses (starting from a characteristic subgroup $\pi'_0$ of $\pi_0$) "doivent bien se refléter" in special properties of $G\subset\mathcal T^{+}(\pi'_0)$, and he doubts that the reduction to characteristic subgroups is as clever as it looked. The properties are not identified.
