**R07 — scan 362 — first-pass model's idea — keep.** Search finite triples $(E,K,G)$ with an element of $N_E(G)$ that normalises $N_E(G)\cap K$ but not $K$, then ask which hypotheses of the manuscript exclude them. Medium value as a logical test of the implication questioned in R05; a finite example says nothing directly about the profinite groups.

**R08 — scan 363 — proposed investigation — correct.** Two test cases: $\pi'$ of index 2 ($G\simeq\mathbb Z/2$), and the kernel of $\pi\to(\pi_{\mathrm{ab}})_2$, with $(\pi_{\mathrm{ab}})_2\simeq(\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z)^{2g}$ for $\nu=0$ (the ink cancels a "+" after $2g$) and $(\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z)^{2g\pm\nu-1}$ for $\nu\ge1$. The ink does not settle the sign; the abelianisation of a group of type $(g,\nu)$ with $\nu\ge1$ is free of rank $2g+\nu-1$, so "+" is the correct mathematics and the witnesses' "−" is wrong.

**R09 — scan 363 — claim without proof — keep.** The example $\pi=\widehat\Pi_{0,3}$ is called the "situation de la courbe de Fermat $x^2+y^2+z^2=0$ (revêtement octaédral de $\mathbb P^1\setminus\{0,1,\infty\}$)" without justification. C02 below gives the computation.

Reason: the first pass misread "pas anodine du tout" as "pour en déduire des …" and doubted "toute la force"; everything constructed on 364–366 rests on these assumptions.

## Checker's own notes

**C01 — scan 361 — checker's own note (check of a claim without proof).** The NB says $G\subset\mathcal T^{+}(\pi'_0)$ and that "$\pi_0$ se reconstitue à partir de $\pi'_0$ et de ce sous-groupe" ($G=\pi_0/\pi'_0$). This holds whenever $\pi'_0$ has trivial centre, as surface groups of hyperbolic type do: an extension of $G$ by a centreless group is determined by its outer action, $\pi_0\simeq\operatorname{Aut}(\pi'_0)\times_{\operatorname{Out}(\pi'_0)}G$. Confidence high; it explains why the NB can treat $G$ as a subgroup of $\mathcal T^{+}(\pi'_0)$.

**C02 — scan 363 — checker's own note (computation for R09/R11).** For $\pi=\widehat\Pi_{0,3}$, $(\pi_{\mathrm{ab}})_2\simeq(\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z)^2$ and the kernel gives the Klein-four cover of $\mathbb P^1$ of degree 4, ramified with index 2 over $0,1,\infty$; Riemann–Hurwitz gives genus 0, and it is the conic $x^2+y^2+z^2=0$ mapped by $(x^2:y^2)$, branched over $0,\infty,-1$. The six points over the branch points ($xyz=0$) are the fixed points of the three commuting involutions, i.e. the vertices of an octahedron, and the normaliser of the Klein group in $\mathrm{PGL}_2$ is the octahedral group: a plausible reason for "revêtement octaédral" (confidence medium for this interpretation, high for the computation).

**C03 — scan 364 — checker's own note (check).** "En d'autres termes" is exact: $J_p\cap J_q=\varnothing$ iff the complements satisfy $\overline J_p\cup\overline J_q=J$ (De Morgan). The ink writes "$J_p,J_q\in J$" where an inclusion is meant; the witnesses' "⊂" is a normalisation.

**C04 — scans 365–366 — implication not pursued (checker).** A connected groupoid is equivalent to the category of torsors under the automorphism group of any object $X_0$, via $X\mapsto\operatorname{Isom}(X_0,X)$; changing $X_0$ changes the equivalence by an inner automorphism. So "s'identifie à la cat. des torseurs sous $\Gamma_0$" needs a reference object, which is what the transitive system of isomorphisms among the $(\widehat\pi_0,\overline\Sigma_{\pi_0})$ on 366 supplies (making the reference object canonical up to unique isomorphism). Confidence high for the general fact, medium for this reading of his intent.

**C05 — scans 367–368 — checker's own note (check of R26–R27).** By the transport formula, $i_{u(\pi_0)}=i_{\pi_0}$ for all $u\in\widehat{\widehat{\mathcal T}}(\pi)_i$ means exactly $\operatorname{int}(u)\circ i_{\pi_0}=i_{\pi_0}\circ u_{\pi'}$: equivariance of $i_{\pi_0}$ for action (1) on $\pi'$ and conjugation on the target. Equivariance gives normality of the image and the stated induced action; the converse needs the action condition on the image *and* injectivity of $i_{\pi_0}$, so normality alone is weaker. Confidence high, given the formula as written.

**C06 — scan 369 — claim without proof (checker).** In (7) $H_{\pi_0}\cap\widehat{\widehat{\mathcal T}}(\pi)_i=H_{\pi_0,i}$ only "⊂" follows from the definitions: an element of $H_{\pi_0,i}$ satisfies the compatibility at $i$, and the equality needs it at every other $j\in I$. (8) $H\supset\widehat{\mathcal T}(\pi_0)$ needs, besides transport of structure for the discrete $\mathcal T(\pi_0)$, that $H$ is closed. Confidence medium, since "dans un sens évident" is not made precise.

**C07 — scan 370 — implication not pursued (checker).** The "structure simpliciale d'extensions successives" for $\nu\ne0$ plausibly comes from the hole-filling maps for subsets $J\subset I$ (forgetting punctures, Birman-type exact sequences), with face maps indexed by the subsets; compatibility of the Galois action with all of them is then a concrete constraint on the image of $\Gamma_{\mathbb Q}$ in $\widehat{\widehat{\mathcal T}}_{g,\nu}$. Confidence medium; it links this paragraph to the hole-filling maps (1) of the same section.


# ===== LM_P038 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P038 (scans 371–380): research notes checked against the corrected text

Each note gives its scan, its kind and a short statement, followed by the verdict on the first-pass note (keep, correct or drop) with the reason. Notes 1–40 are the first pass's notes, numbered as in `LM_P038_notes.md` §5. Notes S1–S7 are the second pass's own notes and are labelled as such. Quotations refer to `LM_P038_checked.tex`.

## First-pass notes

1. **Scan 371 — conjecture.** "le groupe s'envoie (on présume injectivement) dans $\widehat{\widehat{\mathcal G}}(\pi_{g,\nu})$": the map from $\pi_1(\mathcal U_{g,\nu,\mathbb Q})$ is presumed injective. **Correct:** the source group is $\pi_1(\mathcal U_{g,\nu,\mathbb Q})$, the fundamental group of the universal curve over Q, with no base point. The first pass's "α|a" was the subscript Q.

2. **Scan 371 — claim without proof.** $M_{g,\nu}$ is a closed subgroup with $\widehat{\mathcal G}_{g,\nu}\subset M_{g,\nu}\subset\widehat{\widehat{\mathcal G}}_{g,\nu}$, $\widehat{\mathcal G}^+_{g,\nu}$ is normal in it but $\widehat{\mathcal G}_{g,\nu}$ is not, and giving $M_{g,\nu}$ is equivalent to giving $N_{g,\nu}$ as in (11). **Correct:** the parenthesis "(mais pas $\widehat{\mathcal G}_{g,\nu}$ !)" is attached by a line to "normal dans $M_{g,\nu}$". It denies normality, not the inclusions of (10), which the first note treated as possibly qualified.

3. **Scan 371 — conjecture.** (13): the surjection $\Gamma_{\mathbb Q}\to\Gamma_{g,\nu}$ is presumed bijective, "i.e. que les $\Gamma_{g,\nu}$ sont can. isom. entre eux". **Keep:** the corrected text is unchanged here.

4. **Scans 371–372 — claim without proof.** $\Gamma_{g,\nu}$ contains a canonical element of order 2, $\tau_{g,\nu}$, "correspondant aux éléments de $\widehat{\mathcal T}_{g,\nu}-\widehat{\mathcal T}^+_{g,\nu}=\widehat{\mathcal T}^-_{g,\nu}$", and (19) gives $\tau_\alpha^2=1$, $\tau_\alpha\ne1$. **Correct:** the margin reads "correspondant aux", not "comme provenant des". The element is the common image of all orientation-reversing classes. They have one image because $\widehat{\mathcal T}^-$ is the single non-trivial coset of $\widehat{\mathcal T}^+$ in $\widehat{\mathcal T}$, and that image has order 2 by (12).

5. **Scan 372 — claim without proof.** After transport of structure by an isomorphism $\pi_0\simeq\pi_{g,\nu}$, "le résultat n'en dépend pas". **Keep:** the independence follows from (10). Two transporting isomorphisms differ by an automorphism of the discrete group $\pi_{g,\nu}$, which lies in $\widehat{\mathcal G}_{g,\nu}\subset M_{g,\nu}$, so conjugating by it preserves $M_{g,\nu}$ and $N_{g,\nu}$. The text leaves this step unstated.

6. **Scan 372 — implication not pursued.** From a prediscretification α one gets $M_\alpha$, $N_\alpha$, $\Gamma_\alpha$ and $\tau_\alpha$ (16)–(19), and their behaviour under isomorphisms of pairs $(\pi,\alpha)$ is not written down. **Keep.**

7. **Scan 372 — the first pass's own idea.** It proposed drawing the single-hat and double-hat groups before interpreting the arithmetic statements. **Correct:** the two queried hats are slips that Grothendieck flags himself. Comparison with (11), (14) and (16) shows that the left term of (15) should have one hat and the right term of (17) two. The marginal queries "? $\widehat{\mathcal T}$" and "$\widehat{\widehat{\mathcal T}}$ ?" propose exactly these corrections. CSG/IUT print the corrected hats without saying so.

8. **Scan 373 — claim without proof.** $\Gamma_{g,\nu}$ acts freely on the right on $P(\pi)$, and $A(\pi)\simeq P(\pi)/\Gamma_{g,\nu}$ (21)–(22). **Keep:** this follows from (12) once $M_{g,\nu}$ normalises $\widehat{\mathcal G}^+_{g,\nu}$, which the text states but does not prove.

9. **Scan 373 — claim without proof.** For $\alpha\in P_a$, $\widehat{\mathcal G}^+_\alpha$ does not depend on α ("on le notera $\widehat{\mathcal G}_a$") and is the kernel of the action of $M_a$ on $P_a$. **Correct:** the parenthesis most probably introduces notation ("on le notera"), not "vu la notat.". The two other readings are kept as doubtful in the text.

10. **Scan 373 (margin) — implication not pursued.** "mais. Attention $\widehat{\mathcal G}_\alpha$ dépend de α i.e. $\tau_\alpha\in\Gamma_a$ dépend de α." **Correct:** the superscript the first pass read as a star may be a cancelled "+". On either reading, the group that depends on α is the one without the plus, and $\tau_\alpha$ depends on α through it.

11. **Scan 374 — claim without proof.** $\Gamma_a$ is the commutant of $\Gamma_{g,\nu}$ acting on $P_a$ ($\Gamma_{g,\nu}$ twisted by $P_a$), and the choice of α gives (27), changed by an inner automorphism when α changes. **Keep:** this is the standard description of the automorphism group of a torsor. It needs the transitivity and kernel statements of note 9.

12. **Scan 374 — claim without proof.** Exterior arithmetised groups of type $(g,\nu)$ form a connected groupoid with fixed origin $(\widehat\pi_{g,\nu},a_{g,\nu})$. **Keep.**

13. **Scan 374 — claim without proof.** The automorphism group is $N_{g,\nu}$, an extension of $\Gamma_{g,\nu}$ by $\widehat{\mathcal T}_{g,\nu}$, "$\simeq\pi_1(M_{g,\nu\,\mathbb Q})$", with the margin "si $\Gamma_{\mathbb Q}\to\Gamma_{g,\nu}$ injectif !". **Correct:** "injectif" is legible. The comma after $\widehat{\mathcal T}_{g,\nu}$ and the marginal condition show that "$\simeq\pi_1(\dots)$" concerns $N_{g,\nu}$ itself, which favours Q over Q̄ (see S4). The kernel is written without "+".

14. **Scan 374 — withdrawn claim.** The cancelled clause says that the groups $\widehat\pi_0$ from discrete groups are "can. isom." in this category. **Correct:** the same statement reappears, uncancelled, on scan 376 for the category whose morphisms are the arithmetically exterior isomorphisms and whose automorphism group is $\Gamma_{g,\nu}$. The cancellation moves the claim to that category; it does not abandon it.

15. **Scan 374 — the first pass's own idea.** It proposed fixing one element of the torsor $P_a$, computing the identification (27) and then changing the element. **Keep:** the computation shows the inner automorphism stated in the text.

16. **Scan 375 — claim without proof.** Giving an object is "la = chose (à isom unique près)" as giving a universal cover $\widetilde M_{g,\nu\,\mathbb Q}$ of $M_{g,\nu}$, or an $N_{g,\nu}$-torsor. **Correct:** the abbreviation for "même" is an "=" sign. The base of the covered space is written over: a Q̄ with an added Q. The intended base is probably Q (see S4).

17. **Scan 375 — claim without proof.** The family (28) is taken as "la" courbe algébrique whose exterior $\pi_1$ "soit le groupe ext. à lacets donné". **Correct:** the verb is "soit", not "est". It states a required property of the pulled-back curve, not an assertion about it.

18. **Scan 375 — implication not pursued.** For non-exterior arithmetised groups, the automorphism group is $M_{g,\nu}\simeq\pi_1(\mathcal U_{g,\nu\,\mathbb Q})$, and an object amounts to a universal cover of $\mathcal U_{g,\nu\,\mathbb Q}$ that "permet en effet de servir de" reference cover for the relative curve (28). **Keep:** the wording is corrected from the doubtful "permet|pourrait … servir".

19. **Scan 375 — the first pass's own idea.** It proposed working with finite étale covers first and then passing to the inverse system. **Keep.**

20. **Scan 376 — implication not pursued.** The reference cover makes the fundamental group of (28) "un vrai groupe à lacets (pas seulement un groupe ext.)". **Keep.**

21. **Scan 376 — claim without proof.** In the category with "isomorphismes arithmétiquement extérieurs" as morphisms, the groupoid is connected and the automorphism group of the marked origin is $\Gamma_{g,\nu}$. **Correct:** the underlined word is "isomorphismes", which is no longer doubtful. The origin is written $(\pi_{g,\nu},a_{g,\nu})$ without a hat, whereas the witnesses add one.

22. **Scan 376 — claim without proof.** "Maintenant les groupes $\widehat\pi_0$ … sont can. isom. entre eux." **Keep:** this is the statement withdrawn on scan 374 (note 14), here in the category where it is meant to hold.

23. **Scan 376 — conjecture.** The category is "(conjecturalement, admettant $\Gamma_{\mathbb Q}\to\Gamma_{g,\nu}$ iso)" equivalent to that of algebraic closures of Q. **Keep:** the formal part (connected groupoids with isomorphic automorphism groups) should be kept apart from the conjecture.

24. **Scan 377 — claim without proof.** To $(\pi,a)$ there corresponds "donc canoniquement" an algebraic closure Q̄, and the $\Gamma_{\mathbb Q}$-torsor Isom($\overline{\mathbb Q}_0,\overline{\mathbb Q}$) is in one-to-one correspondence with $P_a$. **Correct:** the text reads "canoniquement", not "de … canonique", and the inserted group is $\Gamma_{\mathbb Q}$, not $\Gamma_0$.

25. **Scan 377 — claim without proof.** Every α defines $\tau_\alpha\in\Gamma_a$ of order 2, with $\tau_\alpha=\tau_{-\alpha}$. **Keep.**

26. **Scan 377 — claim without proof, conditional.** If $\Gamma_{\mathbb Q}\simeq\Gamma_{g,\nu}$, then (29) induces a bijection between $P_a/\pm1=P_a^{\natural}$ and the elements of order 2 of $\Gamma_a$. **Correct:** the superscript is the natural sign, not an arrow pair. With ♮, the notation is that used elsewhere in the manuscript for non-oriented objects.

27. **Scan 378 — claim without proof.** The "fait connu": the involutions of $\Gamma_{\mathbb Q}$ are the conjugates of τ, and the centraliser of τ is $\{1,\tau\}$. **Keep:** see S5 for the standard argument.

28. **Scan 378 — implication not pursued.** A non-oriented discretisation $\alpha^{\natural}$ amounts to an archimedean valuation on the Q̄ defined by $(\pi,a)$ ("topologie" is cancelled), i.e. to an isomorphism $\overline{\mathbb Q}_1\simeq\overline{\mathbb Q}_0$ modulo complex conjugation. **Correct:** the subscript 1 is written. The text thus names the closure defined by $(\pi,a)$ $\overline{\mathbb Q}_1$ inside the parenthesis, after calling it $\overline{\mathbb Q}$ just before.

29. **Scans 377–378 — the first pass's own idea.** It proposed writing (30) as $g\langle\tau\rangle\mapsto g\tau g^{-1}$: conjugacy gives surjectivity and $C(\tau)=\{1,\tau\}$ gives injectivity. **Keep:** this is exactly the content of "Cela provient du fait connu".

30. **Scan 378 — the first pass's own idea.** It proposed testing the construction over a number field K. **Keep, with a precision (second pass):** in $G_K$ every involution is a complex conjugation (Artin–Schreier), and its centraliser is still $\{1,\tau\}$. Involutions are conjugate in $G_K$ exactly when they induce the same real place of K, so the analogue of (30) would split according to the real places of K.

31. **Scan 379 — conjecture / aspiration.** Grothendieck wants properties suggested by the arithmetic-geometric context that "puissent se formuler de façon purement algébrique" and are strong enough to characterise the $N_{g,\nu}$. **Correct:** "puissent" may not be struck. "qui puissent … et qui soient" is a parallel construction and is kept as an alternative.

32. **Scan 379 — claim without proof, heuristic.** $(\pi,a)$ corresponds to an algebraic closure and, if π is exterior, to a family over $\widetilde M_{g,\nu\overline{\mathbb Q}}$. **Keep.**

33. **Scan 379 — claim without proof.** Reducing the family to one curve over Q̄ "doit revenir à se donner un noyau de relèvement" of $N_a\to\Gamma_a$ (kernel $\widehat{\mathcal T}^+_a$). **Correct:** the word is "noyau", not "moyen" (see S6 for its meaning).

34. **Scan 380 — conjecture / question.** A lifting over an open $\Gamma'$ is said to amount to a curve over the finite extension $K'$ cut out by $\Gamma'$. A large "?" stands in the margin, and "l'ext." is circled with "hum-|hom-" above it. **Keep.**

35. **Scan 380 — conjecture.** The admissible liftings have special properties, some deep ("du genre Weil"), but "peut être" consequences of much simpler ones. **Keep.**

36. **Scan 380 — conjecture.** The properties "devraient … être stables par passage à un sous-groupe ouvert plus petit" ("invariantes" is cancelled). **Correct:** the subject is probably "Les propriétés à dégager", with "ainsi" struck. The second g and the ending of "dégager" lie outside the strike line, so the first pass's deletion of the whole phrase is doubtful.

37. **Scan 380 — conjecture.** The germs of liftings should lift further to $\widehat{\widehat{\mathcal G}}(\pi)$, in a way that is also "admissible" "en un sens à préciser". **Keep.**

38. **Scan 380 — conjecture.** There should be "beaucoup" of such lifts, counted in "(classes de π-conj.)"; the sentence continues on the next scan. **Keep:** the witnesses continue "pour un germe d'action extérieure … a 'beaucoup de points' rationnels", i.e. many rational points on the curve.

39. **Scan 380 — the first pass's own idea.** The "?" may concern field of moduli versus field of definition. **Keep:** a lifting over $\Gamma'$ gives descent data. Whether the curve is defined over $K'$ is the question raised by "l'ext." and the "?", and the next scans' "points rationnels" bear on it.

40. **Scans 379–380 — the first pass's own idea.** It proposed testing whether the extension splits after restriction to smaller open subgroups. **Keep.**

## Second-pass notes

S1. **Scan 371 — claim without proof (second pass).** The opening recalls that the transcendental construction from a differentiable reference surface gives $\widetilde M_{g,\nu\,\mathbb C}$ and, probably, $\widetilde{\mathcal U}_{g,\nu\,\mathbb C}=\widetilde M_{g,\nu+1\,\mathbb C}$: universal covers of Teichmüller type. Hence it gives canonical universal covers $\widetilde M_{g,\nu\,\mathbb Q}$ and $\widetilde{\mathcal U}_{g,\nu\,\mathbb Q}$, relative to which $\pi_1(\mathcal U_{g,\nu,\mathbb Q})$ is taken. The identification of the universal curve over the cover with the cover of type $(g,\nu+1)$ is stated without argument.

S2. **Scan 371 — implication not pursued / probable slip (second pass).** "induisant un isomorphisme entre son sous-groupe $\pi_1(\mathcal U_{g,\nu\,\overline{\mathbb Q}_0})$ avec $\widehat{\mathcal G}(\pi_{g,\nu})$" has no "+" (checked at 2×). The factors in (9) are $\widehat\pi_{g,\nu}$ and $\widehat{\mathcal T}(\pi_{g,\nu})^+$, so the geometric subgroup maps onto $\widehat{\mathcal G}^+$. If the image were $\widehat{\mathcal G}$, it would be normal in $M_{g,\nu}$, contrary to "(mais pas $\widehat{\mathcal G}_{g,\nu}$ !)". The inclusion $\widehat{\mathcal G}\subset M_{g,\nu}$ of (10) is then accounted for by the image of complex conjugation, which is orientation-reversing.

S3. **Scan 374 — probable slip (second pass).** "$N_{g,\nu}$, ext de $\Gamma_{g,\nu}$ par $\widehat{\mathcal T}_{g,\nu}$": by (12) the kernel of $N_{g,\nu}\to\Gamma_{g,\nu}$ is $\widehat{\mathcal T}^+_{g,\nu}$. The plus is missing in the ink, and CSG/IUT supply it silently.

S4. **Scan 374 — implication not pursued (second pass).** The margin "si … injectif !" is exactly the condition for $\pi_1(M_{g,\nu\,\mathbb Q})\to N_{g,\nu}$ to be an isomorphism. The geometric parts $\pi_1(M_{g,\nu\,\overline{\mathbb Q}})\to\widehat{\mathcal T}^+_{g,\nu}$ correspond and $\Gamma_{\mathbb Q}\to\Gamma_{g,\nu}$ is onto, so the map of extensions is onto, and it is injective if and only if $\Gamma_{\mathbb Q}\to\Gamma_{g,\nu}$ is. This supports Q (not Q̄) in "$\pi_1(M_{g,\nu\,\mathbb Q})$" on scan 374 and in the corrected base field on scan 375.

S5. **Scan 378 — claim without proof (second pass pointer).** The "fait connu" follows from Artin–Schreier: an involution of $\Gamma_{\mathbb Q}$ has a real closed fixed field, and all real closures of Q in Q̄ are conjugate. The centraliser statement follows because an element commuting with τ preserves the real closed field $R=\overline{\mathbb Q}^{\tau}$, and R has no automorphism other than the identity, since its order is defined by squares and Q is dense in it.

S6. **Scan 379 — question (second pass).** "se donner un noyau de relèvement" is an unusual phrase. Scan 380 goes on to liftings given on an open subgroup $\Gamma'$ and to "ces germes de relèvements", so "noyau" may stand here for what is later called a germ of a lifting. The ink supports "noyau", and the sense remains to be fixed from the following pages.

S7. **Scan 380 — conjecture (second pass).** The cancelled "invariantes", replaced by "stables" (and probably "Les propriétés à dégager"), shifts the requirement from invariance to inheritance by restriction to smaller open subgroups. Conditions defining "admissible" germs should therefore pass to restrictions. Whether they can be recovered from a restriction is a separate question that the page does not raise.


# ===== LM_P039 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P039 (scans 381–390): research notes, checked against `LM_P039_checked.tex`

Each first-pass note is kept, corrected or dropped, with the reason. Notes marked "first-pass model's idea" are that model's proposals. Notes marked "checker" are new. Quotations follow the corrected transcription; "(x|y)" marks a reading that stays uncertain.

## First-pass notes

**R381-1** · scan 381 · claim without proof / expectation · **keep.**
Grothendieck links the abundance of π-conjugacy classes of lifting germs to the "fait naïf" that the curve U has "beaucoup de pᵗˢ rat/Q̄". He then says that it would suffice to treat liftings to a true action of Γ_a on π, whose exterior actions are obtained through M_a → N_a ≃ M_a/π. No correspondence between rational points and lifting germs is proved here. The corrected text changes nothing the note relies on.

**R381-2** · scan 381 · implication not pursued · **keep.**
Subgroups conjugate under π, "voire même, parfois, sous M_a", are not regarded as distinct. The "parfois" leaves the equivalence relation open. The reading is unchanged.

**R382-1** · scan 382 · requirement (hypothesis), not a claim · **correct.**
The ink reads "On veut sûrement, pour de tels sous-groupes ouverts (b) π^{Γ′} = (1)", not "On aura évidemment". Condition (b) is therefore demanded of Γ and its open subgroups, and the page then calls it "l'hyp.". It is not asserted as evident, so it needs no proof here; the open question is whether arithmetic Γ satisfy it.

**R382-2** · scan 382 · claim without proof · **correct.**
The rigidity of the category of Q̄-points of U is "paradigmée par celle des germes de (scindages|scindage) de l'extension (1)′", so it concerns germs of splittings (homomorphic sections), not of arbitrary sections. With this reading the claim follows directly from the definitions (checker's note C2).

**R382-3** · scans 382–383 · first-pass model's idea · **keep, with precision.**
The obstruction to surjectivity of (3) lies in nonabelian continuous cohomology. The Γ′-equivariant sequence 1 → π → Ĝ_a⁺ → T̂_a⁺ → 1 gives the exact sequence of pointed sets 1 → π^{Γ′} → (Ĝ_a⁺)^{Γ′} → (T̂_a⁺)^{Γ′} → H¹(Γ′, π). So the image of (3) is the kernel of the connecting map. Confidence: high for the exact sequence; its use here is the model's proposal.

**R383-1** · scan 383 · conjecture / expectation · **correct the quotation.**
The text reads "par notre dictionnaire hypothétique, devrait être [canoniquement] (le|iso) groupe des automs de la courbe U sur K′". The word after "canoniquement" is uncertain, either "le" or an abbreviation of "isomorphe". The note's point stands: the dictionary is hypothetical, and the passage from finiteness at each Γ′ to the inductive limit (stated to be finite) rests on its geometric identification.

**R383-2** · scan 383 · claim without proof · **keep.**
Finiteness of Z "exprime que les groupes d'automorphismes des points de M_{g,ν Q̄} sur Q̄ sont finis". The identification of Z with those groups is not proved. The note's caution about the type (g, ν) is apt, since finiteness holds in the hyperbolic range.

**R383-3** · scans 383–384 · conjecture / expectation · **correct the quotation.**
The text reads "on voudrait que Z soit conjugué dans ~~T̂_a⁺~~ M_a à un sous-groupe de 𝒯⁺ (si on suppose qu'on dispose d'une discrétification π₀ … sinon … il existe une discrétification π₀ de π, compatible avec a, telle que Z ⊂ 𝒯(π₀)…)". The first pass had "ceci suppose" here. The substance (a demand stronger than finiteness) is unchanged.

**R383-4** · scan 383 · first-pass model's idea · **keep.**
The three statements are genuinely distinct: finiteness for each Γ′, finiteness of the germ, and conjugacy into a discrete 𝒯(π₀). The corrected text does not connect them.

**RN-384-1** · scan 384 · claim without proof · **keep, with an addition.**
"Cette image doit être d'ordre 1 ou 2" is conditional on Γ_a ≃ Gal(Q̄/Q). The circled symbol after "≃" is glossed by the marginal "Gal(Q̄/Q) →". Given that identification, the claim is the Artin–Schreier theorem: the only nontrivial finite subgroups of Gal(Q̄/Q) have order 2 and are generated by complex conjugations. The identification itself is the conjectural part.

**RN-384-2** · scan 384 · programme note · **correct.**
The ink has "Ce cas devrait être encore réutilisé dans la suite…", not "considéré". So the order-2 (complex conjugation) case is meant to be used again later, not merely examined.

**RN-384-3** · scan 384 · implication not pursued · **correct.**
The margin reads "ce n'est sans doute pas automatique (si on ne suppose pas N_a = Norm_{T̂̂}(T̂_a))". The first pass lost the negation and read "N′, N′_a". The note's separation of the two hypotheses (a compatible discrete model, and realizability) still holds. The doubt about "G ⊂ 𝒯⁺" is now explicitly tied to whether N_a is the whole normalizer (checker's note C3).

**RN-384-4** · scan 384 · first-pass model's idea · **keep.**
Fixing G and asking which discretifications it preserves remains a sensible test. Nothing in the corrected text answers it.

**RN-385-1** · scan 385 · claim without proof · **keep.**
"Elle est donc réalisable aussi (par|pour) une action de G sur une structure complexe, donc algébrique, ce qui signifie qu'on peut trouver un germe de relèvement admissible Γ_a → N_a qui centralise G." The first pass had "alors". The passage from realization to an admissible lifting germ is not constructed.

**RN-385-2** · scan 385 · claim conditional on conjectures · **correct.**
The text invokes "les conjectures standard topologiques", not conjectures that are "strictement/seulement" topological. So the identification of T̂_a^{+G} with the automorphism group, in the isotopy category, of the G-action on U is explicitly conditional on that named set of conjectures, which are not stated on these pages.

**RN-385-3** · scan 385 · implication not pursued · **correct.**
The ink reads "l'hom. (4) serait à noyau fini, donc serait un iso (des|de) noyaux de groupes"; the final x of "noyaux" is clear, and the first pass had "germes". The argument as written needs an isomorphism of germs of groups (after shrinking to open subgroups). The word on the page is nevertheless "noyaux", and the transcription keeps it.

**RN-385-4** · scan 385 · first-pass model's idea · **keep.**
Writing (4) as an extension with kernel T̂_a^{+G} and comparing local sections remains useful. The corrected subscript N_a (not M_a) makes (4) the map Centr_{N_a}(G) → Γ_a, with N̂_a written under it.

**RN-386-1** · scan 386 · claim without proof · **keep.**
A unique centralizing lifting germ "signifierait qu'il y a une seule façon de réaliser l'opération topologique de G sur U par une opération analytique complexe, donc algébrique". The correspondence is not proved.

**RN-386-2** · scan 386 · claim (checkable) · **correct.**
The text reads "c'est (dans le contexte topologique) une multiplicité (schématique|arithmétique) qui peut être de dim (ℝ|ℂ) quelconque — Elle est de dim > 0 en tous cas, si le quotient U/G n'est pas de genre 0". The first pass had "arithmétique" without an alternative. The positivity claim is correct (checker's note C5).

**RN-386-3** · scans 386–387 · conjecture / expectation · **keep.**
Property (d) is "plausible": N_a ∩ Z(G) → Γ_a "a une image ouverte (et il y a m̄ des germes de relèvements admissibles Γ_a → Z(G))". The two scopes (all finite G ⊂ T̂_a⁺, or only G ⊂ 𝒯⁺) remain distinct.

**RN-386-4** · scan 386 · first-pass model's idea · **keep.**
The proposed test (fix the quotient orbifold and compare with the fixed locus) is answered in general by the standard dimension formula in C5.

**387, ll. 2–10** · scan 387 · claim without proof (conditional implication) · **keep.**
The margin now reads securely "[à] savoir : ∃n ∈ ℕ* tel que gⁿ soit congru mod T̂_a⁺ à un élément de Z(G)…". The derivation (an open subgroup of a profinite group has finite index, so every g has a power inside it) is complete once (d) is granted.

**387, ll. 11–18** · scan 387 · claim without proof · **keep, with an addition.**
"Dans 𝒯(π_{g,ν}) il n'y a qu'un nb fini de classes de conjugaison de sous-groupes finis" is a known result. By Nielsen–Kerckhoff realization each finite subgroup acts conformally on some Riemann surface of type (g, ν), and there are only finitely many topological types of such actions.

**387, ll. 19–21** · scan 387 · claim without proof · **keep.**
Γ_{g,ν} ∩ Z′_{g,ν} is open because it is a finite intersection of the open subgroups Γ_{g,ν} ∩ Z′_G given by (d) for representatives G. The step is sound given (d), and the corrected "on ne trouve qu'un nombre fini" settles the grammar.

**387, own idea** · scan 387 · first-pass model's idea · **keep.**
The example is correct: in an infinite product of copies of ℤ/2 every element squares to 1, but {1} is not open. So "every g has a power in H" does not give openness of H. The text uses only the forward implication.

**388, ll. 4–8** · scan 388 · implication not pursued · **correct the quotation.**
The text reads "car autrement il n'y aurait guère rien à dire" ("guère" is dropped by the witnesses). Which finite subgroups of Ĝ̂(π) or M_a admit a discretification remains open on these pages.

**388, ll. 8–11** · scan 388 · claim without proof · **correct.**
The text reads "On sait qu'alors G est cyclique — (il|et) correspond à une action de G sur un U topologique, avec pt fixe", not "qu'un tel". Cyclicity needs orientation preservation: a finite group of orientation-preserving homeomorphisms fixing a point acts, after conformal realization, faithfully by rotations on the tangent plane. With reflections allowed the stabilizer can be dihedral. The scope "𝒢(π₀)" versus "Ĝ_a⁺" therefore matters.

**388, ll. 11–15** · scan 388 · claim without proof · **keep.**
"On peut la réaliser de façon complexe, ce qu'on (exprimera|exprime) en disant qu'il existe un relèvement admissible Γ_a^♮ → M_a, qui centralise G." The construction is not given.

**388, (5)** · scan 388 · claim admitted in the profinite case · **keep.**
For G ≠ 1, π^G = (1) is "known in the discrete case, admitted in the profinite context". For a proof sketch of the discrete case see C6. The profinite statement remains an assumption.

**388, own idea** · scan 388 · first-pass model's idea · **keep.**
Testing compatible fixed elements through finite characteristic quotients is a reasonable diagnostic for the profinite case.

**389, ll. 1–7** · scan 389 · claim · **keep, with an addition.**
The kernel of Centr_{Ĝ̂}(G) → T̂̂^G is π ∩ Centr(G) = π^G. So injectivity is exactly (5), which is what "ainsi" asserts. Uniqueness of the centralizing lift of a Γ″ (or Γ̃) lying in the image follows.

**389, ll. 8–18** · scan 389 · conjecture / expectation · **correct.**
The text reads "Mais en fait ce qu'on saura … c'est que Γ ⊂ M_a^G (… i.e. G opère sur la courbe alg.) — et on voudrait néanmoins en conclure que lorsqu'on a relevé G → T̂⁺ (en|ou) G → Ĝ⁺ … l'action de Γ^♮ se remonte (i.e. Γ^♮ → M_a^G) automatiquement, en Γ^♮ → M_a, de façon (à) (commuter|commutante)…". The first pass had "ce qu'on donne" and a cancelled "de façon". The available datum (G acts on the curve) is contrasted with the wished-for automatic commuting lift. The note's point that existence is the open issue stands.

**389, ll. 19–20** · scan 389 · question · **correct the quotation.**
"Est-ce là une propriété (du|des) choix des M_a, ou de celui du relèvement Γ^♮ → N_a, ou de G??" ("de celui", not "du choix"). The question stays open on this page.

**389, own idea** · scan 389 · first-pass model's idea · **keep.**
Separating image membership (existence) from injectivity (uniqueness) matches the text exactly.

**390, ll. 1–6** · scan 390 · claim withdrawn · **keep.**
The discrete surjectivity is asserted "sauf erreur" and retracted at once ("Non, il y a erreur").

**390, ll. 6–11** · scan 390 · implication not pursued · **keep.**
Commuting automorphisms permute the G-fixed points instead of fixing P. The subgroup fixing P is the relevant one.

**390, ll. 11–13** · scan 390 · expectation rejected · **keep.**
The text says "il ne faut pas s'attendre à ce que ∀ élémt de (N_a^G|M_a^G), ni m̄ de T̂_a^G, se remonte à M_a^G". The first group remains unsettled in the ink.

**390, ll. 14–21** · scan 390 · claim without proof / replacement proposal · **keep.**
The corrected readings are "pour un G (∈|⊂) 𝒯_a(π₀)", "remontages" and "forment un ens. fini". The finiteness of the set of π-conjugacy classes of lifts and the natural action of T̂̂(π)^G are asserted without proof, and the stabilizer statement continues past the package.

**390, own idea (example)** · scan 390 · first-pass model's idea · **keep, verified.**
On U = P¹ ∖ {±2, ±1/2} (type (0,4)), σ(z) = −z and h(z) = 1/z both preserve the punctures. Since h(−z) = −1/z, h commutes with σ. σ fixes 0 and ∞, both of which lie in U, and h exchanges them. This is exactly the phenomenon behind "Non, il y a erreur".

**390, ll. 19–21** · scan 390 · implication not pursued · **keep.**
If the set of classes is finite, the stabilizer of one class has finite index (at most the orbit size).

## Checker's notes

**C1** · scan 382 · structure of the requirements (checker).
Conditions (b) π^{Γ′} = (1) and (c) Z finite are two parallel demands on an admissible Γ: rigidity of points, and finiteness of their automorphism groups. The corrected "On veut sûrement" makes the "fait pendant à (b)" of scan 383 literal: both are wishes, not consequences.

**C2** · scan 382 · verification (checker).
Two splittings s, s′ of (1)′ over Γ′ are identified when s′ = x s x⁻¹ with x ∈ π. The stabilizer of s is the centralizer of s(Γ′) in π, which is π^{Γ′}. So π^{Γ′} = (1) says exactly that germs of splittings have no nontrivial automorphisms, which is the "rigidité" asserted.

**C3** · scan 384 · implication not pursued (checker).
The margin implies that if N_a = Norm_{T̂̂}(T̂_a), it might be automatic that a finite G ⊂ T̂_a⁺ lies in some 𝒯⁺ for a discretification in the class a. That would be a profinite Nielsen-realization statement attached to the arithmetic class a. It is not pursued on these pages.

**C4** · scan 385 · claim resting on conjectures (checker).
The comparison invoked, between the profinite centralizer T̂_a^{+G} and the discrete isotopy-automorphism group of the G-action, is what the "conjectures standard topologiques" are used for here. Any later use of this identification inherits that conditional status.

**C5** · scan 386 · verification of a claim (checker).
For a finite G acting on a hyperbolic surface of type (g, ν), the fixed locus of G in Teichmüller space is isomorphic to the Teichmüller space of the quotient orbifold, of complex dimension 3g′ − 3 + n′. Here g′ is the genus of U/G and n′ counts branch points and punctures. If g′ ≥ 1, hyperbolicity forces 3g′ − 3 + n′ > 0, as Grothendieck states. For g′ = 0 the dimension is n′ − 3, which is 0 for triangle-group actions, so the condition is sufficient but not necessary. Confidence: high for the formula, medium for its match with the uncertain word "schématique".

**C6** · scan 388 · checker's own idea (proof sketch).
Discrete case of (5): realize G (cyclic, orientation-preserving, fixing P) by isometries of a hyperbolic metric (Nielsen–Kerckhoff), and lift it to the disc fixing a lift of P. An element of π₁(U, P) fixed by G is a deck transformation commuting with the elliptic lift of a generator. In PSL₂(ℝ) the centralizer of a nontrivial elliptic element is the rotation group about its fixed point, which meets the torsion-free π₁ only in 1. Confidence: medium. The profinite case is exactly what the text admits without proof.

**C7** · scan 383 · notation check (checker).
In (c), Γ̃^♮ ⊂ N_a and T̂_a⁺ ⊂ N_a, so the equality Z = (T̂_a⁺)^{(Γ♮)} = Centr(Γ̃^♮) ∩ T̂_a⁺ holds with the centralizer taken in N_a, as in the parallel formula on scan 382. The ink of the subscript is retraced and stays marked (N_a|M_a).


# ===== LM_P040 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P040 (scans 391–400): research notes, checked against the corrected text

Each first-pass note (FP1–FP43, from `LM_P040_notes.md`, section 4) is checked against `LM_P040_checked.tex`: kept, corrected or dropped, with the reason. Quotations follow the corrected transcription. Notes added in this pass are labelled "second pass (own)". "Own idea" notes of the first pass remain the first-pass model's ideas. The first pass's table of doubts and abbreviation list are superseded by `LM_P040_changes.md` and are not repeated here.

## First-pass notes

**FP1. Scan 391, ll.1–7. Unproved claim / deferred examination. Corrected.**
The stabilizer of a point P (a conjugacy class of lifts) "se remonte de façon unique en 𝒢̂̂^G". Grothendieck adds "Il faudrait réexaminer ceci de façon plus soigneuse" and, with "il me semble", that one finds no new properties of the Γ ⊂ M_a, of M_a itself, "i.e. de 𝕀Γ_a comme ss-groupe profini de Γ' = Norm_{T̂̂}(T̂)/T̂". *Reason:* the letters the first pass left doubtful are now read (M_a twice, the doubled Γ_a). The claim remains unproved here.

**FP2. Scan 391, last paragraph. Claim citing a known result. Corrected.**
For cyclic G the text reads "alors sauf erreur il est prouvé que la situation est réalisable topologiquement (donc : algébr. de façon analytique complexe …)". This is a remembered result, hedged by "sauf erreur", from which the complex-analytic realization is drawn. *Reason:* the first pass read "dans ce cas encore … visible topologiquement". On the result itself see SP1.

**FP3. Scan 392, upper half. Unproved claim. Corrected.**
In the discrete case there is a canonical realization ("si U! ≠ ∅") of the fundamental groupoid of the exterior group π0. It is a finite groupoid [U!] with a strict G-action, whose points are the π0-conjugacy classes of maximal partial sections ≠ 1, and T(π0)^G acts on it "de façon également canonique". Finiteness, canonicity and strictness are asserted without proof on this page. *Reason:* "maximales" and "également" replace the first pass's "évidemment" and "rigide".

**FP4. Scan 392, lower half. Argument, closed by the author as a tautology. Corrected.**
The bracket shows that u̇ ∈ T(π0)^G lifts to u commuting with G iff u̇(P) = P. Lift u̇ arbitrarily to v. Then v(G) is π0-conjugate to G iff v(G) = int(g)(G), in which case u := int(g⁻¹)v fixes G "(auquel cas bien sûr il centralise, (par l'hyp. u̇ ∈ T^G))". The author concludes "Donc notre brillante assertion, est une tautologie". *Reason:* the first pass's "Comme on devait s'y attendre! toute notre belle assertion" is not in the ink, and the representatives are v and u := int(g⁻¹)v, not u and u'. The argument is checked in SP2.

**FP5. Scan 393, opening. Unproved claim. Corrected.**
"et donc le relèvement est unique". Since U! is finite, the stabilizer T(π0)^G_P has finite index, "et c'est donc ce ss-groupe d'indice fini qui se remonte gaillardement et canoniquement". *Reason:* "globalement" was a misreading of "gaillardement" ("blithely"), and the text has "ce", not "un". Uniqueness, and with it the multiplicativity of the lift, needs π0^G = 1 (SP2).

**FP6. Scan 393, comparison diagram and a). Question. Kept, made precise.**
"La question de la bijectivité de cette appl." concerns the map from π0-conjugacy classes of lifts G → 𝒢(π0) (splittings of 1 → π0 → E → G → 1) to π0-conjugacy classes of lifts G → 𝒢̂̂(π0) (splittings of 1 → π̂0 = π → Ê → G → 1). *Reason:* the conjugating group in the right column, left open by the first pass, reads π0.

**FP7. Scan 393, b). Question. Kept.**
"La question si π^G = 1 (rigidité), pour G ≠ (1)", for a given lift and "dans E pour simplifier". It must be posed under the page's hypotheses: a faithful exterior action with an invariant discretification π0.

**FP8. Scans 393–394. Implication of the conjecture. Corrected.**
"J'ai envie de conjecturer sans vergogne qu'il en est ainsi". The consequence is that for every such lift there is "un sous-groupe ouvert (T̂̂^G)_P du groupe (peut-être vraiment immense à priori !) T̂̂^G, qui se remonte canoniquement de façon à commuter à G". *Reason:* "ouvert" is written and underlined, not deleted. The subgroup is the stabilizer of P; the number of hats on its T is uncertain in the insertion.

**FP9. Scans 392–393. Own idea of the first pass. Kept.**
Express the other sections and their conjugacies by a cocycle calculation relative to a reference section, keeping the centralizer visible. This remains consistent with the corrected argument (FP4).

**FP10. Scan 393, a). Own idea of the first pass. Kept.**
Treat injectivity and surjectivity of the comparison map separately: does completion identify distinct discrete classes, and do profinite classes exist without a discrete representative?

**FP11. Scan 393, b). Own idea of the first pass. Kept.**
Test rigidity on compatible families of fixed elements in finite quotients. An isolated fixed element in one finite quotient decides nothing.

**FP12. Scan 394, ll.1–4. Conjecture / expectation. Corrected.**
This is the same consequence as FP8. What is claimed is an open subgroup, the stabilizer of P in T̂̂^G, with a canonical lift commuting with G; the size remark ("immense") concerns the ambient T̂̂^G. *Reason:* the first pass did not read "ouvert" and called the qualifying word illegible.

**FP13. Scan 394, ll.5–12. Research programme. Corrected.**
A theory of exterior operations of finite groups on discrete groups "à lacets", "qui est pour le moment extrêmement conjectural(e)", is to be built together with its profinite analogue, "j'aurai sans doute à y revenir par la suite". *Reason:* the ink has "lacets" (not "bouts") and "extrêmement" (not "entièrement").

**FP14. Scan 395, (1). Unproved claim. Kept, made precise.**
N_{g,ν} → 𝔖_ν is "surjectif (puisque T_{g,ν} → 𝔖_ν l'est)", hence the exact sequence (1) with kernel N^!_{g,ν}. The inference needs the image of the discrete group T_{g,ν} (or of T̂⁺_{g,ν}) to lie in N_{g,ν} compatibly with the maps to 𝔖_ν. The margin identity N = N^!·T̂⁺ on the same page presupposes T̂⁺ ⊂ N.

**FP15. Scan 395, (2)–(4). Claim with a stated reduction. Corrected.**
The connectors of (2) are inclusion hooks, so the "diagramme cartésien de ss-groupes" is the square of inclusions T̂^{!+} ⊂ T̂⁺ ⊂ N and T̂^{!+} ⊂ N^! ⊂ N. With the margin identity N = N^!·T̂⁺ this yields the isomorphism (3). The first pass had all four arrows reversed and left their direction open. The missing "!" in the first denominator of (4) is a slip in the ink; see SP3.

**FP16. Scan 395, end. Unpursued implication. Corrected.**
For i ∈ I, the capped group π'_i of type (g, ν−1) is "à lacets (pas ext!)": an honest group, not an exterior one. The margin assumes (g, ν−1) hyperbolic, "i.e. 2g+ν ≥ 4", which is correct since 2g−2+(ν−1) > 0 ⟺ 2g+ν ≥ 4. *Reason:* the first pass read "(gr. ext.!)", the opposite statement (SP4).

**FP17. Scan 396, (5). Unproved claim. Kept.**
For a discretization α compatible with the arithmetization there is an injective hom φ_i: π'_i → T̂^{+!} ⊂ N^! ⊂ T̂̂_i whose composite π'_i → T̂̂_i → 𝒢̂̂(π'_i) is the canonical inclusion. The margin adds that φ_i "ne dépend pas du choix de la discrétification … α"; that wording is partly uncertain and is not a proof.

**FP18. Scan 396, "on veut" / "plutôt : il est vrai que !". Asserted result. Kept.**
The invariance of the image of (5) in N_i, equivalently in N^! (since N_i = N^!·T̂⁺_i and T̂⁺_i "invarie cette image"), is first wished for and then asserted by the margin. It should be tracked as an assertion without a complete proof on these pages.

**FP19. Scan 396, normalizer assertion. Unproved claim. Corrected.**
In the universal case N^!_{g,ν} normalizes the subgroups π'_i ↪ T̂^{!+}_{g,ν} for 0 ≤ i ≤ ν−1, "qui sont donc invariants dans N^!_{g,ν}". The lower-left NB states "Il suffit me semble-t-il de le prouver pour un i pour le déduire pour les autres". *Reason:* the first pass asked for a simultaneous check of all indices and could not read the NB. The reduction to one index is itself an unproved claim, presumably through the transitive action of T̂⁺ on I used on 398.

**FP20. Scan 396, (6). Asserted, later held as a hypothesis. Corrected.**
"De plus T̂̂^!_{g,ν} → 𝒢̂̂^!_{g,ν−1} induit un iso (6) N^!_{g,ν} ⥲ M^!_{g,ν−1}". Scan 397 splits this into an inclusion plus the injectivity and surjectivity of (7); surjectivity is called evident, injectivity remains open. Scan 398 allows that it may fail, and scan 399 adopts it as a working hypothesis through (f). *Reason:* the left NB is now read and does not qualify (6); the target letter is M.

**FP21. Scans 395–396. Own idea of the first pass. Kept.**
Treat generation, intersection and conjugation compatibility as separate obligations. With (2) read as inclusions, generation and intersection are exactly the inputs of the second isomorphism theorem (SP3).

**FP22. Scan 397, ll.2–5. Unproved claim. Kept.**
"ψ_i applique bien N^!_{g,ν} dans M^!_{g,ν−1} — et ceci résulte de la définition arithmético-géom de ces groupes". Checking it needs the definitions from earlier sections.

**FP23. Scan 397, between (7) and (8). Unproved claim. Corrected.**
"l'inj. (resp. surjectivité) de (6) équivaut à celle de (7)". The vertical signs in (7) are isomorphisms (≀|), identifying 𝕀Γ_{g,ν} with N_{g,ν}/T̂⁺_{g,ν} as in (3). *Reason:* the first pass read "(resp. bijectivité)", but the b is struck and "surjectivité" follows; it also read the vertical signs as equalities. A proof combines (3) with the stated isomorphism T̂⁺_{g,ν} ⥲ 𝒢̂⁺_{g,ν−1}.

**FP24. Scan 397, after (8). Claim with argument. Kept.**
Since Θ_{g,ν} and Θ_{g,ν−1} are surjective and (8) commutes, (7) is surjective, hence (6) is surjective by FP23: "bel et bien évident". The argument is complete given FP23 and the surjectivity of the Θ.

**FP25. Scan 397, last paragraph. Question. Kept.**
Bijectivity of (6) means that Θ_{g,ν} and Θ_{g,ν−1} have the same kernel; a priori Θ_{g,ν} could have a smaller kernel, a representation of 𝕀Γ_ℚ "moins infidèle" than Θ_{g,ν−1}.

**FP26. Scan 397. Own idea of the first pass. Kept.**
Organize the study by the successive kernel quotients of the Θ_{g,ν}. This is a sound conditional reformulation.

**FP27. Scan 398, first paragraph. Claim with sketch. Corrected.**
λ_i (7) does not depend on 0 ≤ i ≤ ν−1, "même à priori, sans utiliser la définition ailleurs explicitée des N_{g,ν}": T̂⁺ acts transitively on I and trivially on 𝕀Γ_{g,ν}. *Reason:* the ink has "ailleurs" ("given elsewhere"), not "arithm". The sketch still needs the conjugating element to be compatible with ψ_i, so that the maps agree exactly and not merely up to an automorphism.

**FP28. Scan 398, middle. Open question. Kept.**
"faute de savoir si T̂̂^{(!)}_{g,ν} → 𝒢̂̂_{g,ν−1} est injectif". The "!" on the source is overwritten and uncertain. The question concerns the ambient groups, not only the arithmetic subgroups.

**FP29. Scan 398, middle. Unpursued implication. Kept.**
If that injectivity were known, N^!_{g,ν} could be described "comme l'image inverse de M^!_{g,ν−1}". Image conditions besides injectivity would have to be added.

**FP30. Scan 398, lower paragraph. Conjecture / question. Kept, made precise.**
"Il ne serait pas impossible d'ailleurs que (f) soit faux, i.e. que les Θ_{g,ν} soient infidèles, mais de moins en moins quand on fait augmenter ν". By its "i.e.", (f) names the faithfulness of the Θ_{g,ν} (SP6). The witnesses' "(6)" here is a misreading: the glyph is an f.

**FP31. Scan 398, last lines. Own idea of the first pass. Kept.**
Faithfulness of the limit representation is equivalent to the triviality of the intersection of the kernels of the Θ_{g,ν}. This is a correct reformulation that proves nothing.

**FP32. Scans 398–399. Conjecture / question. Corrected.**
"en passant à la limite projective Θ_{g,∞}, seulement, aurait-on (peut-être !) une représentation fidèle de 𝕀Γ_ℚ". Faithfulness is envisaged only in the limit, as a possibility. *Reason:* the ink has "seulement" (first letters overwritten), not "du moins", which changes the nuance.

**FP33. Scan 399, (9)–(10). Working hypothesis. Kept.**
"je préfère travailler hypothétiquement avec (f)". This gives the "suites exactes fondamentales" (9) 1 → π̂_{g,ν−1} → N^!_{g,ν} → N^!_{g,ν−1} → 1, which extends (10) for the T̂^!. Every later use of (9) inherits the hypothesis.

**FP34. Scan 399, margin. Suggestion. Kept.**
"on pourrait l'inclure dans une suite ex.te un peu plus grande, avec (N_{g,ν})_i et N_{g,ν−1} …". The larger sequence is not written down.

**FP35. Scan 399, sequence between "??". Open question. Kept.**
The sequence for the doubly hatted groups, 1 → π̂_{g,ν−1} → T̂̂^!_{g,ν} → T̂̂^!_{g,ν−1} → 1, is marked "??" and called "peut-être défaillante". Its left-hand exactness is the injectivity question of FP28 in another form.

**FP36. Scan 399, last paragraph. Unproved claim. Kept.**
For a "courbe algébrique virtuelle" (π, a), the preceding defines on each π'_i, i ∈ I(π), an arithmetization canonically associated with a. Canonicity has to be checked against the choices of discretization (FP17) and of representatives.

**FP37. Scan 399. Own idea of the first pass. Kept.**
Test whether two successive cappings, at distinct indices, commute after transport of indices.

**FP38. Scan 400, (11)–(12). Unproved claim. Kept.**
The exact sequence (11) 1 → π'_i → (N_a)_i → N_{a'_i} → 1 and the preimage identity (12) (T̂⁺_a)_i = ψ_i⁻¹(T̂⁺_{a'_i}) are asserted. The introductory word ("alors" or "donc") is uncertain.

**FP39. Scan 400, (11′). Unproved claim. Kept, made precise.**
(11′) restricts (11) to the "!" groups. The vertical marks under N^!_a and N^!_{a'_i} resemble the inclusion hooks of (2) on 395, so they probably express T̂^{+!} ⊂ N^!, as Carmona's "∪ ↑" also suggests. The lower annotation T̂^{+!}_a = ψ'^{−1}_i(T̂^{+!}_{a_i}) is the "!" form of (12).

**FP40. Scan 400, (13). Unproved claim. Kept.**
"par passage aux quotients" gives 𝕀Γ_a ⥲ 𝕀Γ_{a_i}. By FP43, (11) and (12) give (N_a)_i/(T̂⁺_a)_i ≅ N_{a'_i}/T̂⁺_{a'_i}. Identifying the left side with N_a/T̂⁺_a = 𝕀Γ_a also needs N_a = (N_a)_i·T̂⁺_a, which holds because T̂⁺ acts transitively on I (398). The substance lies in (11)–(12).

**FP41. Scan 400, (14). Unproved claim. Kept.**
The canonical map Discrt⁺(π) → Discrt⁺(π'_i) (the discretizations; CSG writes "Discrét⁺") defines ℙ_a ⥲ ℙ_{a_i}, compatible with the actions of 𝕀Γ_a, 𝕀Γ_{a'_i} and (13). Well-definedness and equivariance are to be checked separately.

**FP42. Scan 400, last paragraph. Explicit non-claim. Corrected.**
"Je ne fais ici aucune assertion sur une soi-disante bijectivité entre ens. des arithmétisations de π, et ens. des …". The author disclaims any bijectivity between the arithmetizations of π and those of π'_i (the sentence continues on the next leaf). Capping produces arithmetizations of π'_i, but nothing is said about inverting it. *Reason:* the first pass read "Je me fais ici encore assertion(s)", which turned a disclaimer into a possible claim.

**FP43. Scan 400, (11)–(14). Own idea of the first pass. Kept, sharpened.**
Isolate the formal statement: if ψ: A → B is surjective and H ⊂ B is a subgroup with ψ⁻¹(H) = H', then ψ induces a bijection A/H' → B/H, an isomorphism when H is normal. With (12) this gives (13) from (11), provided T̂⁺_{a'_i} is normal in N_{a'_i}.

## Notes added in this pass (second pass, own)

**SP1. Scan 391. Claim citing a known result (literature link).**
The statement for cyclic G is the cyclic case of the Nielsen realization problem: a finite subgroup of the mapping class group is realized by a finite group of homeomorphisms. Nielsen proved the cyclic case (1942); Kerckhoff proved the general finite case (Annals of Mathematics 117, 1983), after this manuscript. The step "donc … de façon analytique complexe" is the standard passage from a finite group of homeomorphisms to an invariant complex structure, and "algébr." points to the algebraic curve this gives. The hypothesis G ⊂ T(π0) (a finite subgroup of the discrete Teichmüller group) matches that problem.

**SP2. Scans 392–393. Check of the argument.**
Uniqueness of the lift: two lifts of u̇ commuting with G differ by int(h) with h ∈ π0 centralizing G. Since π0^G = 1, the reason the author gives on scan 390 for the injectivity of 𝒢(π0)^G → T(π0)^G, the lift is unique and the stabilizer lifts by a homomorphism. The step "u fixe G, auquel cas … il centralise" holds because G → T(π0) is injective: u g u⁻¹ ∈ G has the same image as g in T(π0), hence equals g. The bracket is therefore correct, and the author's verdict "tautologie" fits.

**SP3. Scan 395, (2)–(4). Check of a formula; slip in the manuscript.**
(3) is the second isomorphism theorem. T̂⁺ ⊲ N (N normalizes T̂⁺), N = N^!·T̂⁺ (the margin) and N^! ∩ T̂⁺ = T̂^{!+} (the square is cartesian) give N^!/T̂^{!+} ≅ N/T̂⁺. In (4) the ink writes the first denominator T̂⁺_a. When T̂⁺_a maps onto 𝔖_I (ν ≥ 2; the parenthesis on this page states this for T_{g,ν}), it is not contained in N^!_a, the kernel in (1). The quotient as written is then undefined, and T̂^{!+}_a, as in (3), is presumably meant. The transcription keeps the ink.

**SP4. Scans 395–396. Implication not pursued.**
"(pas ext!)": capping the hole i turns its conjugacy class of loops into a point, so π'_i comes with a base point and is an honest group. Display (5) and the diagram after it use exactly this, since the composite π'_i → T̂̂_i → 𝒢̂̂(π'_i) is the inclusion by inner automorphisms. The deleted line on 396, "si (g, ν−1) est aussi anabélien", repeats the hypothesis of the 395 margin.

**SP5. Scans 396–399. Status of (6) (correction of the first-pass reading).**
The margin "plutôt : il est vrai que !" upgrades the invariance of the image of (5), not (6). (6) is stated outright and then reduced; only its surjectivity is established on these pages. Its injectivity is replaced by the working hypothesis (f) (FP20, FP33). Results derived from (9) are conditional on (f).

**SP6. Scans 398–399. The label "(f)" (conjecture).**
Read with its "i.e.", (f) is the faithfulness of the Θ_{g,ν}: Γ_ℚ → 𝕀Γ_{g,ν}. Faithfulness of all Θ_{g,ν} gives equal (trivial) kernels, hence the bijectivity of (6) used in (9). It is strictly stronger than what (9) needs, which is only equal kernels for consecutive ν. By (8), Θ_{g,ν−1} factors through Θ_{g,ν}, so faithfulness passes from ν−1 to ν. Suppose Θ_{0,4} is the outer Galois action on the profinite π1 of P¹ minus three points, as the setting of M_{0,4} suggests (the definition of Θ is on earlier pages). Then its faithfulness is Belyi's theorem (1979), which would give (f) for every (0,ν) with ν ≥ 4.


# ===== LM_P041 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P041 (scans 401–410): research notes, checked against the corrected transcription

This file checks each research note of the first pass (`answers/unzipped/LM_P041/LM_P041_notes.md`, section 4) against `second_pass/LM_P041_checked.tex`. Each note is kept, corrected or dropped, and the reason is given. Notes added by the second pass come last and are labelled as such.

The kinds of note are: conjecture (an expectation or presumption of the author), question, claim without proof, implication not pursued, and own idea (the model's).

Notation follows the checked transcription: 𝕀Γ is the doubled Γ (`\mathbb{\Gamma}`); 𝒩, ℳ and 𝒢 are the script letters; T̂ and T̂̂ are the single- and double-hatted T.

## First-pass notes

**R401.1 — scan 401 — conjecture / explicit doubt — corrected.**
The sentence carried over from scan 400 says that a bijection onto the arithmetisations of π′_i would amount to the bijectivity of T̂̂/𝒩_a → 𝒢̂̂′/M^♮_{a′} ≃ T̂̂′/𝒩_{a′}. That bijectivity "n'aurait guère de raison d'être" unless one admitted T̂̂(π) ⥲ 𝒢̂̂(π′_i), which seems to the author "bien problématique". It is a stated doubt, not a claim.
*Reason:* the middle quotient and the target of the doubtful isomorphism carry the looped 𝒢 (the target group of the ψ_i), not T̂̂; the first pass read both as T̂̂ and called the denominator insecure. The sign on M_{a′} and the number of hats on 𝒢(π′_i) remain open.

**R401.2 — scan 401 — claim without proof — kept.**
"Cependant, on trouve, par le foncteur 'bouchage des trous', une équivalence" between arithmetised profinite groups with exterior lacets of type (g,ν) and arithmetised profinite groups with lacets, "pas extérieurs !", of type (g,ν−1). No proof is given: one needs the morphisms of both categories, the functor on morphisms, and full faithfulness and essential surjectivity compatible with the arithmetisations.
*Reason:* the corrected text, with its inserted "de type (g,ν)", states exactly this.

**R401.3 — scan 401 — question / programme — corrected.**
The programme is to make precise the type of properties that characterise 𝒩_{g,ν} in 𝒩′_{g,ν} = Norm_{T̂̂_{g,ν}}(T̂^+_{g,ν}). "Ou encore", it is to characterise 𝕀Γ_{g,ν} in 𝕀Γ′_{g,ν} = 𝒩′_{g,ν}/T̂^+_{g,ν}. The natural homomorphisms (15), from 𝕀Γ′_{g,ν} to Autext(T̂^+_{g,ν}) and to Autext(T̂^{+!}_{g,ν}), follow.
*Reason:* the reformulation "ou encore" is now read, and the struck layers ("Je pense qu'il", "On aura") are abandoned starts. The first pass's warning about an unsettled quotient notation no longer applies.

**R402.1 — scan 402 — conjecture — corrected.**
"Je présume" that 𝕀Γ_{g,ν} can be described as the inverse image of a suitable closed subgroup of one or the other of the "seconds mbs" of (15), i.e. through properties of exterior operations on T̂^+_{g,ν} or on T̂_{g,ν}. Missing are the closed subgroup itself and a proof that its inverse image is exactly 𝕀Γ_{g,ν}.
*Reason:* "image inverse" and "seconds mbs" are now secure; the first pass had a doubtful "l'image inverse" and "deux mbs".

**R402.2 — scan 402 — claim without proof / proposed criterion — corrected.**
The conditions ⓔ are said to be "bien de ce type": properties of normalising the subgroups π′_i of T^{+!}_{g,ν}, called "invariants" or "inscrits" (the reading is open). The author then considers conditions on exterior automorphisms of T̂′^{+!}_{g,ν} that are stable under successive passage to T̂′^{+!}_{g,ν−1}, T̂′^{+!}_{g,ν−2}, … and generated by ⓔ. If "invariants" stands, the π′_i are normal subgroups, and ⓔ is a condition on the outer automorphisms that preserve them.
*Reason:* the object is "automs extérieurs" (masculine "extérieurs"), not "actions extérieures". The adjective is "invariants|inscrits", not "inscrits|associés".

**R402.3 — scans 402–403 — conjecture / explicit doubt — kept.**
"Il n'est pas dit du tout que cela suffira à décrire les 𝕀Γ_{g,ν} ⊂ Γ′_{g,ν}", if only because the condition becomes empty in the limit case ν = 0 (g ≥ 2) and for (g,ν) = (1,1) and (0,3). Any sufficiency argument has to treat these cases on their own.
*Reason:* unchanged by the corrections.

**R403.1 — scan 403 — conjecture — corrected.**
It may be necessary to bring in properties of the π_1 of the M_{g,ν Q̄} "liés à la compactification". Only for (g,ν) = (0,3) "il ne faut pas s'attendre du tout à ce genre de conditions". Which properties are meant, and whether the profinite groups alone determine them, is not said.
*Reason:* the first pass read the last clause as "des tas ! en guise de conditions". The ink singles out (0,3) as the one case where no such conditions are expected.

**R403.2 — scan 403 — provisional definition, claim without proof — corrected.**
A "courbe algébrique potentielle" over an unspecified algebraic closure of Q consists of:

"Elles forment une catégorie (pour les iso, pour le moment)". Before this can be checked, the equivalence of lift germs and the compatibility of isomorphisms with them must be specified.

Compare the proposed criteria before and after each puncture-filling map in the limit types the author names, recording which conditions become empty. Confidence medium; this is a diagnostic, not a result.

Inside the struck block: "L'admissibilité s'exprime peut-être par la condition (24) π′^Γ = (1) — et s'il le faut, on mettra ces conditions (pour tte I′ ⊂ I) dans la définition de l'admissibilité". The cancellation matters: this is an abandoned formulation, not a definition. The uncancelled text of 409 still "hopes" for π′^Γ = 1.

"Je suis vraiment gêné aux entournures, faute d'avoir une définition en forme de 'admissible'". The preceding construction depends on this missing definition.
*Reason:* unchanged.

Test the fixed-point condition (24) on small punctured examples and on actions without geometric origin. Confidence medium.
*Reason:* unaffected.

**409/410-1 — scan 409 — deferred clarification — kept.**
"Je vais y revenir très vite": the definition of admissibility is postponed. Uses of the term in this passage depend on it.
*Reason:* unchanged.

**409/410-2 — scan 409 — claim without proof — kept.**
"Si I′ ≠ ∅, le groupe ext. π′ peut se décrire par un vrai groupoïde, ayant I′ comme ens. d'objets, et sur lequel Γ opère en sens strict"; this is "de l'algèbre pure". The groupoid and the strict action must be constructed.
*Reason:* unchanged; "en sens strict" is now read.

**409/410-3 — scan 409 — claim without proof — kept.**
Because Γ acts trivially on the i′ ∈ I′, each i′ gives a canonical homomorphism (24) Γ → π′(i′) lifting the exterior action. Hence there is a ("uq", unique?) π′-conjugacy class of liftings of the exterior action of Γ on π′ to a genuine action. Canonicity and independence from i′ are not proved; see N409.1 on the target of (24).
*Reason:* unchanged; "uq" is still open.

**409/410-4 — scan 409 — conjecture / expectation — corrected.**
"On espère qu'elle satisfait π′^Γ = 1": the lifted action is hoped to have trivial fixed subgroup, which is condition (20) for π′. The hypotheses that would guarantee this are not identified.
*Reason:* "qu'elle satisfait" is now secure, so the first pass's doubt about the wording can go.

**409/410-5 — scan 410 — claim without proof — kept.**
"Je dis que cette application est injective", for (25) I′ → Pts(π′, α′, Γ). A proof must show that distinct i′ give distinct classes of admissible liftings.
*Reason:* unchanged.

**409/410-6 — scan 410 — claim without proof / request for an internal proof — corrected.**
Injectivity is "évident" when admissibility means realisability by a true algebraic curve and by true rational points over K, the "corps des fixes (or invariants) de Γ". But one wants "des raisons internes à la donnée des (𝒩_{g,ν}), et des propriétés de ces données !". The task is a separation property of the 𝒩_{g,ν} that replaces the geometric argument.
*Reason:* "internes à … ces données" replaces the first pass's "internes : … ces derniers".

**409/410-7 — scan 410, margin — claim without proof (a reduction) — corrected.**
The margin probably reads "On est ramené au cas Card I′ = 2 …"; the witnesses agree, and "va revenir" is not excluded. Read so, it reduces the injectivity to two points rather than promising a return to the question. N410.2 shows in which sense the reduction is formal.
*Reason:* the first pass read "On va revenir au cas card I′ = 2", and the kind of note changes with the reading.

**409/410-8 — scan 410 — conditional construction — corrected.**
"Ce pt étant admis", and setting aside the two "éventuelles" (or "essentielles") definitions of admissibility, one gets a functor "bouchage de trous". Its source is the potential curves over P relative to an open subgroup Γ of 𝕀Γ_P, with a set I′ of points at infinity. Its target is the potential curves over P relative to Γ, with a subset of the set of "Pts ratls (or invts)/Γ". The margin adds "il faut pour ceci les restrictions anabéliennes habituelles"; the functor on morphisms is not given.
*Reason:* the margin reads "restrictions", not "fonctorialités", and belongs to this construction.

**409/410-9 — scan 410 — implication not pursued — kept.**
The source keeps a chosen set I′ of points at infinity, and the target a subset of the rational (or invariant) points. Whether the functor is faithful, full or reversible is not established on the page.
*Reason:* unchanged.

**409/410-10 — scans 409–410 — own idea (first pass) — kept.**
Test injectivity on a connected two-object groupoid with a Γ-fixed isomorphism between its objects. Confidence medium. This is the case Card I′ = 2 to which the margin reduces the question.
*Reason:* confirmed by the corrected margin note.

**409/410-11 — scan 410 — own idea (first pass) — kept.**
Check whether filling two disjoint sets of holes in either order gives canonically isomorphic results. Confidence medium.
*Reason:* unaffected.

**R409.12 — scans 407 and 409 — own idea (first pass) — kept, with a caveat.**
407 assumes I′ is Γ-stable as a set, while 409 uses that Γ acts trivially on each i′ ∈ I′, so either a hypothesis is missing or the action is trivial for another reason. If T̂̂(π) preserves each lacet class, as the definition of each ψ_i on the whole of T̂̂_{g,ν} suggests (CSG l. 8184, the next pages), then Γ ⊂ 𝒩_a fixes every point of I. In that case both conditions hold automatically.
*Reason:* the logical distinction stands. The struck "du fait que Γ opérait tr[ivialement]" on 409 shows the author treating the trivial action as a known fact.

## Second-pass notes

**N401.1 — scan 401 — implication not pursued (second pass).**
The "pas extérieurs !" of the equivalence fits a Birman-type picture: once a hole is filled, its point can serve as a base point, so the result is a genuine group of type (g,ν−1) rather than an exterior one. On that reading the functor needs a chosen hole on the source side, which the sentence leaves implicit. Confidence medium; the page does not state the mechanism.

**N403.1 — scan 403 — claim without proof (second pass).**
The parenthesis reads "(ce qui trivialt. (or moralt.) revient à se donner une clôture alg. Q̄ de Q …)". For G = Gal(Q̄/Q), G-torsors correspond to algebraic closures Ω of Q, via P ≅ Isom(Q̄, Ω). For a torsor under 𝕀Γ_{g,ν} this presupposes 𝕀Γ_{g,ν} ≅ Gal(Q̄/Q), the identification the whole construction is aiming at. So whichever adverb the ink holds, the equivalence is heuristic; the witnesses' "moralement" says so outright.

**N403.2 — scan 403 — implication not pursued (second pass).**
A plausible reason for the (0,3) exception: M_{0,3} is a point over Q, so the π_1 of M_{0,3,Q̄} is trivial and compactification conditions have nothing to act on. For (1,1) the geometric π_1 is the profinite completion of SL_2(Z), so such conditions can bite. This is my gloss, not a reason stated by the author.

**N404.1 — scan 404, margin — claim without proof (second pass).**
"Le cas où P est le torseur trivial, est celui des π ext (?) munis d'une prédiscrétification orientée α (d'où une suite exacte 1 → T̂_α → 𝒩_α → 𝕀Γ_α → 1)". Neither the identification of a trivialised torsor with an oriented prediscretification nor the exactness is argued. Exactness in the middle is the analogue for α of 𝒩_{g,ν}/T̂_{g,ν} = 𝕀Γ_{g,ν}. 𝒩_α must first be defined from α; I assume it is the subgroup of 𝒩_a compatible with α, which the note does not say.

**N405.1 — scan 405 — claim without proof (second pass).**
"Or ceci revient exactement à ⓖ". This assumes, as the surrounding text suggests, that compatibility with the arithmetisations means differing by an element of 𝒩_{g,ν}.
- *ⓖ suffices.* Let u_0 be compatible and u another isomorphism of the stated kind. Then g = u u_0^{−1} normalises T̂_{g,ν} and transports the open subgroup u_0(Γ′·Σ) of 𝒩_{g,ν} into 𝒩_{g,ν}, so ⓖ gives g ∈ 𝒩_{g,ν}.
- *The converse needs a splitting.* Since such g normalise T̂_{g,ν}, ⓖ need only be checked for open 𝒩′ ⊇ T̂_{g,ν}. Recovering it from the stated condition needs each such 𝒩′ to be of the form u_0(Γ′·Σ) with Γ′ ∩ Σ = (1), i.e. a splitting of 𝒩′ → 𝒩′/T̂_{g,ν}. So "exactement" holds only given such quasi-sections.

**N407.1 — scan 407 — textual and mathematical point (second pass).**
The ink reads "on aura un hom canonique T̂(π) → T̂(π′)" with one hat on each T. The double-hatted T̂̂ of 401, 404 (19) and 405 shows two clearly stacked hats. Since 𝒩_a ⊂ T̂̂(π) (404 (19), 405) and 𝒩_a is larger than T̂_a, a map defined only on T̂(π) cannot induce the top row 𝒩_a → 𝒩_{a′} of (22). So T̂̂(π) → T̂̂(π′) is meant, as CSG and IUT print it; the single hats are probably a slip of the pen, and the transcription keeps the ink.

**N408.1 — scan 408 — own idea: proof sketch (second pass).**
Read "quasi-section of 𝒩_{a′} over 𝕀Γ_{a′}" as a closed subgroup that meets T̂_{a′} trivially and maps onto an open subgroup of 𝕀Γ_{a′}, and take Γ ⊂ 𝒩_a to be one over 𝕀Γ_a. Then (23) follows formally from (21)–(22).
1. Since (21) gives 𝕀Γ_a ⥲ 𝕀Γ_{a′} and (22) is compatible with the quotient maps, an element of Γ whose image lies in T̂_{a′} (in particular is 1) has trivial image in 𝕀Γ_a.
2. Such an element therefore lies in Γ ∩ T̂_a = 1, so the composite is injective and its image meets T̂_{a′} trivially; its image in 𝕀Γ_{a′} is open.

Confidence high for that reading of "quasi-section", which these pages do not define.

**N409.1 — scan 409 — claim without proof / notation (second pass).**
(24) is written Γ → π′(i′), yet it is said to "relever son action extérieure". A lift of the outer action Γ → Out(π′) is naturally a homomorphism to Aut(π′(i′)), the automorphisms of the vertex group at the Γ-fixed object i′, or a section of the corresponding extension; it is not a map into π′(i′) itself. The display probably abbreviates one of these. As written, its target does not match the sentence.

**N410.1 — scan 410 — implication not pursued (second pass).**
The margin "il faut pour ceci les restrictions anabéliennes habituelles" presumably requires both types, (g,ν) and (g, ν − Card I′), to stay anabelian, i.e. 2g − 2 + ν > 0 before and after filling. Otherwise π′ is abelian or trivial, and the condition π′^Γ = 1 and the sets Pts(π′, α′, Γ) change character. The exact restrictions are not stated on the page.

**N410.2 — scans 409–410 — own idea: proof sketch (second pass).**
For a fixed π′, the reduction to two points is formal on the groupoid side of 409. Whether i′ and j′ give the same π′-conjugacy class of liftings depends only on the full subgroupoid on {i′, j′}. That subgroupoid is Γ-stable, since Γ fixes both objects, and still describes π′, since any nonempty set of objects does. If "Card I′ = 2" instead means filling only two holes, the reduction also needs the point maps to be compatible with filling the remaining holes, a passage that can merge points; so the groupoid reading is the one under which the reduction holds as stated. Confidence medium.


# ===== LM_P042 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P042 (scans 411–420): research notes, checked against the corrected text

Each first-pass note (numbered as in `answers/unzipped/LM_P042/LM_P042_notes.md`, section 5) is kept, corrected or dropped against `LM_P042_checked.tex`. Notes added in the second pass are labelled **(second pass, own)**. Quotations are from the corrected transcription; π/Π doubts are resolved to π and Γ^♮ is read as a natural sign throughout.

## First-pass notes

1. **Scan 412, condition ① — question. KEEP.** « tt sous-groupe ouvert aussi (et inversement ?) »: the converse, admissibility of Γ deduced from that of an open subgroup, is asked, with a question mark that the witnesses drop.
2. **Scan 412, condition ② — claim stated as axiom. KEEP.** Under « On suppose », bouching a Γ-invariant part I′ gives an admissible operation of Γ on π′. It is a requirement on the notion of admissibility, not a theorem.
3. **Scan 412, lower margin — claim without proof. CORRECT.** The note reads « Il suffit de la poser pour card I′=1, i.e. bouchage d'un trou — du moins si on sait que la condition d'admissibilité ne dépend que de l'action des noyaux des groupes » (tail doubtful). The reduction of ② to one hole is therefore explicitly conditional on that hypothesis, which is nowhere stated or proved on these pages.
4. **Scan 412, ①–② — own idea (first pass). KEEP, reworded.** Testing the one-hole reduction separately for a Γ-fixed hole and for a Γ-orbit of holes is still useful; the corrected margin shows that the author's own reduction rests on a hypothesis about kernels, which is what such a test should probe.
5. **Scan 413, equivalence of systems — claim without proof. KEEP.** « On trouve ainsi une équivalence » between systems (π,a,Γ,ψ,i) of type (g,ν+1) with an admissible exterior operation and a Γ-stable i, and systems (π′,a′,Γ,ψ′) of type (g,ν) with a « vraie » admissible action. No functors on morphisms or natural isomorphisms are given.
6. **Scan 413, pathological lifts — conjecture. KEEP, wording corrected.** « il y aura des relèvements "pathologiques" d'une action cat./ext. admissible donnée… »: the inverse implication is expected to fail; no example is given. The abbreviation before « admissible » is doubtful (cat. or ext.).
7. **Scan 413, condition ③ — claim stated as axiom. KEEP.** If Γ operates effectively and admissibly on π, then π^Γ = (1). Introduced by « On suppose de plus ».
8. **Scan 413, rigidity — implication not proved. KEEP.** « Cela implique que la catégorie des Γ-Pts de π, ou … des sections de B_{π,Γ} (≃ B_u) sur B_Γ, est rigide »: automorphisms of a section are π-elements fixed by Γ, so ③ gives rigidity once every section is shown to come from an admissible effective action.
9. **Scan 413, existence vs rigidity — own idea (first pass). KEEP.** ③ controls automorphisms only; non-emptiness of the category of sections is a separate question, answered on 415 by axiom ④ only after passing to an open subgroup.
10. **Scan 411, opening — explicit doubt. CORRECT.** The margin « pour les isom » (CSG: « [pour les isomorphismes] ») restricts the claimed equivalence of categories to isomorphisms; the author then writes « ce n'est pas du tout évident ».
11. **Scan 411, axiomatic set-up — claim. CORRECT.** The « condition essentielle » is that the ψ_i : T̂̂_{g,ν} → Ŝ̂_{g,ν−1} (script capital G or S) induce M_{g,ν} ⥲ M_{g,ν−1}; the first pass read the ψ without index and left N/M open. This, with closed subgroups N_{g,ν} ⊂ T̂̂_{g,ν} normalising T̂_{g,ν}, is said to suffice to define arithmetisations and the bouchage/forage theory; no verification is given.
12. **Scan 411, inverse functor — own idea (first pass). KEEP.** Comparing « forer des trous » at invariant points with passage to a smaller Γ′ remains a sensible check; the author's own doubt follows immediately.
13. **Scan 414, discreteness — claim without proof. KEEP.** The inductive-limit category of sections of B_{π,Γ} ≃ B_u over the B_{Γ′} « est discrète et correspond à l'ens » Pt_ad(B_{π,Γ^♮}) ≃ lim Pt_ad(B_{π,Γ′}).
14. **Scan 414, groupoid description — claim without proof. CORRECT.** Corrected statement: if Pt(B_{π,Γ}) ≠ ∅, then, because π^{Γ′} = (1) for every open subgroup, « le gr. cat. π peut être décrit can.(?) par un groupoïde profini P̲t̲(B_{π,Γ^♮}) ayant Pt(B_{π,Γ}) comme ens d'objet, lié(s) par le groupe cat. π, sur lequel Γ opère en sens strict, avec le stabilisateur Γ_P de ∀ pt étant ouvert, et Γ_P → Aut(P) (≃ π mod autom. int.) étant continue ». A proof must build the arrows from π, the strict action, and continuity.
15. **Scan 414, NB margin — implication not pursued. KEEP.** The point groupoid « à opérateurs stricts » « dépend fonctoriellement de (π,Γ), pour des morphismes ext. … »; the rest is illegible (CSG: « [??] »), so the class of morphisms is not fixed.
16. **Scan 415, bijection — claim without proof. CORRECT.** The sentence reads « On a ici si à ∀ P ∈ Pt(B_{π,Γ}) on associe la classe d'isom. de sections de B_{π,Γ} sur B_Γ, on trouve une bijection — ce qui caractérise le Γ-groupoïde en question à isom unique près »; it is an assertion, not a definition (the first pass read « par déf. »).
17. **Scan 415, ④ — axiom. KEEP, wording corrected.** An admissible operation of Γ on (π,a) has, on some open Γ′, its « op. cat. » lifted to an effective admissible operation, i.e. Pt_ad(B_{Γ,π}) ≠ ∅.
18. **Scans 415–416, canonical homomorphism of extensions — claim. CORRECT (consequential).** The text says the homomorphism of extensions coming from the exterior action and from p_i « n'est pas la composée » Ŝ̂(π)_i → T̂̂(π)_i →ψ_i Ŝ̂(π′_i); the first pass read the opposite (« qui vient par la composée »). The insertion on 416 also requires the p_i : π → π′_i to commute with the exterior actions of T̂̂(π).
19. **Scan 416, general construction — claim. CORRECT.** It is said to exist whenever a group Γ (here T̂̂(π)) acts exteriorly on two exterior groups π, π′ with a Γ-compatible homomorphism π → π′ such that « le centre de π et le centralisateur de son image(?) dans π′ soient triviaux »; the cancelled « de centre trivial » after « qui » was replaced by this sharper condition. Existence and uniqueness are not separated in the text.
20. **Scan 416, forgotten axiom (g) — claim. CORRECT.** « On a oublié de préciser : (g) L'hom canonique (Ŝ̂_{g,ν})_i → Ŝ̂_{g,ν−1} associé à i ∈ [0, ν−1], envoie (M_{g,ν})_i dans M_{g,ν−1} donc il s'insère dans un hom.(?) de suites exactes » (26). The label is (g) or 9, not 7/2; CSG and IUT misprint the target as M_{g,ν}.
21. **Scan 415, ④ — own idea (first pass). KEEP.** Testing the lifting axiom on finite quotients is reasonable; nothing on the page makes the finite-level choices compatible.
22. **Scan 416, centraliser hypothesis — own idea (first pass). KEEP.** The difference of two candidate extension maps should land in the centraliser of the image of π in π′, so dropping triviality of that centraliser is the natural toy test.
