23. **Scan 417, admissibility transported by φ_i — claim. KEEP.** « On conclut aisément de ② que si le premier est admissible, le deuxième l'est »: the argument is not written out.
24. **Scan 417, (27)–(29) — claim. KEEP.** Passing to the limit gives a map of Γ-sets (28) and a homomorphism of Γ-groupoids (29); the transition maps and the action on arrows are not specified.
25. **Scan 417, canonical point P — implication not pursued. KEEP.** P ∈ Pt_ad(B_{π′_i,Γ}) = (Pt_ad(B_{π′_i,Γ^♮}))^Γ is « le pt canonique », presumably the class of the canonical hom Γ → M_{a′}(π′_i) of 418 a).
26. **Scan 418, ⑤ — requirement. KEEP.** « On demande » that (27) induce a bijection onto Pt_ad(B_{π′_i,Γ}) ∖ {P}, and in the limit a bijection of Γ-sets.
27. **Scan 418, a) — claim. KEEP.** The canonical hom Γ → M_{a′}(π′_i) is not π′_i-conjugate to any φ_i∘λ with λ admissible; no obstruction is given.
28. **Scan 418, b) — claim. KEEP.** The target of φ_i∘λ, φ_i∘μ is written M_a(π) without primes (also in CSG); one expects M_{a′}(π′_i).
29. **Scan 418, c) — own idea (first pass). KEEP.** c) states no exception for P; read with a), the canonical class is simply not admissible-in-image, so c) concerns admissible lifts of Γ → N_{a′}(π′_i), which presumably excludes P by definition. Whether the canonical lift counts as « admissible » is the point to settle.
30. **Scans 417–418 — own idea (first pass). KEEP.** Splitting ⑤ into a) exclusion of P, b) injectivity, c) surjectivity matches the author's « Ceci signifie trois choses ».
31. **Scan 418, Γ-sets — own idea (first pass). KEEP.** A bijection of Γ-sets in the limit needs compatibility over all open Γ′, not only Γ-fixed points.
32. **Scan 419, points — claim. CORRECT.** The equivalence « se précise de façon parfaite au niveau des points : les Points Γ-rationnels de B_π(,Γ^♮?) s'identifient à l'un(?) des pts Γ-rationnels de B_{π′}, distincts de P ». With « l'un » each point corresponds to one point ≠ P; the set-level statement is ⑤.
33. **Scan 419, (30) — claim without proof. KEEP.** Extension to many points (« ce qui n'était pas faisable dans le contexte discret ! »): systems with Γ fixing each point of I′ ⊂ I(π) ↔ systems (π′, α′, I′, Γ, θ′) with I′ ⊂ Pt_ad(B_{π′,Γ})^Γ.
34. **Scan 419, margin — restriction. KEEP.** « avec 2g + (ν − card I′) ≥ 3 », ν′ = ν − card I′, keeps (g,ν′) anabelian.
35. **Scan 419, quasi-inverse — claim. KEEP.** « On a donc un foncteur quasi-inverse : forage de trous en I′ ! » (the doubt on « forage » is removed).
36. **Scan 419, many holes vs successive holes — own idea (first pass). KEEP.**
37. **Scan 420, restriction — claim. CORRECT.** The restriction is to I′ ⊂ I(π) « non seulement stable par Γ, mais ⊂ I^Γ », equivalently I′ ⊂ Pt(B_{π′,Γ^♮}) « non seulement stable par Γ, mais m̄ [même] ⊂ Pt(B_{π′,Γ^♮})^Γ »; removing it is reduced to ⑥, without proof.
38. **Scan 420, ⑥ — claim. CORRECT.** For (π,a) « groupe à lacets cat. profini arithmétisé » (not a groupoid), Γ′ open in Γ acting admissibly (written Γ → N_a(π), no prime), I′ stable: a lift of the action of Γ′ to an admissible action of Γ on N_{a′} invariant on I′ should extend uniquely to an admissible action of Γ (or Γ′, doubtful) on (π,a) inducing the given one on π′. Existence and uniqueness are both open here; the next page (CSG l. 8371) starts on uniqueness.
39. **Scan 420, ⑥ — own idea (first pass). KEEP.** Passing to an open normal subgroup fixing I′ pointwise is exactly the author's next move (« On peut supposer Γ′ invariant dans Γ », next package).

## Notes added in the second pass

40. **(second pass, own) Scan 416, why the extension map is not the composite — own idea, high confidence.** The composite Ŝ̂(π)_i → T̂̂(π)_i → Ŝ̂(π′_i) kills π (π is the kernel of Ŝ̂(π)_i → T̂̂(π)_i), while the left square of the diagram forces φ_i|π = p_i. So φ_i equals the composite only if p_i is trivial: the corrected reading « n'est pas la composée » is the mathematically necessary one.
41. **(second pass, own) Scan 417, diagram (26) — implication not pursued.** The annotations « non comm.? » (left triangle, with φ_i) and « comm. » (right triangle) say that the dashed lift N_a(π)_i → M_{a′}(π′_i) makes the lower-right triangle commute but not, apparently, the upper-left one. Whether the left triangle commutes up to π′_i-conjugation is the question behind a) and b) on 418.
42. **(second pass, own) Scan 413, which axiom is invoked — open point.** The inserted sentence cites « La condition (1) » (ink), but the inference « si Γ opère effectivement, de façon admissible, alors l'action extérieure déduite est admissible » follows from ② applied to (π°,a°) and the new hole, not from ① (open subgroups). The witnesses print (2). Either the author slipped or the ink is (2); the mathematics needs ②.
43. **(second pass, own) Scan 412, admissibility of the arithmetic groups — implication not pursued.** The admissible acting groups may be open subgroups of 𝕀Γ_g, « valeur commune des 𝕀Γ_{g,ν} », or of 𝕀Γ_a for an arithmetisation a. That 𝕀Γ_{g,ν} does not depend on ν is asserted in passing; it is what makes forage/bouchage compatible with the acting group.
44. **(second pass, own) Scan 416, transport of structure — claim.** The extension homomorphism is identified (« C'est aussi ici ») with the transport-of-structure map sending every automorphism à lacets u of π to the corresponding automorphism of π′_i. This identification is asserted, not checked; it presupposes that u preserves the hole i.
45. **(second pass, own) Scan 420, (6) as a splitting problem — own idea, medium confidence.** With E the extension of Γ by L = Ker((N_a)_{I′} → N_{a′}), ⑥ asks that the partial splitting over Γ′ extend uniquely to Γ; uniqueness follows if L^{Γ′} = 1, a generalisation of ③ (this is the author's own continuation, CSG ll. 8371–8383). Existence amounts to Norm_E(Γ′) → Γ being onto.


# ===== LM_P043 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P043 (scans 421–430): research notes, checked against the corrected text

Each first-pass note (numbered as in `LM_P043_notes.md`, section 5) is kept, corrected or dropped, with the reason. Notes added in this pass are labelled "second pass (own)". Source: Grothendieck, *La Longue Marche à travers la théorie de Galois* (1981), Montpellier cote 140-2, scans 421–430.

## First-pass notes

1. **Scan 421: question. Keep.** "L'unicité est-elle de ttes façons claire ?" asks whether the partial splitting over Γ′ extends uniquely to Γ. The corrected text puts the splitting over *the* subgroup Γ′ ("du sous-groupe Γ′"), not over subgroups in general.

2. **Scan 421: conjecture. Keep, with the doubt removed.** "je présume qu'on doit avoir L^{Γ′}=1" is now a secure reading. The condition is said to generalise condition ③ of an earlier page.

3. **Scan 421: unproved claim. Keep, corrected.** The kernel is L = Ker((N_a)_{I′} → N_a), and the extension of Γ is pulled back from the extension (N_a)_{I′} of N_{a′} by L along Γ → N_{a′}. The first pass had N_0, N_{0′}. Identity (31), Norm_𝓔(Γ′) ∩ L = L^{Γ′}, is called "évident". The claim that L^{Γ′}=1 gives uniqueness (Γ̃ = Norm_𝓔(Γ′)), and that existence means Norm_𝓔(Γ′) → Γ is onto, is stated without proof.

4. **Scan 421: own idea (first pass). Correct.** The worry about continuity of an inverse does not arise in the profinite setting of these pages. A continuous bijective homomorphism between compact Hausdorff groups is a homeomorphism, so "épi (donc iso…)" holds once the map is continuous. The remaining point is the injectivity, which comes from L^{Γ′}=1 via (31).

5. **Scans 421–422: proposed investigation. Correct.** The text reads "dans certains contextes (au moins celui des actions arithmétiquement fidèles, i.e. Γ → 𝚪_a injectif — qui correspond normalement au cas de courbes alg. définies sur des ext. algébriques de ℚ)". The first pass had "(on visera alors des …", "arithmétiques" and "Π_a". So the arithmetically faithful case is the minimal context in which "admissible" is to be made precise. Returning to curves and finite coverings, which also gives a way to vary g, remains a plan.

6. **Scan 422: expectation. Correct.** He wants to take up "le formalisme précédent" (not "le problème") under another, perhaps more general, aspect. It is expected to apply also to *discrete* groups with loops (the formalism of the U!). Keep the separation of the discrete and profinite cases.

7. **Scan 423: unpursued implication. Keep.** Strict transitivity is demanded: a covariant functor from the category of cofinite parts U (with inclusions) to groupoids, over the evident functor on objects. Strictness versus coherence up to isomorphism is not discussed here.

8. **Scan 423: unpursued implication. Keep, wording corrected.** The local maps Π_{D_i^*} → Π_{U_I} are to be compatible with the "morphismes de transition" Π_{U_I} → Π_{U_J} (I ⊃ J). For fixed I they make Π_{U_I} a groupoid with loops through Π_{D_I^*} = ∐_{i∈I} Π_{D_i^*} → Π_{U_I}.

9. **Scan 423, margin: own idea (first pass). Correct.** The margin is now mostly read. If 𝒳 is finite and I = 𝒳, Π_∅ is not to be the empty groupoid but a groupoid "qui s'envoie dans les précédents…". Strict transitivity cannot then be required, and "il faudra(it) peut-être … hyp. anabéliennes sur Π_{U_I} dans ce cas". The finite case is thus explicitly set aside from strictness, and anabelian hypotheses are suggested for it.

10. **Scan 424: unproved claim. Keep.** The composite Π_{D_i^*} → Π_{U_J} → Π_{U_I} (i ∈ J∖I, J ⊃ I) is "constant", so that Π_{U_I} is deduced from Π_{U_J} by "bouchage des trous". Constancy alone does not say that Π_{U_I} is the universal such quotient; that would have to be imposed or proved.

11. **Scan 424: unproved claim. Correct.** The model "pouvait se visualiser" with a topological orientable surface X and a part 𝒳 meeting every connected component. For 𝒳 = I (𝒳 finite), the exceptional groupoid is the fundamental groupoid of X∖I = X∖{𝒳}, that is X minus the whole finite set 𝒳; the first pass had "X∖{x}". The text says one will have trouble mapping it into the others with strict transitivity, "qu'à cela ne tienne !". This agrees with the margin of scan 423 (note 9).

12. **Scan 424: own idea (first pass). Keep.** Comparing strict transition maps with maps defined up to natural isomorphism is a sensible test. The text itself only notes the difficulty in the exceptional case.

13. **Scan 425: unpursued implication. Keep.** Γ acts strictly on 𝒳, on the Π_{U_I} and on the Π_{D_i^*}, which it permutes ("N.B. qu'il permute entre eux"), commuting with all transition and local maps. The struck margin copy of "(qu'il permute entre eux)" shows that the phrase was added afterwards.

14. **Scan 425: unproved claim. Keep.** "On aura des difficultés, si 𝒳 est fini … pour la commutation stricte … mais passons" defers the finite case again.

15. **Scan 425: own idea (first pass). Keep.** The axiom read now is "si Γ′ est admissible alors tt ss-groupe Γ″ ⊃ Γ′ aussi" (upward closure), with admissible subgroups ≠ 1. Nothing is said about intersections, so testing closure under finite intersections remains a fair question.

16. **Scan 425: canceled draft. Keep.** The canceled conditions defined admissibility of Γ′ by 𝒳^{Γ′} meeting every connected component of 𝒳 = U_∅. They also considered the operation of Γ_P on π₁(U_I, P). This is abandoned material and not an assumption.

17. **Scan 426: conjecture / aim. Keep.** "Nous voulons des conditions qui assurent que 𝒳 et les Π_{U_I} etc. [peuvent|puissent] se reconstruire à isom. can. près" from the bare ("nues") groupoid or topos data with Γ-operators. This states an aim, not a theorem.

18. **Scan 426: conjecture / aim. Keep, notation corrected.** The single datum is the operation of Γ on the outer group with loops π₁(Π_{U_I}) attached to I (lower-case π₁; the first pass had Π₁). The data to be recovered are the Π_V for V ⊃ U_I, the Π_{D_i^*} (i ∈ I) and the action of Γ.

19. **Scan 426: own idea (first pass). Keep.** Test cases with different stabiliser patterns under condition 1° (Γ_P ≠ {1} of finite index) are a reasonable probe.

20. **Scan 427: unproved claim. Keep.** The direct direction is "immédiat, par l'opération de forage des trous". The task is the converse ("en sens inverse"): rebuilding Π_{U_I} and the action from Π_𝒳 (or rather the topos B_{Π_𝒳}).

21. **Scan 427: conjecture. Keep.** "sous réserve d'anabélianité, disons de Π_𝒳, on peut ± réconstituer Π_{U_I} et l'action de Γ dessus …". The sign after "anabélianité" is still unread.

22. **Scan 427: unproved claim. Correct.** The text reads "On veut au moins une description intrinsèque des éléments de 𝒳, et de l'action de Γ dessus, via cette action « molle »". The first pass had "On veut en avoir une" and "visible". So the minimal aim is to recover the points of 𝒳 with their Γ-action from the *soft* (outer) action alone. For 𝒳 connected this is an outer action of Γ on a group "à lacets (sans lacets !)".

23. **Scans 427–428: unproved claim. Correct.** The continuation reads "du moins si 𝒳 est infini donc qu'on puisse prendre I de cardinal arbitrairement grand". The infinitude of 𝒳 is given as what allows I to be taken arbitrarily large, hence π(I) anabelian for I "assez grand". The size threshold and the precise anabelian property are not stated.

24. **Scan 428: unpursued implication. Keep.** Condition 2°: the evident map U_I → Pt(B_{π(I),Γ♮}) ≅ {classes of π(I)-conjugacy of germs of splittings of the extension 𝓔(I) of Γ by π(I)} should be injective and Γ-compatible. Injectivity separates points but does not identify the image.

25. **Scan 428: open question. Keep.** "il faudrait pouvoir donner une caractérisation de l'image".

26. **Scan 428: unproved claim. Correct.** Compatible arithmetisations on the π(I), compatible with the hole-filling homomorphisms and the Γ-action, give "un hom canonique de Γ dans le groupe commun 𝚪 des autom. arithmétiquement (ext. de ces arithmétisations". The group is the bold 𝚪 (first pass: Π). Making this precise needs the definition of that common group and independence of the choices of representatives.

27. **Scan 428: unproved claim. Correct.** The parenthesis is "(et même Γ → M_{π(∅)=π}, — qui est déjà une donnée nettement plus forte)"; the first pass had "(cad = …" and "chose". The stronger datum is a homomorphism of Γ to M of π = π(∅) itself, compared with the homomorphism to the common group 𝚪. Grothendieck calls it markedly stronger without argument.

28. **Scan 428: own idea (first pass). Keep.** Restriction to open subgroups (germs) and π(I)-conjugation should be kept as separate equivalences when testing injectivity. The written words "germes de scindages" are secure.

29. **Scans 427–428: own idea (first pass). Keep.** A two-step diagram I ⊂ J tests how the point maps and the filling homomorphisms commute.

30. **Scan 428: own idea (first pass). Keep, with 𝚪 for Π.** Model the common group 𝚪 first on a short diagram of puncture sets and compare it with the group at I = ∅.

31. **Scan 429: proposed criterion. Correct.** One may suppose given a notion of admissible lifting making the map a bijection U_I = 𝒳∖I ≅ Pt_ad(B_{π(I)},Γ♮). "Ceci signifie … que *la notion d'admissibilité* satisfait aux conditions 1°) : 6°) vues ci-dessus" (first pass: "ce critère"). The conditions 1°–6° belong to earlier pages.

32. **Scan 429: conjecture. Keep.** What is to be called an admissible operation of Γ on an outer profinite group with loops "reste pour le moment conjectural".

33. **Scans 429–430: conjecture. Correct.** The experimental definition reads: an action Γ → 𝒯̂̂(π) (first pass: 𝓔̂̂) respecting an arithmetisation a, i.e. factoring through N_a, such that the image of Γ → N_a/𝒯̂_a = 𝚪_a is open (first pass: "= Π_a"), together with the non-normalisation conditions on scan 430. CSG elsewhere writes 𝒯̂̂(π) for Autextlac(π) (its line 6359). Whether this definition has the bijection property of note 31 is left open.

34. **Scan 430: unpursued implication. Keep.** For each hole-filling homomorphism π → π′_i, N(π)_i → M(π′_i), neither the corresponding image of Γ_i nor that of any open subgroup Γ′ of Γ_i may normalise an L′_j (j ∈ I(π′_i) = I∖{i}). Requiring this for all open subgroups makes it a condition on germs.

35. **Scan 430: conjecture. Keep, reading secured.** "peut-être même faudrait-il imposer π^{(Γ′+)} = (1), où Γ′+ est le noyau de Γ′ → Ẑ* (χ)". Then "on s'attend que l'on ait alors π^{Γ′} = (1), et si ce n'était le cas, il faudrait l'introduire dans la définition". This is to hold for each relative filling (I′ ⊂ I(π), i ∈ I′) defining Γ_{(I′,i)} → M(π(I′)).

36. **Scan 430, upper margin: conjecture. Correct.** The note is cancelled: long strokes cross it, and no witness carries it while all carry the lower note. It is an abandoned alternative. The condition just stated is perhaps superfluous and premature, and it might be that the subgroup K of M(π) normalising L_i and, modulo Ker(π → π′_i), L_j is "trop petit": its image in 𝚪 (or Π) = M(π)/𝒯̂(π) (letters doubtful) would not be of finite index. Treat it as a cancelled idea.

37. **Scan 430, lower margin: unproved claim. Correct.** CSG prints this note as footnote 118. It reads: "Plus généralement, pour i ∈ I′ ⊂ I = I(π), on impose que la vraie action de Γ_{I′,i} sur π(I′) correspondante [à i] ne normalise aucun sous-groupe L_j (j ∈ I∖I′) — on est ramené pour ceci au cas où I′ = I∖{j}, donc où π(I′) n'a (qu'une) seule classe [de] lacets… (si g ≥ 1)". The reduction to one remaining hole is asserted without proof. The genus condition is now read as g ≥ 1 (si|et still open).

38. **Scan 430: unproved claim. Correct.** The sentence ends "et qu'" and continues on scan 431: "il en est de même pour l'action induite sur chaque π(I′) …" (P044). A true action lifting a given admissible action is itself admissible (defines an admissible action on a π′ of type (g, ν+1)) iff the action, or rather its germ, normalises no L_i, and the same holds for the induced actions.

39. **Scan 430: own idea (first pass). Keep.** Γ′+ ⊂ Γ′ gives π^{Γ′} ⊂ π^{Γ′+}, so the imposed condition π^{(Γ′+)} = (1) implies π^{Γ′} = (1). Grothendieck's "on s'attend que l'on ait alors π^{Γ′} = (1)" must therefore mean "from admissibility" rather than "from the stronger condition", for which it would be trivial.

40. **Scan 430: own idea (first pass). Keep.** Compare one filling with two successive fillings, tracking normalisers of a remaining L_j and images of open subgroups. This tests the reduction claimed in the lower margin (note 37).

## Notes added in the second pass (own)

41. **Scan 421: own idea.** In the ink the kernel's target is N_a without a prime, while the extension is "de N_{a′} par L" and the map is Γ → N_{a′}. For L to be the kernel of the extension (N_a)_{I′} → N_{a′}, the target must be N_{a′}. The unprimed N_a in Ker(…) looks like a slip of the pen; the transcription keeps the ink.

42. **Scans 422 and 429: own idea.** With Γ profinite, "arithmétiquement fidèle" (Γ → 𝚪_a injective) makes Γ isomorphic to a closed subgroup of 𝚪_a. Adding the open-image condition of the experimental definition, the arithmetically faithful admissible actions are exactly those where Γ maps isomorphically onto an open subgroup of 𝚪_a. In that case condition 2° compares points of 𝒳 with sections over open subgroups of the arithmetic group, which is the setting of the section conjecture.

43. **Scan 424: own idea.** For I = 𝒳 the object set U_I = 𝒳∖I is empty, so restricting the fundamental groupoid of X∖I to U_I, as in the general case, would give the empty groupoid. That is what the margin of 423 excludes ("pas le groupoïde vide"). Grothendieck therefore takes the whole fundamental groupoid of X∖I = X∖𝒳, written "X∖{𝒳}" (the braces are a notational slip), whose objects are points outside 𝒳. Its object set is not U_∅ = ∅, so the maps from it cannot be inclusions on objects. This is exactly why strict transitivity fails in this case.

44. **Scan 428: own idea.** The step from "Γ → 𝚪" (exterior, arithmetic) to "Γ → M_{π}" (the stronger datum) is a lifting problem along M_π → 𝚪, whose kernel is of the type 𝒯̂(π). A lift exists iff the extension of Γ pulled back from M_π → 𝚪 splits. This is the same kind of splitting question as on scan 421. The comparison "nettement plus forte" is therefore measured by such a nonabelian obstruction, which the text does not discuss.

45. **Scan 427: own idea.** The word "molle" is the key term. The reconstruction is to use only the soft (outer) action of Γ on π, not a true action. This matches the later criterion on scan 430, where a true action lifting an admissible outer one is examined separately ("Notons qu'une vraie action …").


# ===== LM_P044 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P044 (scans 431–440): research notes, checked against the corrected text

Each first-pass note from section 5 of `LM_P044_notes.md` is checked against `LM_P044_checked.tex` and marked **keep**, **correct** or **drop**, with a reason. The second-pass notes that follow are labelled *Claude, second pass*. Scan numbers are file scans (manuscript pages 218–222). Quotations follow the corrected transcription.

## First-pass notes, checked

- **431 — conjecture — keep (updated).** "Mais je présume que la première condition (action de Γ♮ ne normalisant aucun des L_i) implique les autres." The acting group is now read Γ♮; this is no longer ♮ or ♯. A proof would have to follow the normalisers of the loop subgroups through the hole-filling passages π′ → π′(I) and π(I) → π′(I).

- **431 — conjecture / expectation — correct.** The first pass referred to a condition "(3°)". The ink reads (4°), the digit 4 clearly closed. The inserted clause says that (4°) "implique l'existence de suffisamment de relèvements", and the author wants it built into the definition of admissibility. Its relation to the infinitude of germs of splittings in 432 c) is still to be compared.

- **431–432 — own idea (first pass) — keep, with a correction of context.** Separating a), b) and c) of the provisional conjecture is still a sensible test. The first pass read the margin beside the heading as "Corrigé". It reads "Canulé cf plus bas…": the author himself withdraws this provisional conjecture, and the reason follows on 433–434.

- **432, diagonal margin — conjecture / explicit doubt — keep.** Condition c) "exclut sans doute" cases like Γ = N_{g,ν} with (g,ν) ≠ (0,3): the extension M_{g,ν} of N_{g,ν} by π̂_{g,ν} probably admits no germs of splittings. The author links this, with "⟺?", to the absence of étale multisections of U_{g,ν} over M_{g,ν}. The letter forms and the word after "germes de" are still unsettled. The CSG's "(g,ν) = (0,3)" contradicts the ink's ≠.

- **432 a) — conjecture — keep.** For effective admissible actions π^Γ = (1), and "peut-être même" π^{Γ⁺} = (1). The exponent mark (+ or †) is still uncertain, so the stronger statement should not be formalised yet.

- **432–433, cancelled b)–c) — withdrawn conjecture — keep.** The cancelled text claims "itou sur π(I′)" for a Γ-stable I′ ⊂ I(π). The surviving b)–c) on 433 restrict to one fixed hole i.

- **433 b)–c) — unproved claims — keep (updated).** b) concerns an action that is exterior ("extérieurement", now certain). In c) the quotient action is on π′♮; the superscript is the same sign as in Γ♮. "= R(i)" or "= π(i)" is unresolved, and the right-hand letter differs from the π of b), so the target of c) should not be identified with π(i) from the witnesses alone.

- **433 d), injectivity — conjecture — keep (updated).** The map is Pt_ad(B_{π,Γ♮}) → Pt_ad(B_{π′,Γ♮}), "définie par c)". B is indexed by the pair (π, Γ♮); the subscript reading is now settled. It is claimed to be injective, without proof.

- **433 d), image; final paragraph — conjecture explicitly doubted — keep (strengthened).** The insertion makes "le complémentaire de son image" equal to {P}, and the author then calls this part "tout à fait douteuse". The next page gives the reason: for Γ = π₁(S) of a parameter scheme S, "il est manifeste que la surjectivité déconne", because two distinct sections need not be disjoint.

- **433 d) — own idea (first pass) — keep, with a note.** Keeping injectivity and the description of the image as separate problems is sound. The failure of the image description, however, is not left open on these pages: the author names its mechanism on 434, non-disjoint sections over a positive-dimensional base. The first pass's "missing admissible lifts" is a different mechanism, which the text does not support.

- **434 — unproved claim — correct.** The verb phrase reads "correspond. moralement". An admissible exterior action of π₁(S) on π corresponds "morally" to a family of curves of type (g,ν) parametrised by S. This is a heuristic dictionary, not an asserted equivalence. The first pass left the adverb as "maintenant|manifestement".

- **434 — unproved claim — correct (the first-pass statement is dropped).** The first pass read "il est manifeste que la surjectivité demeure" and noted an asserted "persistence of surjectivity". The ink says "la surjectivité déconne": surjectivity onto the complement of {P} fails. The author asserts, without proof, that condition c) holds for instance when U comes from a curve over the base field, and that surjectivity then visibly fails.

- **434 — implication not pursued — keep.** "deux sections distinctes de U sur S peuvent ne pas être disjointes !" Equality of sections and meeting in some fibre are different notions over a base of positive dimension.

- **434 — own idea (first pass): coincidence locus of two sections — keep.** It fits the corrected text exactly. The locus where two sections meet is what "pas disjointes" refers to.

- **435 — implication not pursued — keep (updated).** "Strictly distinct" sections are to be detected by comparing splittings with the admissible germs of splittings "sur ℾ′ ⊂ ℾ de Γ (en tant qu'extension de ℾ_Q par une partie géométrique)". Both letters are now read as double-struck Γ, not Π. Only a low dot after the prime is uncertain.

- **435 — doubt — keep.** "On a l'impression de tourner dans un cercle vicieux ou presque." Admissible germs are meant to characterise points, but they are defined through the points they should recover.

- **435 — scope restriction — keep.** "il ne faudrait pas trop vouloir avaler à la fois" ("courir" in the first pass): first restrict to groups that "moralement" correspond to π₁ of schemes of finite type over Q, K(π,1)'s or "m̄ des variétés élémentaires à la Artin".

- **435 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Testing the criterion on an explicit parameter scheme remains reasonable. The low confidence the first pass gave stands.

- **436 — strategy — keep.** "se tirer par les lacets de souliers, en récurrant sur la dimension." The spelling is now certain. No induction step is given.

- **436 — expectation — correct.** The ink reads "(modulo tt au moins passage à un ss-groupe ouvert) “arithmétiquement fidèles” p.e. Γ → ℾ_a injectif". Injectivity of Γ → ℾ_a is offered as an example of arithmetical faithfulness ("p.e."), not as its definition (the first pass read "i.e."). The condition is wanted only up to passing to an open subgroup.

- **436 — assessment — correct (quotation).** He writes "semble raisonnable, ou du moins pas nécessairement déconnante", not "vraisemblable". This is a provisional assessment, not evidence.

- **436 — proposed decisive test — keep.** The "courbes" obtained should be realisable as coverings of ℙ¹ ∖ {0,1,∞}. No method is given.

- **436 — own idea (first pass): a finite-index subgroup of the three-punctured-line group — keep.** Low confidence, as the first pass said.

- **437, upper-left margin — research aim — correct.** The margin is now fully read: "pour essayer de retrouver par voie “algébrique”, sur l'auto ext. d'ordre 2 dans π₁, la disposition des “points à l'infini” de la courbe sur les composantes connexes de X^σ (X = Û)". This is an explicit programme for the non-compact case, which the author sets aside ("Finalement je ne regarde que les surfaces compactes"). The first pass treated it as a fragment that could not be recovered.

- **437 — unproved claims (compactification, extension of homeomorphisms, orientations, antiinvolutions) — keep (updated).** The parenthesis reads "(supposée orientée)": U is assumed oriented, which is what the notion of antiinvolution needs. The first-pass reading "supp. non orienté" is dropped. Homeomorphisms "U sur lui-m̄" extend to X and preserve S; no proof is given.

- **437 — unproved equivalence (U,σ) ↔ (Y,T) — keep.** "On trouve alors une équivalence" between the two categories, together with the inserted claim that the "double orienté" is a compact oriented surface. The morphisms are not specified.

- **437 — own idea (first pass): fixed versus interchanged punctures — keep.** See also the second-pass note on 437 below.

- **438 — unproved claim — keep.** The general case "se ramènera" to ν = 0.

- **438 — claim and the author's own correction — keep.** The text says "il faut que Y soit connexe, et non orientable"; the margin says this holds only when X^σ = ∅. A complete classification must allow orientable quotients.

- **438 — construction of (1) — keep (sharpened).** The margin beside (1), "Y surface compacte connexe sans bord non orientable de genre γ", fixes the convention. A closed non-orientable Y "of genus γ" is ℝP² with γ discs replaced by Möbius bands, so χ = 1 − γ: γ is the cross-cap number minus one. Removing μ open discs gives χ(Y) = 1 − (γ + μ). In the first display the sign before μ is still marked uncertain in the ink, but (1) requires −.

- **438 — unproved claim: X^σ is a union of circles — keep.** It is used to remove the fixed locus without changing χ.

- **438 — own idea (first pass): the involution (x,y) ↦ (x,−y) of the torus — keep.** It is consistent with the author's own margin. X^σ is two circles, and the quotient is an annulus, an orientable Y. This is allowed because X^σ ≠ ∅.

- **439 — unproved supporting claims (étale double cover of Int Y; χ(∂Y) = 0) — keep.**

- **439 — Proposition and left margin — keep.** The Proposition claims an equivalence with compact non-orientable bordered surfaces of type (γ,μ) with γ + μ = g. The margin restricts this to "une sous-catégorie pleine" and adds the orientation doubles Y ∐_{∂Y} Y of orientable compact Y with non-empty boundary.

- **439 — left margin, g = 2γ + μ − 1 — keep (reading adjusted).** It reads "X est de genre g avec g = 2γ + μ − 1 (où γ ≥ 0, μ ≥ 1)". Here γ is the ordinary genus of the orientable Y, a different convention from 438.

- **439 — Corollaire and right margin — keep.** The count "exactement g+1 classes … 0 ≤ μ ≤ g" holds only for (X,σ) with X/σ non-orientable ("Ça ne marche qu'en se limitant …").

- **439 — inverted cancelled block — keep.** "Nous ~~admettrons~~ savons qu'elles sont classifiées, mod isomorphisme près, par les systèmes" breaks off and is cancelled. It must not be completed.

- **439 — own idea (first pass): small-genus inventory — keep.** It is carried out in the second-pass note on 439–440 below.

- **440 — unproved claim — keep.** There are "exactement g+1 classes de conjugaison" in A_g = Aut(X_g) satisfying (4), which give classes in A_g/A_g° = 𝒯_g. By the 439 margins this is the count for non-orientable quotients only.

- **440 — conjecture — keep.** "Nous montrerons (on l'espère!)" that these classes stay distinct in 𝒯_g, and that every σ ∈ 𝒯_g satisfying (4) lies in one of them. These are a separation problem and a realisation problem.

- **440 — assumed lemma — keep (updated).** Every antiinvolution of the unit disc, with an inserted "(cas de ℂ)" or "(ou de ℂ)" that is still uncertain, is conjugate to z ↦ z̄. Its fixed set is therefore homeomorphic to ℝ, hence connected. It is assumed ("On admettra"), not proved.

- **440 — theorem set-up — keep.** An oriented surface U not isomorphic to S², an antiinvolution σ, the universal covering Ũ, π = Aut(Ũ/U), and an extension E of ℤ/2 by π, "formée" (now read); the statement continues past the package.

- **440 — own idea (first pass): split into lifting and conjugacy problems — keep.**

- **440, display (4) — own idea (first pass): check the definition of sg — keep.** The ink, and every witness, has sg(σ) = +1. For an orientation-reversing involution the orientation character would be −1. Either "sg" is defined otherwise earlier in the manuscript, or this is a slip; check before using (4).

- **436 — literature: Belyi — keep.** Relevant as a benchmark. The correction to "p.e." does not affect it.

- **440 — literature: Kerckhoff, Nielsen realisation — keep.** It is a retrospective comparison only.

## Second-pass notes (Claude)

- **431–434 — the author's withdrawal and its reason — implication made explicit (Claude, second pass).** The "Conjecture provisoire profinie" is marked "Canulé" in the margin, and 433–434 give the reason. Take Γ = π₁(S) for a parameter scheme S of positive dimension. A section of the filled family U(i) → S that meets the section P given by the hole i over some, but not all, points of S is neither a section of U nor equal to P. So the complement of the image of Pt_ad(B_{π,Γ♮}) → Pt_ad(B_{π′,Γ♮}) is larger than {P}: this is "la surjectivité déconne", while injectivity is untouched.

- **431, margin — implication not pursued (Claude, second pass).** The reduction to subsets I′ = I ∖ {j} is qualified "du moins si g ≠ 0" (ink ≠; the CSG prints "= 0"). For genus 0, whether the induced conditions on all π(I′) follow from those on the I ∖ {j} is left open.

- **432 — a slip in a definition (Claude, second pass).** "Une action effective de Γ sur π est dite effective, si l'action ext. est effective, et si elle ne normalise aucun ss-groupe à lacets" is circular as written. The next clause, "Si Γ opère effectivement de façon admissible", shows that "admissible" is meant in both places. The ink says "effective" twice, and the transcription keeps it.

- **435 — conjecture: the section conjecture in germ (Claude, second pass).** Points of S, over finite extensions of the ground field, are said to correspond to germs of splittings of Γ = π₁(S) over open subgroups ℾ′ of ℾ, with Γ seen as an extension of ℾ_Q by its geometric part. This is stated without proof, and is the content Grothendieck later stated as the section conjecture (letter to Faltings, 1983). Here it is used to define "admissible" germs, and that is the circularity he points out.

- **436 — implication (Claude, second pass).** With "p.e." restored, "arithmétiquement fidèle" is wider than injectivity of Γ → ℾ_a, and is wanted only "modulo tt au moins passage à un ss-groupe ouvert". The dimension-0 step of the proposed induction therefore covers every Γ that is faithful on some open subgroup. A definition of faithfulness is still missing on these pages.

- **437 margin and 440 theorem — own idea, medium confidence (Claude, second pass).** The marginal aim, recovering how the points at infinity lie on the components of X^σ from the order-2 outer automorphism, fits the theorem begun on 440. There each component of U^σ should give a π-conjugacy class of order-2 elements of E outside π. A real puncture lying on a component Z_i should then appear as a cusp (loop) subgroup normalised by the corresponding involution σ_i. The distribution of the punctures could be read off from which loop subgroups each σ_i normalises; the pages do not state this, and it needs proof.

- **439–440 — checking the count (Claude, second pass).** The two marginal corrections together give the full count of conjugacy classes of antiinvolutions on a closed oriented surface of genus g.
  - Non-orientable quotient: (γ,μ) with γ, μ ≥ 0 and γ + μ = g, giving g + 1 classes.
  - Orientable quotient: γ ≥ 0, μ ≥ 1 and 2γ + μ − 1 = g, giving ⌊g/2⌋ + 1 classes.

  The total is ⌊3g/2⌋ + 2, the classical number of topological types of real structures in genus g (Weichold, 1883). Harnack's bound μ ≤ g + 1 is reached only in the orientable case with γ = 0. The "exactement g+1" of the Corollaire and of 440 is therefore the non-orientable count alone, as the right margin of 439 says. (Check: g = 1 gives 3 = 2 + 1: Klein bottle, Möbius band, annulus.)

- **433 c) — open question (Claude, second pass).** In c) the target is written π′♮ = R(i)|π(i), with a letter unlike the π of b) and a ♮ exponent that no witness keeps. How π′♮ relates to π′ = π(i) of b) ("par passage au quotient") is not explained on these pages. Settle it before using c) in d).


# ===== LM_P045 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P045 (scans 441–450): research notes, checked against the corrected text

Each first-pass note (R01–R29, section 5 of `answers/unzipped/LM_P045/LM_P045_notes.md`) is checked against `second_pass/LM_P045_checked.tex` and marked **keep**, **correct** or **drop**, with a reason. Notes C1–C4 are new and labelled *Claude, second pass*. Scan numbers are file scans (manuscript pages 223–227). The theorem's hypotheses and the admitted Lemma are on scan 440 (package LM_P044): U an oriented surface (séparée, connexe, paracompacte), not S², σ an antiinvolution, ℰ the extension of Z/2 by π = Aut(Ũ/U).

## First-pass notes, checked

- **R01 — 441 — unproved claim — keep, context corrected.** "(Pour mémoire) U^σ est une sous-variété fermée de U de dim 1." The hypotheses are on 440, not missing. The claim is classical: a periodic homeomorphism of a surface is locally conjugate to an orthogonal map (Brouwer, Kerékjártó; Eilenberg 1934), and an orientation-reversing involution is then locally a reflection. develop: no.

- **R02 — 441 — own idea — keep.** Sc(ℰ, Z/2), now read with certainty, is the set of π-conjugacy classes of splittings, i.e. the non-abelian cohomology set H¹(Z/2, π) once one splitting is fixed. A standard reformulation. develop: no.

- **R03 — 441–442 — unpursued implication — keep, updated.** By (7) and d), the fixed subgroup of a lift is trivial for a line component and infinite cyclic for a circle, with π^{σ_i} = Im φ_i after replacing φ_i (the ink has φ_i, not g_i) by a conjugate. The text proves this on 444–445 ((11), (14)), so the "detection of compactness" is established here, modulo the Lemma. develop: no.

- **R04 — 442 — unpursued implication — correct.** The ink reads "quitte à remplacer φ_i par un conjugué". Replacing φ_i by int(α)∘φ_i is the same as replacing g_i by αg_iα⁻¹, so the point stands: the equality concerns the fixed subgroup of the chosen lift σ_i, up to that conjugation.

- **R05 — 442 — dependency on the Lemma — keep, context corrected.** The Lemma is stated on 440 and explicitly admitted ("On admettra le Lemme"): every antiinvolution of the disk or of C is conjugate to z ↦ z̄, so its fixed set is ≃ R, non-empty and connected. It is applied to Ũ ≃ R², since U is oriented and ≠ S². Classical (Brouwer, Kerékjártó; Eilenberg 1934). develop: no.

- **R06 — 443 — unpursued implication — correct.** The parenthesis reads "(assez triviale de ttes façons)": the author calls the factorisation of (5) through π₀(U^σ) rather trivial anyway. The first pass's "triviale de ses fibres", a claim about fibres, is not in the ink. The substantive step is the bijectivity of (6), which comes from (10).

- **R07 — 444, (10) — compressed step — keep, updated.** In the main-line insertion the target is written Ũ^{σ_i} with no index; the diagonal margin writes Ũ_i^{σ_i}, the consistent form. The step is complete modulo the Lemma: Z̃_i → Ũ_i|Z_i is a morphism of coverings of Z_i, so its image is open and closed; it lies in the connected Ũ_i^{σ_i} and so equals it; Ũ_i^{σ_i} ≃ R is simply connected, so the covering Z̃_i → Ũ_i^{σ_i} has one sheet. develop: no.

- **R08 — 444 — own idea — drop.** Testing the covering argument on a line and on a circle adds nothing: the text treats both cases explicitly, (11) for Z_i ≃ R and (12)–(14) for Z_i ≃ S¹.

- **R09 — 445 — claim without proof — keep.** "σ_i (opérant trivial^t sur T_i, et sur π_i par transport de structure) commute à l'hom injectif φ_i" is asserted. It holds because σ_i acts on the whole square (9)/(8) and fixes Z_i and Z̃_i pointwise, so conjugation by σ_i on deck groups is compatible with Z̃_i → Ũ_i. Routine. develop: no.

- **R10 — 445, margin — restriction — correct.** The margin "et X_g/σ non orientable...." is now read with certainty; it has no insertion mark, and the witnesses put it into the statement. It carries over the restriction in the margins of 439–440: the labelling σ_i ↔ i = card π₀(X^σ) classifies (X, σ) only when X/σ is non-orientable. Parts a)–c) do not need it: by the theorem, the number of Π_g-conjugacy classes of order-2 lifts is the number of fixed circles for every antiinvolution.

- **R11 — 445–446 — own idea — correct.** Comparing antiinvolutions with the same i but quotients of different orientability is the separating / non-separating distinction of Weichold's classification of real structures (non-separating: 0 ≤ i ≤ g; separating: 1 ≤ i ≤ g+1, i ≡ g+1 mod 2). This is known (Weichold 1883; see Natanzon's surveys on real algebraic curves). For the count of lift classes it changes nothing (see R10). develop: no.

- **R12 — 446 — slip of the pen — keep.** "Soit Y_{g,i} = X_g^{σ_i}, surface compacte à bord non orientable connexe de type (g−i, i)": the exponent is in the ink, but the description fits the quotient X_g/σ_i, not the fixed set. The witnesses keep the slip. The first index of Y is overwritten.

- **R13 — 446–447 — claim without proof — keep.** "Il est bien connu, et immédiat" that the automorphism group of Y acts transitively on π₀(∂Y): any permutation of the boundary circles of a connected compact surface is induced by a homeomorphism (classification of surfaces). Lifting to (X_g, σ_i) uses the correspondence of 439–440 for non-orientable quotients. develop: no.

- **R14 — 447 — compressed step — keep, updated.** The ink closes a parenthesis after h ("$h$), le choisir") with no opening one. The step is immediate: if u′_i = int(α)(int(h)u_i) with α ∈ Π_g, replace h by int(α)h, which has the same image ḣ and stays in 𝒢_g^+ because inner automorphisms preserve orientation. develop: no.

- **R15 — 447–448 — claim proved in the text — keep, with a warning on the witnesses.** "L'indice de 𝒢_g^{+u_i} dans 𝒯_g^{+σ_i} est i" means the image of 𝒢_g^{+u_i}, whose kernel is T. The proof on 448 is an orbit–stabiliser count: 𝒯_g^{+σ_i} acts on the i classes of 1 a) transitively (interlinear "transitivement", from c)) and the stabiliser of the class of u_i is the image. The CSG, IUT and CK print "opère trivialement", under which the index would be 1; readers of those editions should use the manuscript reading.

- **R16 — 447 — claim without proof — keep.** The two vertical inclusions have index 2 because u_i lies in 𝒢_g^{u_i} and σ_i in 𝒯_g^{σ_i}, and both reverse orientation; the "+" subgroups are the kernels of the orientation character. Immediate. develop: no.

- **R17 — 445, 447 — boundary case — keep, confirmed.** The ink has (0 ≤ i ≤ g); the witnesses print (1 ≤ i ≤ g). For i = 0 the theorem gives no order-2 lift (X^σ = ∅), so a) holds with 0 classes and b), c) and Corollaire 2, which choose u_i, are vacuous.

- **R18 — 447, (16) — own idea — keep; develop: yes.** The extension in (16) has kernel T ≅ Z, on which the quotient acts through ±1 (orientation of the fixed circle; see C4). **Statement to attack:** for g ≥ 2, 1 ≤ i ≤ g and X_g/σ_i non-orientable, decide whether 1 → Π_g^{u_i} → 𝒢_g^{+u_i} → Im(𝒢_g^{+u_i} → 𝒯_g^{+σ_i}) → 1 splits. **Status:** the orientable analogue, the boundary-capping extension 1 → Z → Mod(S_g^1) → Mod(S_{g,1}) → 1, is a non-split central extension classified by the Euler class; for this non-central, real (Klein-surface) version I know no reference.

- **R19 — 448 — own idea — keep, downgraded.** The passage from "int(γ)u_i Π-conjugué à u_i" to α⁻¹γ ∈ 𝒢_g^{+u_i} is the standard orbit–stabiliser argument in 1 → Inn(Π) → Aut(Π) → Out(Π) → 1 for a centre-free Π. Complete in the text. develop: no.

- **R20 — 448, cancelled Corollaire 3 — cancelled claim — correct.** With the corrected reading, the cancelled text says: for U a connected surface with boundary, not RP², Π = π₁(U) has torsion iff U is non-orientable and ∂U has at least one compact component; torsion elements have order 2, and their conjugacy classes correspond to the compact components of ∂U (not "non compactes"). Since π₁ of a surface other than RP² is torsion-free, Π must stand for the group ℰ of the theorem for the oriented double of U; but then, by (6)–(7), every boundary component, compact or not, gives a class of order-2 elements, and orientability of U plays no role. As read, the statement conflicts with the theorem; it is struck in the manuscript. develop: no.

- **R21 — 449 — construction aim — keep, updated.** "J'ai envie de construire une figure géométrique où on puisse mettre en évidence simultanément" the antiinvolutions giving the σ_i and the associated u_i, "(ce qui sera alors facile, par la recette générale)": the author expects the u_i to follow at once from the figure by the construction of (5) on 442. The construction begins on 449–450 and continues past this package.

- **R22 — 449 — claim invoked as known — keep.** "Nous savons que σ_i ∈ 𝒯_g s'obtient en regardant un X_g comme doublement orienté d'un Y = Y_{g−i,i}" refers to the Proposition on 439 (non-orientable quotients of type (γ, μ), γ + μ = g). The explicit model from RP² with g holes, g−i of them closed by Möbius bands, is checked in R25. develop: no.

- **R23 — 449 — unpursued implication — keep, updated.** The boxed V_{0,j} = Y_0 ∖ ⋃_j D̊_j (index j, now read) is called V_0 on 450; the j in V_{0,j} is a slip, since j is the union index. V_0 lies in both Y_0 and Y_{g−i,i} and agrees with Y along ∂Y = ⋃_{g−i+1 ≤ j ≤ g} ∂D_j, so it is the common piece from which all the σ_i can be built.

- **R24 — 450 — claim without proof — drop.** The restriction of the orientation cover X_0 → Y_0 to a closed disk D_j is trivial because D_j is simply connected, and its two sheets are the two orientations of D_j by definition of the orientation cover. Nothing to prove.

- **R25 — 450 — own idea — correct (check done).** χ(V_0) = χ(RP²) − g = 1 − g and the Möbius bands add 0, so Y has χ = 1 − g and i boundary circles; its oriented double has χ = 2 − 2g, genus g. So (21) must read Y_{g−i,i}, as on 449, whatever the doubtful sign in the ink. The author's "genre" g−i of Y is the number of Möbius bands added to RP² (crosscap number g−i+1), and the index of M_i in (20) is a slip for M_j.

- **R26 — 450, cancelled map — cancelled claim — correct.** The target of the cancelled map is X (X_{g−i,i} with the first index struck, or X_{g,i}), not Y. The witnesses show that the map returns after these pages as (24) U_0 → X_{g,i}, a homeomorphism once the ∂Δ_j for g−i+1 ≤ j ≤ g are contracted, so the abandoned formulation was resumed in corrected form.

- **R27 — 450 — own idea — keep.** X_{g,i} = X_g ∖ Int(M̃), the complement of the lifted Möbius bands, is the common subsurface on which the involutions for the various i can be compared; the text confirms this use in (23)–(24) beyond this package. develop: no.

- **R28 — 441–445 — own idea — keep; develop: yes.** The theorem is the discrete, topological core of the real section conjecture: π₀(U^σ) ≅ π-conjugacy classes of splittings, fixed subgroup 1 or ≅ Z. **Statement to attack:** for g ≥ 2 and an antiinvolution class σ_i with i ≥ 1 fixed circles, every order-2 lift u of σ_i to Aut(Π̂_g) is Π̂_g-conjugate to the completion of one of the i discrete lifts, and its fixed subgroup Π̂_g^{u} is the closure of Π_g^{u_k}, ≅ Ẑ. **Status:** the first part is equivalent to the real section conjecture for real curves (sections up to conjugacy ↔ π₀(X(R))), proved by Mochizuki (2003) and by Pál (2011) with Smith theory for pro-spaces; see also Stix, LNM 2054 (2013). I have not found the fixed-subgroup statement; given both, the profinite form of Corollaire 2 (index i) follows by the same orbit–stabiliser argument.

- **R29 — 443–445 — reading aid — keep.** Hatcher, *Algebraic topology*, §1.3 (lifting criterion, deck transformations) covers the covering-space steps; it does not replace the admitted Lemma. develop: no.

## New notes (Claude, second pass)

- **C1 — 445–446 — implication not pursued — Claude, second pass.** Corollaire 1 a) settles half of the hope stated on 440, "ces classes sont distinctes": the number of Π_g-conjugacy classes of order-2 lifts is an invariant of the class in 𝒯_g, so σ_i and σ_j with i ≠ j are not conjugate, as the text says. The other half, that every σ ∈ 𝒯_g satisfying (4) lies in one of the g+1 classes, is not addressed here. It needs Nielsen realisation for Z/2 (Nielsen 1942; Kerckhoff 1983), and without the margin's restriction it is false, since separating antiinvolutions give further classes (Weichold). develop: no (known).

- **C2 — 448 — cancelled claim, false as stated — Claude, second pass.** The struck first proof begins "1°) 𝒢_g^{+u_i} → 𝒯_g^{+σ_i} est surjectif". By Corollaire 2 the image has index i, so this fails for i ≥ 2. The struck argument cannot work: u′_i = int(γ).u_i is already conjugate to u_i by γ, so c) yields nothing new, and the author struck the exponent u_i of the h that c) provides (change 37), since c) gives h only in 𝒢_g^+. He replaced the claim by the stabiliser count.

- **C3 — 446 — notation — Claude, second pass.** 𝒢_g = Aut_lac(Π_g) (read lac, not loc). On earlier pages Aut_lac denotes automorphisms that respect the conjugacy classes of the loops ("lacets") around the holes; a closed surface group Π_g has none, so 𝒢_g = Aut(Π_g), 𝒢_g^+ is its orientation-preserving part, and 𝒯_g = A_g/A_g° ≅ 𝒢_g/Inn(Π_g) by Dehn–Nielsen–Baer. develop: no.

- **C4 — 447–448 — own idea — Claude, second pass.** Topological reading of Corollaire 2: an order-2 lift u_i corresponds to σ_i with a base point on one fixed circle Z, 𝒢_g^{u_i} to mapping classes of (X_g, σ_i) fixing that point, and T ≅ Z to pushing the point once around Z, on which the quotient acts by ±1 according as Z keeps or reverses its direction. In the quotient Y the index i is the index of the stabiliser of one boundary circle in a group permuting the i boundary circles transitively (R13), which checks Corollaire 2 independently. This dictionary, through the correspondence between the centraliser of σ_i and the mapping classes of Y, is my heuristic and is not stated in the manuscript; it frames the splitting question of R18.


# ===== LM_P046 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P046 (scans 451–460): research notes, checked against the corrected text

Each research note of section 5 of `LM_P046_notes.md` (numbered 1–39 as there) is checked against `LM_P046_checked.tex` and marked **keep**, **correct** or **drop**, with a reason. Notes added in the second pass follow, labelled *Claude, second pass*. Scan numbers are file scans (manuscript pages 228–232). Formulas follow the corrected transcription: $\dot\tau(z,t)=(-z e^{i\pi t},-t)$, $\dot\tau'(z,t)=(z,-t)$ on $T_j=S_j\times[-1,1]$.

## First-pass notes, checked

1. **451, (23)–(24) — claim without proof — correct.** The text now reads that $U_0\to X_{g,i}$ "définit un homéom. de $X_{g,i}$ avec la surface obtenue en contractant les $\partial\Delta_j\subset U_0$ … à l'aide des projections $\partial\Delta_j\simeq(\partial D_j)\times\varepsilon_j\to D_j$", and the gluing (23) is "avec une partie ouverte et fermée de $\partial X_{g,i}$"; the unread noun is "surface". The claim is a routine cut-and-paste statement whose proof needs the definitions of $U_0$, $D_j$ from scans 449–450. develop: no.

2. **451, Möbius paragraph — claim without proof — keep.** The orientation cover of the Möbius band is $S^1\times I$ with deck transformation $(z,t)\mapsto(-z,-t)$, which is free; this is standard and agrees with the ink "$-z$" (an overwritten exponent remains). develop: no.

3. **451, last paragraph — claim without proof — correct.** The base is read securely as $Y_0$: $X_0=S^2$ with $x\mapsto -x$ is the orientation cover of $Y_0=\mathbb RP^2$. Standard. develop: no.

4. **452, Proposition — claim without proof — keep.** With "$0\le i\le g$" (the witnesses print $1\le i\le g$), the Proposition says that every anti-involution of $X_g$ with non-orientable quotient $Y_{g-i,i}$ is conjugate to the $\tau_{I'}$ built from the antipodal sphere with $\operatorname{card}I'=i$. This is the non-separating half of Weichold's classification of real structures (Weichold 1883; Gross–Harris, Ann. ENS 1981; Natanzon's surveys). develop: no.

5. **452, NB on orientations — own idea — drop.** The audit it proposed is settled by the reading: the NB says that (25) "respecte comme il se doit l'orientation", and the line under (25) adds "i.e. $\partial T\simeq\partial V_0$ [la] renverse". There is no correction of "respecte" into "renverse"; the convention is the usual one, the boundary identification used for gluing reverses the induced orientations.

6. **453, a)–b) and (26) — implication not pursued — keep, verified.** τ is free on $V_0$; $\dot\tau_j$ is free (at $t=0$ it gives $-z$); $\dot\tau'_j$ fixes exactly $S_j\times\{0\}$; on the seams τ exchanges the two boundary circles. Hence $X_g^{\tau_{I'}}=\bigcup_{j\in I'}S_j\times\{0\}$, $\operatorname{card}I'$ disjoint circles. develop: no.

7. **453, (27) and foot of page — claim without proof — keep, verified.** $\dot\tau(\dot\tau(z,t))=(z e^{i\pi t}e^{-i\pi t},t)=(z,t)$; $\dot\tau'^2=\mathrm{id}$; at $t=\pm1$, $\dot\tau(z,t)=(z,-t)=\dot\tau'(z,t)$. The ink order is "On a bien $\dot\tau^2=\mathrm{id}$, $\dot\tau'^2=\mathrm{id}$" and then the boundary equality. develop: no.

8. **453, lower-left NB — claim without proof — keep, verified.** $\dot\tau'(z,t)=(z,-t)$ uses only the product structure and the reflection of $\mathbf I^{\varepsilon_j}$, which commutes with the exchange of $\varepsilon_j$; no identification $S_j\simeq\mathbb U$ is needed. The note ends with "(27)". develop: no.

9. **453, product of the two local involutions — own idea — keep, settled.** $\rho_j=\dot\tau'_j\dot\tau_j:(z,t)\mapsto(-z e^{i\pi t},t)$ is the identity on $\partial T_j$ and turns the circle $S_j\times\{t\}$ by $\pi(1+t)$, a full turn as $t$ runs over $[-1,1]$: one Dehn twist about $S_j\times\{0\}$. develop: no.

10. **454, top display and (28) — claim without proof — keep, verified.** $(\dot\tau'\dot\tau)^2=\rho^2:(z,t)\mapsto(z e^{2i\pi t},t)$, with fixed points exactly where $e^{2i\pi t}=1$, i.e. $t\in\{-1,0,1\}$; composition is read right to left ($\dot\tau$ first). develop: no.

11. **454, margin "Sauf erreur" — explicit doubt — correct.** The group symbol reads $S\Gamma^{!+}(T_j)$ (with "!"), and "(30)" belongs to the main text, not to this note. The doubted assertion is true: the mapping class group of the annulus rel boundary is $\mathbb Z$, generated by the Dehn twist (Farb–Margalit, *Primer*, Prop. 2.4), and the twists about the $g$ disjoint, pairwise non-isotopic, non-separating cores $S_j\times\{0\}$ generate $\mathbb Z^g$ in the mapping class group of $X_g$ (*Primer*, Lemma 3.17). develop: no.

12. **454, (32)–(33) — claim without proof — keep, verified.** On $T_j$ the product $\tau_{I'}\tau_{I''}$ is $\mathrm{id}$ if $j$ lies in both or neither set, $\rho_j$ if $j\in I'\setminus I''$, $\rho_j^{-1}$ if $j\in I''\setminus I'$, and $\mathrm{id}$ on $V_0$; this is (32), and (33) holds because the $\rho_J$ are products of maps with disjoint supports. The definition (30) "l'identité sur $V_0=X_g-\bigcup_{j\in I-I'}T_j$" is a slip: what is meant is the identity off $\bigcup_{j\in I'}T_j$. develop: no.

13. **454, (29) as a single twist — own idea — keep, settled** (see 9). develop: no.

14. **455, torsor formulation and (36) — claim without proof — keep, verified.** Changing the bijection $\varepsilon_j\simeq\{\pm1\}$ acts by $\varphi(z,t)=(\bar z,-t)$ (the twist of $\mathbb U$ by $\varepsilon_j$), and $\varphi\dot\tau\varphi^{-1}=\dot\tau$, $\varphi\dot\tau'\varphi^{-1}=\dot\tau'$; rotations of $S_j$ commute with both. So (27) depends only on the $\mathbb U^{\varepsilon_j}$-torsor structure. develop: no.

15. **455, symmetries commuting "en un sens évident" — claim without proof — correct.** The text reads "et notamment tt autom. qui respecte la structure métrique" (not "évidemment") and "au système des $\tau_j,\tau'_j$". The "sens évident" is equivariance: an automorphism that sends $D'_j$ to $D'_{\pi(j)}$ and respects the torsors carries $\dot\tau_j,\dot\tau'_j$ to $\dot\tau_{\pi(j)},\dot\tau'_{\pi(j)}$. develop: no.

16. **456, centres and torsors — claim without proof — correct and settle.** The margin now reads "On ne suppose plus néc. que $D_j$ soit un cercle, seulement que $\partial D_j$ pas trop sauvage". For a Jordan domain $D$ with interior point $a$, the Riemann map $f$ with $f(a)=0$ is unique up to rotation and extends to a homeomorphism of the closures (Carathéodory), so a Jordan boundary is enough. For a round disc the induced torsor agrees with the metric one if and only if $a$ is the centre, since harmonic measure seen from $a$ is uniform exactly then. develop: no.

17. **456, "Re-NB" conditions a)–c) — implication not pursued — keep.** Condition b) at $t=0$ gives $u_0=u_0^{-1}$, so $u_0$ is an involution; a fixed-point-free involution of $S^1$ preserves orientation and is conjugate to the rotation by π, which is why "et d'ordre 2" was inserted. The ansatz (37) assumes that $\tau_j$ maps each circle $S_j\times\{t\}$ to $S_j\times\{-t\}$; this restriction is discussed in the second-pass note C. develop: see C.

18. **456, winding data of admissible paths — own idea — correct.** The question is answered within the ansatz (37) in note C: the free extensions form a family indexed by even integers, all conjugate rel boundary to $\dot\tau$. develop: see C.

19. **456, closing parenthesis — implication not pursued — keep.** For $\dot\tau$, $u_t(z)=e^{i\pi(1+t)}z$, so $s\mapsto u_{1-s}$ (rotation by $-\pi s$ up to a constant) comes from the one-parameter group of rotations. Every $\rho^m\dot\tau$ also has $u_t$ a rotation affine in $t$, so these examples all come from one-parameter groups; whether every admissible path is isotopic to such a one is part of note C. develop: see C.

20. **457, lines 1–6 — question (author's programme) — keep.** Up to homeomorphism the answer is given on 458–459: two classes, with quotient a Möbius band (free, $\dot\tau$) or an annulus with one selected boundary circle ($\dot\tau'$). Conjugacy rel boundary is note C. develop: no.

21. **457, the doubling correspondence — claim without proof — keep.** The text reads "en associant à une telle $(Y,(\partial Y)')$ son doublement orienté" and "à $X,\sigma$ correspondant $(X/\sigma,\ldots)$". This is the classical correspondence between symmetric surfaces and their quotients (Klein; Alling–Greenleaf, *Foundations of the Theory of Klein Surfaces*, LNM 219, 1971). develop: no.

22. **457, margin "pas vrai !" — implication not pursued — keep, verified.** The margin withdraws "Y non orientable" as a general claim and gives the orientable case: with $\chi(Y)=2-2\gamma-j$, $\chi(X)=2\chi(Y)$ and $\nu=2(j-i)$ one gets $g+\nu/2=2\gamma+j-1$, as in the margin's (39). develop: no.

23. **457, Euler characteristic and (38) — claim without proof — keep, verified.** $X\setminus X^\sigma$ is a two-sheeted covering of $Y\setminus(\partial Y)'$ and circles have $\chi_!=0$, so $\chi(X)=2\chi(Y)$; with the author's convention $\chi(Y_{\gamma,j})=1-(\gamma+j)$ (e.g. $Y_{g-i,i}$, $\mathbb RP^2$ minus $g$ discs plus $g-i$ Möbius bands) this is (38). develop: no.

24. **457, testing (38) and (39) — own idea — keep, done.** On the cylinder ($g=0$, $\nu=2$) the non-orientable case gives $(\gamma,j,i)=(0,1,0)$ (Möbius band, $\dot\tau$) and the orientable case $(0,2,1)$ (annulus, $\dot\tau'$), as on 458–459; for $Y_{g-i,i}$ one gets $g=\gamma+i$, $\nu=0$. develop: no.

25. **458, ν even — claim without proof — keep, with argument.** If σ preserved a boundary component $C$, then $\sigma|_C$ would reverse the boundary orientation of $C$ (σ reverses the orientation of $X$ and keeps the inward normal inward), and an orientation-reversing involution of a circle has two fixed points, against the hypothesis. develop: no.

26. **458, "j'ai oublié de noter" (39) — claim without proof — keep, verified.** Each unselected component of $\partial Y$ lifts to two boundary circles of $X$, each selected one to a circle of $X^\sigma$, so $\nu=2(j-i)$ and $i=\operatorname{card}\pi_0(X^\sigma)$. develop: no.

27. **457–458, eliminating ν — own idea — correct.** The formal consequences $g=\gamma+i$ (non-orientable quotient) and $g=2\gamma+i-1$ (orientable) stand. Drop the remark about "the visibly written preliminary equation $\gamma+i=2$": it lies inside the cancelling loop of the abandoned attempt with $g=1$, $\nu=2$, and is not a live statement. develop: no.

28. **458, case a) — claim without proof — keep.** The text reads "$T^\sigma=\varnothing$ … se déduit du plan projectif réel en y faisant un trou à bord (d'où ruban Möbius), et en prenant le doublement orienté": the oriented double of the Möbius band is the annulus with $(z,t)\mapsto(-z,-t)$, which is $\dot\tau$ in other coordinates. develop: no.

29. **458, "Le cas où $T^\sigma\neq\varnothing$" — claim without proof — keep, verified for the cylinder.** In the non-orientable case $\gamma+j=1$ and $j-i=1$ force $i=0$, so $i\ge1$ forces an orientable quotient, and $2\gamma+j=2$, $j\ge1$ leave only $(\gamma,j,i)=(0,2,1)$. develop: no.

30. **459, "C'est bien les deux cas" — claim without proof — keep.** Up to homeomorphism this follows from 21 and the classification of the Möbius band and the annulus; the finer statement rel boundary is note C. develop: no.

31. **459, "une situation … mettant en évidence un maximum de symétries" — question — keep.** The word is read "maximum". The ingredients are now read as automorphisms of $X_0$ commuting with τ, "invariant l'ens des $D'_j$", respecting torsor structures "sur l'ens. des $\partial D'_j$". develop: see 35.

32. **459, lifting to the sphere — claim without proof — correct.** The margin reads "non, sans indétermination, en relevant … de façon à respecter l'orientation(s)": a homeomorphism of $\mathbb RP^2$ has two lifts to $S^2$, differing by τ, and exactly one preserves orientation. The multiplier in "(à indétermination de multiplication par $\tau\|2$ près)" is still undecided in the ink; τ is what the lifting statement needs, the witnesses print 2. develop: no.

33. **459, "On prévoit que" — conjecture — keep.** The parenthesis reads "(travaillant mod isotopie)", and probably "on aura un groupe". develop: see 35.

34. **459, lifting problem — own idea — keep, partly settled by 32.** The kernel question disappears once lifts are required to preserve orientation; what remains is the isotopy question of note 35. develop: no.

35. **459–460, "voisin d'un groupe de tresses, et sans doute calculable sans grand mal" — conjecture — keep. develop: yes.** Statement to attack: let $\mathcal M_g$ be the group of isotopy classes of homeomorphisms of $\mathbb RP^2$ that preserve a union of $g$ disjoint closed discs $D_i$ and the $\mathbb U$-torsor structures on the circles $\partial D_i$; describe $\mathcal M_g$ as an extension of a braid group of $\mathbb RP^2$ (permuting the discs, with the orientation data of the holes) by the $\mathbb Z^g$ of boundary twists, and decide whether the orientation-preserving lifts of $\mathcal M_g$ to $X_g$, together with the involutions $\tau_{I'}$ ($I'\subset I$), generate the extended mapping class group of $X_g$. Status: presentations of the braid groups $B_n(\mathbb RP^2)$ are known (Van Buskirk, Trans. AMS 1966; Gonçalves–Guaschi, AGT 2004); mapping classes of a non-orientable surface lift into those of its orientation cover (Birman–Chillingworth 1972); the extended mapping class group is generated by three orientation-reversing involutions (Stukow, C. R. Acad. Sci. 2004). I know of no treatment of this framed $\mathbb RP^2$ group or of this generating statement.

36. **460, generators and relations — question — correct.** The ink reads "on trouvera peut être un démarrage pour engendrer p.ex. $\mathcal T_g$ (or $\Pi_g$) par générateurs (anti-involutifs ?) et relations": "démarrage", not "dévissage", and the group letter is probably the script T of the continuation (LM_P047). Mapping class groups are generated by involutions (McCarthy–Papadopoulos 1987) and the extended one by orientation-reversing involutions (Stukow 2004); presentations exist by Dehn twists (Hatcher–Thurston 1980, Wajnryb 1983), not to my knowledge by anti-involutions. develop: no (the precise part is folded into 35).

37. **460, normality of $\mathcal H$ — claim without proof — keep, verified.** $\tau_{I'}\rho_j\tau_{I'}=(\tau_{I'}\tau_{\{j\}})(\tau_\varnothing\tau_{I'})$ is a product of two elements of $\mathcal H$ by (32), and all $\tau_{I'}$ coincide mod $\mathcal H$, so $G/\mathcal H$ is generated by one involution and detected by the orientation character. The text cites "la formule (31) or (32)", singular. develop: no.

38. **460, semidirect product — own idea — keep, verified.** $\tau_\varnothing\in G$ is an involution mapping to $-1$, so $G=\mathcal H\rtimes\langle\tau_\varnothing\rangle$. develop: no.

39. **454 and 460, generalized dihedral group — own idea — keep, settled.** On $T_j$, $\dot\tau\rho\dot\tau=\dot\tau\dot\tau'=\rho^{-1}$, so $\tau_\varnothing\rho_j\tau_\varnothing=\rho_j^{-1}$; $\mathcal H\simeq\mathbb Z^g$, and $G\simeq\mathbb Z^g\rtimes\mathbb Z/2$ with inversion. $G$ injects into the extended mapping class group of $X_g$: the twists are independent (*Primer*, Lemma 3.17) and the other coset reverses orientation. develop: no.

## Notes added in the second pass (Claude)

A. **452, $\varepsilon_j=\{j'\in J\|\mathbf I\mid j'\text{ sur }i\}$ — own note (slip).** The ink ends with "sur $i$" while the cylinder is indexed by $j\in I$; the set letter is ambiguous between $J$ and a heavy $\mathbf I$. What is meant is the two-element set of indices in $J$ above $j$, identified with the orientations of $S_j$. No mathematical consequence. develop: no.

B. **454, (30) — own note (slip in a definition).** As written, "$\rho_{I'}$ … l'identité sur $V_0=X_g-\bigcup_{j\in I-I'}T_j$" names a set that is not $V_0$ (which is $X_g-\bigcup_{j\in I}T_j$). The formulas (32), (33) and the NB $\rho_j=\tau_{\{j\}}\tau_\varnothing$ only hold if $\rho_{I'}$ is the identity off $\bigcup_{j\in I'}T_j$, so that is the definition in use. develop: no.

C. **453–459, anti-involutions of the cylinder rel boundary — implication not pursued. develop: yes.** Computation: $(\rho^m\dot\tau)^2=\mathrm{id}$ for all $m\in\mathbb Z$; $\rho^m\dot\tau$ is free if and only if $m$ is even (at $t=0$ it is $z\mapsto(-1)^{m+1}z$); $\rho^{2k}\dot\tau=\rho^k\dot\tau\rho^{-k}$ and $\rho^{2k+1}\dot\tau=\rho^k\dot\tau'\rho^{-k}$, where $\rho^k$ is the identity on $\partial T$. Within the ansatz (37), the paths satisfying a)–c) fall into components indexed by $\pi_1(\mathrm{Homeo}^+(S^1))=\mathbb Z$ (the space of fixed-point-free involutions of $S^1$ is weakly contractible), represented by the $\rho^{2k}\dot\tau$; replacing c) by $u_0=\mathrm{id}$ gives the $\rho^{2k+1}\dot\tau$. Statement to attack: every anti-involution of $S^1\times[-1,1]$ that restricts to $(z,\pm1)\mapsto(z,\mp1)$ on the boundary is conjugate, by a homeomorphism that is the identity on the boundary, to exactly one of $\dot\tau$ and $\dot\tau'$. Status: the pieces are Kerékjártó's theorem on periodic homeomorphisms (Constantin–Kolev, Enseign. Math. 1994) and $\mathrm{Mod}(A,\partial A)\simeq\mathbb Z$; I know no printed statement of this rel-boundary version, which would make "C'est bien les deux cas" and the free choice of $\dot\tau_j$ in the Proposition precise.

D. **455–456, the conformal variant — own idea.** The antipodal map is anticonformal, so it carries the torsor of $\partial D'_j$ defined by the centre $a'_j$ to the conjugate torsor of $\partial D'_{\tau j}$ defined by $\tau(a'_j)$; the condition "de façon compatible avec l'involution" ($a'_{\tau j}=\tau(a'_j)$) is exactly what makes the structure descend as a torsor under the twisted group $\mathbb U^{\varepsilon_j}$. Together with note 16 this gives the conformal version of the symmetric situation of 459 without the round-disc hypothesis. develop: no.

E. **458, the cancelled first attempt — own note.** The cancelled block ("Dans le cas actuel $g=1,\nu=2$ … $\gamma+i=2$ … $\gamma=2,\ i=0$") used $g=1$ and omitted $\nu=2(j-i)$; with the cylinder's data $g=0$, $\nu=2$ and both relations, the enumeration that follows is correct and complete (notes 24, 29). develop: no.


# ===== LM_P047 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P047 (scans 461–470): research notes, checked against the corrected text

Each first-pass note (`answers/unzipped/LM_P047/LM_P047_notes.md`, §5) is kept, corrected or dropped, with the reason.
Unnumbered first-pass bullets are numbered here by scan and order (464-1, 464-2, …). Notes marked **[2nd pass]** are
mine. Quotations follow `LM_P047_checked.tex`. Notation: X_g = V_0 ∪ ⋃_j T_j (sphere X_0 minus 2g antipodal disks,
plus g tubes); ρ_j the twist of (29) in T_j (a full Dehn twist: ρ_j(z,t) = (z·e^{iπ(t+1)}, t)); ℋ = ⟨ρ_j⟩;
𝒢 = ℋ ⋊ {1,τ}; F = 𝔻_{2g} × ⟨τ⟩.

## A. First-pass notes

**461-R1 — scan 461 — unproved claim — keep, sharpened.** The semidirect product 𝒢 = ℋ ⋊ {1,τ} follows from (41)
(τ_{I'} = ρ_{I'}τ), the normality of ℋ shown on scan 460, τ ∉ ℋ (orientation character) and (42). As homeomorphisms
ℋ ≅ ℤ^I is immediate (twists of infinite order with disjoint supports); in the mapping class group it is the standard
independence of Dehn twists about disjoint, pairwise non-isotopic essential curves (here a cut system).

**461-R2 — scan 461 — unpursued implication — keep.** The whole coset τℋ consists of anti-involutions; the conjugacy
question is taken up on scan 463. The ink calls this coset ℋ⁻ (l. 2) and ℋ^{-1} (l. 9), not 𝒢⁻; the note is unaffected.

**461-R3 — scan 461 — unproved claim — corrected.** The gap is smaller than stated: σ = τ on V_0 has no fixed point
there, (44)–(45) give the fixed set in each tube exactly (empty or the middle circle S_j×{0}), and the tubes are disjoint,
so X_g^σ is the disjoint union of the circles with n_j odd and (46) counts all components.

**462-R1 — scan 462 — unpursued implication — keep.** The index |I'_u| forgets which tubes carry odd exponents; the
parity vector in ℋ/ℋ² is the finer invariant used on scan 463 (see 463-R4 for its orbits).

**462-R2 — scan 462 — unproved claim — corrected (reading and status).** The sentence reads "Il en résulte par des
raisons générales que u est conjugué dans 𝒜_g = Aut(X_g) (par un élément de A_g^+) à τ_{I'} = ρ_{I'}τ = τρ_{I'}^{-1}
ou aussi ρ_{I'}^{-1}τ = τρ_{I'}" (not "Il est visible"; the last formula is not cancelled). The claim is proved by the
criterion of scan 463: for ρ' = ∏ρ_j^{n_j}, ρ'' = ρ_{I'_u}^{-1}, ρ'ρ''^{-1} = ∏ρ_j^{n_j+δ_j} (δ_j = 1 iff n_j odd) lies in
ℋ², so ρ = ∏ρ_j^{(n_j+δ_j)/2} ∈ ℋ ⊂ 𝒜_g^+ conjugates u to τ_{I'_u}. The "general reasons" (Weichold's classification of
real structures by number of ovals and dividing type) give the same, since X_g minus part of a cut system is connected.

**463-R1 — scan 463 — unpursued implication — keep.** ℋ-classes of anti-involutions in 𝒢 ↔ ℋ/ℋ² ≅ (ℤ/2)^g (2^g
classes), against g+1 classes in Aut(X_g) (one for each number |I'| of ovals).

**463-R2 — scan 463 — conjecture (expectation) — keep.** An informal preference, with no extremal statement; the
symmetry group realised is 𝔻_{2g} × {±1} ⊂ O(3), of order 8g (scan 464).

**463-R3 — scan 463 — unproved claim — keep.** Scan 465 asserts the equivariant construction ("la surface X_g est
déterminée sans ambiguïté, et le groupe 𝔻_{2g} y opère par transport de structure"); what has to be checked is the
μ_{2g}-equivariance of the tube gluings and that the half-turns act on the tubes compatibly with the (z,t) coordinates.

**463-R4 — scan 463 — own idea (first pass) — corrected: computed.** 𝔻_{2g} acts on the g diagonals through a dihedral
group of order 2g (the half-turn about the polar axis acts trivially) and τ does not change parities, so the
𝔻_{2g}^~·ℋ-classes of the anti-involutions τρ (ρ ∈ ℋ) are the subsets of ℤ/g up to dihedral symmetry, i.e. binary
bracelets of length g. For g ≤ 3 these are g+1, as in Aut(X_g); from g = 4 on there are more (g = 4: 6 against 5,
adjacent versus opposite pairs of diagonals), so the remaining fusion needs mapping classes outside 𝔻_{2g}^~ ⋉ ℋ.

**463-R5 — scan 463 — own idea (first pass) — kept, answered by 463-R4.** Small genera g ≤ 3 already realise all
Aut(X_g)-classes of the τρ inside 𝔻_{2g}^~ ⋉ ℋ; g = 4 is the first genus where they do not.

**464-1 — scan 464 — unproved claim — corrected.** τz = −1/z̄ is the antipodal map −id ∈ O(3), which is central, so the
commutation with 𝔻_n ⊂ SO(3) and 𝔻_n^~ ≅ 𝔻_n × ℤ/2 (order 8g for n = 2g) are immediate; z ↦ 1/z̄ is the reflection in
the equatorial plane and z ↦ −z the half-turn about the polar axis, as written. The extra ζ-like glyph in
"u_{ζ,α,β} = ζu_{ζ,α}τ^β" stays unread; the two case formulas below it contain no extra factor.

**464-2 — scan 464 — unpursued implication — corrected (reading).** The reservation reads "τ renverse l'orientation,
donc est à manier avec réserve pour ce qui concerne le 'transport de structure' dans la situation présente" ("est à"
and "manier" written over cancelled words; no "à priori"). Its mathematical content is (42): transport by an
orientation-reversing map turns a Dehn twist into the inverse twist, so 𝔻_{2g}^~ acts on ℋ by signed permutations.

**464-3 — scan 464 — unproved claim — keep.** The margin condition "D'_0 ne rencontre pas ζD'_0, où ζ = exp 2iπ/2g"
makes the 2g holes disjoint; as τ(ζ) = −ζ on 𝕌, the holes form g antipodal pairs, as needed.

**464-4 — scan 464 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** A spherical cap about P_0 = 1 of angular radius < π/2g is disjoint
from its rotates by μ_{2g} (centres 2π/2g apart on the equator), which makes the margin condition explicit.

**465-1 — scan 465 — unproved claim — keep.** (49) for orientation-preserving u ∈ 𝔻_{2g} follows once u maps tubes to
tubes in the standard coordinates and commutes with τ on V_0. The ink writes "le polygone à 2μ côtés" (a slip for 2g).

**465-2 — scan 465 — unproved claim — keep.** For orientation-preserving u the twist of T_j is carried to the twist of
T_{u(j)} whether or not u exchanges the ends of the tube, so the display with the cancelled number (51) holds as an
identity of homeomorphisms when the tube coordinates are transported by u.

**465-3 — scan 465 — unpursued implication — corrected.** There is no kernel to compute: on V_0 an element uh
(u ∈ 𝔻_{2g}, h ∈ 𝒢) acts as u or as uτ ∈ O(3), which forces u = 1 and h ∈ ℋ, and a non-trivial element of ℋ moves
points of some tube; the action of 𝔻_{2g}·𝒢 on X_g is faithful.

**465-4 — scan 465 — question — keep.** The extension of the 𝔻_{2g}^~ action is settled on scan 466 (τ_{X_0} ↦ τ_g).

**465-5 — scan 465 — own idea (first pass) — keep, answered.** On one tube, orientation-preserving symmetries
transport twists to twists and τ inverts them (42): "permute versus invert" is exactly the orientation character.

**466-1 — scan 466 — unproved claim — keep.** τ_g commutes with 𝔻_{2g} because −id is central in O(3) and the tube
extension of τ is chosen equivariantly.

**466-2 — scan 466 — alternative convention — keep.** τ_I has the g middle circles as fixed set (g ovals, non-dividing),
τ_g none: two non-conjugate real structures, either of which could represent τ_{X_0}. The raised mark on τ_I in the
fixed-set sentence is still unread.

**466-3 — scan 466 — unproved claim — keep.** τ_I = ρ_Iτ_g commutes with 𝔻_n because ρ_I = ∏ρ_i is 𝔻_{2g}-invariant
by the display uρ_ju^{-1} = ρ_{u(j)} of scan 465; the argument is complete given that display.

**466-4 — scan 466 — opinion ("il ne semble pas important") — keep.** The two conventions differ by ρ_I, all of whose
exponents are odd, so ρ_I ∉ ℋ² and τ_g, τ_I are not even ℋ-conjugate (nor conjugate in Aut(X_g): 0 versus g ovals).

**466-5 — scan 466 — unproved claim — keep.** 𝔻_{2g}^~.ℋ ⊃ 𝒢 = {1,τ_g}.ℋ: τ_g ∈ 𝔻_{2g}^~ acts by inversion and
𝔻_{2g} by permuting the ρ_j; this signed-permutation module is what (52) must encode (see 469-1). The ink numbers this
display (51); the witnesses print (52).

**466-6/466-7 — scan 466 — own ideas (first pass) — merged and answered.** The change of convention is the change of
splitting of 𝒢 → {±1} from τ_g to τ_I = ρ_Iτ_g; by 466-4 the two splittings are not ℋ-conjugate.

**467-1 — scan 467 — programme (conjecture kind) — keep. develop: yes.** "Expliciter l'action extérieure de ce
produit ½ direct sur le groupe fondamental π_g, avec une attention toute particulière à l'action du groupe (ℤ/2ℤ)³ …
qui stabilise un des cylindres T_j." Statement to attack: *for the antipodal-tube model of X_g (holes at μ_{2g} on the
equator, g tubes joining antipodal holes), give a presentation of π_1(X_g, pole) adapted to the tubes (the surface
relation in this "panachée" arrangement) and compute ε_0, ε_1, τ_g, ρ_j and the stabiliser (ℤ/2)³ of one tube as
explicit outer automorphisms.* Status: the action of twists on π_1 is classical (e.g. Zieschang–Vogt–Coldewey;
Farb–Margalit, *A Primer on Mapping Class Groups*); I know no treatment of this configuration; a finite computation.

**467-2 — scan 467 — unproved claim — keep, verified.** The stabiliser in 𝔻_{2g} of the diagonal {ζ_0, −ζ_0} is
{1, c, ε, εc} (c the half-turn about the polar axis, ε the half-turn about the axis through ζ_0); with −id this gives the
eight sign changes in the orthonormal frame (axis through ζ_0, orthogonal equatorial axis, polar axis), for g ≥ 2.

**467-3 — scan 467 — programme — corrected (reading changes the content).** Item 2 reads "Étendre l'action sur π_g du
groupe de Teichmüller (plus ou moins) 'spécial' de chacun des T_j, en l'action d'un groupe analogue d'un ensemble plus
grand obtenu en lui rajoutant une 'lanière' L (d'où un tore à 1 trou …)", not "Étudier … ou l'action": the plan is to
extend the action from the tube's group to the groups of the one-holed torus T_j ∪ L and of the two-holed torus
T_j ∪ L ∪ L'; "spécial" presumably means fixing the boundary pointwise.

**467-4 — scan 467 — unproved claim — keep, verified.** T_j ∪ L (annulus plus a band joining its two boundary circles
through V_0) has χ = −1 and one boundary circle: a one-holed torus; adding L' = τ(L) gives χ = −2 and two boundary
circles: a two-holed torus. Both statements are right; the drawings agree.

**467-5 — scan 467 — margin claim — keep; known.** The mapping class group of the one-holed torus fixing the boundary
is the braid group B_3, a central extension 1 → ℤ → B_3 → SL(2,ℤ) → 1 (the boundary twist (ab)^6 generates an index-2
subgroup of the centre), and B_3 is the preimage of SL(2,ℤ) in the universal cover of SL(2,ℝ) (classical; cf. Milnor,
*Introduction to Algebraic K-Theory*, §10). Grothendieck's "extension centrale remarquable" is exactly this.

**467-6 — scan 467 — own idea (first pass) — merged into 467-1.**
