**467-7 — scan 467 — own idea (first pass) — keep, answered.** Boundary fixed pointwise gives B_3 (the central
extension); boundary only preserved, or a puncture, gives SL(2,ℤ). The "spécial" group of the margin is the first.

**468-1 — scan 468 — conjecture — corrected (reading changes the content).** The text reads "Il est possible qu'il
faudra considérer de près ce dernier, pour étudier les relations entre les éléments de 𝒯_g provenant des ensembles
précédents": the two-holed torus just described is to be studied closely for the relations among mapping classes
coming from the tube, the one-holed and the two-holed tori; nothing about pairs ("pris a deux"). Later presentations
of Mod(S_g) do state relations through such small subsurfaces: chain relations on one- and two-holed tori, lantern
relations on four-holed spheres (Hatcher–Thurston 1980; Wajnryb 1983; Gervais 2001).

**468-2 — scan 468 — conjecture — keep.** Writing ∏[a_i,b_i] = 1 for handles arranged "panachée" (all tubes crossing
the equator) instead of "à la queue-leu-leu" is part of the computation of 467-1.

**468-3 — scan 468 — unproved claim — corrected.** All listed relations hold for the geometric generators (ε_1 fixes
the tube j_0 and preserves orientation, so it commutes with ρ_0 and τ; c = (ε_0ε_1)^g preserves every tube; τ commutes
with 𝔻_{2g}); the defect is completeness, see 469-1. The NB margin now reads "ε_0 bouge le sommet du repère, ε_1 l'arête
et pas le sommet, donc ε_1(j_0) = j_0": ε_0, ε_1 are the reflections of a flag (vertex, edge) of the 2g-gon.

**468-4 — scan 468 — unpursued implication — kept, answered by 469-1.** The information missing from the relations is
exactly the commutation of ρ_0 with its 𝔻_{2g}-conjugates.

**469-1 — scan 469 — unproved claim ("sauf erreur") — corrected: false for g ≥ 2. develop: yes.** (52) says that
⟨ε_0,ε_1,τ⟩ = F = 𝔻_{2g} × ⟨τ⟩ and that τ' is an involution commuting with K = ⟨ε_1, (ε_0ε_1)^g⟩ ≅ (ℤ/2)²; the group
presented is therefore the amalgam F *_K (K × ⟨τ'⟩) of two finite groups, which is virtually free and cannot be
𝔻_{2g}^~ ⋉ ℋ ⊃ ℤ^g when g ≥ 2 (for g = 1 both are (ℤ/2)² × D_∞, and (52) is right). Since τ(τ'τ)τ = (τ'τ)^{-1} already
follows from τ² = τ'² = 1, the missing relations are only that x = τ'τ (= ρ_0) commute with its conjugates. Statement to
attack: *for g ≥ 2, (52) together with [τ'τ, (ε_0ε_1)^k τ'τ (ε_0ε_1)^{-k}] = 1 for 1 ≤ k ≤ ⌊g/2⌋ is a presentation of
𝔻_{2g}^~ ⋉ ℋ, while (52) alone presents the virtually free group (𝔻_{2g} × ℤ/2) *_{(ℤ/2)²} (ℤ/2)³.* Status: elementary
(presentations of semidirect products with signed permutation modules; Karrass–Pietrowski–Solitar for virtual
freeness); no published treatment of this presentation known to me.

**469-2 — scan 469 — unproved claim — keep, verified.** With ε'_i = τε_i and τ commuting with ε_i: ε'_i² = (τε_i)²,
(τε'_i)² = ε_i², (ε'_0ε'_1)^{2g} = (ε_0ε_1)^{2g}, (τ'(ε'_0ε'_1)^g)² = (τ'(ε_0ε_1)^g)², and (τ'ε_1)² becomes
(τ'(τε'_1))². So (52bis) is a Tietze transform of (52), "les mêmes relations sauf la dernière de la deuxième ligne",
and equally incomplete.

**469-3 — scan 469 — unpursued implication — keep.** The torus analogy is developed on scan 470; see 470-3 and the
second-pass note C-3.

**469-4 — scan 469 — own idea (first pass) — kept, superseded by 469-1.** The g = 2 test was right; it generalises to
every g ≥ 2.

**470-1 — scan 470 — conjecture — corrected (reading).** "on a vraiment envie d'en avoir (?) une caractérisation
intrinsèque dans 𝒯_g — ou dans 𝒮_g (?)": the second group letter is unresolved (S, G or C; witnesses S_g). The question
itself is open-ended; 470-3 shows the first candidate characterisation fails.

**470-2 — scan 470 — unproved claim — keep.** Realised by homeomorphisms supported in the tubes, ℋ fixes V_0 pointwise,
hence acts on π_1(X_g, pole) by genuine (based) automorphisms; correct.

**470-3 — scan 470 — conjecture — corrected: false as stated. develop: yes (variant).** "Ce sont peut-être les
sous-groupes abéliens-libres maximaux" fails in 𝒯_g for g ≥ 2: V_0 is a sphere with 2g ≥ 4 holes and carries 2g − 3
disjoint essential non-peripheral curves, which with the g tube cores form a pants decomposition; its 3g − 3 twists
generate ℤ^{3g−3} ⊋ ℋ (3g − 3 is the maximal rank: Birman–Lubotzky–McCarthy 1983). Even among subgroups inverted by τ_g,
ℋ is not maximal (a τ_g-invariant curve in V_0 separating the holes into two antipodal halves adds a twist). Statement
to attack: *for a fixed-point-free anti-involution τ of X_g (g ≥ 3), the maximal rank of a free abelian subgroup
A ⊂ Mod^±(X_g) with τaτ^{-1} = a^{-1} for all a ∈ A is 2g − 1.* Status: lower bound 2g − 1 from the lifts of g + 1
disjoint one-sided curves of the quotient N_{g+1} plus one inverted product T_cT_{τc} per τ-orbit of a pants decomposition
of the two lifted copies of S_{0,g+1} (my construction; for g = 2 it gives 3 = 3g − 3); the upper bound is open to my
knowledge.

**470-4 — scan 470 — question — corrected: answered in substance.** "On aimerait étudier le normalisateur — est-ce
exactement ce qui provient des autom de X_g − ⋃T̊_i ≃ V_0 ?" The normaliser of the twist group of a multicurve C is the
stabiliser of C (fT_cf^{-1} = T_{f(c)}, and a twist T_d lies in ⟨T_C⟩ only if d ∈ C), and the stabiliser of the cut
system maps, with kernel ℋ, onto the mapping classes of V_0 that permute the 2g boundary circles compatibly with their
pairing into tubes (cutting homomorphism; cf. Farb–Margalit, *Primer*, ch. 3). So: yes, modulo ℋ and with the pairing
condition on the boundary circles.

**470-5 — scan 470 — conjecture (analogy) — corrected (reading), keep.** "Les SL(2,ℤ) associés aux 'lanières' à travers
le tube T_j jouent-ils un rôle analogue à celui des groupes SL(2,K) ou GP(1,k) 'de rang 1' … ? Si Jean ne 'mord' …"
(one tube; "ne"). No precise statement is posed; the analogy belongs with Harvey's curve complex as an analogue of the
Tits building (1981) and its later development (Ivanov); see C-3.

**470-6 — scan 470 — own idea (first pass) — kept, settled.** The essential curve in V_0 exists for g ≥ 2 and its twist
is independent of ℋ; this is the refutation in 470-3.

**470-7 — scan 470 — own idea (first pass) — keep, refined.** Based formulas at a pole hold for the subgroup fixing the
poles: ℋ and the rotations μ_{2g}; ε_0, ε_1 (half-turns about equatorial axes) and τ_g exchange the poles and need a
connecting path.

**470-8 — scan 470 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Homological actions of the lanière groups: see C-3, where the
symplectic image changes the analogy.

## B. Notes on cancelled material

**[2nd pass] B-1 — scan 462 — unpursued implication (cancelled).** The cancelled paragraph after "ou aussi
ρ_{I'}^{-1}τ = τρ_{I'}" finds 𝒢 "trop petit" to conjugate all τρ and proposes, "d'une part en introduisant des groupes
de symétries supplémentaires, d'autre part en complétant chaque T_j par une configuration plus" [grande]: the two moves
carried out on scans 463–468 (the dihedral symmetries; the lanières). The cancelled margin computes
(τρ)(τρ')(τρ)^{-1} = τρ²ρ'^{-1}, so τρ' ~ τρ'' as soon as ρ² = ρ'ρ'', which is the criterion of scan 463.

## C. Second-pass notes

**[2nd pass] C-1 — scans 461–463 — own idea.** The 2^g ℋ-classes and the g+1 Aut(X_g)-classes of the anti-involutions
τρ are linked by the bracelet count of 463-R4; a natural next step is to name, for g = 4, a mapping class of X_g
conjugating τ_{{0,1}} to τ_{{0,2}} (it must move the cut system, e.g. a handle exchange in the "panachée" arrangement).
develop: no.

**[2nd pass] C-2 — scan 469 — own idea.** The corrected presentation of 469-1 has generators ε_0, ε_1, τ, τ' and
4 + ⌊g/2⌋ relation types; written with ε'_0, ε'_1 as in (52bis) all four generators are anti-involutions and the
commutation relations become [τ'τ, (ε'_0ε'_1)^k τ'τ (ε'_0ε'_1)^{-k}] = 1, since ε'_0ε'_1 = ε_0ε_1. develop: no (part of
469-1).

**[2nd pass] C-3 — scans 469–470 — unpursued implication.** Under H_1, each ρ_j maps to the transvection
v ↦ v + ⟨v, s_j⟩s_j, so ℋ maps to commuting unipotent elements in the unipotent radical of the Siegel parabolic of the
Lagrangian spanned by the tube cores, not to a torus of Sp(2g,ℤ). In the dictionary of Harvey and Ivanov, stabilisers
of multicurves play the role of parabolic subgroups and twist groups that of (centres of) unipotent radicals; the
"maximal torus" picture fits the pants twist groups ℤ^{3g−3}, whose stabiliser modulo the twists is finite, better than
ℋ, whose normaliser modulo ℋ (470-4) is infinite. The lanière groups B_3 → SL(2,ℤ) then look like root SL_2's attached
to the parabolic of the cut system. develop: no (interpretive; no single statement to prove).

**[2nd pass] C-4 — scan 465 — remark.** The ink's "polygone à 2μ côtés" is a slip for 2g sides (the g diagonals of the
regular 2g-gon with vertices μ_{2g}); no mathematical consequence.


# ===== LM_P048 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P048 (scans 471–480): research notes, checked against the corrected text

Each first-pass note (`answers/unzipped/LM_P048/LM_P048_notes.md`, §5, R01–R44) is kept, corrected or dropped, with
the reason. Notes marked **[2nd pass]** are mine. Quotations follow `LM_P048_checked.tex`. Notation: 𝕀Γ is the
doubled-stem Γ (`\mathbb{\Gamma}`), Γ, Γ′, Γ″ the single-stem Galois groups; 𝒯̂, 𝒯̂̂, 𝒢̂, 𝒢̂̂, 𝓜, 𝒩 as written on the
pages. Literature named in my notes is from memory and is to be checked before it is cited.

## A. First-pass notes

**R01 — scan 472 — conjecture — keep, corrected.** The text reads "d'où une application injective Discrét(π) ↪
Discrét(π′); on voudrait prouver qu'elle est compatible avec la relation d'équivalence [de l'arithmétisation, doubt]",
so that every arithmétisation (and prédiscrétification) of π gives one of π′. The goal is well-definedness on
classes; the injectivity on discrétifications is asserted, not proved (see C-1).

**R02 — scan 472 — conjecture — keep.** Injectivity of P⁺(π) → P⁺(π′) and A(π) → A(π′) is the stated aim; 473
reduces it to the relations (2). The A-map is drawn as a plain stroke, which does not change the statement.

**R03 — scan 472 — unproved claim — keep, corrected.** The reduction is "au cas où π′ est invariant caractéristique"
("invariant" is not cancelled). Existence is standard: a topologically finitely generated profinite group has finitely
many open subgroups of a given index, and their intersection is an open characteristic subgroup inside π′; injectivity
for π″ ⊂ π′ ⊂ π descends to π′ because the map for π″ factors through the map for π′.

**R04 — scan 473 — unproved claim — keep, corrected.** ① "L'hom 𝒢̂̂(π₀) → 𝒢̂̂(π′₀) envoie 𝓜(π₀) dans 𝓜(π′₀)" is the
"assertion repérée" of 474. The horizontal links of the diagram are the hand's inclusion sign (hook, no head, as in
Discrét(π) ↪ Discrét(π′) on 472), so the rows read 𝒢̂⁺ ⊂ 𝓜 ⊂ 𝒢̂̂; the first pass's doubt about their direction is
settled.

**R05 — scan 473 — unproved claim — keep.** The diagram is used to show that π₁ and π₀ giving the same oriented
prédiscrétification (resp. arithmétisation) of π implies the same for π′₁, π′₀; the claim is a well-definedness step,
separate from injectivity.

**R06 — scan 473 — unproved claim — keep, sharpened.** The intersections in (2) are taken in 𝒢̂̂(π′₀) through the
restriction map of ①; unless that map is injective (not stated) they mean preimages. The equivalence with injectivity
is asserted ("qui revient aux relations").

**R07 — scan 473 — own idea — keep, sharpened.** If r: G → G′ maps H ⊂ M ⊂ G into H′ ⊂ M′, then M/H → M′/H′ is
injective iff r⁻¹(H′) ∩ M = H. The first relation of (2), 𝒢̂⁺(π₀) = r⁻¹(𝒢̂⁺(π′₀)) ∩ 𝒢̂̂(π₀), implies this because
𝓜(π₀) ⊂ 𝒢̂̂(π₀); this is exactly why ① plus (2) give the injectivity of (3) on 474.

**R08 — scans 472–473 — own idea — keep.** Separating preservation, well-definedness on classes and injectivity
matches the order of the text (472, 473 ①, 473 (2)).

**R09 — scan 474 — unproved claim — corrected.** The sentence is "L'assertion repérée étant admise, l'assertion non
repérée signifie aussi que l'hom. can. (3) est injectif": ① (the marked assertion) makes (3) defined, (2) (the
unmarked one) gives its injectivity (R07), and surjectivity "par construction (via 𝕀Γ_Q)" gives ② "(3) est bijectif".
The first pass's "assertion propre / non propre" has no basis in the ink.

**R10 — scan 474 — conditional — keep.** The bijection (4) P_a(π) → P_{a′}(π′) is conditional ("on trouverait"),
compatible with the isomorphism (3).

**R11 — scan 474 — unproved claim — keep.** "inv. car." is "invariant caractéristique" of 472; the extension to an
arbitrary open π′ ⊂ π is the reduction of R03 read backwards.

**R12 — scan 474 — unpursued implication — keep.** The "correspondances arithm. extérieures" refer to § 28; to be
compared there.

**R13 — scan 475 — unproved claim — keep.** The connected groupoid of potential curves whose π₁ ext. is the common
value of the 𝕀Γ_{g,ν} rests on the conditional bijections (3).

**R14 — scan 475 — proposed construction — keep, corrected.** "comme des sous-groupes finis du m̄ π₀,₃" is a slip for
subgroups of finite index (476: "ss-groupe d'indice fini de π₀,₃"; CSG supplies "[d'indices]"). The cancelled first
version "un multiple commun N de 2g−2+ν et 2g′−2+ν′" fixed the index; see C-3.

**R15 — scan 475 — question — corrected.** The ink reads "indépendant de toute conjecture", not "de notre conjecture":
he asks which parts of the yoga hold unconditionally. The example ("le fait que les hom surjectifs can.
𝕀Γ_{g,ν} → 𝕀Γ_{g,ν′} (g le m̄, ν′ constant) n'est pas établi") omits its predicate; the transcription supplies none.

**R16 — scan 475 — conditional premise — corrected.** The premise is 𝒯̂̂(π′₀) ∩ 𝒢̂⁺(π₀) = 𝒢̂⁺(π₀), with the script T
of 𝒯̂_{g,ν} (479), not a double-hatted 𝒢. An equality A ∩ B = B says B ⊂ A: every element of 𝒢̂⁺(π₀), seen on π′,
lies in 𝒯̂̂(π′₀). It is therefore not the first relation of (2) restated; it is the input from which he expects the
bijectivity of 𝕀Γ_{π₀} → 𝕀Γ_{π′₀} (476).

**R17 — scan 475 — own idea — keep.** Testing the canonical identifications 𝕀Γ_{g,ν} ≅ 𝕀Γ_{g′,ν′} around a triangle
of correspondances remains the right coherence check.

**R18 — scan 476 — conditional consequence — keep.** "(qui est un énoncé de nature relativement anodin …) on
conclurait à la bijectivité": all conclusions down to "iso pour ∀(g,ν)" depend on the premise of R16.

**R19 — scan 476 — unproved claim — keep.** Factorisation through 𝕀Γ₀,₃ and isomorphism for ν large are distinct
claims; the condition "ν assez grand" is satisfiable for every g (C-5).

**R20 — scan 476 — cancelled draft — keep, corrected.** The cancelled block reads "l'image des noyau(x) Θ … est finie";
this weaker goal is the one proved without hypothesis on 477–478 ("sont finis"), so the cancellation postpones it.

**R21 — scan 476 — sufficient condition — keep, corrected.** The ink has "rev. étale de X₀,₃" (not 𝒰₀,₃). The inserted
"(avec sa str. à lacets)" is automatic for the subgroup of an étale cover of the thrice-punctured sphere, whose
punctures all lie over the three cusps.

**R22 — scan 476 — proof step — keep, corrected.** The ink reads "d'indice fini et car. dans π₀,₃" (CSG/IUT "contenu
dans", which is vacuous). Characteristic in π₀,₃ puts π″ in the situation (π, π′) with π′ characteristic, where the
hypothetical bijection gives 𝕀Γ₀,₃ ⥲ 𝕀Γ_{g″,ν″} (top row of the diagram on 477); such π″ exist (C-4).

**R23 — scan 476 — own idea — keep, made concrete.** For an étale cover of degree d of the thrice-punctured sphere,
multiplicativity of the Euler characteristic gives 2g−2+ν = d, so [π̂₀,₃ : π_{g,ν}] = 2g−2+ν; this is the index the
cancelled text on 475 was choosing.

**R24 — scan 477 — conditional claim — corrected.** "Notons p.ex. que les 𝕀Γ₀,ν → 𝕀Γ₀,₃ (ν ≥ 3) sont alors
bijectifs" holds under the premise of 475 ("si on savait"); the margin before "Soit une courbe algébrique" marks where
that hypothesis is dropped (C-6).

**R25 — scan 477 — unpursued implication — keep.** "ceci ne dit rien sur la fidélité éventuelle p.ex. de
𝕀Γ₀,₃ → 𝕀Γ_{g,0} = 𝕀Γ_g (g ≥ 2) ou 𝕀Γ₀,₃ → 𝕀Γ_{1,1} = 𝕀Γ₁": the closed and once-punctured cases stay open here;
see C-7 for the literature.

**R26 — scan 477 — own idea — keep.** Comparing kernels before and after forgetting punctures is the natural check
for the cases ν < 3.

**R27 — scan 478 — unproved claim — corrected.** "OPS que U′ est un rev. étale de (U₀,₃)_K", after enlarging K and
puncturing, is Belyi's theorem (any curve over a number field maps to P¹ unramified outside 0, 1, ∞, with a prescribed
finite set of algebraic points sent into {0, 1, ∞}). The reduction is not elementary; it rests on the "résultat du
russe" cited on 479.

**R28 — scan 478 — unproved claim — keep.** The finiteness of the image of Θ′ = Γ′ ∩ Θ in the outer automorphisms of
π′ ⊂ π̂₀,₃, then in 𝕀Γ_{π′}, 𝒩(π) and 𝕀Γ_π; the proof is R29.

**R29 — scan 478 — own idea — keep, simplified.** Elements of Θ act on π̂₀,₃ by inner automorphisms (Θ is the
kernel to 𝕀Γ₀,₃), and those of Θ′ preserve π′, so on π′ they act through N(π′)/π′, which is finite because π′ is
open. No centraliser argument is needed.

**R30 — scan 478 — unproved claim — keep.** The based action on π₁(U, P) (P rational over K) is obtained by applying
the outer result to U∖{P}; the comparison between the two is what remains to be written.

**R31 — scan 478 — claim — keep, status corrected.** Under the margin of 477 the finiteness of the kernels of
𝕀Γ₀,ν → 𝕀Γ₀,₃ (ν ≥ 3) is claimed without hypothesis: take U = P¹ minus ν rational points; the kernel is the image of Θ,
finite because Θ′ has finite image and Θ/Θ′ embeds in the finite Γ_Q/Γ′. By Belyi it is even trivial (C-7).

**R32 — scan 478 — claim — corrected.** The worry about one finite quotient for all ℓ is answered by the same
argument: ∏_ℓ T_ℓ(Jac C) = π̂₁(C)^{ab} and inner automorphisms act trivially on the abelianisation, so Θ′ acts on it
through its finite image in Out(π̂₁(C)); every abelian variety over K is a quotient of a Jacobian, whence "On voit
facilement que pour tte V.A. A" for all ℓ at once. The quantifier is "toute V.A." (not "la/les").

**R33 — scan 478 — conjecture — keep, with its answer.** "Il devient très difficile de s'imaginer comment il pourrait
se faire que Θ′ [doubt Θ] n'opère pas, en fait, trivialement !" The expectation is right: Θ = 1 by Belyi's theorem,
which he invokes on the next page (C-7).

**R34 — scan 478 — own idea — keep as a remark.** Of its two gaps, the first (one finite image for all ℓ) is closed by
R32 and the second (triviality) by Belyi (C-7).

**R35 — scan 479 — expectation — keep, identified.** "le résultat du russe que m'a signalé Deligne" is Belyi's theorem
(G. V. Belyi, Izv. Akad. Nauk SSSR, 1979), which gives the injectivity of Γ_Q → 𝕀Γ₀,₃; surjectivity is "par
construction" (474), so "est iso" follows if 𝕀Γ₀,₃ is the image of Γ_Q. GLOBAL_PASS T3 makes the same identification.

**R36 — scan 479 — unpursued implication — keep, letters settled.** 𝒩_{g,ν} is the extension of 𝕀Γ_{g,ν} by 𝒯̂_{g,ν}
(outer actions), 𝓜_{g,ν} the extension of 𝕀Γ_{g,ν} by 𝒢̂_{g,ν} (effective actions); outer actions of 𝕀Γ_Q on π₁ ≃
π̄_{g,ν} would be splittings of these, for ν large and g fixed.

**R37 — scan 479 — programme — keep.** The "question essentielle": an algebraic characterisation of 𝕀Γ₀,₃ and of the
𝒩_{g,ν}, and of the admissible splittings (those coming from curves over number fields).

**R38 — scan 480 — conjecture — corrected.** The opening reads "qu'[il suffit, struck] ce sont exactement les actions
relevant l'action ext. donnée, qui ne normalisent aucun ss-groupe à lacets, ou encore telle que π̂₀,₃^{Γ″+} [struck
sign] (1)": the admissible splittings are conjectured to be exactly the lifts that normalise no loop subgroup, with an
inserted equivalent ending in "(1)", his sign for the trivial group (relation sign struck). This is an early form of a
section-conjecture-type criterion, the excluded lifts being those attached to the cusps; compare the 1983 letter to
Faltings and the work of Nakamura and Stix on cuspidal sections.

**R39 — scan 480 — expectation — keep.** "il faille y apporter des conditions plus subtiles, faisant intervenir les
Frobenius" (ink "apporter"; CSG/IUT "ajouter").

**R40 — scan 480 — programme — keep.** An intrinsic description of admissible outer actions "sans avoir à passer par
des actions effectives admissibles".

**R41 — scan 480 — claim — keep.** An outer action on π̂₀,₃ does not induce one on an open subgroup π′; R43 gives the
elementary reason.

**R42 — scan 480 — unpursued implication — keep.** He assumes an outer action on π′ compatible with π′ → π̂₀,₃ and
deducing the action on π "(mod iso)"; the page ends at "de".

**R43 — scan 480 — own idea — keep.** C₃ ⊂ S₃: conjugation by a transposition is inner on S₃ but inverts C₃, which is
not inner on the abelian C₃. Correct.

**R44 — scan 480 — own idea — keep.** N_P(H)/H → Out(H) measures the ambiguity of restriction; self-normalising H is
the first test case.

## B. Second-pass notes

**[2nd pass] C-1 — scan 472 — unproved claim.** "d'où une application injective Discrét(π) ↪ Discrét(π′)" is asserted
without argument. In the characteristic case it reduces to: an element y ∈ π′ normalising D = π₀ ∩ π′ lies in D
(write x₁ = x₀y with x₀ ∈ π₀, using π₀π′ = π). That follows if π′ has trivial centre and an automorphism of the surface
group D that becomes inner on its completion π′ is inner (Grossman, 1974; to check).

**[2nd pass] C-2 — scans 473–474 — structure.** With "repérée" restored, 473–474 read: ① gives the map
𝓜(π₀)/𝒢̂⁺(π₀) → 𝓜(π′₀)/𝒢̂⁺(π′₀); (2) gives its injectivity; the construction via 𝕀Γ_Q gives surjectivity, hence ②;
(4) is the induced bijection of arithmétisations of types a, a′. Only (2) and the surjectivity are left unproved there.

**[2nd pass] C-3 — scan 475 — unpursued implication.** By C-5's Euler-characteristic count the index of π_{g,ν̃} in
π̂₀,₃ is forced to be 2g−2+ν̃; "quitte à passer de g,ν à g,ν̃ avec ν̃ ≥ ν" is the freedom to adjust that index, which is
what the cancelled "multiple commun N de 2g−2+ν et 2g′−2+ν′" was arranging before he replaced it by an arbitrary
correspondance.

**[2nd pass] C-4 — scan 476 — claim with proof.** Every open π_{g,ν′} ⊂ π̂₀,₃ contains an open subgroup π″ that is
characteristic in π̂₀,₃ (the intersection of the finitely many open subgroups of the same index), of some type
(g″,ν″) with 2g″−2+ν″ = [π̂₀,₃ : π″]. So the π″ "d'indice fini et car. dans π₀,₃" of 476 always exists.

**[2nd pass] C-5 — scan 476 — claim with proof.** For every g ≥ 0, (x,y) ↦ x^{2g+1} on y² = x^{2g+1} − 1 is branched
only over 0, 1, ∞, has degree 4g+2 and 2 + (2g+1) + 1 = 2g+4 points over them; so π_{g,2g+4} is an open subgroup of
index 4g+2 of π̂₀,₃ with its loop structure. The hypothesis "il suffit que la surface de type (g,ν) (ou (g,ν′) avec
ν′ ≤ ν) puisse s'obtenir comme rev. étale de X₀,₃" therefore holds for all ν ≥ 2g+4.

**[2nd pass] C-6 — scan 477 — status of the argument.** The margin "On ne fait plus d'hypothèse conjecturale (telle que
l'injectivité dans (3)" separates the conditional yoga of 474–477 (resting on the injectivity of (3)) from the argument
that begins "Soit une courbe algébrique": finite image of Θ′ in Out(π′), finite kernels of 𝕀Γ₀,ν → 𝕀Γ₀,₃, finite
action on Tate modules. That argument still uses Belyi's theorem in the reduction "OPS que U′ est un rev. étale de
(U₀,₃)_K" (R27).

**[2nd pass] C-7 — scans 477–479 — literature.** Belyi's theorem makes the outer action of Gal(Q̄/Q) on π̂₁(P¹∖{0,1,∞})
faithful, so Θ = 1 and the "impression" of 479 is correct. The faithfulness asked about on 477 for closed curves and
type (1,1) is, for the action on π̂₁ of a hyperbolic curve, due to Matsumoto (1996, affine) and Hoshi–Mochizuki (proper);
whether this settles 𝕀Γ₀,₃ → 𝕀Γ_g and 𝕀Γ₀,₃ → 𝕀Γ₁ as defined in LM (quotients by 𝒯̂⁺) is to be checked (GLOBAL_PASS T4).

**[2nd pass] C-8 — scan 475 — question answered later in the batch.** The corrected question "indépendant de toute
conjecture" (R15) is answered on 477–479: unconditionally, finiteness of the kernels (478); with Belyi, 𝕀Γ_Q ≅ 𝕀Γ₀,₃
(479); the identification of all 𝕀Γ_{g,ν} with 𝕀Γ₀,₃ still needs the bijectivity of (3).


# ===== LM_P049 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P049 (scans 481–490): research notes, checked against the corrected text

Each first-pass note (`answers/unzipped/LM_P049/LM_P049_notes.md`, §5) is kept, corrected or dropped, with the reason. The
first pass numbered its notes in four series; they are cited here as R481.1 …, "483–485/1" …, 486-R1 …, "489–490/1" ….
Notes marked **[2nd pass]** are mine. Quotations follow `LM_P049_checked.tex`. Notation: ℾ is Grothendieck's doubled Γ
(`\boldsymbol{\Gamma}`; the first pass read it Π), so ℾ_{0,3} ≃ ℾ_Q, ℾ_{g,ν}, ℾ_π are arithmetic (Galois-type) groups;
𝒯̂̂ = Autextlac, 𝒯̂ the profinite Teichmüller group; a in (π,a) is an *arithmétisation* (the first pass's "authentisation").

## A. First-pass notes

**R481.1 — scan 481 (to 482 l. 1) — conjecture/question — keep, corrected.** The criterion reads: (π,a) with a partial
lift of ℾ_a is admissible iff there are (π′,a′) of type (g,ν′), π′ ⊂ π̂_{03} of finite index, an open Γ″ ⊂ ℾ_{03} and an
admissible effective action of Γ″ on π̂_{03} lifting the outer action "et qui invarie π′" (not "laisse"), from which (π,a)
and the germ of action of Γ′ come by filling holes. The open task is to say "algébriquement" what an admissible action of
Γ″ ⊂ ℾ_{0,3} on π̂_{0,3} is (it must correspond to points of U_{0,3} rational over the field of Γ″).

**R482.1 — scan 482 — conjecture/expectation — keep, corrected.** The groups described "en termes de ℾ_{0,3}" are N_{g,ν}
"(donc des ℾ_{g,ν})": arithmetic quotients, not fundamental groups Π_{g,ν}. The expectation is ℾ_{g,ν} ≃ ℾ_Q ≃ ℾ_{0,3},
the vertical isomorphism of the diagram on 488.

**R482.2 — scan 482 — unproved claim — corrected.** The existence is settled for a cofinal set of ν, which is all the
transition maps 𝒯̂̂_{g,ν} → 𝒯̂̂_{g,ν′} need: see B2. The first pass's caution stands only for "every sufficiently large ν",
which the text does not claim ("pour des ν grands").

**R482.3 — scan 482 — kind corrected: definition, not claim.** The verb is "on décrète" (not "décide"): N_{g,ν} is defined
as the subgroup of 𝒯̂̂_{g,ν} generated by 𝒯̂_{g,ν} and ℾ_{0,3}, under the hypothesis that ℾ_{0,3} acts outerly on π_{g,ν}
compatibly with π_{g,ν} → π_{0,3}. The obligation is consistency (independence of the cover and embedding, agreement with
the moduli N_{g,ν} of 488), not a proof of an identity; see B3.

**R482.4 — scans 481–482 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Comparing two covering presentations through a common
refinement remains a sensible test of the independence required by R482.3.

**R481.2 — scan 481 — own idea (first pass) — keep, low.** Comparing liftings from tangential base points with those from
rational points is a reasonable test of the admissibility criterion; nothing on these pages includes tangential points.

**483–485/1 — scan 483 — conjecture — keep, corrected.** The lacet structure is conjectured to be the only one invariant
under the outer action "de Γ⁺ (ou de Γ^♮)": the group carries a +, as in display (1), where the first pass read Γ′.

**483–485/2 — scan 483 — conjecture — keep.** L_i = the maximal subgroups "dans π" whose normaliser in ℰ maps onto an open
subgroup of Γ⁺; margin: any subgroup ≠ 1 with this property lies in a unique L_i. See B4 for the needed restriction.

**483–485/3 — scan 483 — own idea (first pass) — keep, sharpened.** π itself satisfies the normaliser condition, so
"maximaux" needs a restricted class. B4 gives a sharper witness that also refutes the literal margin statement.

**483–485/4 — scans 483–484 — conjecture — keep.** "tout hom entre extensions ℰ de Γ⁺ par un π, ℰ′ de Γ par un π′ … tel
que l'image de ℰ dans ℰ′ soit ouverte, respecte nécessairement la structure à lacets, et … provient d'un hom (unique, on le
sait) de courbes". This is the Hom form of the anabelian conjecture for curves; for later results see B5.

**483–485/5 — scan 484 — unproved claim — keep.** Conditional on the description of the L_i, the image of an L_i is (1) or
lies in a unique L′_j; the argument needs the normaliser property to pass along the open homomorphism u.

**483–485/6 — scan 484 — unpursued implication — corrected.** The insertion reads "(morphismes les morphismes dominants)",
not "applications dominantes", and base change corresponds to "restriction d'un gr. extérieur (ou d'une structure
d'extension) de Γ à Γ′", not of an "op. extérieure". The compatibility question of the first pass is otherwise unchanged.

**483–485/7 — scan 484 — kind corrected: standard fact.** Finite étale covers of U ↔ finite continuous ℰ-sets, once Ũ is
chosen, is the Galois theory of the étale fundamental group (SGA 1, exp. V); nothing is left unproved here.

**483–485/8 — scan 484 — unproved claim — keep, sharpened.** "on n'est pas réduit aux conjectures" rests on Belyi's
theorem: a Belyi map sending the missing points of a curve U over a number field into {0,1,∞} exhibits U as an étale
cover of U_{0,3} with some holes filled, so filling the holes outside a Γ-stable I gives every object. The insertion 2g + Card(I) ≥ 3 is 2g − 2 + Card(I) > 0, the
hyperbolicity ("anabéliennes") imposed on 483; exhaustivity up to isomorphism still needs the full faithfulness.

**483–485/9 — scan 485 — conjecture/question — corrected.** The text reads "pleine fidélité pour les isomorphismes", not
"monomorphismes"; with that reading the deduction is direct (B6).

**483–485/10 — scans 484–485 — own idea (first pass) — dropped.** It rested on a change of category from dominant
morphisms to monomorphisms; the ink has "isomorphismes", which are dominant morphisms, so no change of scope occurs.

**483–485/11 — scan 485 — conjecture — keep, corrected.** "le commutant de ℾ_{0,3} dans 𝒯̂̂_{0,3} est réduit à 𝔖_3": the
group is the arithmetic ℾ_{0,3}, not Π_{0,3}. For the later status see B7.

**483–485/12 — scan 485 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Testing the commutant on finite characteristic quotients can
detect extra centralising elements but cannot prove the profinite statement.

**483–485/13 — scan 485 — unpursued implication — corrected.** The added structure is an *arithmétisation* of π
("arithmétisés", "les arithmétisations"), the datum a of the pairs (π,a) defined earlier in the manuscript, not an
"authentisation"; the question how much it adds beyond the outer action and the lacets stands.

**486-R1 — scan 486 — unpursued implication — keep, term corrected.** Homomorphisms must carry the arithmétisation of π′,
pushed to the image π″, to the one induced by π; automorphisms must lie in N(π). (The verb is "on voit que … soit", where
the witnesses print "on veut que".)

**486-R2 — scan 486 — unproved claim — corrected.** The ink reads "le centralisateur dans ℾ_π de tt sous-groupe ouvert de
ℾ_π est (1)": the ambient is the arithmetic group ℾ_π (the witnesses' "dans π" loses the letter). The argument is then
complete given that property of ℾ_π; see B8.

**486-R3 — scan 486 — unproved claim — keep.** N_{0,3} ≃ 𝔖_3 × ℾ_{0,3} and trivial centre of ℾ_{03} ≃ ℾ give automorphism
group 𝔖_3; checked in B9. The sign before 𝔖_3 is Grothendieck's "à" ("réduit à").

**486-R4 — scan 486 — conjecture — keep, term corrected.** "il y a une unique arithmétisation invariante par Γ" (object
level).

**486-R5 — scan 486 — conjecture — keep, term corrected.** Admissible homomorphisms respect "automatiquement cette
arithmétisation"; otherwise "introduire les arithmétisations dans la structure".

**486-R6 — scan 486 — unpursued implication — keep, corrected.** The cancelled draft, under the inserted hypothesis
"si Γ = ℾ_Q", says the arithmétisation "doit être décrite … par une prédiscrétification orientée bien déterminée α", then
"stricte"; 489 takes this up as an expectation (489–490/1).

**487-R1 — scan 487 — conjecture/question — keep, corrected.** "Il se pose la question" (not "Je me pose"); the starting
datum is a lacet structure "et une arithmét. a" (invariées par Γ), so that Γ ⊂ N(π) is a germ of splitting of
1 → 𝒯̂(π) → N(π) → ℾ_π → 1.

**487-R2 — scan 487 — conjecture — keep, quotation corrected.** "les “Γ^♮-points” de π′ déduits de π en bouchant tous les
trous, soient distincts"; the g = 1 case is the abelian one.

**487-R3 — scan 487 — conjecture — keep.** The symbol in "pas de condition pour α|ν = 3" is still unread (B13); the
independence of the choice of I′ is a genuine obligation.

**487-R4 — scan 487 — unpursued implication — keep.** No a priori condition in the limit cases (g,0) (g ≥ 2), (1,1), (0,3).

**488-R1 — scan 488 — conjecture — keep.** The author's own doubt that formal admissibility might admit non-geometric
lifts.

**488-R2 — scan 488 — conjecture — keep, extended.** Admissible germs of splittings of 1 → 𝒯̂_{g,ν} → N_{g,ν} → ℾ_{g,ν} → 1,
with ℾ_{g,ν} ≃ ℾ_Q ≃ ℾ_{0,3}, should be the algebraic points of the moduli multiplicité M_{g,ν,Q}; see B10.

**488-R3 — scan 488 — conjecture — keep, quotation corrected.** The sentence reads "On travaillera donc pour le moment
avec|sur cette notion sous la forme “constructive” (via U_{03})"; "constructive" is legible.

**487/488-R4 — scans 487–488 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** The type (0,4) case is the right first test of the
genus-0 criterion.

**486/488-R5 — scans 486, 488 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** Separating object-level uniqueness of the arithmétisation
from morphism-level preservation is a useful organisation; the moduli interpretation of 488 addresses the object level.

**489–490/1 — scan 489 — conjecture — keep, corrected.** "une prédiscrétification orientée canonique sur π_x (pas
invariante par Γ, bien sûr … mais telle que l'arithmétisation correspondante le soit)": Γ moves the prédiscrétification
inside the fibre of one Γ-invariant arithmétisation. The witnesses print "stricte" for "orientée".

**489–490/2 — scan 489 — unproved claim — corrected.** The m̄ after "définissent une" is not struck: all the oriented
discretifications define "une m̄ prédiscrétification orientée — et m̄ une m̄ prédiscr. or. stricte". The claim is that one
and the same (strict) prédiscrétification arises from every lift.

**489–490/3 — scan 489 — unproved claim — keep.** The construction starts "on prend un sous-groupe ouvert ℰ′ ⊂ ℰ_{03}"
(not "en prenant"); exhaustivity of the construction remains to be shown.

**489–490/4 — scan 489 — unproved claim — keep, answered in part.** "en I′" is right as written: the hole i ∈ I′ is filled
and supplies the lift (B11).

**489–490/5 — scan 490 — unproved claim — keep.** "je dis qu'elle ne dépend pas des choix", tested by the automorphisms of
(π,a,Γ^♮) fixing π_0.

**489–490/6 — scan 490 — unproved claim — corrected.** The text reads "quand on se limite à ceux-ci" (not "les prend en
masse"); the subgroupoid of Π(π,Γ^♮) consists of the path classes sending π_0(x) into π_0(y). The inclusion is
automatically an equality (B12), so closure under inverses is free; connectedness is the real claim.

**489–490/7 — scan 490 — unpursued implication — keep.** Γ acts on the objects but not on the subgroupoid.

**489–490/8 — scans 489–490 — own idea (first pass) — keep.** A comparison category of choices is a reasonable frame for
the independence claim.

**489–490/9 — scan 490 — own idea (first pass) — keep, low.** As stated by the first pass.

## B. Second-pass notes [2nd pass]

**B1 — scans 485–489 — [2nd pass] unpursued implication.** The recurring datum is the arithmétisation a of (π,a) (ten
occurrences, all misread "authentisation"). On 489 each prédiscrétification has a "corresponding" arithmétisation; the
expectation of 486 (a unique Γ-invariant one) and of 489 (a canonical prédiscrétification whose arithmétisation is
Γ-invariant) fit together.

**B2 — scan 482 — [2nd pass] proof sketch. Confidence high.** For each g, arbitrarily large ν occur: take X over Q of
genus g and a Belyi map f: X → P¹ over Q; U = X − f⁻¹{0,1,∞} is of type (g,ν) and étale over U_{0,3}. Composing with
h(z) = z² (branched only over 0, ∞, with h{0,1,∞} ⊂ {0,1,∞}) gives a Belyi map with punctures f⁻¹{0,1,∞,−1}, so ν strictly
increases.

**B3 — scan 482 — [2nd pass] question. Confidence medium.** The curve U over Q is a Q-point of M_{g,ν}, and the arithmetic
fundamental group of M_{g,ν,Q} is its geometric part extended by the section of that point. So if the outer action of
ℾ_{0,3} on π_{g,ν} is the one coming from U (with ℾ_{0,3} ≃ G_Q), the decreed subgroup is the image of π_1(M_{g,ν,Q}) in
𝒯̂̂_{g,ν}, independent of the cover; whether every compatible outer action is of this kind is the open point.

**B4 — scan 483 — [2nd pass] own idea. Confidence high.** The literal margin statement fails for the closure of [π,π]: it
is characteristic in π, hence normal in ℰ (normaliser ℰ, open image), it is ≠ 1 and non-abelian, so it lies in no
procyclic L_i. The criterion must be read for a restricted class (abelian or procyclic closed subgroups), the form of
later group-theoretic characterisations of cuspidal inertia groups (H. Nakamura).

**B5 — scans 483–485 — [2nd pass] later results.** The full faithfulness for open homomorphisms and dominant morphisms is
the Hom form of the anabelian conjecture for hyperbolic curves, proved by S. Mochizuki (1999) over sub-p-adic fields,
number fields included; the isomorphism form used on 485 was proved by A. Tamagawa (1997) for affine hyperbolic curves
over number fields.

**B6 — scan 485 — [2nd pass] unpursued implication. Confidence medium.** With "isomorphismes" the deduction is direct: an
admissible lift comes from a pointed curve (U′,x′) whose Γ-outer group is isomorphic to that of U; full faithfulness for
isomorphisms gives U′ ≅ U, carrying x′ to a K-point of U whose lift is conjugate to the given one.

**B7 — scan 485 — [2nd pass] later result.** The commutant statement for U_{0,3} is the genus-0 Galois rigidity of
H. Nakamura (Galois rigidity of the étale fundamental groups of punctured projective lines, 1990); injectivity of
G_Q → 𝒯̂̂_{0,3} is Belyi's. The exact ambient (all outer automorphisms or lacet-preserving ones) should be checked
against Nakamura's statement before citing.

**B8 — scan 486 — [2nd pass] unproved claim. Confidence medium.** The inserted argument needs: in ℾ_π every open subgroup
has trivial centraliser. This is a standard property of absolute Galois groups of number fields, so it holds once ℾ_π is
identified with G_Q, the identification the page assumes (ℾ_{03} ≃ ℾ).

**B9 — scan 486 — [2nd pass] check. Confidence high.** 𝒯̂_{0,3} = 𝔖_3 (mapping classes of the thrice-punctured sphere
permute the punctures, the pure part is trivial), and 𝔖_3 = Aut(U_{0,3}) is defined over Q, so it commutes with ℾ_{0,3}.
The centraliser of ℾ_{0,3} in 𝔖_3 × ℾ_{0,3} is 𝔖_3 × Z(ℾ_{0,3}) = 𝔖_3, as the page says.

**B10 — scan 488 — [2nd pass] conjecture.** Admissible germs of splittings ↔ algebraic points of M_{g,ν,Q} is the section
conjecture for the moduli multiplicité (germs = sections over open subgroups = points over finite extensions). Grothendieck
formulated the section conjecture for hyperbolic curves in his 1983 letter to Faltings; for M_{g,ν} it is not known.

**B11 — scan 489 — [2nd pass] unpursued implication. Confidence medium.** Γ′ acts trivially on I(π′), so filling the hole
i ∈ I′ produces a point of the partially compactified curve rational over the field of Γ′; its decomposition group is a
splitting, which is how the outer action "est relevée en une action effective grâce à i". Hence "en I′", not its
complement.

**B12 — scan 490 — [2nd pass] proof. Confidence high.** If a path class φ: π(x) → π(y) sends π_0(x) into π_0(y), then
φ(π_0(x)) is a finitely generated subgroup of π_0(y), dense in π(y). π_0(y) is free or a surface group, hence LERF
(M. Hall 1949; P. Scott 1978), so that subgroup is closed in the profinite topology of π_0(y) and equals π_0(y): "dans"
is automatically "sur".

**B13 — scan 487 — [2nd pass] question.** The symbol of "pas de condition pour α|ν = 3" has a closed loop and differs from
the ν of "ν > 3" on the same line; the case meant is type (0,3), listed among the limit cases two lines below.

**B14 — scan 481 — [2nd pass] unpursued implication.** With "qui invarie π′" the construction starts: the effective action
of Γ″ on π̂_{03} normalises π′, so it restricts to an effective action on π′, from which filling holes and forgetting the
lift gives the outer action on π. The first pass's "laisse π′" left the condition incomplete.


# ===== LM_P050 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P050 (scans 491–500): research notes checked against the corrected transcription

Package LM_P050 covers manuscript pp. 249–253 (archive pages 490–499) and the text in `LM_P050_checked.tex`. The 33 first-pass notes keep their numbers. Each note gives the scan, the kind, a verdict (keep, correct or drop) and a reason. Notes A–E are mine (second pass). Literature named in a note is a lead to check unless the note says it was read.

## First-pass notes

**1. Scan 491, margin · claim without proof (declared dependency) · keep.** The margin, now read without doubt as "démontré modulo le lemme 2 plus bas —", makes Théorème 1 conditional on Lemme 2. Lemme 1 uses both parts of Lemme 2. Part a), the trivial centre, turns int(g)^m = id into g^m = 1, and part b), torsion-freeness, then gives g = 1. Grothendieck admits he is stuck on part a) ("je cale sur a)", scan 496). *Reason:* the reading is settled and the dependency stands. The precise use of a) and b) has been added.

**2. Scan 491, Lemme 1 · claim without proof · keep.** "Il suffit de voir que l'opération extérieure de K sur π₁(V̄), où V = U₀,₃ ∖ {x}, est triviale" reduces the effective action based at x to the outer action on the punctured curve. The way back is "Repassant à π₁(V̄) … l'image de K dans Aut Π̂₀₃ est finie" on scan 493, which goes through the loop subgroup at the new cusp x and is not spelled out. *Reason:* the text is confirmed.

**3. Scan 491, Белый · claim without proof · correct.** The Белый map must be "défini sur Q" and send x ↦ 0. The step "π(V̄) est un quotient de π′" also needs U′ = f⁻¹(U₀,₃) ⊂ V, that is, f({0, 1, ∞, x}) ⊂ {0, 1, ∞}. The text leaves this condition implicit; Belyi's construction for the finite set {0, 1, ∞, x} provides it. *Reason:* the first-pass note did not name the implicit condition.

**4. Scan 492, normaliser paragraph · implication not pursued · correct.** The bracket ends "or N/π′ est fini" (first pass: "où"). The ink has N/π′ → Autext(π′) where the witnesses print N/Z, and "groupe extension" where they print "groupe extérieur". The argument runs as follows. E_K acts on π through π/Z, because K = Ker Θ acts by inner automorphisms. E′_K lands in N/Z. Hence the image of K in Autext(π′) lies in the image of the finite group N/π′. *Reason:* the reading is corrected. The extension lemma behind the step is elementary once K acts by inner automorphisms.

**5. Scan 493, Lemme 2 · claim without proof · keep.** Lemme 2 (trivial centre and no torsion in Π̂₀,₃) is deferred: "que je reporte pour plus tard". Part b) is proved on scans 496–497 by the Artin argument. Part a) stays open in this package. *Reason:* confirmed.

**6. Scan 494 · claim without proof · keep.** "mais cela n'est pas suffisant pour notre propos": finiteness of the outer action of K does not by itself give triviality. The rational point y ∈ V(ℚ) and the effective action it allows are the extra input. *Reason:* confirmed.

**7. Scan 494 · implication not pursued · keep.** Replacing U "par n'importe quelle courbe algébrique qu.proj. lisse géométriquement connexe" keeps the finiteness stage. Grothendieck pursues this in Corollaire 1 (scans 497–499). There a closed point over a finite extension replaces the rational point, and the finite kernel that remains is removed by Artin–Schreier. *Reason:* confirmed, with a cross-reference added.

**8. Scan 494 · model's idea (first pass) · drop.** The proposed comparison "with a version based on a closed point after finite scalar extension, keeping track of the residual finite kernel" is the proof of Corollaire 1 on scans 498–499. That proof takes y rational over k′, sets K′ = Γ′ ∩ K, concludes "Il en résulte que K est fini", and then uses Artin. *Reason:* the idea is not open; it is already in the text (see note 7).

**9. Scan 495, Lemme 4 · claim without proof · correct.** The action of K on I = S(ℚ̄₀) is said to be "déduite de l'action extérieure de K sur π₁(V̄)", through the conjugacy classes of "ss-groupes lacets". In both places K is written in bold over a lighter Γ; the three witnesses read Γ. No proof is given. A proof needs that distinct points of I have non-conjugate loop subgroups, and that these subgroups are Galois-equivariant. *Reason:* the subject letter is K in the ink. See note 11 for the case card I = 2.

**10. Scan 495 · implication not pursued · keep.** "Ceci étant vrai pour tt V" needs a cofinal family. The complements V = ℙ¹_ℚ ∖ S serve, where S is ∞ together with the zero set of a polynomial over ℚ; every algebraic number lies in such an S. *Reason:* confirmed, with the family made explicit.

**11. Scan 495 · model's idea (first pass) · correct (settled).** The proposed test at card I = 2 settles the question. In that case π₁(V̄) ≅ Ẑ(1), and the two loop subgroups coincide, so conjugacy classes of loop subgroups cannot separate the two points. The deduction needs card I ≥ 3, or oriented loops (generators) together with χ|_K = 1. The latter holds because K acts trivially on the abelianisation Ẑ(1)² of Π̂₀,₃. This does no harm to Lemme 4, since V may be shrunk. Confidence: high. *Reason:* the check has been carried out.

**12. Scan 496 · question (Grothendieck) · keep.** "Finalement je cale sur a)" leaves the trivial centre of a free profinite group of rank ≥ 2 unproved, with "Consulter Deligne à ce sujet !". The result is known; see notes 31 and C. *Reason:* confirmed.

**13. Scan 496 · question (Grothendieck) · keep.** He also does not see why "le centre de Γ (et de tt sous-groupe ouvert)" should be trivial; the abbreviation is now read as "tt". This is a separate question about absolute Galois groups (note C). *Reason:* the reading is settled.

**14. Scan 496 · claim without proof · correct.** The torsion argument uses "Comme π est libre" to obtain a continuous section of E_L → π, which holds because free profinite groups are projective. It also uses π ≅ π₁(U) with U = ℙ¹_K ∖ (n+1 points) and n = card I (the letter is n, not ν). *Reason:* the reading is corrected.

**15. Scan 496 · model's idea (first pass) · correct (settled).** With the corrected reading, the revised formula is U = ℙ¹_K ∖ (n+1 points), (n = card I). The n + 1 punctures give a free group of rank n = card I, matching "π libre sur I". *Reason:* the audit is complete and nothing in it remains open.

**16. Scan 497 · claim without proof (cited input) · keep.** "d'après Artin": a nontrivial automorphism τ of finite order of an algebraically closed field L̄ exists only when L̄ is a quadratic extension of an ordered maximal field R fixed by τ, so τ has order 2. This is the Artin–Schreier theorem, cited and not proved here. *Reason:* confirmed.

**17. Scan 497 · implication not pursued · keep.** The contradiction uses only that −1 is a square in K ⊂ L. The same argument makes Gal(L̄/L) torsion-free for any field L that cannot be ordered. That K is algebraically closed of characteristic 0 is needed earlier, to realise π as π₁(U) and as a quotient of E_L. *Reason:* confirmed.

**18. Scan 497 · conjecture · keep.** "Je présume que l'action extérieure de E_k^{k̄} sur π₁(X_{k̄}) est fidèle si U anabélien": the curve letter is X overwritten on U, while "si U anabélien" keeps U. Later literature is a lead to check, not used here: Matsumoto (1996) for affine hyperbolic curves, Hoshi–Mochizuki for proper ones. *Reason:* confirmed.

**19. Scan 497 · claim without proof · keep.** Corollaire 1 asserts only that some nonempty open V has a faithful outer action. Its proof (scans 498–499) rests on Lemme 5, which is admitted, and on the χ(τ) step (note A). *Reason:* confirmed.

**20. Scan 497 · model's idea (first pass) · correct.** The method transfers faithfulness upward, not downward. Corollaire 2 passes it from U to the étale covers V of U. The "présume" for X would need it on π₁(X̄), a quotient of π₁(V̄), and faithfulness on π₁(V̄) does not give that. Confidence: high. *Reason:* the first-pass idea was vague; it is replaced by the precise obstruction.

**21. Scan 498 · claim without proof · keep.** "D'après Белый, on sait qu'on peut trouver V ⊂ X, qui soit un revêtement étale de (U₀₃)_k": this needs a Belyi map X → ℙ¹ defined over k, with V the preimage of U₀,₃. V is geometrically connected because X is. *Reason:* confirmed (the text reads "qui soit", not "qui est").

**22. Scan 498, margin · claim without proof · correct.** The margin reads "prouvé seulement modulo vérification de (2),(4) ci-dessous — pour le corollaire (2) est d'ailleurs suffisant…". Grothendieck himself marks Lemme 5 as conditional on (2) (scan 500) and on (4), which lies beyond this package. *Reason:* the opening words are now read as "seulement", not "simplement".

**23. Scan 499 · claim without proof · keep.** "Admettons pour l'instant ce lemme": the conclusions K′ = (1) and K finite depend on Lemme 5. The faithfulness of Γ′ on π comes from Théorème 1 by restriction to the open subgroup Γ′ ⊂ Γ_ℚ. *Reason:* confirmed.

**24. Scan 499 · claim without proof · correct.** "par Artin on sait que les seuls sous-groupes finis ≠ (1) de Γ sont ceux engendrés par une conjugaison complexe τ". The ink revises the following phrase towards the singular, "correspondant à un sous-corps ordonné maximal". Then χ(τ) = −1 is said, without argument, to make the outer action of τ nontrivial. That step holds for hyperbolic curves and fails for ℙ¹_ℝ ∖ {±i} (note A). *Reason:* the reading is revised and the gap located.

**25. Scan 499 · model's idea (first pass) · keep.** Separating the finite-kernel step (Lemme 5 with Théorème 1) from the torsion step (Artin–Schreier with χ) shows which hypotheses each step uses. Corollaire 2 records that only these two steps were used. *Reason:* the suggestion is organisational and correct.

**26. Scans 499–500 · implication not pursued · correct.** Corollaire 2 is numbered in the ink; the witnesses print "Corollaire". It replaces "extension de type fini de ℚ" by "corps de car 0". The proof uses only a closed point of V, Lemme 5 for π₁(U_{k̄}), the hypothesis of faithfulness on U and Artin–Schreier. All of these are available over any field of characteristic 0, subject to the χ(τ) step (note A). *Reason:* the label is corrected and the scope made precise.

**27. Scan 500 · claim without proof · correct (settled).** "Il suffit de le prouver dans le cas respé — l'autre s'en déduit aussitôt". Suppose u induces int(g) on π′ with g ∈ π′. Then int(g)⁻¹ ∘ u is an automorphism of π that is the identity on π′, so the identity case gives u = int(g). *Reason:* the reduction is immediate and nothing in it remains unproved.

**28. Scan 500 · claim without proof · correct.** The revised passage reads "Si π a au moins une classe de lacets (cas d'une courbe alg. affine i.e. non propre), alors π est libre — et le lemme 5 est valable justement pour de tels groupes"; the first pass had "un disque de lacets". That π₁ of an affine curve in characteristic 0 is free is standard, by comparison with the topological fundamental group. "est valable" is then argued modulo (2). *Reason:* the reading is corrected.

**29. Scan 500 · claim without proof · keep.** Display (2), L_i = Centr_π(ℓ_i^n), is introduced by "nous admettrons" and marked "pas prouvé !". Whether the argument is ℓ_i^n or L_i^n, the centraliser is the same, because ℓ_i^n topologically generates L_i^n. See note E. *Reason:* confirmed; the letter ambiguity does not touch the mathematics.

**30. Scan 500 · model's idea (first pass) · correct.** The continuation on scan 501, as all three witnesses show, supplies the step from u(ℓ_i)ⁿ = ℓ_iⁿ to u(ℓ_i) = ℓ_i. By (2), u(ℓ_i) lies in L_i ≅ Ẑ, and since Ẑ is torsion-free, u(ℓ_i) = ℓ_i. *Reason:* the obligation is discharged in the text, modulo (2).

**31. Scans 493, 496 · literature (first pass) · keep.** Belyi's corollary c(F, F) = 1, for F the free profinite group on two generators, supplies Lemme 2 a). The page numbers were not rechecked in this pass.

**32. Scans 491, 498 · literature (first pass) · keep.** Belyi's construction for a finite set of algebraic points (his Theorem 4) gives the pointed and field-of-definition requirements of note 3. Not rechecked in this pass.

**33. Scans 497, 499 · literature (first pass) · keep.** Artin–Schreier (1927) is the source of the order-2 and real-closed statements used on scans 497 and 499. Not rechecked in this pass.

## Second-pass notes (mine)

**A. Scan 499 · claim without proof (gap located) · second pass.** The step "χ(τ) = −1, donc l'action extérieure de τ ne peut être triviale" fails for V = ℙ¹_ℝ ∖ {±i}. There π₁(V_ℂ) ≅ Ẑ(1), and complex conjugation sends the unit (z − i)/(z + i) to its inverse while also inverting μ_n, so it acts trivially. The step does hold for hyperbolic V. If τ acted trivially on H₁, then τ[ℓ_P] = −[ℓ_{τP}] would be impossible with two or more cusps, except for exactly two conjugate cusps. In that case, or with one cusp or none, the genus is at least 1, and τ acts on H² of the compactification by χ(τ)⁻¹ = −1. The V of Corollaire 1, étale over U₀,₃, is hyperbolic. Confidence: high.

**B. Scan 496 · model's idea · second pass.** Théorème 2 b) has a direct proof that also covers an infinite I. A free profinite group is projective, so it and all its closed subgroups have cohomological dimension ≤ 1. A nontrivial finite cyclic group has infinite cohomological dimension. This avoids Artin–Schreier and the realisation as π₁ (standard, e.g. Serre, *Cohomologie galoisienne* I §3–§4). Confidence: high.

**C. Scan 496 · literature lead · second pass.** For a): a free profinite group of rank ≥ 2 has trivial centre (Belyi's c(F, F) = 1, note 31; the general rank is standard, e.g. in Ribes–Zalesskii, *Profinite Groups*; locus to check). For Grothendieck's question on Γ and its open subgroups: absolute Galois groups of number fields have trivial centre (Neukirch–Schmidt–Wingberg, *Cohomology of Number Fields*, ch. XII; locus to check). Confidence: medium, from memory.

**D. Scan 496 · model's idea · second pass.** The inserted "fini" is needed only to realise π as π₁ of ℙ¹ minus finitely many points. For an infinite I, Grothendieck's own route still works through Douady's theorem: Gal(L̄/L) for L = K(x), with K algebraically closed of characteristic 0, is free of rank card K. Combined with projectivity and Artin–Schreier, this gives the result. Confidence: medium-high (lead: Douady 1964).

**E. Scan 500 · literature lead · second pass.** For a basis element ℓ of a free profinite group, (2) states that Centr(ℓⁿ) is the closed subgroup generated by ℓ. This follows from two facts: centralisers of nontrivial elements are procyclic, and ⟨ℓ⟩ is a free factor (Herfort–Ribes on centralisers in free profinite products; Ribes–Zalesskii; exact statements to check). So (2) is true in the free case that the manuscript treats first. Confidence: medium.


# ===== LM_P051 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P051 (scans 501–510): research notes checked against the corrected transcription

Package LM_P051 covers manuscript pp. 254–259 (archive pages 500–509): the end of §35 (the proof of the lemma, the
Remarque on faithfulness) and the start of §36 (L'isomorphisme $\Gamma_{\mathbb Q}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Gamma_{1,1}$). The
text is in `LM_P051_checked.tex`. The 45 first-pass notes keep their order and are numbered 1–45. Each gives the scan,
the kind, a verdict (keep, correct or drop) and a reason. Notes A–L are mine (second pass). Literature named in a note
is a lead to check, not something read for this package.

## First-pass notes

**1. Scan 501, ll. 15–20 · claim without proof · keep.** "J'admets que ces groupes sont ≃ Ẑ", with the inserted "c'est d'ailleurs trivial en passant à π_ab" replacing the cancelled "doit pas être dur". Note A gives the argument. *Reason:* the reading is confirmed.

**2. Scan 501, ll. 20–22, (4) · claim without proof · correct.** The exponent condition now reads "pour tout n ∈ ℕ*" (first pass "1 ≤ / ∀"). So (4) is admitted for every n ≥ 1, the exact analogue of (2), which scan 500 flags "pas prouvé !". The centraliser identities are assumed ("J'admets … et qu'on a"), not proved. *Reason:* "tout" is settled in the ink.

**3. Scan 501, ll. 13–15 · claim without proof · keep.** "(Ils sont d'ailleurs tous conjugués sous Aut(π)…)" is given without argument. *Reason:* confirmed.

**4. Scan 502, ll. 2–13 · conjecture · keep, symbol corrected.** He expects the outer action of $\Gamma=\mathcal E_k^{\bar k}$ on $\pi_1(V_{\bar k})$ to be faithful for an anabelian curve over a number field. The group is written with a script E (as in LM_P050), not a G. Note B records the later status. *Reason:* reading corrected; the content is unchanged.

**5. Scan 502, ll. 13–19 · conjecture · keep.** He asks that even the composite $\Gamma\to\mathcal N(\pi)/\widehat{\mathcal T}^{+}(\pi)\simeq\Gamma_\pi$ ("devrait être injectif") be injective. That is stronger than faithfulness. *Reason:* confirmed.

**6. Scan 502, ll. 17–20 · claim without proof · keep.** "puisqu'il est surjectif par construction même de $\mathcal N(\pi),\Gamma_\pi$". The margin now reads, with doubt, "cet hom. se factorise automatiquement par le sous-groupe $\mathcal N(\pi)$ qui [nous intéresse | normalise] $\widehat{\mathcal T}(\pi)$". If "normalise" is right, $\mathcal N(\pi)$ is the normaliser of $\widehat{\mathcal T}(\pi)$, and the factorisation is the statement that Γ normalises the profinite Teichmüller group. *Reason:* the margin reading was revised; the claim stands.

**7. Scan 503, ll. 1–7 · conjecture · keep.** The heuristics, "moins convainquants sans doute que ceux du présent § !", suggest injectivity when V is an étale cover of $U=(U_{03})_k$. *Reason:* confirmed.

**8. Scan 503, ll. 9–16 · claim without proof (conditional reduction) · keep.** "Si on pouvait montrer que $\Gamma_{\pi_0}\to\Gamma_{\pi'}$ est injectif (ce qui est ± une histoire d'algèbre profinie)". The diagram's cancelled first curve shows that the curved arrow was meant to be $\Gamma_{\pi_0}\to\Gamma_{\pi'}$ from the start. *Reason:* confirmed.

**9. Scan 503, ll. 16–18 · implication not pursued · keep.** "Donc modulo cette hypothèse sur les groupes profinis, et utilisant Белый" (Cyrillic in the ink). Every later statement on scans 504–505 inherits this profinite hypothesis. *Reason:* confirmed.

**10. Scans 502–503 · own idea · drop.** The suggestion to follow the kernel of $\Gamma\to\Gamma_\pi$ through the diagram restates the manuscript's own reduction without adding a check. *Reason:* no content beyond the text.

**11. Scan 504, ll. 1–4 · claim without proof · keep.** "pour toute courbe algébrique V sur k, ∃ V′ ⊂ V ouvert non vide" with $\Gamma\to\Gamma_{\pi'}$ injective. This is conditional on the hypothesis of scan 503 and on Белый. *Reason:* confirmed.

**12. Scan 504, ll. 4–11 · question · keep.** Is $\Gamma\to\Gamma_\pi$ itself injective, that is, are $\Gamma\to\Gamma_g$ ($g\ge 2$) and $\Gamma\to\Gamma_{1,1}$ injective? "peut-être est-ce tout à fait faux ?" §36 takes up the case (1,1). *Reason:* confirmed.

**13. Scan 504, ll. 12–19 · claim without proof · keep.** The earlier argument "(à supposer qu'on arrive à le justifier)" would only give the passage from π to its open subgroups π′. *Reason:* confirmed.

**14. Scan 504, ll. 16–18 · implication not pursued · keep.** "(ce qui ne dépend que de son type (g,ν))". *Reason:* confirmed.

**15. Scan 505, ll. 1–5 · claim without proof · correct.** The ink reads "pour les types (0,ν) (ν ≥ 3) exactement — ni plus, ni moins" (first pass "exclusivement"): injectivity is known, "modulo peu de chose", for exactly these types, and perhaps for types obtained from them "par revêtement fini". *Reason:* reading corrected.

**16. Scan 505, ll. 6–9 · claim without proof · keep.** The exclusion of (g,0) for g ≥ 2 and of (g,ν) for g ≥ 1, ν ∈ {0,1,2} is stated without a computation. Note D supplies one. *Reason:* confirmed.

**17. Scan 505, ll. 10–17 · question · correct.** "Le premier cas bien intéressant serait donc le cas (1,1)" ("donc", not "dans"), annotated "tore à un trou !". Is the arithmetic action on $\widehat\Pi_{1,1}$, that is, the outer action mod $\widehat{\mathcal T}^{+}_{1,1}\simeq\mathrm{Sl}(2,\mathbb Z)^\wedge$, faithful? *Reason:* reading corrected; the question stands.

**18. Scan 505, ll. 11–13 · claim without proof · keep.** "$\widehat{\mathcal T}^{+}_{1,1}\simeq\mathrm{Sl}(2,\mathbb Z)^\wedge$" and "groupe libre à deux générateurs" are stated, not proved. *Reason:* confirmed.

**19. Scan 505, ll. 6–8 · own idea · correct.** The proposed numerical test has been carried out (note D). Counting punctures alone proves the stated exclusion, with no extra hypothesis. *Reason:* the idea was replaced by a proof.

**20. Scan 506, heading · claim without proof · correct.** The heading announces the isomorphism $\Gamma_{\mathbb Q}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Gamma_{1,1}$ and the injectivity of $\Gamma_{\mathbb Q}\to\mathrm{Aut\,ext}_{\mathrm{lac}}(\widehat{\mathcal T}^{+}_{11})\simeq\mathrm{Aut\,ext}_{\mathrm{lac}}(\mathrm{Sl}(2,\mathbb Z)^\wedge)$. The subscript "lac" is a doubtful reading supported by §35's heading and the author's table of contents: outer automorphisms respecting the loop classes. *Reason:* the subscript was illegible in the first pass.

**21. Scan 506, margin · claim without proof (deferred) · correct.** "Les réflexions du présent §, un peu cahin-caha, seront reprises de façon moins pesante au § suivant…" (first pass "gauche/gratuite"). *Reason:* reading corrected.

**22. Scan 506, ll. 3–11 · claim without proof · correct.** The insertion reads "pour structures triangulées", and the maps "pouvant avoir des boucles aplaties" (no longer "bords aplatis"). Finite $\Pi'_{0,3}$-sets are said to correspond to oriented finite maps with triangles or monogons as faces. Note E identifies the group. *Reason:* readings settled.

**23. Scan 506, ll. 11–14 · claim without proof · keep.** The arithmetic outer action descends to $\widehat\Pi'_{0,3}$, "mais non celle de $\mathfrak S_3$ !". Note F gives the reason. *Reason:* confirmed.

**24. Scan 506, ll. 16–17 · claim without proof · keep.** "Proposition. L'action extérieure de $\Gamma_{\mathbb Q}$ sur $\widehat\Pi'_{0,3}$ est fidèle." The proof is only begun on these pages. *Reason:* confirmed.

**25. Scan 506, ll. 18–22 · claim without proof · keep.** The plan is to embed $\Pi_{0,3}$ in $\Pi'_{0,3}$ with finite index, obtain (2) $\widehat\Pi_{0,3}\hookrightarrow\widehat\Pi'_{0,3}$ compatible with the outer action, and deduce faithfulness. Note L sketches the deduction. *Reason:* confirmed.

**26. Scan 506, (1) · own idea · correct.** The finite permutation quotient in question is the one of (6) on scan 509, $\Pi'_{0,3}\to\mathfrak S_3$, and the embedded group is its kernel $\Pi'^{\circ}_{03}$. Notes I and J carry out the check. *Reason:* made specific.

**27. Scan 507, ll. 7–11 · claim without proof · keep.** "On sait que Y est une droite projective (sur $\overline{\mathbb Q}$ a priori)", pinned by $a_0,a_1,a_\infty$ as 0, 1, ∞. *Reason:* confirmed.

**28. Scan 507, ll. 17–20 · claim without proof · keep.** The quotient is Galois with group $\mathfrak S_3$ and ramification indices 2, 2, 3. Verified in note G. *Reason:* confirmed.

**29. Scan 507, cancelled parenthesis · claim without proof (cancelled) · keep.** "(en fait, f peut se définir comme morphisme $\mathbb P^1_{\mathbb Z}\to\mathbb P^1_{\mathbb Z}$, mais ici peu nous importe)" is struck out. Note H shows that it fails for the normalised f. *Reason:* confirmed.

**30. Scan 507 · own idea · correct (confirmed).** The candidate $f(x)=27x^2(x-1)^2/\bigl(4(x^2-x+1)^3\bigr)$ is right; note G gives the checks. The confidence rises from medium to high. *Reason:* verified.

**31. Scan 507 · own idea · correct (done).** Reduction at 2 and 3 settles the cancelled assertion negatively for the normalised f (note H). *Reason:* carried out.

**32. Scan 508, ll. 7–10 · claim without proof · keep.** "Tout revêtement étale topologique de $U_{03}(\mathbb C)\subset X$ donne ainsi un rev. étale topologique de $U_{0,3}(\mathbb C)\subset Y$, ayant au dessus de 1 la ramification 2, au dessus de ∞ la ramification 3 exactt". *Reason:* confirmed.

**33. Scan 508, ll. 11–13 · implication not pursued · keep.** The functor "oubli" (de la pondération). *Reason:* confirmed.

**34. Scan 508, display (4) · claim without proof · keep.** "hom. de groupes extérieurs" is replaced by "équiv. de catégories". The target is the slice over the object $X\setminus\{0,1,\infty\}$, or $E$. *Reason:* confirmed.

**35. Scan 508, display (4) · own idea · keep.** Compute the monodromy on the fibre E and compare stabilisers. Note I does this. *Reason:* sound and now carried out.

**36. Scan 508 · own idea · correct (resolved).** "3 exactt" and "ramification sub. à" do not conflict. For an étale cover Z of $X\setminus\{0,1,\infty\}$, viewed over Y, $e_{Z/Y}=e_{Z/X}e_{X/Y}=e_{X/Y}$, so the indices are exactly 2 over 1 and 3 over ∞. Conversely, a cover over the object X with indices dividing the signature has $e_{Z/X}=1$. The slice over X therefore restores exactness. *Reason:* the check is done.

**37. Scan 509, (6) · claim without proof (unfinished calculation) · keep.** "à calculer !". Completed in note J. *Reason:* confirmed.

**38. Scan 509, "On choisit…" · claim without proof · keep.** $(\mathrm{Ens}\,\Pi'_{03})/E\simeq\mathrm{Ens}(\Pi'^{\circ}_{03})$ via $F\mapsto F_{Q_0}$. *Reason:* confirmed.

**39. Scan 509, last paragraph · claim without proof · keep.** The equivalence is described by a bitorsor under $(\Pi'^{\circ}_{0,3},\Pi_{03})$, and, up to a non-unique isomorphism, by an isomorphism $\Pi_{03}\simeq\Pi'^{\circ}_{0,3}$. *Reason:* confirmed.

**40. Scan 510, first paragraph · claim without proof · correct.** "les isomorphismes ainsi obtenus … forment/formant exactement une classe de conjugaison" (first pass "font"), and "Quand de plus le choix de $Q_0$ varie, on trouve ~~comme~~ un composé $\Pi_{0,3}\to\Pi'^{\circ}_{03}\to\Pi'_{03}$ … une classe de $\Pi'_{03}$-conj, i.e. un hom extérieur". *Reason:* readings corrected; content unchanged.

**41. Scan 510, "J'ai beaucoup turbiné…" · claim without proof (missing framework) · correct.** "à défaut d'avoir écrit les fonctorialités [générales] nécessaires(?) pour les 'groupes fondamentaux avec ramification'" (first pass "élémentaires"). "le bifoncteur I mystérieux de tantôt" is now settled. *Reason:* readings corrected.

**42. Scan 510, (7) · methodological proposal · keep.** Define the inverse functor by "image inverse, suivi d'une normalisation". Note K gives the local fact it needs. *Reason:* confirmed.

**43. Scan 509, (6) · own idea · keep, superseded.** Testing transpositions and a 3-cycle is the right computation; note J completes it. *Reason:* carried out.

**44. Scan 510, (7) · own idea · keep.** Test pull-back and normalisation on local cyclic covers over 1 and ∞. This is the content of note K. *Reason:* sound.

**45. Scan 510 · own idea · keep.** Groupoids and path torsors make the two choices (origin of the bitorsor, choice of $Q_0$) explicit. "le bifoncteur I … formé des classes de chemins de P … vers $Q_0$" is exactly such a path torsor. *Reason:* consistent with the corrected text.

## Notes added in the second pass (mine)

**A. Scan 501 · claim without proof (proof supplied).** Relation (3) dies in the abelianisation, so in $\pi_{\mathrm{ab}}\simeq\widehat{\mathbb Z}^{2g}$ the images of $x_i,y_i$ form a basis. The composite $\widehat{\mathbb Z}\to\Lambda_i\to\pi_{\mathrm{ab}}$, $1\mapsto\bar x_i$, is therefore injective, so $\widehat{\mathbb Z}\to\Lambda_i$ is an isomorphism, and likewise for $\Lambda'_i$. This is the "trivial en passant à π_ab" of the insertion.

**B. Scan 502 · conjecture (later status).** The first expectation was later proved: faithfulness of the outer Galois action on $\pi_1$ of a hyperbolic curve over a number field. Belyi (1979) proved it for $\mathbb P^1\setminus\{0,1,\infty\}$, M. Matsumoto (J. reine angew. Math. 474, 1996) for affine hyperbolic curves, and Hoshi–Mochizuki (Hiroshima Math. J. 41, 2011) for proper ones. The stronger injectivity into $\Gamma_\pi=\mathcal N(\pi)/\widehat{\mathcal T}^{+}(\pi)$ is a separate question.

**C. Scan 501 · claim without proof (dependency).** The free case cites "(∗)" (CSG/IUT print "(2)") for $u(l_i)\in L_i$, that is, the centraliser hypothesis $L_i=\mathrm{Centr}_\pi(L_i^n)$, marked "pas prouvé !" on scan 500. The non-free case rests on the equally admitted (4). Lemme 5, and through it Corollaire 2, therefore remain conditional on centraliser identities in profinite surface groups.

**D. Scan 505 · claim without proof (proof supplied).** Let V → U be a finite étale cover of degree d of a curve U of type (0,ν). Every puncture of U has at least one puncture of V above it, so ν′ ≥ ν ≥ 3, and Riemann–Hurwitz gives 2 − 2g′ − ν′ = d(2 − ν). So no type with ν′ ≤ 2 arises from (0,ν) by finite covering: in particular no (g,0) and no (g,ν) with g ≥ 1, ν ∈ {0,1,2}. This is the stated exclusion.

**E. Scan 506 · own idea.** $\Pi_{0,3}$ is free on $l_1,l_\infty$ (with $l_0l_1l_\infty=1$), so (1) gives $\Pi'_{0,3}=\langle l_1,l_\infty\mid l_1^2=l_\infty^3=1\rangle\simeq\mathbb Z/2*\mathbb Z/3\simeq\mathrm{PSL}_2(\mathbb Z)$. In the map picture, $l_\infty^3=1$ makes every face a triangle or a monogon, and the fixed points of $l_1$ are folded edges. This matches "boucles aplaties" and the "arête (repliée)" of the universal map on scan 508.

**F. Scan 506 · claim without proof (proof sketch).** σ ∈ $\Gamma_{\mathbb Q}$ sends each loop to a conjugate of $l^{\chi(\sigma)}$ (χ the cyclotomic character). So it sends $l_1^2$ and $l_\infty^3$ into the closed normal subgroup they generate, and the outer action descends. The $\mathfrak S_3$ of automorphisms of $\mathbb P^1\setminus\{0,1,\infty\}$ permutes the three loop classes, while the relations treat 1 and ∞ differently and leave 0 free. Hence "mais non celle de $\mathfrak S_3$ !".

**G. Scan 507 · claim without proof (verified).** In the anharmonic group, the stabiliser of 0 is $\{x,\ x/(x-1)\}$, that of −1 is $\{x,\ 1/x\}$, and that of −j is generated by $x\mapsto 1/(1-x)$, whose fixed points are the roots of $x^2-x+1$, namely $-j$ and $-\bar j$. The orbits $\{0,1,\infty\}$, $\{-1,\tfrac12,2\}$, $\{-j,-\bar j\}$ thus have ramification indices 2, 2, 3 over $a_0,a_1,a_\infty$, as the text says. This also confirms the minus signs (the witnesses' $\{j,\bar j\}$ is wrong). The normalised quotient is $f(x)=27x^2(x-1)^2/\bigl(4(x^2-x+1)^3\bigr)=1728/j(x)$, with $j(x)=256(x^2-x+1)^3/(x^2(x-1)^2)$ the Legendre j-invariant. It has double zeros at 0, 1, ∞, triple poles at $-j,-\bar j$, and $f-1=-(x+1)^2(2x-1)^2(x-2)^2/\bigl(4(x^2-x+1)^3\bigr)$.

**H. Scan 507 · own idea (the cancelled claim checked).** For the f pinned by $a_0,a_1,a_\infty\mapsto 0,1,\infty$, the cancelled remark is false. The primitive pair $(27x^2y^2(x-y)^2,\ 4(x^2-xy+y^2)^3)$ has the common zero (0:1) mod 2 and (1:−1) mod 3, so f is a morphism only over $\mathbb Z[1/6]$. In the quotient, $a_0$ and $a_1$ meet mod 2 and $a_1$, $a_\infty$ meet mod 3. The quotient map does extend over $\mathbb Z$ with the target coordinate $u=(x^2-xy+y^2)^3/(x^2y^2(x-y)^2)$, which has no common zero in any characteristic. So the remark holds only after the pinning is given up, which may be why it was struck out.

**I. Scans 508–509 · own idea (consistency check).** Classically, $\pi_1(\mathbb P^1\setminus\{0,1,\infty\})\simeq\Gamma(2)/\{\pm1\}$ is normal of index 6 in $\mathrm{PSL}_2(\mathbb Z)\simeq\Pi'_{0,3}$, with quotient $\mathrm{PSL}_2(\mathbb F_2)\simeq\mathfrak S_3$, and $f=1728/j$ is the map $X(2)\to X(1)$. This is the embedding (2) that §36 builds through (4)–(6), with $\Pi'^{\circ}_{03}$ the kernel of (6). The Euler characteristics agree: $\chi(\mathbb Z/2*\mathbb Z/3)=-1/6$, and a torsion-free subgroup of index 6 has χ = −1, so it is free of rank 2.

**J. Scan 509, (6) · claim without proof (calculation completed).** Send $l_1$ to a transposition τ and $l_\infty$ to a 3-cycle ρ. Then $l_0=(l_1l_\infty)^{-1}\mapsto(\tau\rho)^{-1}$, again a transposition, as (6) requires. The six admissible pairs (τ, ρ) form a single free orbit of $\mathrm{Aut}(\mathfrak S_3)=\mathrm{Inn}(\mathfrak S_3)$. So the homomorphism is unique up to inner automorphism, and its kernel $\Pi'^{\circ}_{03}$ does not depend on the choice.

**K. Scan 510, (7) · claim without proof (proof sketch).** Let Z → Y be étale over $Y\setminus\{0,1,\infty\}$, with indices dividing 2 over 1 and 3 over ∞. By Abhyankar's lemma (in characteristic 0 the local extensions are Kummer), the normalisation of $Z\times_Y X$ is étale over $X\setminus f^{-1}(0)=X\setminus\{0,1,\infty\}$. Together with note 36, this gives the inverse functor and hence the equivalence (4).

**L. Scan 506 · implication not pursued (proof sketch).** Given (2), the Proposition follows from Belyi's faithfulness on $\widehat\Pi_{0,3}$. If σ acts on $\widehat\Pi'_{0,3}$ as int(g), it acts on the normal open subgroup $\widehat\Pi_{0,3}$ as int(g), whose outer class is the deck symmetry $s\in\mathfrak S_3=\widehat\Pi'_{0,3}/\widehat\Pi_{0,3}$ of X. σ preserves each of the three conjugacy classes of loop subgroups at 0, 1, ∞ (they are already distinct in $\pi_{\mathrm{ab}}$), while s ≠ 1 permutes them; so s = 1, σ is inner on $\widehat\Pi_{0,3}$, and σ = 1. What still needs writing out is the identification of the outer action of $\widehat\Pi'/\widehat\Pi$ with the deck group of X → Y.


# ===== LM_P052 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P052 (scans 511–520): research notes, checked against the corrected text

Source of the notes: `answers/unzipped/LM_P052/LM_P052_notes.md`, section 5 (R01–R38). Each note is checked against `second_pass/LM_P052_checked.tex`. A verdict of **keep** means the note stands as written. **Correct** gives the replacement wording. No note was dropped. Notes S01–S08 at the end are the second pass's own; they are labelled as such.

## First-pass notes

**R01, scan 511, unproved claim. Keep.** The passage from the exactness of (7 bis) to a torsor is asserted without proof. The corrected text reads "défini par un (Π03, Π'03) ens. qui soit un torseur (à droite) pour Π'03"; the torsor property is stated as the required condition.

**R02, scan 511, unpursued implication. Keep.** The wording is now "Le choix d'une origine pour ce torseur définit alors aussi un hom correspondant Π0,3 → Π'03". The dependence on the origin is the inner-conjugation ambiguity stated after (9).

**R03, scan 512, unproved claim. Keep.** The ambiguity is literally "modulo conjugaison par un autom. int. de Π'03". The witnesses' "défini modulo composition" is not in the ink.

**R04, scan 512, unproved claim. Correct.** The text says "l'opération ext. de K sur le sous-groupe *ouvert* Π̂03 se fait par un groupe fini"; the first pass read "normal". Théorème 1 of the preceding § is therefore applied to an open subgroup. That theorem's hypotheses are outside this package and should be checked against it.

**R05, scan 512, unproved claim. Correct.** Read "τ la conjugaison complexe". The inserted "(ni m̄ sur (Π'03)ab*)", with the margin "* ≃ Z/2Z+Z/3Z", claims that τ acts nontrivially already on the abelianization. That is a stronger, checkable statement (see S03). Artin's result here is the theorem that a nontrivial finite subgroup of Gal(Q̄/Q) has order 2 and is generated by a complex conjugation.

**R06, scan 513, conjecture. Keep.** The "J'ai envie … d'interpréter Π'03 comme T⁺₁,₁/centre", "compatible à l'action extérieure de Γ", is a programme stated without proof.

**R07, scan 513, unproved claim. Keep.** In (11) Π₁₀ is identified with Z² by an isomorphism (rotated ≃, not ‖). The target of (24) is Gl(2,Z), consistent with (10).

**R08, scan 513, unproved claim. Keep.** The ink orders the generators λ3, λ2 and u6, u4, matching (14) and (15) term by term. The lifting is qualified "Sauf erreur" and is verified in S01.

**R09, scan 514, unproved claim. Keep.** The text reads "conjugué à une puissance de [un struck] u4, ou u6". The claim is true; see S02.

**R10, scan 514, unproved claim. Keep.** The orders-4 and -6 cyclic groups are the automorphism groups (fixing the origin) of the curves with j = 1728 and j = 0 in characteristic 0. The text gives no proof.

**R11, scan 514, conjecture or expressed doubt. Correct.** After the unclosed parenthesis "(ce qui implique sans doute qu'ils engendrent Sl(2,Z)", the text reads "— mais [on fera attention | enfin. Attention] à la relation supplémentaire" (unresolved). The phrase "s'ajoutant à (15) des relations fondamentales" is struck. The "sans doute" implication is true; see S01.

**R12, scan 514, first pass's own idea. Keep, now settled.** With u4 = [[0,−1],[1,0]] and u6 = [[0,−1],[1,1]] one has u4² = u6³ = −I. The relation (16) and the identification with the central −1 hold for these standard generators (S01).

**R13, scan 515, conjecture. Keep.** The goal "Je veux maintenant me convaincre que … (20) est compatible avec l'opération extérieure de Γ = Π_Q" is stated, not proved. The ink writes Π_Q for the second letter, where the witnesses print Γ_Q.

**R14, scan 515, conjecture. Keep.** The direct definition of Π̂03 → T̂⁺₁,₁/±1 and its lift (21) are proposed, not established.

**R15, scan 515, first pass's own idea. Keep.** Separating the existence of a lift through ±1 from its Γ-compatibility remains a sound plan. The later pages (outside this package) take up exactly this question.

**R16, scan 516, unproved claim. Correct, resolved.** The claim is true. SL(2,Z) is the amalgam Z/4 *_{Z/2} Z/6 on u4, u6, so (22) is onto and its kernel is the *invariant* (normal) subgroup generated by ℓ1⁴, ℓ∞⁶ and ℓ1²ℓ∞⁻³ (S01). "Invariant" is in the ink; all three witnesses omit it.

**R17, scan 516, unproved claim. Correct.** The margin reads "en effet, T^!_{g,ν} est ext. de T^!_{g,ν−1} par Π_{g,ν−1} (si (g,ν−1) anab.) et itou pour les T⁺, T^{!+}_{0,3} = (1) !". The condition is that the type (g, ν−1) be anabelian, not "Π_{g,ν−1} non ab.". This is the Birman-type exact sequence for forgetting a point, asserted without proof.

**R18, scan 516, conjecture. Keep.** "dont je soupçonne qu'il se prolonge en un hom" (24) has target T₁₁ ≀ Gl(2,Z). A natural constraint is in S05.

**R19, scan 516, unproved claim. Correct.** The domain of (24) is "ext. de {1,τ} par Π0,3, qui n'est autre que le groupe cartographique triangulé pondéré non orienté".
