The first pass read "triangulé|triangulaire pointé|ponctué". The marginal "pas encore défini" is certain. Its referent, (24) or the extension, is not marked.

**R20, scans 516–517, unpursued implication. Keep.** It reads "Π1 'géométrique transcendant' des|du topos modulaire(s) M^!_{0,4}"; the article and number are unresolved in the ink. The continuation on 517 is singular ("classifiant les droites projectives…").

**R21, scan 516, first pass's own idea. Keep.** Testing a candidate image of the extra coset against the extension action is still the right check (see also S05).

**R22, scan 517, conjecture. Correct.** "On devrait donc pouvoir définir … (25)" stands. The mark above the target of (25) is probably a struck "!", so the target is M₁,₁, the moduli of elliptic curves with origin.

**R23, scan 517, unproved claim. Correct.** The ink reads "on a un isom can." (first pass "bon cas"). So (26) M^!_{0,4} ≃ U_{0,3} is asserted to be a *canonical* isomorphism, which a proof must exhibit (S04). Turning (25) into "une famille de courbes elliptiques sur U0,3" follows from that.

**R24, scan 518, unproved claim. Keep.** "resp." after "quatre points marqués" is struck. The note's verification programme (genus 1, the involution is −1, the labels of the 2-torsion points) is unaffected.

**R25, scan 518, unproved claim. Keep.** The corrected details are "sauf que 2 y soit inv (car. résid. ttes ≠ 2)" and "(un système local de F2-modules) de S". The base-change and local-constancy checks remain to be done.

**R26, scan 518, unpursued implication. Keep.** γ = α+β as "un troisième larron" and "(β,γ) etc sont … aussi des bases" point to the action of Sl(2,F2) ≃ S3 on {α,β,γ}, used in (30). The struck pair is not evidence for a formula.

**R27, scan 518, unproved claim. Keep.** That E/±1 is a P¹-bundle with four disjoint sections, and the functor on morphisms, are asserted only.

**R28, scan 518, first pass's own idea. Keep.** Comparing Aut(E, α, β) with Aut(P; u0, u1, u∞, λ) is exactly what 519 does: the defect is the involution −1.

**R29, scan 519, unproved claim or proposed simplification. Correct.** The margin reads "il suffit d'y aller brutalement [probable] avec la courbe elliptique y² = √(x(x−1)(x−λ)), sur U0,3, pour … avoir U0,3 → M1,1 cf plus bas…". The formula as written is a slip for the Legendre family (S06). The comparison with M'₁,₁ is left for later pages.

**R30, scan 519, unproved claim. Correct.** The construction reads "en commençant par prendre Isom(E,F)' = Isom(E,F)/±1, puis on prendra le champ associé à un préchamp". That is two explicit steps: quotient the presheaf of isomorphisms by ±1, then stackify. The first pass read "en convenant de prendre … puis en prenant le champ associé à ce préchamp". Full faithfulness after the quotient and effective descent remain unproved.

**R31, scan 519, unproved claim. Keep.** Representability by a modular multiplicity with geometric π1 = Sl(2,Z)^/±1, and the presentation (27) at level n, are asserted.

**R32, scan 519, unproved claim. Keep.** The clause opens "[en outre|on note] que le ss-groupe central {±1} de Sl(2,Z/nZ) opère trivialement sur M₁₁[n]" (opening words unresolved). Its meaning, triviality on objects up to isomorphism, still needs stating.

**R33, scan 520, unproved claim. Keep.** The level letter is n ("ne dépend pas du choix de n, n ≥ 2"). The first pass left it as m|n.

**R34, scan 520, unproved claim. Keep.** (30) M^!_{0,4} ≃ M'₁,₁[2] → M'₁₁ is asserted as a Galois cover with group Sl(2,F2) ≃ S3. The first link is a stretched ≃; see S08 for why it is plausible.

**R35, scan 520, unproved claim. Keep.** The identification of the geometric π1 of M₁₁[2] with the kernel of Sl(2,Z)'^ → Sl(2,Z/2Z) is asserted only.

**R36, scan 520, unpursued implication. Keep.** Diagram (31) is the start of the comparison. Base points and inner-conjugation ambiguity are not recorded on these pages.

**R37, scans 519–520, first pass's own idea. Keep.** Checking inertia at j = 0 and j = 1728 before and after removing ±1 remains a sensible verification.

**R38, scans 520 and 513–516, first pass's own idea. Keep.** An index comparison between the map from (29)–(31) and the generator map (22) is concrete. The image of Π̂0,3 should be the kernel of a surjection onto S3, of index 6 in Sl(2,Z)^/±1.

## Second-pass notes (own)

**S01, scans 513–516, claim checked (own).** SL(2,Z) = ⟨u4, u6 | u4⁴ = u6⁶ = 1, u4² = u6³⟩, i.e. Z/4 *_{Z/2} Z/6. For example u4 = [[0,−1],[1,0]] and u6 = [[0,−1],[1,1]] give u4² = u6³ = −I. Hence (22) is surjective, with kernel the normal subgroup generated by ℓ1⁴, ℓ∞⁶ and ℓ1²ℓ∞⁻³, as the ink says with "invariant". This also settles the "sans doute" on 514: −1 = u4² lies in ⟨u4, u6⟩, so generation mod ±1 gives generation of SL(2,Z).

**S02, scan 514, claim checked (own).** Every element of finite order in SL(2,Z) has order 1, 2, 3, 4 or 6 and is conjugate to a power of u4 or u6. The elements of order 4, and those of order 6, each form two conjugacy classes interchanged by inversion. This is exactly the qualification "à conjugaison et passage à l'inverse près".

**S03, scan 512, implication (own).** The inserted "(ni m̄ sur (Π'03)ab)" makes the argument purely abelian. Π'03,ab ≃ Z/2 × Z/3 is generated by the classes of the inertia generators ℓ'1 (order 2) and ℓ'∞ (order 3), and Γ acts on inertia classes through the cyclotomic character. So τ, with χ(τ) = −1, inverts the Z/3 factor and acts nontrivially even on the abelianization.

**S04, scan 517, claim without proof (own).** The canonical isomorphism (26) M^!_{0,4} ≃ U_{0,3} is the cross-ratio: move three numbered points to 0, 1, ∞ by the unique projective transformation, and the fourth point becomes λ ∈ P¹ ∖ {0,1,∞}. "Canonical" presupposes a fixed convention for which numbered points go to 0, 1 and ∞. The S3 of (29)–(30) acts by changing it.

**S05, scan 516, implication (own).** If (24) extends (23), elements of T^!_{0,4} that reverse orientation (the coset of τ in the extension of {1,τ} by Π0,3) should map to Gl(2,Z) ∖ Sl(2,Z). Otherwise the restriction to the "+" parts would not match (12). This is a necessary condition to test any candidate image of τ, not a statement in the text.

**S06, scan 519, slip in the ink (own).** As written, y² = √(x(x−1)(x−λ)) gives y⁴ = x(x−1)(x−λ), which is not the intended elliptic curve. The context ("la courbe elliptique … sur U0,3, pour avoir U0,3 → M1,1") requires the Legendre family y² = x(x−1)(x−λ), which all witnesses print. The diplomatic text keeps the radical; an edition should flag it.

**S07, scan 518, claim checked (own).** The margin "distinctes et partant ≠ 1" is a valid inference. If α, β, γ = α+β are pairwise distinct at every point, none is the origin (α = 0 would give γ = β). Conversely, α, β form an F2-basis of ₂E exactly when α, β, α+β are the three nonzero points. The margin therefore restates the basis condition pointwise.

**S08, scan 520, implication (own).** At level 2, −1 lies in the kernel of SL(2,Z) → SL(2,Z/2Z), so it acts trivially on level structures. M₁,₁[2] is then a μ2-gerbe over the λ-line, and removing the symmetry ("à symétrie près") gives M'₁,₁[2] ≃ P¹ ∖ {0,1,∞} = U0,3, as (30) says. The Galois group Sl(2,F2) ≃ S3 then permutes α, β, γ, i.e. the branch points 1, ∞, λ. Identifying this S3 with the S3 = Π̂'03/Π̂03 of (9 bis) is not done in this package; it is the open point behind (31).


# ===== LM_P053 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P053 (scans 521–530): research notes, checked against the corrected text

Source of the notes: `answers/unzipped/LM_P053/LM_P053_notes.md`, section 5 (numbered here R01–R44). Each is checked against `second_pass/LM_P053_checked.tex`. **Keep** means the note stands as written; **correct** gives the replacement; **drop** gives the reason. Notes S01–S10 at the end are the second pass's own, labelled as such. Matrix notation: A = [[1,2],[0,1]], B = [[1,0],[−2,1]], S = [[0,−1],[1,0]], T = [[1,1],[0,1]]; Γ(2) = Γ₂ = Ker(Sl(2,ℤ) → Sl(2,ℤ/2ℤ)).

## First-pass notes

**R01, scan 521, unproved claim. Correct.** The map is (22), not (27): "cet hom. (22) n'a rien d'injectif, par contre il est surjectif — et ses valeurs sont non dans Sl(2,ℤ)/±1, mais dans Sl(2,ℤ) lui-m̄!". (22) is Π₀₃ → Sl(2,ℤ) on scan 516 (LM_P052); (27) is the description of M₁₁ as a quotient stack. Non-injectivity is clear (Π₀₃ is free, Sl(2,ℤ) has torsion); surjectivity is asserted without proof.

**R02, scans 521–522, conjecture (stated programme). Correct.** The ink reads "une façon de donner un sens arithmético-géométrique remarquable à (22), et son extension aux groupes profinis correspondants" ("compatible à (27)" in the first pass). The programme is left open here; a later footnote to an earlier page (witness fn. 143) calls these pages "inutilement compliquées" and points to the Legendre curve, which appears on scan 527.

**R03, scan 522, unproved claim. Keep, with a reading note.** "analytique complexe" is now settled. Under T′⁺₁₁ in (34) the ink has Sl(2,ℤ/2ℤ)/±1, a slip for Sl(2,ℤ)/±1 (as in (35)); the witnesses correct it silently.

**R04, scan 522, conjecture. Keep.** The reading "Π̂′₀,₃ à T̂′⁺₁₁" is settled. The interpretation of (35) and (33) through diagram (9) (LM_P052) is deferred ("J'y reviendrai tantôt").

**R05, scans 522–523, conjecture. Keep.** The margin answer "Oui, on peut remonter, et c'est plus ou moins trivial… cf plus bas…" points ahead to the Legendre family of scan 527.

**R06, scan 523, unproved claim. Correct.** The parenthesis reads "(qui nécessairement, dans le cadre profini, respectent les réseaux discrets)": every profinite lift sends the discrete Π₀,₃ into the discrete Sl(2,ℤ). This and the count "d'exactement quatre façons" are checked in S01.

**R07, scan 523, conjecture. Keep.**

**R08, scan 523, conjecture. Keep.** The margin "pas tout à fait cf pg suivante pour une formulation plus raisonnable…" refers to scan 524, where the question moves from outer actions to the extension E₁₁ (R12).

**R09, scan 523, unproved claim. Keep.** The equivalence between a lift U₀,₃ → M₁₁ and a family with Jacobi rigidification of level 2 and invariant λ is the moduli interpretation of M₁₁ → M′₁₁, stated without proof.

**R10, scan 523, own idea (first pass). Drop.** Redundant: Grothendieck introduces the Γ-set E of the four lifts himself on scan 524 and derives the index "2 ou 4" and the contrast with the lift of extensions on scan 525.

**R11, scan 524, conjecture. Correct.** "Je doute qu'il en existe une" is withdrawn by the author on scan 527: the Legendre family is such a curve and its existence is "tout à fait triviale" (S02). The doubt should be recorded as superseded.

**R12, scan 524, unpursued implication. Keep.** Since T̂⁺₁₁ = Sl(2,ℤ)^ has centre ±1, outer actions lose information, and the question is moved to the extension E₁₁ = E_{M₁,₁} of Γ by T̂⁺₁,₁.

**R13, scans 524–525, unproved claim. Keep.** "ce qui est plus fort que de trouver un relèvement Π̂₀,₃ → T⁺₁₁ compatible avec les op. ext. de Γ": the T here is written without hat, a slip, since the target must be the profinite group.

**R14, scan 525, unproved claim. Correct.** The index is clearly "2 ou 4" (replacing a struck "divisant … 4, tel que" and a struck "4! = 24"). The argument is complete: if the continuous action of Γ on the four-element set E fixes no element, the orbit sizes are 2+2 or 4, so a stabilizer has index 2 or 4; the kernel of Γ → Aut(E) is open, so over a finite extension all four lifts commute with the action.

**R15, scan 525, conjecture. Correct.** The text is "rien ne prouve que l'on puisse trouver un “germe de relèvement” de E₀,₃ → E′₁,₁ en E₀,₃^G → E₁,₁^G (germes pris p.r. aux sous-groupes ouverts de E₀,₃ contenant Π̂₀,₃, ou m̄ de tt les sous-groupes ouverts)"; the first pass read "grand relèvement". A germ of lift is a lift on some open subgroup; the obstruction on E₀,₃ itself is α ∈ H²(E₀,₃, ℤ/2) (36). The doubt that α dies on an open subgroup is answered on scan 527 (S03).

**R16, scan 525, own idea (first pass). Keep, with the corrected wording "germe de relèvement".** For open subgroups containing Π̂₀,₃ the class does die (S03); the comparison with arbitrary open subgroups remains a sensible check.

**R17, scan 526, unproved claim. Keep.** The subscript "U₀,₂ | U₀,₃" and a possible strike through "les" ("forment les sections") remain open in the ink.

**R18, scan 526, unproved claim. Keep, with a reading note.** The ink has "(il suffirait qu'elle ne soit de type 0,0)" without "pas"; the sense is "not of type (0,0)", i.e. not ℙ¹. The underlying fact: a smooth connected curve over an algebraically closed field of characteristic 0 other than ℙ¹ is a K(π,1) for torsion coefficients, and the Hochschild–Serre spectral sequence carries this to the arithmetic group E_U.

**R19, scan 526, unproved claim. Keep.**

**R20, scan 526, own idea (first pass). Correct.** Pic(U₀,₃) = 0 (ℙ¹ minus three rational points), so the Kummer sequence gives H²(U₀,₃, μ₂) ≅ Br(U₀,₃)[2] with μ₂ = ℤ/2: here the "groupe de Brauer" identification is exact, with no line-bundle contribution.

**R21, scan 527, unproved claim. Keep.** See S03 for the derivation from (38).

**R22, scan 527, unproved claim. Keep.** H^q(Ū₀,₃) = 0 for q ≥ 2 because Ū₀,₃ is an affine curve over an algebraically closed field. The displayed sequence is the exact sequence of a spectral sequence with two nonzero rows (differentials d₂: E₂^{p,1} → E₂^{p+2,0}); S04 shows that both d₂ vanish here.

**R23, scan 527, unpursued implication. Correct.** The identification under the inner H¹(Ū) is drawn as ≀, an isomorphism with 𝔽₂². The Galois action on it is trivial (S05).

**R24, scan 527, unproved claim. Keep, with a check.** The Legendre curve y² = x(x−1)(x−λ) is smooth over U₀,₃ (discriminant 16λ²(λ−1)²), its 2-torsion points (0,0), (1,0), (λ,0) are sections, which give the level-2 rigidification, and its Legendre invariant is λ. This settles R11.

**R25, scan 528, conjecture. Keep.** "Je ne vais pas approfondir la question ici, dans quelle mesure ce choix est “naturel”": the choice is one of four quadratic twists (S06).

**R26, scan 528, unproved claim. Correct.** In (40) the identifications Π̂₀,₃ ≀ T̂^{!+}₀,₄, E₀,₃ ≀ N^!₀,₄ and T̂⁺₁₁ ≀ Sl(2,ℤ)^ are drawn as isomorphisms; E₁₁ = N₁,₁ and Γ = Γ as equalities.

**R27, scan 528, unproved claim. Keep.**

**R28, scan 528, unproved claim. Keep, with a check.** For the Legendre family the discrete monodromy is injective, with image ⟨A, B⟩ = {γ ∈ Γ(2) : a ≡ 1 mod 4} (S07). Injectivity after completion holds because ⟨A, B⟩ has finite index (12) in Sl(2,ℤ), so the profinite topology of Sl(2,ℤ) induces the full profinite topology on it and its completion embeds in Sl(2,ℤ)^.

**R29, scan 528, unproved claim. Correct.** The reading is "le sous-groupe de congruence du deuxième mb(?) …, soit Γ₂, soit produit direct de ce sous-groupe Π₀,₃ et de son centre ±1". It is true: Γ(2) = ⟨A, B⟩ × {±1} with ⟨A, B⟩ free of rank 2 (S07).

**R30, scans 527–528, own idea (first pass). Keep, made precise.** The four quadratic twists by 1, λ, 1−λ, λ(1−λ) realize the four lifts of E (S06).

**R31, scan 528, own idea (first pass). Keep.** A and B are the standard Legendre monodromies around λ = 0 and λ = 1 in a suitable basis. Their product AB = [[−3,2],[−2,1]] has trace −2, so the monodromy at ∞ is minus a unipotent (fibre of type I₂* there).

**R32, scan 527, own idea (first pass). Correct (answered).** The action is trivial and E₂^{1,1} ≅ H¹(ℚ, ℤ/2)² ≅ (ℚ*/ℚ*²)² (S05).

**R33, scan 529, unproved claim. Correct (checked).** There are exactly four complements of ±1 in Γ₂, and their intersection has index 8 in Γ₂ (a divisor of 16) and 4 in Π₀,₃ (a divisor of 8) (S07, S08).

**R34, scan 529, upper margin, unproved claim. Correct (checked).** The NB is true: the intersection is Γ(4) = Ker(Sl(2,ℤ) → Sl(2,ℤ/4ℤ)) (S08); in the profinite setting it is the corresponding kernel of Sl(2,ℤ)^ → Sl(2,ℤ/4ℤ).

**R35, scan 529, conjecture. Correct: the guess is false.** "Ça m'étonnerait pas que ce soit juste le groupe des commutateurs de Π₀,₃,) qui est d'indice 4" is false: the commutator subgroup of a free group of rank 2 has infinite index (abelianization ℤ², or ℤ̂² for the profinite group). The intersection is Π₀,₃²[Π₀,₃, Π₀,₃] = Ker(Π₀,₃ → H₁(Π₀,₃, 𝔽₂)), of index 4. The margin "c'est vrai et c'est ≃/m̄ trivial…" stands beside "il est d'indice un diviseur de 8", which is true.

**R36, scan 529, own idea (first pass). Keep.** It is the correct diagnosis of R35.

**R37, scan 529, lower margin, conjecture. Correct (answered).** The margin reads "Il n'est pas clair ~~si Π₀,₃~~ est invariant …, je présume que oui, puisque 𝔖₃ opère aussi extérieurement sur Π₀,₃". For the untwisted Legendre image the answer is no; exactly one of the four complements is normal in Sl(2,ℤ) (S09).

**R38, scan 529, lower margin, unpursued implication. Correct (answered).** For the normal complement, T⁺₁₁/Π₀,₃ is the dicyclic group of order 12, a non-split central extension of 𝔖₃ by ±1 (S09).

**R39, scan 529, own idea (first pass). Keep.** Carried out in S09: 𝔖₃ permutes the four complements with orbits of sizes 1 and 3.

**R40, scan 529, unproved claim. Keep, with readings.** "(défini mod autom. intérieurs)" is now read; the generators are written l₀, l₁, l₂ (the witnesses print l∞).

**R41, scan 530, unproved claim. Keep.** A proof sketch along the indicated lines is in S10.

**R42, scan 530, unproved claim. Keep.** According to the witnesses, the next scan completes the proof with the composite Γ_ℚ → Γ₁,₁ → Autext T̂⁺₁,₁ → Autext T̂′⁺₁,₁, injective by the theorem.

**R43, scan 530, unpursued implication. Keep.** The passage to the central quotient is covered by the argument of S10, which works with T̂′⁺₁,₁ directly.

**R44, scan 530, own idea (first pass). Keep.** It is the argument of the next page (per the witnesses).

## Second-pass notes (mine)

**S01, scan 523, unproved claim checked (mine).** The preimage of Sl(2,ℤ)/±1 under Sl(2,ℤ)^ → Sl(2,ℤ)^/±1 is Sl(2,ℤ)·{±1} = Sl(2,ℤ), so every lift of Π̂₀,₃ → Sl(2,ℤ)^/±1 sends the discrete Π₀,₃ into Sl(2,ℤ): "nécessairement … respectent les réseaux discrets" is immediate. Two lifts of a map into G/Z with Z central differ by a homomorphism into Z, so the lifts form a torsor under Hom(Π̂₀,₃, ±1) ≅ (ℤ/2)², which gives "d'exactement quatre façons".

**S02, scans 524 → 527, conjecture withdrawn by the author (mine).** The doubt of scan 524 is answered on scan 527 by the Legendre curve (R24). Since that curve is defined over ℚ, it gives (39) and the map of extensions (40), i.e. the strong form of the question (a lift E₀,₃ → E₁₁), and a fortiori a Γ-invariant element of E.

**S03, scans 525/527, unpursued implication (mine).** By (38) and H^q(Ū₀,₃) = 0 for q ≥ 2, a class of H²(U₀,₃, ℤ/2) dies over a finite extension of ℚ: its component in H¹(K, H¹(Ū)) dies over a finite extension, and the rest comes from H²(K, ℤ/2), which dies over a further one. Through H*(E_U, −) ≅ H*(U, −) this answers the "rien ne dit" of scan 525 for open subgroups containing Π̂₀,₃. Grothendieck does not draw this conclusion; he passes directly to the explicit curve.

**S04, scan 527, own idea (mine; confidence high).** U₀,₃(ℚ) is nonempty (λ = 2), so π₁(U₀,₃) → Γ has a section and H^n(ℚ, M) → H^n(U₀,₃, M) is injective (U₀,₃ is a K(π,1)). Hence both d₂ of the displayed sequence vanish and 0 → H²(ℚ, ℤ/2) → H²(U₀,₃, ℤ/2) → H¹(ℚ, ℤ/2)² → 0 is exact, using S05.

**S05, scan 527, unpursued implication answered (mine; confidence high).** By Kummer theory (Pic Ū₀,₃ = 0, μ₂ = ℤ/2), H¹(Ū₀,₃, ℤ/2) ≅ O(Ū)*/O(Ū)*² is spanned by the classes of λ and 1−λ, functions defined over ℚ; so Gal(ℚ̄/ℚ) acts trivially on the 𝔽₂² and E₂^{1,1} ≅ H¹(ℚ, ℤ/2)² ≅ (ℚ*/ℚ*²)².

**S06, scans 524–528, own idea (mine; confidence medium).** Twisting the Legendre curve by u multiplies its monodromy by the Kummer character of u, and the characters of u ∈ {1, λ, 1−λ, λ(1−λ)} are exactly Hom(Π̂₀,₃, ±1); so the four twists u·y² = x(x−1)(x−λ) realize the four lifts of E, and this is the ambiguity in Grothendieck's "choix". All four twists are defined over ℚ, which suggests that Γ fixes E pointwise; this should be checked against the outer-homomorphism convention and the "quitte à passer à une extension un peu plus grande" of scan 525.

**S07, scans 528–529, claims checked (mine).** γ ↦ a mod 4 (γ = [[a,b],[c,d]]) is a homomorphism ε: Γ(2) → ±1, since bc′ ≡ 0 mod 4; so do β(γ) = (−1)^{b/2} and κ(γ) = (−1)^{c/2}. Γ(2) is generated by A, B and −1, and ⟨A, B⟩ (free of rank 2, Sanov) lies in Ker ε, which does not contain −1; so ⟨A, B⟩ = Ker ε and Γ(2) = ⟨A, B⟩ × {±1}, which verifies (42) and the "produit direct". The four complements of ±1 are Ker ε, Ker εβ, Ker εκ, Ker εβκ: "exactement quatre".

**S08, scan 529, claims checked, one refuted (mine).** The intersection of the four complements is Ker ε ∩ Ker β ∩ Ker κ = Γ(4), of index 8 in Γ(2) and 4 in Π₀,₃: the NB of the upper margin is correct, and so are "diviseur de 16" and "diviseur de 8". The commutator-subgroup guess is false (R35).

**S09, scan 529, lower margin, conjecture answered (mine; confidence high for the group theory).** Conjugation by S fixes ε and swaps β, κ; conjugation by T sends ε ↦ εκ, β ↦ βκ, κ ↦ κ. So 𝔖₃ = Sl(2,ℤ)/Γ(2) permutes the four complements with orbits {Ker εβκ} and {Ker ε, Ker εβ, Ker εκ}: exactly one complement is normal in Sl(2,ℤ). The Legendre image Ker ε is not normal, so "je présume que oui" holds for one of the four lifts only (with R31's matrices, the twist by λ(1−λ)). For that one, Sl(2,ℤ)/Ker εβκ has order 12 and S has order 4 in it (S² = −1 ∉ Ker εβκ), so it is the dicyclic group ℤ/3 ⋊ ℤ/4, a non-split central extension of 𝔖₃ by ±1.

**S10, scan 530, own idea: proof of the theorem (mine; confidence medium).** Let K ⊂ Γ_ℚ act by inner automorphisms on T̂′⁺₁,₁. Since Π̂₀,₃ is normal in T̂′⁺₁,₁ with quotient 𝔖₃ (33), and the inclusion is Γ-compatible, K acts on Π̂₀,₃ through the image of 𝔖₃ in Out(Π̂₀,₃); the outer action of Γ_ℚ on Π̂₀,₃ is faithful (Belyi), so K embeds in a group of order dividing 6. A finite normal subgroup of Γ_ℚ is trivial (Artin–Schreier: its nontrivial finite subgroups have order 2 and are generated by complex conjugations, which are not normal), so K = 1; faithfulness on T̂⁺₁,₁ follows. The step through Out(Π̂₀,₃) needs care with the choice of the outer homomorphism.


# ===== LM_P054 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P054 (scans 531–540): research notes, checked against the corrected transcription

The first pass's notes are `answers/unzipped/LM_P054/LM_P054_notes.md`, § 5. Each is checked here against `second_pass/LM_P054_checked.tex` and kept, corrected or dropped, with the reason. Section C adds the second-pass checker's own notes, labelled as such. Kinds: *question/programme* (Grothendieck's), *expectation* (Grothendieck's unproved belief), *claim without proof*, *implication not pursued*, *model's idea* (the first pass's), *checker's note* (mine).

## A. First-pass notes (§ 5.1)

**A1. Scan 531. Question/programme. Keep.** "Il est temps … de s'assurer qu'il en est bien ainsi": the homomorphism Π̂₀,₃ → T̂^{+′}₁,₁ just used should agree with Π̂₀,₃ → Π̂′₀,₃ of (10)–(20) through an isomorphism "qui reste à décrire", commuting with the exterior Γ-actions. *Reason:* the reading is confirmed. The target is a single T̂^{+′}₁,₁; the first pass had written the group twice (change 5).

**A2. Scan 532. Grothendieck's own diagnosis. Keep.** The "natural" homomorphism Π₀,₃ → T^{+′}₁,₁, obtained by factoring through Π₀,₃ →(can., surj) Π′₀,₃, "n'était pas du tout celui qu'on avait en vue". He gives up that route ("revenir à la charge"), and § 37 restarts from equivariant coverings. *Reason:* the text is confirmed.

**A3. Scan 533. Expectation (was: claim). Corrected.** The ink reads "en fait les deux doivent être équivalents". Grothendieck *expects* local faithfulness (each G_x faithful near x) and global faithfulness of G to coincide; he does not assert it. "Donc G_x est un groupe cyclique" and "Y = X/G est une surface top. paracompacte" remain claims without proof. *Reason:* the first pass's "les deux notions sont équivalentes" misread the ink (change 18). Note C1 says when the expectation holds.

**A4. Scan 533. Claim without proof. Keep.** The fixed-point set X^! = {x | G_x ≠ (1)} and its image Y^! are called discrete, and the ramification index is d_y = ord G_x. This needs local quotient charts of the form z ↦ z^d.

**A5. Scan 533, margin. Claim without proof. Corrected.** The Variante reads as follows. 𝒢 acts on X with a normal ("invariant") subgroup G satisfying the conditions "ci-contre". Setting Y = X/G "avec opér. de 𝒢/G = ℋ dessus", one gets Rev.(X,𝒢) ≊ Rev((Y,d),ℋ), hence Π₁(X,𝒢,x) ⥲ Π₁((Y,d,ℋ),y). A proof must check that ℋ preserves the signature d and define the covering category of (Y,d) with its ℋ-action. *Reason:* the first pass lost "opér. de", the action of ℋ on Y on which the statement rests (changes 27–30).

**A6. Scan 533, margin. Claim without proof. Corrected.** "Application: 𝔖₀,₃ ≃ T′₁,₁ (≃ Gl(2,Z)/±1), ext de T₀,₃ = 𝔖₃ × Z/2 par Π₀₃." This is the Variante with 𝒢 = T₀,₃ acting on U₀,₃(ℂ); the factor Z/2 is presumably complex conjugation. *Reason:* the group letter is the ornate S, not 𝒢 (change 34). Gl versus Sl stays open in the ink, but only Gl fits the stated extension (C2).

**A7. Scans 531–533. Model's idea. Keep.** Compare the failed and the intended homomorphisms through their actions on covering categories, with explicit path data. *Reason:* still a sensible check. § 37 itself takes this route through Rev(X,G).

**A8. Scan 534, (1). Claim without proof. Keep.** X′ ↦ X′/G gives an equivalence Rev(X,G) ⥲ Rev_d(Y) with ramified covers subordinate to d.

**A9. Scan 534, NB. Claim without proof. Keep.** The quasi-inverse is the "normalised" (X,G)-cover of X ×_Y Y′. The analogue for "schémas loc. noeth. réguliers de dim 1" is obtained by normalisation.

**A10. Scan 534, (2). Claim without proof. Keep.** A canonical isomorphism Π₁(X,G;·) ⥲ Π₁((Y,d),y). The basepoint letter in (2) is still a/x.

**A11. Scan 535, (3). Claim without proof. Keep.** The exact sequence 1 → Π₁(X,x) → Π₁((Y,d),y) → G → 1 for connected X. Grothendieck circles the term "Π₁((X,G→1),x)" and writes "Π₁(X,x)" in the margin, which confirms the kernel is the ordinary fundamental group.

**A12. Scan 535, NB. Expectation. Keep.** "On a ? un corollaire analogue" for regular 1-dimensional schemes. For smooth relative curves over S "il faudrait (si j'en ai besoin) faire un peu attention de préciser l'énoncé".

**A13. Scan 535, (4). Claim without proof. Keep, now checked.** The three orbits, their images 0, 1, ∞ and the indices 2, 2, 3 are correct; see A14, C3 and C4.

**A14. Scan 535. Model's idea. Corrected (carried out).** The invariant the first pass proposed to construct is f(λ) = 27λ²(λ−1)² / 4(λ²−λ+1)³ = 1728/j(λ). It sends {0,1,∞} to 0 with index 2, {2,−1,½} to 1 with index 2, by 4(λ²−λ+1)³ − 27λ²(λ−1)² = (λ+1)²(λ−2)²(2λ−1)², and the roots of λ²−λ+1 to ∞ with index 3. *Reason:* checked by the second pass.

**A15. Scan 536. Claim without proof. Keep.** One cannot take x = P = j, which is an orbifold point. One takes x outside the eight special points, with y = f(x) the "pt-base typique Q = j".

**A16. Scan 536. Claim without proof. Corrected.** A 𝔖₃-class of paths from the "point-base *initial*" P = j to x gives an "iso. *corr.*" (corresponding, not canonical) of the first member of (5bis) with Π₀,₃. Hence (6), which changes by an inner automorphism when the class of paths changes. *Reason:* the first pass read "orbital" and "iso. can." (changes 48–49). The ink separates the path-dependent "iso. corr." from the "iso. can." (6), which is canonical up to inner automorphisms.

**A17. Scan 536. Implication not pursued. Keep.** (7) is said to be (9bis) of § 36, now read as the homotopy exact sequence of 𝔖₃ acting on U₀,₃(ℂ). The comparison with § 36 is not carried out.

**A18. Scan 536. Model's idea. Keep.** Track the inner-automorphism ambiguity of (6) with explicit loops around the branch values.

**A19. Scan 537, (8). Claim without proof. Corrected.** The paired strokes beside the diagram are equality signs: Π̂₀,₃ = T̂⁺₀,₄, ℰ₀,₃ = N^!₀,₄ (the letter N is uncertain) and Γ₀,₄ = Γ_Q. The first two identify the geometric and arithmetic fundamental groups of U₀,₃ = M^!₀,₄ with Teichmüller-type groups of type (0,4). The equality Γ₀,₄ = Γ_Q amounts to the faithfulness of the outer Galois action on Π̂₀,₃, which is Belyi's theorem. *Reason:* the first pass only described "deux courts traits"; CSG/CK read "=" (change 53).

**A20. Scan 537. Implication not pursued. Keep.** "Mettre en relation U₀,₃, avec l'action de 𝔖₃ dessus, avec la multiplicité modulaire M₁₁" (indices Q understood) is the programme of § 37, carried out on scans 538–540 and beyond.

**A21. Scan 537. Model's idea. Corrected.** The base is Z[1/2], "i.e. en car. rés. ≠ 2" (the reading rés./tj. is still open). The first pass's suggestion to look at residue characteristic 3 separately is right, for a precise reason given in C5.

**A22. Scan 538. Claim without proof. Corrected.** Grothendieck calls his earlier identification U₀,₃ = M^!₀,₄ ≃ M₁,₁[2]′ "un peu vaseusement" (muddled). He corrects it to "(sur lequel M₁,₁[2] est une gerbe liée par Z/2)": M₁,₁[2] is a Z/2-gerbe over M₁,₁[2]′. *Reason:* the first pass read "oiseusement" (change 57); the witnesses' "sous lequel" is wrong.

**A23. Scan 538. Claim without proof. Corrected.** For elliptic curves, the statement that an automorphism inducing the identity on ₙE is the identity for n ≥ 3 is Serre's rigidity lemma. For abelian varieties "de dim quelconque" it is false without a polarisation (C6).

**A24. Scan 538. Claim without proof. Keep.** For n = 2, an automorphism trivial on ₂E is id_E over an open and closed part of S and −id_E on the complement. This holds over Z[1/2], where the only automorphisms acting trivially on E[2] are ±1.

**A25. Scan 538. Implication not pursued. Corrected.** "Coiffer M₁,₁[2] par un M₁,₁(n), où n est un multiple de 2": for M₁,₁(n) to be a scheme, the margin of scan 539 requires n ≥ 3, so n must be even and at least 4. Its image in Spec Z is the open set Spec Z[1/n]. *Reason:* the ink reads M₁,₁(n), and the "2n" alternative is dropped (change 60).

**A26. Scan 538. Model's idea. Keep.** Follow the residual sign automorphism through the presentation by M₁,₁(n) and its descent to level 2.

**A27. Scan 539, (9). Claim without proof. Keep.** Over Spec Z[1/n], M₁,₁ "se récupère comme" the pair (M₁,₁[n], Gl(2,Z/nZ)). The margin insists on Gl and not Sl.

**A28. Scan 539, second margin. Claim without proof. Keep.** The functor of full level-n structures is "représentable ssi n ≥ 3 (donc il ne l'est pas pour n = 1, 2)".

**A29. Scan 539, NB. Claim without proof. Keep.** M̃₁,₁ over Spec Z is "l'enveloppe représentable" of the functor of isomorphism classes, "itou sur un schém. de base … quelconque". In modern terms this is the coarse moduli space; see C7.

**A30. Scans 539–540. Implication not pursued. Keep.** Gl rather than Sl is needed because the determinant of the level structure gives the morphism to μₙ^*, along which M₁,₁[n] splits into geometric components.

**A31. Scan 539. Model's idea. Keep.** Before (10) the ink says "au dessus de Z/nZ", and after it "Z[1/n]"; all three witnesses also have Z/nZ. It is probably a slip of the pen for Z[1/n].

**A32. Scan 540. Model's idea. Corrected.** The Weil pairing identifies Λ²(ₙE) with μₙ, not μₙ^{⊗−1}. Either convention gives the next line, because an isomorphism Z/nZ ≅ μₙ^{⊗−1}(S) is the same thing as one Z/nZ ≅ μₙ(S), up to inversion. The ⊗−1 is a duality convention or a slip, and the argument does not depend on it.

**A33. Scan 540. Claim without proof. Corrected.** "Et c'est *ce* morphisme qui est à fibres géom. connexes *en car. 0*": the doubly underlined insertion is confirmed, and the subject is (11) itself (change 75). The fibres of M₁,₁[n] → μₙ^* are known to be geometrically connected in every characteristic prime to n (Igusa; Deligne–Rapoport). Grothendieck's restriction is therefore more cautious than necessary.

**A34. Scan 540, (12). Claim without proof. Keep.** Passing to the limit gives M₁,₁[∞] = lim M₁,₁[n] → μ_∞^* ≃ Spec Σ, marked "iso. can". Σ is defined in the lines that follow (scan 541 in the witnesses: the maximal cyclotomic extension).

**A35. Scans 539–540. Model's idea. Keep.** Track the change-of-basis action of Gl(2,Z/nZ) through the exterior square, level by level, and ask whether it is compatible with the limit.

## B. First-pass literature comparisons (§ 5.2)

**B1. Scans 533–537. Model's idea (Noohi, covering stacks). Keep.** It is relevant to the Variante (A5) and to (2)–(3).

**B2. Scans 539–540. Model's idea (Hain, level structures). Keep.**

**B3. Scans 535, 537–539. Model's idea (Deligne–Rapoport). Keep.** It is also the reference for A24, A33 and C7.

## C. Checker's notes (second pass)

**C1. Scan 533. Checker's note on Grothendieck's expectation "les deux doivent être équivalents".** Local faithfulness implies global faithfulness at once. Conversely, suppose 1 ≠ g ∈ G_x acts trivially near x. Then g has finite order, and by Newman's theorem a non-trivial periodic homeomorphism of a connected manifold cannot fix a non-empty open set pointwise. So the two conditions are equivalent for connected X. For disconnected X they differ: take Z/2 acting by an involution on one component and trivially on another.

**C2. Scan 533, margin. Checker's note supporting the reading Gl.** The map GL(2,Z) → GL(2,F₂) × {±1}, g ↦ (g mod 2, det g), is surjective with kernel Γ(2), which contains −1. So PGL(2,Z) = GL(2,Z)/±1 is an extension of 𝔖₃ × Z/2 by Γ(2)/±1, a free group of rank 2 (= Π₀,₃), exactly as the margin says. With Sl one gets PSL(2,Z), an extension of 𝔖₃ alone.

**C3. Scans 535–536. Checker's note on notation.** The fixed points of the order-3 elements of the anharmonic group (λ ↦ 1/(1−λ)) are the roots of λ²−λ+1, that is e^{±iπ/3}. With the usual French j = e^{2iπ/3} these are −j² and −j. So "(j, j̄) ↦ ∞" in (4), and P = j on scan 536, need j to name a primitive sixth root of unity, or the conventions of § 36. The same letter also names the base point Q = j of Y. Check § 36 before relying on these names.

**C4. Scans 535–536. Checker's note: claim confirmed.** Y = ℙ¹ ∖ {0} with signature 2{1}+3{∞} has orbifold fundamental group ⟨a, b | a² = b³ = 1⟩ ≅ Z/2 ∗ Z/3 ≅ PSL(2,Z). So (5bis)–(7) identify Π′₀,₃ with PSL(2,Z) and Π₀,₃ with its free normal subgroup Γ(2)/±1 of index 6. This agrees with T⁺₁,₁ = SL(2,Z) in § 36.

**C5. Scan 537. Checker's note: an implication not pursued.** Over Z[1/2], the tame description of U₀,₃/𝔖₃ breaks down at residue characteristic 3. Modulo 3, λ²−λ+1 = (λ+1)² and −1 = 2 = ½, so the single point λ = 2 is fixed by all of 𝔖₃: its stabiliser has order 6, divisible by 3, so the ramification is wild. The signature 2{1}+3{∞} holds over Z[1/6], not Z[1/2]. Diagram (8), over Q, is unaffected.

**C6. Scan 538. Checker's note: the claim needs a hypothesis.** On A = E × E the automorphism (P, Q) ↦ (P + nQ, Q) is the identity on A[n] for every n, yet it is not the identity. So the rigidity statement for abelian varieties "de dim quelconque" needs a polarisation, which makes the automorphism group finite; Serre's lemma then gives it for n ≥ 3. For elliptic curves no hypothesis is needed.

**C7. Scan 539. Checker's note (literature).** M̃₁,₁ is the affine j-line A¹ over Z (Deligne–Rapoport; Katz–Mazur). "L'enveloppe représentable" is the universal map from the stack to schemes, that is, the coarse moduli space (Keel–Mori). Over Z[1/n] this agrees with the quotient (10).


# ===== LM_P055 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P055 (scans 541–550): research notes checked against the corrected text

Each first-pass research note (section 5 of `answers\unzipped\LM_P055\LM_P055_notes.md`, numbered as there) is kept, corrected or dropped, with the reason. The text is checked against `LM_P055_checked.tex`. The second pass's own notes follow and are labelled "second pass". Kinds: unproved claim, conjecture or expressed doubt, author's question, unpursued implication, own idea (the model's).

## First-pass notes

1. **Scan 541, (13)–(14). Unproved claim. Keep.** For $n'\mid n$, $n'\ne1,2$, $M_{1,1}[n']$ is recovered as the contracted product $M_{1,1}[n]\wedge_{\Gamma_n}\Gamma_{n'}$ and as the quotient $M_{1,1}[n]/K_{n,n'}$ over $\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z[1/n]$, with $K_{nn'}=\operatorname{Ker}(\Gamma_n\to\Gamma_{n'})$. No proof is written here, and (9)–(10) lie outside the package. *Reason:* the readings are confirmed. The ink has the contracted-product sign ∧, where the witnesses print ×.

2. **Scan 541, margin NB. Unproved claim. Keep, with a proof route.** $K_{n,n'}$ "opère librement sur le schéma $M_{1,1}[n]$" for $n'\mid n$, $n'\ne1,2$, "mais pas bien sûr si $n'=1$ ou $2$". The stabiliser of a point $x$ in $K_{n,n'}$ is the group of automorphisms of $E_x$ that fix the level-$n'$ structure. This group is trivial for $n'\ge3$ by the rigidity lemma, while $-1\in K_{n,n'}$ acts trivially when $n'=1,2$. *Reason:* the reading is confirmed. The witnesses' footnote omits "$M_{1,1}[n]$".

3. **Scan 541, last paragraph. Unproved claim. Keep, wording corrected.** Grothendieck proposes to describe $M_{1,1}$ and $\widetilde M_{1,1}$ "au dessus des deux ouverts" $\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z[1/n]$, $\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z[1/n']$ with $(n,n')=1$, and to glue them over $\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z[1/nn']$ "par ces mêmes formules, appliquées à $nn'$". The gluing compatibilities, for the multiplicity and for the coarse object, are not checked. *Reason:* the corrected text reads "dessus des" and has the cancelled word "dans". The substance of the note is unchanged.

4. **Scan 542, Th. Unproved claim. Keep.** "$\widetilde M_{1,1}\simeq\mathbb E^1_{\mathbb Z}$ (sur $\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z$)" is recorded "pour mémoire", without proof. *Reason:* unchanged.

5. **Scan 542, the two privileged sections. Unpursued implication. Correct.** The two sections, whose generic values are the two anharmonic classes, are used to pin ("épingler") the isomorphism, which is defined only modulo $X\mapsto\pm X+n$. These pages do not say whether the sections stay distinct at every prime. A later passage of the manuscript, outside this package (CSG draft around line 11197), states that they are not disjoint and meet in characteristics 2 and 3. *Reason:* the first pass treated the question as left open, but the manuscript itself answers it later.

6. **Scan 542, the two sections. Own idea (first pass). Correct: the check can be completed.** In the $j$-coordinate the sections are $j=0$ (automorphism group $\mathbb Z/6\mathbb Z$) and $j=1728=2^6\cdot3^3$ (automorphism group $\mathbb Z/4\mathbb Z$). They therefore meet exactly over the primes 2 and 3, which agrees with the later passage. Since $X\mapsto\pm X+n$ changes the difference of two sections only by its sign, the pinning works: exactly one coordinate puts the $\mathbb Z/6\mathbb Z$-section at $0$ and the other at $+1728$. *Reason:* the proposed computation can be finished with standard facts on $j$ (Silverman, *AEC*, III.10 and Appendix A). Confidence high.

7. **Scan 542, groups $\mathbb Z/4\mathbb Z$, $\mathbb Z/6\mathbb Z$. Unproved claim. Keep.** The proof is waved through as "très facile p.ex. par voie transcend.te". *Reason:* unchanged; the abbreviation remains doubtful.

8. **Scan 542, "sauf erreur". Expressed doubt (terminology). Keep.** The hesitation concerns the name "anharmoniques", not the automorphism groups. *Reason:* unchanged.

9. **Scans 542–543, (15) and "énoncés idoines". Unproved claim. Keep.** The identification $\operatorname{Aut}(E_x)\simeq(\Gamma_n)_x$ is said to have analogues ("énoncés idoines") over any base scheme. *Reason:* "idoines" is now certain; the alternative "identiques" is dropped.

10. **Scan 543, Proposition. Unproved claim. Keep.** Over $\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Q$, with the marginal condition $n\ne1,2$, there are exactly two "critical" orbits, with stabilisers $\mathbb Z/4\mathbb Z$ and $\mathbb Z/6\mathbb Z$. A proof must say what kind of orbits are meant and derive exhaustiveness from the classification of automorphisms. *Reason:* unchanged. The restriction to $\mathbb Q$ is essential (see note 17).

11. **Scan 544, (17). Unproved claim. Correct.** Grothendieck says that one finds "aussitôt" that the mixed topos $(M_{1,1}[n],\Gamma'_n)$ does not depend on $n$, up to canonical equivalence. In the margin this holds over a base where $n,n'$ are "tous deux inversibles". He identifies it with the multiplicity $M_{1,1}'$ of the preceding §. The comparison between levels is presented as immediate and is not written out. *Reason:* the first pass omitted "aussitôt" and read "devenus inversibles"; the corrected margin has "(tous?) deux inversibles".

12. **Scan 544, Remarque. Unproved claim. Keep.** Validity "pour un $n$" implies validity "pour tous", "(grâce au fait que les $\Gamma_{n,n'}$ ($n'\mid n$, $n'\ne1,2$) … opèrent librement)". *Reason:* the opening parenthesis is restored; the substance is unchanged.

13. **Scans 544–545, Corollary. Unproved claim. Correct.** In characteristic 0 the stabilisers in $\Gamma'_n$ of points of the two critical orbits are $\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z$ and $\mathbb Z/3\mathbb Z$, that is $\mathbb Z/4\mathbb Z/\{\pm1\}$ and $\mathbb Z/6\mathbb Z/\{\pm1\}$. The proof is immediate from the Proposition, because $-1$ lies in every stabiliser and is the kernel of the action. The marginal qualification is "valable pour $n\ne|=|\ge2$, cf. plus bas", with the sign unresolved; the witnesses read "$n\ge2$". *Reason:* the margin sign is now recorded as uncertain. The witnesses' "$\mathbb Z/4\mathbb Z$ et $\mathbb Z/6\mathbb Z$" in the Corollary is a witness error.

14. **Scan 545, (19). Unproved claim. Keep.** The "résultat général de rigidité rappelé au début" is invoked to conclude that $\Gamma'_{2m,2}=\Gamma_{2m,2}/\pm1$ acts freely on $M_{1,1}[2m]$. The extra prime on the numerator in (19) is kept as written. *Reason:* unchanged; second-pass note B verifies the claim.

15. **Scan 545. Unpursued implication. Keep.** "Le cor. de la prop. est valable encore" for $\Gamma'_2\,(\xleftarrow{\sim}\Gamma_2\simeq\mathfrak S_3)$ acting on $M_{1,1}[2]'$. Scans 547–548 take up what is lost in passing to the effective quotient. *Reason:* unchanged. The arrow is now read as $\xleftarrow{\sim}$ without doubt.

16. **Scan 546, the representing equivalence. Unproved claim. Keep.** "donc par construction on a pour tt schéma $S$ une équivalence $\mathcal Ell_n(S)\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}(S,M_{1,1}[n])$", with Hom taken in locally ringed topoi, "i.e. hom de multiplicités". Functoriality in $S$ and descent are not written out. *Reason:* unchanged; "tt" is settled.

17. **Scan 544, the Corollary in characteristics 2 and 3. Own idea (first pass). Correct: the test can be answered.** In characteristics 2 and 3 the two anharmonic curves reduce to a single supersingular curve, $j=0=1728$, whose automorphism group has order 24 (characteristic 2) or 12 (characteristic 3). Over a base where 2 or 3 is not invertible there is therefore one critical orbit, with stabiliser in $\Gamma'_n$ of order 12 or 6, and the Proposition and Corollary hold over $\mathbb Z[1/6n]$ but not over $\mathbb Z[1/n]$ when $6\nmid n$. This is consistent with "Sur $\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Q$", "(car. 0)", "($\overline{\mathrm m}$ en car. 0)" and the later remark of note 5. *Reason:* the first pass only proposed the test; it follows from standard facts (Silverman, *AEC*, Appendix A). Confidence high.

18. **Scan 546, multiplicity and stacks. Own idea (first pass). Keep.** Compare "multiplicité schématique", a locally ringed topos locally isomorphic to the étale topos of a scheme, with a stack whose groupoids keep the objects and their isomorphisms. *Reason:* the corrected text does not affect it.

19. **Scan 547, threshold $n\ge3$. Unproved claim. Keep, reading corrected.** The automorphisms of an elliptic curve "respectant une rigid. de Jacobi d'échelon $n$" are the identity iff $n\ge3$. *Reason:* the first pass marked "respectant" as deleted, but it is not. Without the word the condition on the automorphisms disappears from the sentence.

20. **Scan 547, the $\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z$-gerbe. Unproved claim. Keep.** "$M_{1,1}[2]\to M_{1,1}[2]'$ est un isom local (décrit entièrement par une $\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z$-gerbe sur $M_{1,1}[2]'$)" is stated without proof. Second-pass note B supplies the key fact: the automorphisms acting trivially on $E[2]$ are $\pm1$ at every point. *Reason:* unchanged.

21. **Scans 547–548, (21)–(22), coarse quotient. Unproved claim. Keep.** The inverse-image functor of $(X,G)\to X/G$ "est évident". When a normal subgroup $z$ (written as a cursive or Fraktur z) acts trivially and $G/z$ acts freely, (21) factors through an equivalence $(X,G/z)\xrightarrow{\sim}X/G$. The generality ("disons") and the existence of $X/G$ are left open. *Reason:* "évident" is now settled.

22. **Scan 548, (23). Unpursued implication. Keep, wording corrected.** The morphism (21) "s'identifie aussi, simplement" (not "ainsi") to $(M_{1,1}[2m],\Gamma_{2m,2})\to(M_{1,1}[2m],\Gamma'_{2m,2})\simeq M_{1,1}[2]'$. Independence from the auxiliary level $2m$ ($m\ge2$) is not discussed. *Reason:* the reading is corrected; the substance is unchanged.

23. **Scan 548, (24). Unproved claim. Keep.** $(M_{1,1}[n],\Gamma'_n)=M_{1,1}'\to M_{1,1}[n]/\Gamma'_n=\widetilde M_{1,1}$ is not an equivalence, because $\Gamma'_n$ has ramification of degrees 2 and 3 "($\overline{\mathrm m}$ en car. 0)", that is, even in characteristic 0. *Reason:* unchanged.

24. **Scan 548, final bracket. Conjecture or expectation. Keep.** "Si cependant on exclut les courbes elliptiques anharmoniques, sa (ça) marcherait." Second-pass note E checks it. *Reason:* unchanged.

25. **Scan 547, a global object of the gerbe. Own idea (first pass). Keep.** It remains a sensible test: does an explicit family over $M_{1,1}[2]'$, for instance a Legendre-type family, trivialise the gerbe? *Reason:* not affected by the corrections.

26. **Scan 548, local parameters at the critical orbits. Own idea (first pass). Keep.** Compute $t\mapsto t^2$ and $t\mapsto t^3$ at the two orbits in characteristic 0. *Reason:* not affected. In characteristics 2 and 3 note 17 shows the picture changes.

27. **Scan 549, (25). Unproved claim, proof referred to the preceding §. Correct.** The isomorphism $M_{1,1}[2]'\simeq M^!_{0,4}\simeq U_{0,3}$ is said to follow from "les raisonnements du § précédent". It is "valable sur le Spec … de $\mathbb Z[1/2]$" and again "(valable sur $\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z[1/2]$)" under (25). *Reason:* the first pass transcribed the first base as $\mathbb Z[1/n]$; the ink has $\mathbb Z[1/2]$.

28. **Scan 549, margin. Unpursued implication. Keep.** The image of $M_{1,1}[2]'$ in $\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z$ is $\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z[1/\cdot]$, while that of $M^!_{0,4}\simeq U_{0,3}$ is all of $\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z$. So (25) cannot extend over the prime 2 as an isomorphism. The denominator in the margin still looks like $n$; the main text has $2$ twice. *Reason:* the doubt is retained; it is now settled in the main text.

29. **Scan 549, "Il faudrait expliciter cette compatibilité". Author's question. Correct.** Grothendieck asks for the compatibility between $\Gamma'_2\simeq\Gamma_2=\operatorname{Gl}(2,\mathbb F_2)$ acting on $M_{1,1}[2]'$ and $\mathfrak S_3$ acting on $U_{0,3}$ to be made explicit, "en considérant l'action naturelle de $\mathfrak S_4$ sur $M^!_{0,4}$". The relevant group is $\mathfrak S_4$, of which (27) uses a copy of $\mathfrak S_3$. *Reason:* the first pass read $\mathfrak S_n$.

30. **Scan 549, (27)–(28). Unproved claim. Keep, with an addition.** $\Gamma_2\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak S_3$ is the permutation action on the three non-zero vectors $\alpha,\beta,\gamma$ of $\mathbb F_2^2$. It is faithful because $\alpha,\beta$ form a basis, and bijective by order count. In $\mathbb F_2^2$, $-v=v$, so the relations in (28) hold with either sign. The doubtful $+|-$ in (28) therefore does not affect the mathematics. *Reason:* the addition removes a false appearance of mathematical uncertainty.

31. **Scan 550, NB. Unproved claim. Correct.** The text reads: "c'est représentable bel et bien par une multiplicité ssi $n\geq3$, i.e. si le champ en (des?) groupoïdes qu'on veut représenter est 'rigide' — et alors la multiplicité est $\overline{\mathrm m}$ [même] un schéma, isomorphe d'ailleurs canonique(ment) au ss-schéma $\underline{\operatorname{Mon}}(I_{n-3},U_{0,3})$". The criterion is rigidity (trivial automorphisms) of the stack; "représentable et" does not occur. The doubtful insertion "co|en" did not exist: those strokes are the margin note's "il faut des". *Reason:* reading corrected (changes 31, 33, 34).

32. **Scan 550, left margin. Author's expectation. Correct.** "Il ne suffit plus de travailler avec la (les) topologie(s) étale(s) — il faut des top. fppf p.ex." The author names the remedy, the fppf topology, which the first pass lost. For $n=0,1,2$ the automorphism groups ($\mathrm{PGL}_2$ and the stabilisers of one or two points, of dimensions 3, 2 and 1) are smooth and positive-dimensional. The stacks are then algebraic for the fppf (or smooth) topology but not étale-locally schemes. *Reason:* reading corrected; the dimension remark replaces the first pass's vaguer "positive-dimensional".

33. **Scan 550, second paragraph. Unpursued implication. Keep.** Only the action of $\mathfrak S_3\times\mathfrak S_{n-3}$ is visible on $\underline{\operatorname{Mon}}(I_{n-3},U_{0,3})$, while $\mathfrak S_n$ acts on $M^!_{0,n}$. The sentence breaks off at the end of the package ("des opérations naturelles du|des …"; the witnesses continue "du sous-groupe (31) $S_3\times S_{n-3}\subset S_n$"). *Reason:* unchanged.

34. **Scans 549–550, a renormalising transposition. Own idea (first pass). Keep.** With $s_1,s_2,s_3$ normalised to $\infty,0,1$ and $s_4$ at $a$, exchanging $s_3$ and $s_4$ gives $x\mapsto x/a$ on the other points and $a\mapsto1/a$. *Reason:* consistent with the corrected text; the check is sound as stated.

35. **Scans 549–550, the case $n=4$. Own idea (first pass). Keep.** Compute the $\mathfrak S_4$ action on the fourth coordinate, check that the double transpositions act trivially, and compare $\mathfrak S_4/V_4\simeq\mathfrak S_3$ with (27). *Reason:* this is what "l'action naturelle de $\mathfrak S_4$ sur $M^!_{0,4}$" (note 29) asks for. In the cross-ratio picture the Klein group does act trivially on $\lambda$, which is classical.

No first-pass note is dropped: each still matches the corrected text, and the corrections above change their wording or settle their open points.

## Second-pass notes

A. **Scan 544, Corollary. Claim checked (second pass).** The ink gives the stabilisers in $\Gamma'_n$ as $\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z$ and $\mathbb Z/3\mathbb Z$, whereas all three witnesses print $\mathbb Z/4\mathbb Z$ and $\mathbb Z/6\mathbb Z$. The ink is the mathematically right statement: by (15) every stabiliser in $\Gamma_n$ contains $-1$, which generates the kernel $\{\pm1\}$ of $\Gamma_n\to\Gamma'_n$. Confidence high.

B. **Scans 545 and 547, freeness of $\Gamma'_{2m,2}$ and the gerbe. Claim checked (second pass).** Over an algebraically closed field of characteristic $\ne2$, the kernel of $\operatorname{Aut}(E)\to\operatorname{GL}(E[2])$ is $\{\pm1\}$. For $y^2=x^3-x$, the automorphism $(x,y)\mapsto(-x,iy)$ swaps $(1,0)$ and $(-1,0)$. For $y^2=x^3+1$, the order-3 automorphisms permute the three 2-torsion points cyclically. In characteristic 3, $x\mapsto x+1$ and $x\mapsto -x$ make the group of order 12 surject onto $\mathfrak S_3$. Hence $\Gamma'_{2m,2}$ acts freely on $M_{1,1}[2m]$ over $\mathbb Z[1/2m]$, as (19) asserts, and the inertia of $M_{1,1}[2]$ is $\pm1$ everywhere, as the $\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z$-gerbe of scan 547 requires. Confidence high.

C. **Scan 550, $I_{n-3}$. Claim checked (second pass).** $I_{n-3}=\{i\in\mathbb N\mid 3<i\le n\}=\{4,\dots,n\}$ has $n-3$ elements. This matches $\dim M^!_{0,n}=n-3$ and the factor $U_{0,3}^{I_{n-3}}$, and the ink has $<$ in both definitions. The witnesses' "$3\le i\le n$" in the first definition would give $n-2$ elements and is wrong. Confidence high.

D. **Scan 550, $\underline{\operatorname{Mon}}(I_{n-3},U_{0,3})$. Own idea (second pass).** Read Mon as the scheme of injections of the finite set $I_{n-3}$ into $U_{0,3}$, an open subscheme of $U_{0,3}^{I_{n-3}}$, as the "$\subset$" in (30) indicates. The identification sends a curve with sections to the coordinates of $s_4,\dots,s_n$ after the unique projective transformation that puts $s_1,s_2,s_3$ at $0,1,\infty$ in some fixed order. Disjointness of the sections is exactly injectivity together with avoidance of $0,1,\infty$. $\mathfrak S_3$ then acts diagonally through the six Möbius maps permuting $\{0,1,\infty\}$, and $\mathfrak S_{n-3}$ acts by permuting $I_{n-3}$. Confidence medium-high.

E. **Scan 548, the bracketed expectation. Claim checked (second pass).** "Si cependant on exclut les courbes elliptiques anharmoniques, ça marcherait" is correct, and not only in characteristic 0. Over any field, the curves with automorphisms other than $\pm1$ are those with $j=0$ or $j=1728$, which coincide in characteristics 2 and 3. Removing the inverse images of the two sections of note 5 therefore leaves a $\Gamma'_n$-stable open set on which $\Gamma'_n$ acts freely. There (24) restricts to an equivalence over all of $\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z[1/n]$. Confidence high.

F. **Scan 549, base of (25). Unpursued implication (second pass).** With the base read as $\mathbb Z[1/2]$ in both places, the margin's comparison of images in $\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z$ says that $U_{0,3}=M^!_{0,4}$ extends over the prime 2 while $M_{1,1}[2]'$ does not. The text does not pursue the question of what, on the elliptic side, the fibre of $U_{0,3}$ at 2 corresponds to. Level structures defined only over $\mathbb Z[1/2m]$, such as $M_{1,1}[4]$ or $M_{1,1}[6]$, do not reach the prime 2. A comparison would need level-2 structures that make sense in characteristic 2, such as Drinfeld-style ones in the sense of Katz–Mazur. Confidence low to medium; this is a direction, not a claim.


# ===== LM_P056 (checked notes) =====

# LM_P056 (scans 551–560): research notes, checked against the corrected text

Each first-pass note from `answers\unzipped\LM_P056\LM_P056_notes.md` §5 is listed with its scan, kind and verdict (keep, correct, drop) and the reason. Notes added in the second pass follow in part B and are labelled as such. Readings quoted are those of `LM_P056_checked.tex`. The text runs 553 → 554 → 551 → 552 → 555 (see the head of `LM_P056_changes.md`), so the "gymnastique" question on 553 comes before the partition construction on 554 and 551.

## A. First-pass notes

**A1. Scan 551, (33), unproved claim. Keep.** The elements of 𝔖_I attached to the three partitions of type (2,2) are said to be the even permutations of order 2, which with the identity form V(I); no proof is written. The claim is right: the elements of order 2 in 𝔖_4 are the six transpositions (odd) and the three double transpositions (even). The reading is unchanged, except that the interlinear "(paire)" after "permutation" may be live (see changes no. 2).

**A2. Scan 551, (34)–(36), unproved claim. Keep, wording corrected.** The text reads "Cette bijection étant compatible avec le transport de structure(s)", not "des structures". The map (34) is first called "application" and then "bijection"; that the isomorphism (35) equals the composite (36) depends on (34) being equivariant for the stabiliser of i, which is asserted, not checked.

**A3. Scan 551, last line, own idea (first pass). Keep, reclassified as a slip in the manuscript.** The ink and all three witnesses read "d'indice 3", but the stabiliser 𝔖_{I∖{i}} has order 6 in a group of order 24, so its index is 4. The transcription keeps "3".

**A4. Scan 552, "syst. transitif d'isom.", unproved claim. Correct.** The inserted sentence "Cette bijection en effet est le composé I∖{i} → Ĩ → I∖{j}" is itself the argument: bijections that all factor through the one set Ĩ compose transitively. What the text does not check is that (37) is the transposition on I∖{i,j}; that check is B1.

**A5. Scan 552, Proposition, unproved claim. Correct.** The text returns to the Proposition on 556: "Il faut quand m̄ démontrer la prop. 4, qui équivaut bien sûr à ceci", followed by the four-section corollary and its proof by u(z) = λ/z, λ = s_3 s_4, after sending two sections to 0 and ∞. So a proof is given; what stays implicit is the equivalence (see B4) and the passage from the normalised local case to an arbitrary base.

**A6. Scan 552, Corollaire, unpursued implication. Keep.** The corollary makes the comparison of the stabiliser actions go through 𝔖_Ĩ; the explicit case is (38)–(39) on 555. The first-pass caution that the canonical bijections are not the naive ones is borne out by the cancelled "ne … pas" and "égales [à l'identité]" (B1).

**A7. Scan 553, closing question, conjecture (author's question). Correct.** The ink reads "On a envie de prouver que ce sont les m̄s !", not "penser": the author wants to prove that the two actions of 𝔖_3 coincide. The answer on 555–556 is that they correspond through int(σ_1) (B2), so they agree up to conjugation by σ_1 and not literally.

**A8. Scan 553, own idea (first pass): compare the two copies of 𝔖_3 on the three partitions. Keep, now carried out.** See B2.

**A9. Scan 553, "qui fixe les trois premiers éléments", own idea (first pass). Keep, as a slip.** The ink reads "fixe" (the witnesses agree), but 𝔖_3 × 𝔖_1 ⊂ 𝔖_4 fixes the fourth element and only stabilises the set of the first three. The transcription keeps "fixe".

**A10. Scan 554, kernel of 𝔖_I → 𝔖_Ĩ and the torsor, unproved claim. Keep.** The kernel consists of the identity and the three double transpositions; each non-identity element is fixed-point-free, so V(I) acts simply transitively on I. Note the ink "i.e. un vectoriel sur F_2" (no "espace").

**A11. Scan 554, unique affine plane structure, unproved claim. Keep.** Supported by the count in B3.

**A12. Scan 554, "ce qui suggère d'interpréter Ĩ …", unpursued implication. Keep.** The suggestion is carried out at once: 554 ends "on constate que l'on a une" and continues on 551 with "bijection canonique (33)". The bijection is equivariant because conjugating the involution attached to a partition gives the involution attached to the transported partition.

**A13. Scan 554, ternary operation x + y + z, own idea (first pass). Keep (medium).** On three distinct points of the affine plane over F_2, x + y + z is the fourth point, which is a permutation-invariant description of the affine structure.

**A14. 555-R1, specified conjugacy, unpursued implication. Keep.**

**A15. 555-R2, ψ = σ_1⁻¹·φ, own idea (first pass). Keep.** The computation ψ(u.x) = σ_1⁻¹σ_1 u σ_1⁻¹ φ(x) = u ψ(x) is formal once (40) is read with σ_1; in the corollary and in (40) the index (1 or i) stays unresolved in the ink, while (39) has σ_1.

**A16. 556-R1, "prop. 4 … équivaut", unproved claim. Correct.** The reference reads "prop. 4" without doubt. The proposition on 552 carries no visible number, so "4" refers to a numbering that these pages do not show.

**A17. 556-R2, normalisation and uniqueness, unproved claim. Keep.** In addition, the ink mixes two index conventions in the proof ("s_1 = 0, s_{i_2} = ∞", then "pour échanger s_2, s_3"); see B5.

**A18. 556-R3, weakened disjointness, unpursued implication. Keep.** The second triple most likely reads (s_1, s_2, s_4) (the witnesses agree; the middle index is not settled in the ink). The proof uses only that s_3 and s_4 are units, i.e. disjoint from s_1 = 0 and s_2 = ∞, and λ/s_3 = s_4, λ/s_4 = s_3 are the same equation λ = s_3 s_4, so nothing is needed where s_3 and s_4 meet.

**A19. 556-R4, the matrix [[0, ab], [1, 0]], own idea (first pass). Keep.**

**A20. 557-R1, (41)–(42), unproved claim. Keep.** The reading "avec m̄ grain de sel pour n = 2" is now confirmed: the same proviso as in (41), where M_{1,1}[2] is replaced by M_{1,1}[2]'. Why level 2 needs it is B6.

**A21. 557-R2, connectedness and the margin note, unproved claim. Keep, reading corrected.** The margin reads "c'est le th. de Kronecker d'irréductibilité de l'équation cyclotomique" (singular). The mechanism is given in B7.

**A22. 557-R3, recovering the extension by pullback, unproved claim. Keep, with the corrected diagram.** The left vertical arrow of (43) carries the isomorphism sign ∼, not "2"; with it, the middle square is cartesian (B6), which is what "par image inverse" uses.

**A23. 557-R4, the extension class, own idea (first pass). Keep (medium).**

**A24. 558-R1, Sl(2,Z)^ against Sl(2,Ẑ), unpursued implication. Keep, made precise.** The kernel of Sl(2,Z)^ → Sl(2,Ẑ) is the congruence kernel of Sl(2,Z); it is non-trivial because Sl(2,Z) has subgroups of finite index that are not congruence subgroups. The marginal "Attention … !!!" marks exactly this.

**A25. 558-R2, the compatibility (46), unproved claim. Keep, wording corrected.** The sentence before (45) reads "et la théorie transcendante fournit des isomorphismes ext. canoniques", not "le théorème transcendant". The word after "la com-" introducing (46) is "commutativité" in the witnesses but is overwritten in the ink (changes no. 32).

**A26. 558-R3, Weil pairing and the determinant, own idea (first pass). Keep.** This is the standard reason for the right-hand square of (46): the determinant of the Galois action on E[n] is the cyclotomic character, through the Weil pairing.

**A27. 558-R4, passage to the limit (47), unproved claim. Keep.**

**A28. 559-R1, (48)–(49), unproved claim. Correct.** The text reads "Pour l'instant je vais exploiter le deuxième isomorphisme (42), n = 2", not "expliciter le dernier": the isomorphism for M'_{1,1} is used, not made explicit. In (49) the right-hand vertical is an equality, so the claimed isomorphism is one of extensions of IΓ_Q over the identity ("l'isomorphisme d'extension").

**A29. 559-R2, (50)–(51), unpursued implication. Keep.** Context corrected: "D'ailleurs, reprenant ces réflexions dans le contexte transcendant".

**A30. 560-R1, request for explicit formulas for (51), conjecture (author's request). Keep.** The text reads "alors que sa définition ici sort de façon purement conceptuelle".

**A31. 560-R2, "ne devrait pas présenter de difficulté particulière", conjecture (author's expectation). Keep.** "difficulté" is probably underlined in the ink (changes no. 44).

**A32. 560-R3, a candidate computation for (51), own idea (first pass). Keep (medium).**

**A33. 560-R4, the coarse scheme ≃ E¹_Z with two marked sections, unproved claim. Correct.** The sections are described by their values "en car. 0" (in characteristic 0), not "en ℂ", and "deux" is inserted after a cancelled "aux". The exponent of E is 1.

**A34. 560-R5, the fibres at 2 and 3, own idea (first pass). Keep, now answered.** See B9.

## B. Notes added in the second pass

**B1. Scan 552, claim verified (second pass).** The cancelled "ne … pas" and "égales [à l'identité]" show that Grothendieck first wrote that the isomorphisms between the four stabilisers are not induced by the bijections equal to the identity on I∖{i,j}, and then that they are induced by the canonical bijections (37), equal to the transposition there. Check for i = 4, j = 1: through Ĩ, 1 ↦ {1,4}|{2,3} ↦ 4, 2 ↦ {2,4}|{1,3} ↦ 3, 3 ↦ {3,4}|{1,2} ↦ 2, the bijection written on 555, which is the transposition of {2,3} = I∖{1,4}.

**B2. Scans 553 and 555, claim verified (second pass).** Identifying [1,2,3] and [2,3,4] with (0,1,∞) in increasing order turns 1 ↦ 4, 2 ↦ 3, 3 ↦ 2 into 0 ↦ ∞, 1 ↦ 1, ∞ ↦ 0, the transposition of the elements ≠ 1, that is σ_1. This confirms int(σ_1) in (39) and answers the question of 553: the two actions of 𝔖_3 are conjugate by σ_1 rather than equal.

**B3. Scan 554, unproved claim supported (second pass).** The affine group AGL(2,F_2) = F_2² ⋊ GL(2,F_2) has order 4 · 6 = 24 and acts faithfully on the four points, so it is all of 𝔖_I: every permutation is affine. GL(2,F_2) acts faithfully on the three non-zero vectors, which gives 𝔖_Ĩ ≅ Aut(V(I)) through (33).

**B4. Scan 556, implication (second pass).** "équivaut bien sûr": since M!_{0,I} represents the functor (rigid for card I ≥ 3), an element σ of 𝔖_I acts trivially if and only if every family (X, (s_i)) is isomorphic to its relabelling (X, (s_{σi})), i.e. there is an automorphism u of X with u ∘ s_i = s_{σi}. The uniqueness of u, noted as "évidemment unique", is what lets the local isomorphisms glue, so the corollary on 556 for the three even involutions is the Proposition of 552.

**B5. Scan 556, slip (second pass).** The proof writes "s_1 = 0, s_{i_2} = ∞", then "… , s_{i_3} s'identifient à des sections de O_S^*", then "pour échanger s_2, s_3" followed by λ/s_3 = s_4, λ/s_4 = s_3. The prose indices should be those of the two remaining sections (s_3, s_4, or s_{i_3}, s_{i_4}); the transcription keeps the ink and the witnesses normalise it.

**B6. Scan 557, own idea (second pass, medium).** In (43) the left vertical arrow is an isomorphism, so a diagram chase shows that the upper extension is the pullback of the lower one along Γ_n → Γ'_n = Γ_n/±1; the kernel of π_1(M_{1,1}) → π_1(M'_{1,1}) is then {±1}, central because −1 is central in GL(2, Z/n). This is the Proposition "extension centrale … par {±1}". For n = 2 one has −1 = 1 in GL(2, F_2), so Γ_2 = Γ'_2 and the sign cannot be seen at level 2: the reconstruction must use n ≥ 3 (as in the first line of (41)), and level 2 needs the replacement M_{1,1}[2]' ("m̄ grain de sel pour n = 2").

**B7. Scan 557, unproved claim supported (second pass).** The Weil pairing of a basis of E[n] is a primitive n-th root of unity, so the components of M_{1,1}[n] over Q̄ are indexed by the φ(n) primitive n-th roots of unity, and Gal(Q̄/Q) permutes them transitively exactly because the cyclotomic polynomial Φ_n is irreducible over Q. Hence M_{1,1}[n] is connected but, for n ≥ 3, not geometrically connected, which is the content of the margin note.

**B8. Scan 559, slip (second pass).** The annotation over π_1(M̄'_{1,1}) in (49) reads "≃ Sl(2,Z)^" without the prime, while (45) and (50) identify this group with Sl(2,Z)'^ = Sl(2,Z)^/±1. Since M'_{1,1} is the quotient by −1, the primed group is the right one; the transcription keeps the ink.

**B9. Scan 560, own idea (second pass, high).** If the coordinate on the coarse scheme is the j-invariant, the two marked sections are j = 0 and j = 1728 (the curves with automorphism groups of orders 6 and 4), and since 1728 = 2⁶ · 3³ they meet exactly over the primes 2 and 3. This agrees with the next manuscript page as given by the CSG witness ("elles se rencontrent en caractéristique 2 et en caractéristique 3"), and the integer "±2^a 3^b" that the author says he does not know by heart there would be 1728, i.e. a = 6, b = 3, once j = 0 is taken as the zero section and the sign is fixed as plus.


# ===== LM_P057api (checked notes) =====

# LM_P057api (scans 561–570): research notes checked against the corrected text

Each first-pass research note (R1–R23, from `LM_P057_notes.md`, section 4) is checked against `LM_P057api_checked.tex` and kept, corrected or dropped, with a reason. Quotations follow the corrected transcription. "Own idea" notes R11, R17 and R21 remain the first-pass model's ideas. Notes added in this pass are labelled "second pass (own)". The first pass's table of doubts and list of abbreviations are superseded by `LM_P057api_changes.md`.

These pages lie in §37 of the author's later table of contents, "Modules des courbes elliptiques via Legendre, ou M1,1[2]′ ≃ U0,3 = M0,4", which starts at ms p. 272. The present pages are ms pp. 285–289.

## First-pass notes

**R1. Scan 561. Unproved claim. Kept.**
"Il est d'ailleurs facile de vérifier a priori … que $\widetilde M_{1,1}$ sur Spec Z tt entier est de la forme $Y-\mathrm{Im}\,s$". Then "ce qu'on connait p.ex. sur la fibre générique, et le fait que Z soit principal, impliquent que $Y\simeq\mathbb P^1_{\mathbb Z}$". *Reason:* the wording is confirmed ("générique", not the witnesses' "géométrique"). The Deligne–Mumford step and the passage to $Y\simeq\mathbb P^1_{\mathbb Z}$ are asserted without proof.

**R2. Scans 561 and 563. Implication not pursued. Corrected (quotation).**
The section of the points at infinity is called ∞, "non par 0, comme en théorie des cartes !". The isomorphism on 563 sends $(0,1,\infty)$ to ∞ and $(j,\bar j)$ to 0, the reverse of the carte normalisation $f$, whence $j=2^a3^bf^{-1}$ in (57). *Reason:* the first pass read "non p."; the ink has "non par".

**R3. Scan 562. Unproved claim. Kept.**
"Ces deux sections ne sont pas disjointes, elles se rencontrent en car. 2 et car. 3". Only the coincidence in characteristic 3 is explained, through (53), which is valid over Z[1/2]. Characteristic 2 is asserted. *Reason:* the reading is unchanged.

**R4. Scans 562–563. Unproved claim. Kept.**
The choice of zero section determines the isomorphism "au signe près", and the sign is fixed by asking for "plus". The Théorème (54) is stated "pour mémoire - c'est bien connu". *Reason:* the reading is unchanged. Existence of the integral coordinate rests on R1, uniqueness on the automorphisms ±1 of $\mathbb E^1_{\mathbb Z}$ that fix 0.

**R5. Scans 562, 563 and the 565 margin. Implication not pursued. Corrected.**
The integers a, b are "bien déterminés dont je ne sais pas la valeur par cœur" (562) and "par moi oubliés" (563). The 565 margin reads "et en particulier pour déterminer l'autre invariant modulaire critique $2^a3^3$, i.e. déterminer a". *Reason:* the first pass had "j'ai" plus three unread words, and an uncertain "l'entier invariant" with two unread words. These pages fix b = 3 but not a (see S2).

**R6. Scan 564, (58). Unproved claim ("on trouve immédiatement"). Kept, with a check.**
$f(z)=\frac{3^3}{2^2}\frac{z^2(z-1)^2}{(z^2-z+1)^3}$ has double zeros at 0, 1 and ∞ and triple poles at the roots of $z^2-z+1$, so it has degree 6. A direct computation gives $f(2)=f(-1)=f(1/2)=1$. The "normalisation" on the sections $2,-1,1/2$ is therefore $f=1$ there, which matches "(et non 1)" on 563.

**R7. Scan 564. Unproved claim. Kept.**
"Comme $j(\lambda)$ doit garder un sens en car. 3 et ne pas être constante, cela montre d'ailleurs b=3". The argument needs (59) to be the reduction of an integral coordinate near 3, which is asserted. *Reason:* the reading is unchanged.

**R8. Scan 565. Programme. Corrected.**
To describe (60) "aussi au voisinage de 2 - disons en car. ≠ 3", one needs a study that no longer goes through the Legendre form, "ou encore, par la rigidification de Jacobi d'échelon 2, mais par contre par celle d'échelon 3". *Reason:* the first pass read "points d'ordre 2 … par exemple … d'ordre 3". The ink names Jacobi rigidifications of level 2 and level 3 and sets them against each other, so level 3 is the stated alternative, not one example among others.

**R9. Scans 565–566. Unproved claim. Corrected (quotations).**
The heading is "Remarques", and the text reads "On voit tout de suite que le quotient $\mathbb P^1_{\mathbb Z}/\mathfrak S_3$ s'identifie à $\mathbb P^1_{\mathbb Z}$", "car on note que …" and "On voit donc a priori que la troisième section sera de la forme $2^\alpha/3^\beta$". A construction of the quotient over Z and the claimed coincidences in characteristics 2 and 3 are asserted, not proved. *Reason:* the first pass had "Remarque" and twice "sait".

**R10. Scan 566, (61)–(63). Corrected.**
The ink settles the exponent in (62) as 2: $f_1(\lambda)=\lambda^2(\lambda-1)^2/(\lambda^2-\lambda+1)^2=\frac{2^2}{3^3}f(\lambda)$. With (58) the right-hand side has the denominator $(\lambda^2-\lambda+1)^3$, so the middle expression is the author's slip. With the cube, $f_1(-1)=4\cdot1/27=2^2/3^3$, as in (63); with the square one would get $4/9$. The conclusion "$\alpha=2$, $\beta=3$" is unaffected.

**R11. Scan 566, last paragraph. Own idea (first pass). Corrected.**
The ink reads "Mais on fera attention qu'au voisinage de la car. 2, ces calculs ne se rapportent plus à $\widetilde M_{1,1}$ et $\widetilde{\widetilde M}_{1,1}$ (or $\widetilde{\widehat M}_{1,1}$), où la configuration des trois sections n'est pas la même qu'ici". The first pass read "ces calculs faits au voisinage de la car. 2". The idea of comparing the integral models locally at 2 still makes sense; S4 makes the difference in configuration explicit. Confidence: medium.

**R12. Scan 567, (64)–(65). Programme. Corrected.**
"$\Gamma_4=\mathrm{Gl}(2,\mathbb Z/4)$ via $\Gamma'_4=\Gamma_4/\pm1$": the group acts through its quotient by ±1, which acts trivially. At the foot of the page the reworked text reads "On a une factorisation de $M_{1,1}[4]\to M_{1,1}[2]'$", followed by (67). *Reason:* the first pass read "ou $\Gamma'_4$" and left the reworking largely unread.

**R13. Scan 567, (66). Unproved claim. Corrected.**
The left margin states the structure of the kernel: "$\mathrm{Ker}(\mathrm{Gl}(2,\mathbb Z/4\mathbb Z)\to\mathrm{Gl}(2,\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z))$ est un groupe commutatif, et m̄ un vectoriel sur $\mathbb F_2$ - il est isom. à $\mathbb F_2^4$". This agrees with $|\Gamma_4|=96=16\cdot6$ and with $|\Gamma'_{4,2}|=8$. The failure of geometric connectedness over Q comes from the determinant $\Gamma_4\to(\mathbb Z/4\mathbb Z)^*$ (Weil pairing values in $\mu_4^*$). *Reason:* the first pass read the margin as mostly illegible and ending in $\mathbb F_2^2$.

**R14. Scan 568, (68). Unproved claim. Kept, with a check.**
$I+2A\mapsto A \bmod 2$ identifies $S\Gamma_{4,2}$ with trace-zero matrices, since $\det(I+2A)\equiv1+2\,\mathrm{tr}A \pmod 4$ and $(I+2A)(I+2B)\equiv I+2(A+B)$. The "ss-gp ±1" is $\{I, I+2I\}$, the scalars, and the quotient has order 4. This is consistent with "qui est d'ordre 4".

**R15. Scan 568, last paragraph. Implication not pursued. Corrected (quotation).**
The text reads "on trouve géométriquement "le" revêtement abélien universel de $U_{0,3}$ de groupe annulé par 2 (et pour cause)". The quotation marks enclose the article "le", so the author flags the "the" himself. The note's point about base points and canonicity over S′ stands. *Reason:* the first pass placed the opening quote before "revêtement".

**R16. Scan 569. Proposed calculation, not carried out. Corrected (quotation).**
"un calcul amusant à faire sera de "redécrire l'action de $\mathrm{Sl}(2,\mathbb F_2)$ sur $\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb F_2)$/matrices scalaires" de façon à l'identifier à l'action de $\mathfrak S_3$ sur $\mathbb F_2^{\{0,1,\infty\}}$/diagonale". What is wanted is an equivariant identification, not an abstract one. *Reason:* the "re" of "redécrire" is inserted in the ink.

**R17. Scan 569. Own idea (first pass). Kept; checked in this pass.**
Put $\varphi_A(v)=\det(v,Av)$ on the three nonzero $v\in\mathbb F_2^2$. Then $\varphi_{gAg^{-1}}(v)=\varphi_A(g^{-1}v)$, scalars give 0, and on $(e_1,e_2,e_1+e_2)$ one gets $\varphi_E=(0,1,1)$ and $\varphi_F=(1,0,1)$. The image is therefore the even-weight plane, which maps isomorphically onto $\mathbb F_2^3/\mathrm{diag}$ because $(1,1,1)$ has odd weight. This gives an $\mathrm{Sl}(2,\mathbb F_2)\simeq\mathfrak S_3$-equivariant isomorphism; which bijection $\mathbb P^1(\mathbb F_2)\leftrightarrow\{0,1,\infty\}$ matches the author's labels is not fixed here.

**R18. Scan 569, (70). Conjecture. Corrected.**
The sentence reads "Ce serait bien rare (or venu) qu'on n'ait pas un isom (70) $\mathrm{Sl}(2,\mathbb Z/4\mathbb Z)'\simeq\mathfrak S_4$ … et que $M_{1,1}[4]_{S'}$ en soit le revêtement de $\mathbb P^1_{S'}\setminus\{0,1,\infty\}$, avec ramification compatible avec $2\{1\}+3\{\infty\}$". "étale" is struck. With "rare" the expectation is affirmative, in line with "on devrait avoir … un isom. canonique". The group isomorphism itself is the standard $\mathrm{PSL}(2,\mathbb Z/4)\simeq\mathfrak S_4$ (order 24). *Reason:* the first pass kept "étale" and marked the whole clause "qu'on n'ait pas" as uncertain; that clause is clear, and only the word after "bien" is not.

**R19. Scan 569, (71). Conjecture. Kept; margin quotation corrected.**
"on devrait avoir sur $S'=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb Z[1/2][i]$ un isom. canonique" with the Fermat conic. The margin reads "il vaudrait mieux écrire l'équation de Fermat ici $x_0^2+x_1^2+x_\infty^2=0$", which indexes the coordinates by the three cusps. Canonicity still has to be checked against the choice of $i$ and the level structure.

**R20. Scans 569–570. Implication not pursued. Kept, with a check.**
Requiring $x,y,z\in\Gamma(S,\mathcal O_S^*)$ removes the six points where a coordinate vanishes. Over S′, each coordinate line meets $x^2+y^2+z^2=0$ in two points ($y=\pm iz$, and so on). Six punctures match the six cusps of level 4 (24 divided by the cusp width 4).

**R21. Scan 570, (72)–(73). Own idea (first pass). Kept; algebra checked.**
From $X^2+Y^2=-1$, putting $\lambda=-X^2$ gives $1-\lambda=-Y^2$. So the open conic is the $(\mathbb Z/2)^2$-cover obtained by adjoining $\sqrt\lambda$ and $\sqrt{1-\lambda}$ (up to the factor $i$). Matching it with the author's level-4 conventions remains open.

**R22. Scan 570, opening paragraph. Conjecture. Corrected (quotation).**
The text asks for the relation between curves with a Jacobi level-4 rigidification, $e_1\wedge e_2=[i]^{-1}$, and the non-trivial unit solutions of (72) modulo a common scalar $\alpha$. It goes on: "- ou encore, rompant la symétrie", the equivalent affine form (73). *Reason:* the first pass read "en commun, rompant".

**R23. Scan 570, last paragraph. Unproved claim. Corrected.**
The ink reads "en relation avec deux cartes triangulaires pondérées régulières - la carte "dihédrale" … ou carte triangulaire pondérée universelle (…, type $(p,q)=(3,3)$), et la carte octogonale". The first pass had "(3,2)" and two unread words, both "pondérée(s)". "octogonale" is the ink; it is discussed in S7.

## Second-pass notes (own)

**S1. Scan 561. Claim without proof (second pass, own).**
"dont l'existence implique déjà que Y est globalmt isom. à $\mathbb P^1_S$". A form of $\mathbb P^1$ with a section is $\mathbb P(E)$ for a rank-2 vector bundle E, and it is isomorphic to $\mathbb P^1_S$ when E is a twist of the trivial bundle. That holds here because $\mathrm{Pic}(\mathbb Z[1/2])=0$; over a base with non-trivial Picard group the section alone would not suffice.

**S2. Scans 562–565. Implication not pursued, with a check (second pass, own).**
The classical value $j(i)=1728=2^6\cdot3^3$ would give $a=6$ together with the $b=3$ found on 564. With it, (59) becomes the Legendre formula $j=2^8(\lambda^2-\lambda+1)^3/(\lambda^2(\lambda-1)^2)$. These pages do not establish a; the author defers it to the study near 2 (565 margin).

**S3. Scan 565. Slip in a formula (second pass, own).**
As written, "$y^{2}=\sqrt{x(x-1)(x-\lambda)}$" combines the Legendre equation $y^2=x(x-1)(x-\lambda)$ with its solved form $y=\sqrt{\cdots}$. The slip is harmless; it is transcribed as written.
